# 64. Decisions recovered from code comments: the controllers

Date: 2026-11-22

## Status

Accepted. This record is a catalog produced by the comment ban in
[ADR 0062](0062-code-carries-no-comments.md). Each entry was a code comment that recorded a
non-obvious decision in `internal/controller`. The comment was deleted, and the decision now lives here.

## Context

Round 2 of the comment cleanup classified every comment left after
[ADR 0063](0063-code-comment-decisions-round-1.md). In `internal/controller` it found 2366 distinct decisions
that a reader of the code alone could get wrong. Comments that stated the same rule in several
places are merged into one entry. Where an earlier ADR already records a decision, the entry
links it.

## Decision

Each entry names the symbols it governs, so `rg '<symbol>'` finds the code. Entries are
grouped by package and ordered by source position as of round 2.

### `internal/controller`

- **ACP rejects enabled Agent.spec.tools** (`controller.validateACPAgentTools`): validateACPAgentTools rejects any enabled Agent.spec.tools reference for built-in ACP runtimes. Agent.spec.tools is additive for the managed AI worker, but a non-empty ACP allowlist replaces the provider-native default set, so merging would silently drop native tools with no defined precedence; explicitly disabled references are inert and ignored.
- **Codex supports only the Glob/Grep/Read read-only policy** (`controller.codexReadOnlyNativePolicy`): codexReadOnlyNativePolicy accepts only the exact {Glob, Grep, Read} native surface because codex has no per-tool switches; the RuntimeSession boundary instead rejects elevation requests, mediates writes through the supervisor, and fails read-intent turns whose workspace delta shows a modification. Brokered and custom tool names are ignored because the MCP broker enforces them.
- **OpenCode system prompts need no provider validation** (`controller.validateACPProviderSystemPrompt`): validateACPProviderSystemPrompt performs no check for opencode because the supervisor delivers its instructions as literal, protected additive instructions rather than through a size-limited environment or provider config.
- **Continuations run only after the predecessor links them** (`controller.TaskReconciler.continuationLinkPending`): continuationLinkPending holds a continuation Task until its parked predecessor records this exact Task in its resumed wait; until then the continuation provably never ran, so abandoning it discards no work. It reports pending while the predecessor still reserves the Task and orphaned once nothing can link it; reads are authoritative and a failed read holds. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Continuation link checks use one predecessor snapshot** (`controller.TaskReconciler.continuationLinkPending`): continuationLinkPending decides from a single uncached read of the predecessor Task so that a link completed between two reads is never mistaken for an orphan.
- **Unlinked continuations are cancelled directly** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelUnlinkedContinuation`): cancelUnlinkedContinuation cancels a continuation its predecessor can no longer link directly, like a queued Task past its deadline, without a prompt attempt or Session transcript, because the continuation never ran.
- **Workspace credential Secret versions are frozen at reservation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.freezeWorkspaceCredentialVersions`): freezeWorkspaceCredentialVersions records only the non-secret resourceVersions of workspace credential Secrets at reservation. The credential broker later requires the exact version to remain current, so rotation after reservation fails closed instead of silently changing the operation's authority.
- **Prompt streams run in a leader-elected dispatcher, not reconcile** (`controller.ACPDispatcher`): ACPDispatcher owns long-lived v2 prompt streams outside reconcile workers: Task reconciliation only persists queued demand, while this leader-elected runnable reserves capacity and advances the durable attempt state machine, so reconcile workers never block on network streams.
- **Stale recovery stops before the dispatcher exits** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.Start`): ACPDispatcher.Start acquires staleRecoveryMu on shutdown so any periodic recovery using the Start context finishes before the dispatcher relinquishes its controller lifecycle, preventing recovery from running after leadership is lost.
- **Terminal recovery owns cleanup for deleting Tasks** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.scheduleACPDeliveryRecoveries`): scheduleACPDeliveryRecoveries does not require a cleanup receipt for deleting Tasks because current-epoch terminal recovery itself owns their pending runtime cleanup; requiring the receipt first would block the retries that produce it.
- **Stale-epoch recovery runs outside dispatch slots** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.scheduleACPDeliveryRecoveries`): scheduleACPDeliveryRecoveries skips Tasks from an older controller epoch or with unvalidated source identity because the separate stale scan owns their recovery while that epoch is outside admission, so their network waits never consume dispatch slots.
- **Idle reaping skips pools mid cold-resume** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapIdlePools`): reapIdlePools skips a workspace-backed pool whose adapter lifted suspension and raised replicas for a cold resume before the continuation attached or registered demand, because scaling it to zero would recycle the sole resumed checkpoint mid-transition.
- **Pending Suspend blocks idle scale-down** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.workspaceResumeTransitionPending`): workspaceResumeTransitionPending holds a pool whose frozen action is Suspend before DesiredState flips, because settlement can be delayed past IdlePoolTTL and ordinary scale-down in that window would delete the actor before the requested checkpoint exists.
- **Resume hold applies regardless of attachment** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.workspaceResumeTransitionPending`): workspaceResumeTransitionPending holds suspended, suspending, or resumed-lineage workspaces even with no attachment, because the attachment is persisted before queueACPRuntimeTask writes the pool label and execution status; in that gap neither shields the restored pool and scale-to-zero would recycle the sole restored checkpoint.
- **Only stopped workspace pools are deleted by the reaper** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool deletes a scaled-to-zero workspace-backed pool only after it proved Stopped and stayed idle another TTL; recovery treats a missing pool as proof of RuntimeSession cleanup and fresh demand recreates it by name. Plain pools are shared infrastructure and are never deleted here.
- **Class-backed pools are torn down via their workspace** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool deletes a class-backed pool's ExecutionWorkspace rather than the pool, so the controller-first workspace lifecycle records the terminal disposition and the ACP workspace adapter then deletes the pool; only pools with no linked workspace are deleted directly.
- **Stale pools never delete a new workspace incarnation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool returns without deleting when the linked workspace is a different incarnation than the pool is pinned to, such as a Session recreated under the same name, so a stale pool never deletes the new incarnation's workspace.
- **Idle reaper retains suspended workspaces** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool never retires a suspended or suspending workspace because it is retained for cold resume and retention machinery owns its expiry; a suspension that settled Failed preserved no checkpoint, so its stopped pool, template, and Secrets are reaped.
- **Idle reaper skips workspaces flipped Ready for resume** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool skips a workspace a continuation flipped to Ready for cold resume before it attached or registered pool demand, since the adapter may already be restoring the actor and deletion would destroy the sole resumed checkpoint.
- **Class idle timeout is the earliest reap time** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool treats the frozen class Lifecycle.IdleTimeout as the earliest allowed idle transition; the global pool reaper may run later but must never delete a workspace before class policy allows.
- **Attached workspaces are never idle-reaped** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool skips any workspace with an attachment because the attachment can exist before its Task acquires the pool label, so a zero active count does not prove idleness; the attached Task's own settlement owns that workspace's retirement.
- **Quarantined workspaces are not idle-reaped** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool never deletes a quarantined workspace because detach-timeout settlement deliberately preserved it as fail-closed evidence; pool teardown stays with quarantine settlement.
- **Workspace reap deletes use UID and resourceVersion preconditions** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapStoppedWorkspacePool`): reapStoppedWorkspacePool deletes the workspace with UID and resourceVersion preconditions so a Task attaching between the idle check and the delete turns the race into a retried conflict instead of deleting a newly attached workspace.
- **Resident sessions block scale-down only for plain pools** (`controller.runtimePoolHasActiveDemand`): runtimePoolHasActiveDemand ignores resident sessions for workspace-backed pools because they are single-session physical workspaces whose authenticated scale-down drain retires an idle reusable RuntimeSession; plain shared pools must stay resident while any RuntimeSession remains.
- **Only the controller writes the cancel-request annotation** (`controller.acpTaskCancelRequested`): acpTaskCancelRequested trusts the cancel-requested annotation as an API cancel request because only the controller identity may write that annotation.
- **A recorded cancel request stops the run immediately** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.watchTaskCancellation`): watchTaskCancellation stops the run as soon as a cancel request is recorded, as deletion does, whether or not the Cancelled status was written, because that status write can keep failing while the agent's work would otherwise continue.
- **Dispatch authority comes only from the frozen snapshot** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.loadVerifiedACPDispatchExecution`): loadVerifiedACPDispatchExecution re-establishes dispatch authority after pool reservation and immediately before the first supervisor RPC, deliberately not reading Agent or Tool resources; every executable value comes from the encrypted, content-addressed snapshot.
- **A claimed serving pool keeps its frozen plan** (`controller.validateFrozenACPDispatchPlan`): validateFrozenACPDispatchPlan accepts the frozen plan when dispatch already claimed the exact serving pool, because queue reconciliation moves an unbound Reserved attempt to the current image only after that pool stops admitting; this avoids stranding the attempt between rotations.
- **Requester Tasks reject pre-approving runtimes** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask rejects a Requester Task on any runtime that would pre-approve its tools, so permission decisions always reach the requester.
- **Retry failed parked releases before claiming a slot** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask retries a parked turn's failed RuntimeSession release before the new turn claims a resident slot, whether or not one is free, because the failed release still holds a slot. See ADR 0041. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Cancellation supersedes admission requeue** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask clears the Session admission requeue when the run was cancelled so the deferred Session reconciler finalizes the open turn and releases its mutation lease instead of requeueing it.
- **Durable-resume expectation is not part of the binding digest** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask stamps the durable-resume expectation after computing the workspace binding digest because it asserts a transient lineage property (a committed checkpoint must exist), not workspace identity, so it must never change which pool binding a Session reuses.
- **Task-scoped sessions record workspace digest before creation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask records RuntimeSessionWorkspaceDigest on the Task before creating a task-scoped RuntimeSession, because it has no Session binding to carry workspace identity and recovery of an abandoned validation barrier must prove the rebuilt baseline matches the one the session was created with.
- **Pre-submission requeue keeps its RuntimeSession** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask skips RuntimeSession cleanup on a pre-submission requeue because the requeue preserves the exact binding for adoption; retiring it would strand the retry on a tombstone.
- **Adopt sessions created by an earlier send of the same attempt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): When create-session returns a digest conflict, executeReservedTask adopts the session an earlier send of the same attempt created if the runtime still reports it admissible, because the reconcile rebuilt the request with a fresh expiry and workspace capability; adoption is bounded by the remaining creation budget measured from when the earlier send entered Creating.
- **Tombstoned create conflicts fail instead of requeueing** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask fails the attempt rather than requeueing when the conflicting create record is a deletion tombstone, because that generation can never be recreated and planned-session reconciliation only advances generations for reused sessions, so a requeue would rebuild the same conflict forever; an inconclusive window still requeues.
- **Session high-water mark lives on the durable Session** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask persists the RuntimeSession high-water mark on the durable Session only after the provider session is proven live, so it survives controller restarts, physical runtime replacement, and deletion of prior Task objects.
- **Durable checkpoint stamp runs at the convergence point** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.markLinkedWorkspaceDurableSessionCommitted`): executeReservedTask calls markLinkedWorkspaceDurableSessionCommitted after both the create and reuse branches converge and before any prompt submission, because the stamp gates resume verification and a missed stamp would fail open, letting a lost snapshot be silently replaced by a fresh baseline. Failure requeues and the stamp is idempotent.
- **Identity claims are reconfirmed before prompt submission** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.confirmIdentityWhilePlanned`): executeReservedTask reconfirms the Agent identity claim while the attempt is still Planned because admission and any capacity wait can outlast the claim; an unreadable claim then requeues an attempt that was provably never sent. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Toolbox note is sent once per provider process** (`controller.acpToolboxNoteDue`): The toolbox prompt note is built only from the frozen profile and sent once per provider process: standalone Tasks always get it, and a Session runtime gets it until its current process accepted a prompt, so a pre-submission retry on a never-prompted process still sends it.
- **Admission resubmission requires a live identity claim** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.identityUnavailableBeforeResubmission`): executeReservedTask resubmits a rate-limited prompt only while the identity claim still covers the Task; a claim that stays unreadable requeues the attempt, relying on the rejection as proof the prompt was never accepted.
- **Unsent prompts keep their RuntimeSession for retry** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask retains the exact live RuntimeSession when no prompt request reached the runtime, so reconciliation reuses it for the same sealed prompt identity.
- **Unproven task-scoped cleanup keeps the binding** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): On an admission requeue, executeReservedTask retires the task-scoped RuntimeSession and records its cleanup receipt while the Task still names it; if cleanup cannot be proven the Task keeps naming the session so a deferred retry or instance retirement can record the receipt, and no new instance is bound until then.
- **Commit cancelled settlement before runtime cleanup** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask finalizes a proven-cancelled PromptAttempt's Session marker and carried checkpoint before the runtime cleanup defer runs, because the earlier Session finalization defer would otherwise run only after cleanup had already failed closed.
- **Workspace validation outlives execution cancellation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask runs workspace delta validation for a completed prompt under a context detached from cancellation but bounded by the operation's own deadline, retaining the frozen cleanup authority; publication afterwards remains cancellable.
- **ACP settlement is authoritative over delivery** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask transitions the attempt to Succeeded before delivery because ACP settlement is authoritative for execution; delivery may fail or stay ambiguous but must never rewrite a successfully settled prompt into a generic execution failure.
- **Write sessions are recreated from the verified branch** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): executeReservedTask retires write-intent RuntimeSessions after publication because they are always recreated from the independently verified remote branch; task-scoped sessions are also deleted after finalization.
- **Parked turns release resident capacity under the Session lease** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.executeReservedTask`): When a read-only Session turn parks, executeReservedTask marks the binding for recreation while still holding the Session lease and releases the resident RuntimeSession, so no later turn can adopt the runtime being deleted. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Inconclusive adoption is not absence** (`controller.errRuntimeSessionAdoptionInconclusive`): errRuntimeSessionAdoptionInconclusive means the runtime did not confirm within the adoption window whether an earlier send's session is admissible; callers must never treat it as proof the session is absent.
- **Digest conflicts imply an earlier send was processed** (`controller.reconcileRuntimeSessionCreateDigestConflict`): reconcileRuntimeSessionCreateDigestConflict treats a digest_conflict as proof an earlier send of the same create identity reached the runtime: it adopts an admissible exact generation, waits while it is creating, and reports absence without polling for a deletion tombstone since a tombstoned generation can never reappear. (false, nil) means confirmed absence; an unfinished window returns errRuntimeSessionAdoptionInconclusive.
- **Creation budget starts at the runtime's original create** (`controller.waitForRuntimeSessionAdmissionState`): waitForRuntimeSessionAdmissionState bounds the wait by LastTransitionAt plus the creation timeout because Creating starts when the runtime recorded the original create, not when this reconcile observed its replay.
- **Continuations keep the chain's absolute deadline** (`controller.acpTaskDeadline`): acpTaskDeadline clamps a parked turn's continuation to its chain's absolute deadline even though its timeout is measured from its own later creation, so parking cannot extend the total run time. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Cancel request blocks admission before status write** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleTaskBeforeRuntimeAdmission`): settleTaskBeforeRuntimeAdmission requires a live read and treats a recorded cancel-request annotation as cancelled even before the controller writes the Cancelled phase, so admission stops immediately.
- **Session Tasks reserve only prompt capacity first** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reserveTask`): reserveTask claims only prompt capacity for Session Tasks because Session planning is the authority on whether an existing resident RuntimeSession can be reused; a resident slot is claimed only once the plan proves a new one is required.
- **Toolbox failure releases existing reservations** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reserveTask`): reserveTask rechecks toolbox availability after claiming because an already-existing reservation is returned without rechecking pool lifecycle; a toolbox failure after reservation or a controller restart must fail the Task instead of renewing the reservation forever.
- **Recover attempts left ahead of a Reserved Task** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.preparePromptAttemptReservation`): preparePromptAttemptReservation re-enters the idempotent recovery barrier before strict dispatch verification because the pre-submission path advances the PromptAttempt before acquiring the Session lease; a failed fenced rollback can leave the Task Reserved with the attempt one step ahead, and recovery retries the exact lease and turn without replaying a prompt.
- **Watcher cancellations settle as Cancelled** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.handlePreSubmissionContextDone`): handlePreSubmissionContextDone settles a run stopped by the cancellation watcher as Cancelled so the aborted runtime call is not reported as a start failure; other stops such as controller shutdown keep their own handling.
- **Routed native runtimes go through the Substrate router** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.runtimePoolClient`): runtimePoolClient reaches routed native runtimes only through the configured Substrate router with no HTTP client timeout, because the harness client applies its own control deadlines.
- **External runtime endpoints are revalidated and pinned per call** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.externalRuntimeClient`): externalRuntimeClient revalidates the endpoint policy immediately before sending the bearer and signed capabilities, because the Service, EndpointSlices, and backend Pods are mutable after conformance; for Service endpoints it captures verified backend Pod addresses so the authenticated connection is pinned to them rather than the mutable ClusterIP.
- **Non-Service endpoints enforce per-dial address control** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.externalRuntimeClient`): externalRuntimeClient dials Service endpoints only at verified backend Pod IPs and enforces per-dial public-address control for non-Service endpoints, because a hostname that resolved publicly at conformance can rebind to an internal address from the controller's privileged position.
- **Retained cleanup authority for external sessions** (`controller.externalRuntimeCleanupAuthority`): externalRuntimeCleanupAuthority retains the exact non-secret route and credential identity that admitted a RuntimeSession, so AgentRuntime spec changes stop new admission but never make an already-created session impossible to cancel, settle, or delete while the same runtime instance still proves ownership.
- **External runtime HTTP client sets no Client.Timeout** (`controller.externalRuntimeHTTPClient`): externalRuntimeHTTPClient leaves http.Client.Timeout unset because it includes response-body reads and would cut off long-running prompt streams; the harness client applies control timeouts to unary operations and StartPrompt inherits the Task context.
- **MaxNativeSessionBytes is negotiated, not registered** (`controller.validateExternalRuntimeCapabilities`): validateExternalRuntimeCapabilities copies MaxNativeSessionBytes from observed limits before comparing because native bundle policy is an additive negotiated capability absent from the AgentRuntime registration; the full capability envelope is still frozen and revalidated before each mutation.
- **Unknown external mutations require admission** (`controller.externalRuntimeMutationRequiresAdmission`): externalRuntimeMutationRequiresAdmission treats any unknown mutation as an admission, failing closed; this also covers create_runtime_session and start_prompt.
- **Repo-less baselines derive from Session or Task UID** (`controller.emptyRuntimeWorkspace`): emptyRuntimeWorkspace derives the repo-less baseline from the Session UID for session-bound Tasks, because every turn must present the exact baseline the session was created with, and from the Task UID otherwise.
- **patchExecution is resourceVersion-fenced and uncached** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchExecution`): patchExecution carries the read resourceVersion so a concurrent status write such as a cancellation forces a fresh read instead of being overwritten by a phase decided from stale state, and reads bypass the informer cache so cache lag after the dispatcher's own writes cannot exhaust conflict retries.
- **Lease renewal shutdown is serialized with cancellation** (`controller.newPromptLeaseContext`): newPromptLeaseContext serializes renewal shutdown with failure cancellation because the runtime context is still needed for delivery after the prompt stream ends; once stopLease returns, even a renewal past its ctx.Err check cannot cancel it, while a failure that wins the lock first still cancels the runtime.
- **Ambiguous lease renewals replay verbatim** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop keeps the exact sealed mutation of an ambiguous renewal and replays it verbatim, so a renewal the supervisor already applied is classified as a duplicate instead of a digest_conflict on a rebuilt request with fresh timestamps.
- **Expired ambiguous renewal cancels the prompt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop cancels the prompt when an ambiguous renewal can no longer be delivered before expiry, because resealing with fresh timestamps would collide with the supervisor's recorded operation if it was applied; since the mutation is sealed to outlive the retry window, reaching this point means the lease itself is about to expire.
- **Lease renewal mutations live as long as MCP authorization** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop seals renewal mutations to expire with the MCP authorization so a transient failure can replay the identical sealed request for the whole retry window instead of resealing.
- **Stream completion stops renewal without cancelling runtime** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop returns without cancelling the runtime when its context ends, because stream completion stops renewal while the runtime context must stay live for delivery.
- **Settled prompts stop renewal without cancelling** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop simply stops when the supervisor reports the prompt settled, because its terminal event is delivered or recovered through the prompt stream; cancelling the runtime context would abort that stream and turn a completed prompt into a client-error or outcome-unknown settlement.
- **Transient lease renewal failures are retried** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.renewPromptLeaseLoop`): renewPromptLeaseLoop retries transient renewal failures by replaying the identical sealed request while the current lease remains valid, so one slow or dropped renewal under load cannot cancel a healthy prompt; only definitive rejection, generation mismatch, or an expired lease ends it.
- **Lease renewal logs only the error class** (`controller.promptLeaseRenewalErrorClass`): renewPromptLeaseLoop logs only the low-cardinality error class from promptLeaseRenewalErrorClass because a supervisor rejection message is runtime-supplied text and must not reach controller logs.
- **Permission answer retries share the sealed expiry** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.resolvePermissionRequest`): resolvePermissionRequest retries only zero-write failures by reusing the same sealed operation, and bounds backoff and authority revalidation by that operation's original expiry and the caller deadline.
- **Local allow-once grants no broker approval** (`controller.frozenMCPPermissionDecision`): frozenMCPPermissionDecision may answer a provider's local permission callback with allow-once for a brokered tool, because the MCP broker still requires the controller's exact-call approval before executing it; the allow-once grants no approval authority.
- **Retire released task-scoped sessions before replanning** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleRetainedTaskScopedRuntimeSession`): settleRetainedTaskScopedRuntimeSession retires a task-scoped RuntimeSession the Task still names without a cleanup receipt before another generation is planned, because planning overwrites that binding. A binding the reserved attempt still owns is reused and left alone; on a different instance, or when deletion is not proven, the Task is held with the binding untouched until a receipt exists.
- **Rate-limited prompts requeue only for retiring instances** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.promptAdmissionRejectionRequeues`): promptAdmissionRejectionRequeues requeues a proven-unaccepted rate-limit rejection for external runtimes always, and for a dedicated execution-workspace pool only while its instance is draining, stopping, or evicted; a Serving and Accepting pool's 429 means its prompt slot is busy, so it keeps waiting for capacity like a shared pool.
- **Pool retirement read failure keeps waiting** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.externalWorkspacePoolRetiring`): externalWorkspacePoolRetiring reads current pool state uncached; a read failure keeps the caller waiting for capacity, which re-evaluates retirement on the next pass.
- **Retiring instances requeue on capacity refusal** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.waitForPromptCapacityReservation`): waitForPromptCapacityReservation requeues a never-accepted prompt for the replacement when a retiring execution-workspace instance refuses for either admission or capacity, since its running prompts may still hold every slot.
- **Do not use capacity freed on a retiring instance** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.waitForPromptCapacityReservation`): waitForPromptCapacityReservation releases and requeues when capacity frees on an instance that started retiring during the wait, because a prompt-only reservation is not gated on admission and would otherwise run on the retiring instance.
- **Workspace validation reasons are projected onto receipts** (`controller.acpWorkspaceValidationFailureMessage`): acpWorkspaceValidationFailureMessage projects the supervisor's categorized workspace-validation rejection onto the Task delivery receipt so operators need not read controller logs; only the client error text is used and it is re-bounded even though the supervisor already bounds it.
- **Redact runtime messages before truncation** (`controller.boundedRuntimeSessionServerMessage`): boundedRuntimeSessionServerMessage redacts the complete message before bounding it, because truncating first could cut a credential-shaped value ahead of the text its recognizer needs.
- **Strip control and format runes before redaction** (`controller.stripACPControlRunes`): stripACPControlRunes removes C0, DEL, C1, and Unicode format runes from runtime-supplied text; dropping every separator before redaction reassembles credentials split across lines or tabs and keeps terminal escapes and invisible runes out of status and logs.
- **Format runes are stripped like controls** (`controller.stripACPControlRunes`): stripACPControlRunes drops Unicode Cf format runes (zero-width spaces, joiners, directional marks) because they are as invisible as controls and equally capable of splitting a token past redaction.
- **Digest conflict signals an earlier processed create** (`controller.runtimeSessionCreateDigestConflict`): runtimeSessionCreateDigestConflict detects the runtime's answer for an existing operation record under a different digest; because the controller rebuilds the create request with fresh expiry and workspace capability on every reconcile of one attempt, a re-admitted attempt whose earlier send was processed is answered exactly this way.
- **Digest conflicts mean the session may exist** (`controller.runtimeSessionCreationMayHaveApplied`): runtimeSessionCreationMayHaveApplied returns true for a create digest conflict because an earlier send of the same attempt was processed, so its session may be resident and must be retired with the attempt.
- **4xx create rejections prove non-application** (`controller.runtimeSessionCreationMayHaveApplied`): runtimeSessionCreationMayHaveApplied treats a received non-retryable 4xx rejection as authoritative proof the supervisor rejected rather than ambiguously applied the create.
- **Cancel retries keep acknowledgment grace** (`controller.cancelPromptWithUnsentRetry`): cancelPromptWithUnsentRetry retries only the same sealed cancel operation before its original settlement deadline, and keeps the caller's acknowledgment grace once a send starts because a proven response may arrive just after the settlement deadline.
- **Idle-resume failure is terminal, never replayed** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.handlePromptStreamError`): handlePromptStreamError records a terminal failure when the supervisor proves idle recovery failed before submitting the ACP prompt, even though the HTTP body was written; transport evidence never becomes permission to replay.
- **Journal flush timeouts are long due to shared SQLite** (`controller.acpInterruptedOutputFlushTimeout`): acpInterruptedOutputFlushTimeout is 60s because the journal shares one SQLite connection with every other controller write, so a short deadline would turn ordinary contention between concurrent ACP prompts into a failed Task with ExecutionEventPersistenceFailed.
- **Lifecycle journal appends use the flush timeout** (`controller.acpPromptLifecyclePersistTimeout`): acpPromptLifecyclePersistTimeout equals acpInterruptedOutputFlushTimeout because the detached journal append for stream failure or settlement shares the same single SQLite connection; a failed append fails the Task with ExecutionEventPersistenceFailed.
- **Cancel-settlement logs stay low-cardinality** (`controller.logACPCancelSettlementUnknown`): logACPCancelSettlementUnknown logs why an explicit cancellation could not prove settlement using only low-cardinality fields: transport status, code, and kind for client errors, and barrier, forced-termination, and terminal-event facts on success.
- **Cancellation message is read from the live Task** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.requestedCancellationMessage`): requestedCancellationMessage reads the cancel request from the live Task uncached because the dispatcher's copy predates any request made during the run; it returns empty when the Task is being deleted.
- **Unreadable identity claims neither send nor fail** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.identityUnavailableBeforeSubmission`): identityUnavailableBeforeSubmission returns an error, not a failure reason, when the identity claim cannot be read or the enforcement period is unrecorded, so the prompt is neither sent nor failed and is retried. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Prompts wait briefly for a new leader's enforcement period** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.identityUnavailableBeforeSubmission`): identityUnavailableBeforeSubmission waits up to identityEnforcementPeriodWait for errEnforcementPeriodPending to clear, because a controller that just won leadership records its enforcement period within moments.
- **Prompt failure diagnostics stay in the journal** (`controller.acpPromptFailedMessage`): acpPromptFailedMessage is a fixed string because runtime diagnostics stay in the journal, whose redaction accounts for prior prompt fields; repeating them in status, Session outcomes, or TaskFailed events would bypass that redaction and create more public copies.
- **Failed attempts persist the projected reason** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.transitionAttemptToFailed`): transitionAttemptToFailed records the same reason and message on the durable PromptAttempt that the Task projection exposes, so controller-restart recovery reproduces that classification instead of the generic default.
- **Terminal projections copy the full frozen execution identity** (`controller.terminalProjectionExecution`): failTaskWithProjection builds its payload from a deep copy of the frozen execution identity via terminalProjectionExecution and overlays only the terminal classification, because reclamation validates the payload against the Task's complete runtime identity and a field subset would make the source PromptAttempt impossible to retire.
- **Admission retry status omits underlying errors** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.requeueReservedTask`): requeueReservedTask surfaces only the bounded stage without changing execution state or consuming a Task retry, and deliberately excludes the underlying error because runtime and credential errors are not a safe status surface.
- **ToolboxUnavailable pools fail queued Tasks** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleRuntimePoolToolboxUnavailable`): settleRuntimePoolToolboxUnavailable fails a queued or reserved Task whose frozen RuntimePool reports ToolboxUnavailable, because that pool cannot roll out and waiting would never end.
- **Live events require runtime-confirmed redaction** (`controller.acpLivePublisher`): acpLivePublisher publishes a prompt's events to live consumers only when the runtime confirmed on acceptance that it redacts every event before it leaves; an older runtime's content is safe only after the journal redacts complete text, so none of its events are published live.
- **Completed answers get pattern redaction before live publish** (`controller.acpLivePublisher.observe`): acpLivePublisher.observe applies the journal's pattern redaction to the whole completed answer before publishing, while streamed chunks carry only the runtime's exact-value redaction because no complete text exists to match yet.
- **Missing sessionRef with create=false fails immediately** (`controller.errACPSessionNotFound`): errACPSessionNotFound fails a Task whose sessionRef has create=false and names a nonexistent Session, because retrying cannot create it and waiting for the deadline would only delay the failure.
- **Finalized-turn cache is bounded by the live scan** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.pruneFinalizedSessionTurns`): pruneFinalizedSessionTurns keeps only entries for Tasks in the current cluster-wide scan so cumulative throughput cannot grow the cache indefinitely; a Task deleted mid-scan may leave one entry until the next pass.
- **Recover open SessionTurns of terminal attempts** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery`): sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery flags session-bound Tasks whose attempt settled terminal while the SessionTurn is still open, for Finalizing and every terminal phase, because inline finalization skips when its in-memory turn is missing and still terminalizes the Task; otherwise artifact retirement blocks until the deadline. Succeeded additionally waits for terminal delivery since publication recovery owns the turn until then.
- **SessionTurnFinalized does not prove the activation tail** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery`): sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery keeps recovering until the finalized-turn cache records the turn, because SessionTurnFinalized proves only the durable turn commit, not the cross-store tail (lease release, status projection, outbox activation), and the Task controller can terminalize independently; ResumeSessionTurnFinalization is idempotent.
- **Publication recovery owns open turns until delivery** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileUnfinalizedTaskSession`): reconcileUnfinalizedTaskSession leaves a Succeeded attempt's SessionTurn and lease alone until delivery reaches a durable terminal receipt, because publication recovery owns them until then.
- **Reflect recovered finalization on the live turn** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileUnfinalizedTaskSession`): reconcileUnfinalizedTaskSession copies the known durable finalization onto the live SessionTurn because recovery reconstructs a separate turn object, and runtime cleanup must see the completed finalization.
- **Lineage digest excludes the harness WorkspaceDigest** (`controller.acpSessionLineageConfigurationDigest`): acpSessionLineageConfigurationDigest hashes the provider-backed execution-workspace binding frozen into the RuntimePool plan, deliberately not the harness RuntimeSession WorkspaceDigest, whose repo-less baseline may rotate after the Session lease is acquired without changing protocol lineage.
- **Finish fallible planning reads before taking the Session lease** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.planTaskSession`): planTaskSession completes every fallible transcript and runtime planning read before acquiring the Session mutation Lease, so once the Lease is held the only next durable action is opening the exact SessionTurn.
- **Always re-enter exact Session lease acquisition** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.bindAndOpenTaskSessionTurn`): bindAndOpenTaskSessionTurn re-enters exact lease acquisition even when the Lease exists, because acquisition is idempotent and completes a lineage payload projection that may have failed after the Kubernetes-authoritative status CAS.
- **Session lifecycle events use a fixed message** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.finalizeTaskSessionResult`): finalizeTaskSessionResult includes a fixed lifecycle message because the outbox may publish the terminal phase before or after live settlement, and either ordering must record a meaningful event without copying assistant or runtime text.
- **Only complete bindings replace the live binding** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.finalizeTaskSessionResult`): finalizeTaskSessionResult replaces the live RuntimeSession binding only with a complete binding (Generation > 0), so a recovered session that could not rebuild its generation never clobbers the binding the live path recorded.
- **Successfully finalized turns are excluded from recovery** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.markTaskSessionFinalized`): markTaskSessionFinalized records a turn whose finalization store call succeeded, because that call already completed the activation tail; letting the recovery sweep resume it would rerun the tail and resettle the Task as if live settlement were interrupted.
- **Session projections keep the full frozen identity** (`controller.taskSessionProjectionExecution`): taskSessionProjectionExecution overlays only the terminal classification on the Task's frozen execution identity because Session finalization projections are immutable reclamation evidence; omitting the request digest or runtime identity would make the source PromptAttempt impossible to retire safely.
- **Recovered bindings survive only for reusable read sessions** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.retireRecoveredRuntimeSessionBinding`): retireRecoveredRuntimeSessionBinding keeps the in-memory binding only for a session-bound read RuntimeSession whose prompt succeeded with a reusable delivery state and a complete generation, so the next continuation plans a reuse instead of a restart-style recreation with full transcript bootstrap. Task-scoped, write, non-successful, and parked turns drop it, since parked continuations must start from the Session transcript, not the parked process. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Clear candidate Session authority before terminal settlement** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.requeueSessionLeaseFailure`): requeueSessionLeaseFailure wraps a failed rollback after a configuration mismatch in errSessionAdmissionRollback, because candidate Session authority must be cleared before the Task can be terminally settled.
- **Missing turns on settled Tasks count as finalized** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery`): sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery caches a missing SessionTurn as finalized for a settled terminal Task, because explicit Session deletion removes settled turns and a retained terminal Task proves the finalization tail completed; this avoids rereading on every dispatcher scan.
- **External-effect leases keep a settlement margin** (`controller.externalEffectLeaseSettlementMargin`): externalEffectLeaseSettlementMargin is extra lease time beyond an effect's bounded call duration so a response returned at the call deadline can still be committed under a valid lease.
- **External effect calls are bounded below their lease** (`controller.maxACPExternalEffectCallDuration`): maxACPExternalEffectCallDuration bounds one brokered custom-Tool call so its outcome can always be committed while the ledger lease is valid; otherwise a call outliving the lease plus reconciliation grace is marked OutcomeUnknown by reconcileExpiredExternalEffects and a late success can never settle. Publisher-backed effects size their lease from the publish timeout so lease > call duration holds for every kind.
- **External effect call timeouts by kind** (`controller.externalEffectCallTimeout`): externalEffectCallTimeout keeps brokered custom-Tool calls at the fixed clamp their descriptors were admitted under (customACPMCPToolDescriptor rejects longer HTTP timeouts), while publisher-backed effects honor the controller-visible publish timeout so long publisher operations are not truncated; the lease is always call timeout plus settlement margin.
- **External effects persist identity before execution** (`controller.runACPExternalEffect`): runACPExternalEffect persists a canonical pre-execution identity before one idempotent publisher or broker operation; a committed response is replayed from the ledger, and an in-flight identity is reclaimed only under the current epoch and exact request digest.
- **Non-idempotent effects are never reclaimed** (`controller.runExternalEffectOnceWithReplay`): runExternalEffectOnceWithReplay never reclaims an identity a previous delivery claimed, even after its lease expires, so a non-idempotent request that may have been delivered is never sent twice.
- **Non-reclaiming runs replay settled failures** (`controller.runExternalEffectWithReplayMode`): runExternalEffectWithReplayMode replays the stored answer of a definite failure for a run that never reclaims, because that run settles definite failures with their answer and a redelivery must return the same answer.
- **Capture call context error before cancelling** (`controller.runExternalEffectWithReplayMode`): runExternalEffectWithReplayMode reads callCtx.Err() before calling cancelCall so a deadline that elapsed during the call stays observable instead of being overwritten by context.Canceled.
- **Failed effect calls stay in flight** (`controller.runExternalEffectWithReplayMode`): runExternalEffectWithReplayMode leaves an effect whose call errored in flight so a later reconciliation can reclaim and classify the same identity and digest; a different request cannot reuse it.
- **Results after the call deadline are not committed** (`controller.runExternalEffectWithReplayMode`): runExternalEffectWithReplayMode does not commit a response returned after the bounded call deadline elapsed, because the configured tool or publisher timeout expired; the effect stays in flight for explicit reconciliation.
- **Revoked prompt authority leaves effects in flight** (`controller.runExternalEffectWithReplayMode`): runExternalEffectWithReplayMode leaves the ledger in flight rather than claiming success when prompt authority was revoked before the response could be committed, because the side effect may already have crossed its external boundary.
- **External effects retry the same immutable operation** (`controller.runACPExternalEffectWithRetry`): runACPExternalEffectWithRetry retries the same immutable operation while retaining its lease until it commits, a non-retryable response is proven, or the bounded context expires; this is safe because publisher operations are idempotent by operation ID and prompt input is never replayed.
- **Transport failures are retryable and ambiguous** (`controller.retryableACPExternalEffectError`): retryableACPExternalEffectError treats transport failures, which expose no typed upstream response, as retryable because their side-effect outcome is ambiguous and must be reconciled under the same durable identity rather than classified as a deterministic request error.
- **Only exactly Pending effects are settled externally** (`controller.SettlePendingExternalEffect`): SettlePendingExternalEffect transitions only an effect still exactly Pending; one in flight or settled belongs to another execution and is reported with ErrExternalEffectNotPending, never transitioned.
- **Pending settlement is version-pinned** (`controller.SettlePendingExternalEffect`): SettlePendingExternalEffect pins its transition to the Pending state and version just read, so an execution that claims the record meanwhile makes the transition fail instead of being overwritten.
- **Test proxies enable full duplex for prompt streams** (`controller.newExternalRuntimeStatusProxy`): The external runtime test proxies (newExternalRuntimeStatusProxy, newExternalRuntimeCapabilitiesProxy) enable HTTP full duplex because prompt acceptance can flush before the transport's final request-body EOF check, and HTTP/1 would otherwise close that shared body during the flush.
- **Policy isolation tests join background work before teardown** (`controller.startExternalPolicyTask`): startExternalPolicyTask and startExternalPolicyMCPCall register cleanups that join dispatch and effect settlement even when an assertion fails, because dispatch and effect settlement can outlive their contexts and the shared fixture database and epoch manager must not close before terminal delivery.
- **Reply policy projection never mutates the Task spec** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPReplyPolicy`): projectACPReplyPolicy runs only for new bindings before profile and descriptor sealing, and returns a copied Task used as snapshot input, never a persisted spec change, so restored bindings never gain the reply tool.
- **Pending gateway linkage defers binding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPReplyPolicy`): projectACPReplyPolicy returns the ResolveReplyEligibility error so a Task whose durable gateway linkage is still pending defers freezing its binding instead of sealing without the reply tool.
- **No reply injection into external registered policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPReplyPolicy`): projectACPReplyPolicy never injects the reply tool for external runtimes, because their registered policy must remain byte-for-byte exact.
- **Implicit reply grant must still fit the Agent ceiling** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPReplyPolicy`): projectACPReplyPolicy keeps the implicit reply grant only when the projected policy passes validateAgentRuntimeCeiling, because the grant turns an implicit native set into an explicit allowlist that dispatch judges with exact-match deny entries.
- **Approval settlement uses detached bounded contexts** (`controller.acpApprovalSettleTimeout`): acpApprovalSettleTimeout bounds approval settlement work that must outlive the cancelled request context; acpApprovalInterruptCheckTimeout is shorter because the interrupt check only reads.
- **Approval call arguments live only in a Task-owned Secret** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalCall`): acpMCPApprovalCall is private executable input, never an event payload; an immutable Task-owned Secret keeps the original arguments across redelivery and controller restart without exposing them in Task status or approval APIs.
- **Approvals bind the frozen Connection digest** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalCall.ConnectionDigest`): acpMCPApprovalCall.ConnectionDigest names the frozen Connection a connector-backed call acts under (policy, UID, generation digest, never the token) and is bound into RequestDigest, so the approval and its effect record identify the linked account.
- **Approval identity binds the call ID, not arguments** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalIdentity`): acpMCPApprovalIdentity derives identity from the transport call ID independently of arguments, so a changed payload with the same call identity conflicts instead of silently creating a second review, while distinct genuine calls always need a new review.
- **Approval identities use a call ID digest** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalIdentityFromCallDigest`): acpMCPApprovalIdentityFromCallDigest uses a digest of the call ID so the identity survives event redaction even when the call ID contains a URL query or other sensitive text; the acp-tool-approval-v2 domain separates it from legacy raw-ID identities.
- **Connector calls must name their Connection before approval** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.serveApprovedCall`): serveApprovedCall binds a connector-backed call's frozen Connection digest into the approval and effect record, and refuses a call that cannot name its Connection before anything is recorded.
- **Only unstarted calls record a review request** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.serveApprovedCall`): serveApprovedCall records the review request only for a Pending effect, because started or terminal effects already have one and a terminal receipt must replay from durable storage alone without depending on event-store availability.
- **Reviewers see forwarded body fields, not values** (`controller.maskForwardedWriteArguments`): requestToolApproval masks forwarded write request bodies with maskForwardedWriteArguments because a body can carry a secret under any field name, such as a function key's value, so reviewers see field names but not values.
- **Approval events carry a call binding, not raw call IDs** (`controller.approvalCallBinding`): requestToolApproval records approvalCallBinding rather than the raw runtime call ID because call IDs can contain private text that generic redaction misses.
- **Approval start requires the retained request** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.approvalOutcome`): approvalOutcome fails when the start record cannot be written because, unlike optional receipt projections, the start record is required before remote execution and missing history must never count as success.
- **Only unstarted approval reads are retried** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.readUnstartedApproval`): readUnstartedApproval retries reads only while the handler has never invoked the executor; failed claim mutations and execution must never enter this loop because they may already have taken effect.
- **Concurrent deliveries join, never take over** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.waitAndExecuteApproval`): waitAndExecuteApproval joins a concurrent delivery until it records a result; waiting never transfers its execution lease or starts another action.
- **Started approval calls are never reclaimed** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.waitAndExecuteApproval`): waitAndExecuteApproval never reclaims a started approval call even after its lease expires, reporting an unknown outcome instead, because the action may already have reached an external system.
- **Resolve approval credentials outside the polling path** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.continueApprovedCall`): continueApprovedCall resolves credentials and policy only when approval could authorize execution, outside the polling path, while the prompt authority watcher cancels a pending wait when its durable lease ends.
- **Verified approval receipts are authoritative** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.replayApprovalResult`): replayApprovalResult returns the verified terminal receipt and ignores event projection errors, because the receipt is authoritative and recovery repairs a failed projection without requiring the runtime to redeliver.
- **Transport interruption does not erase pending review** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.interruptedApproval`): interruptedApproval keeps a pending review on a transport interruption so exact redelivery can resume it while the original authority survives.
- **Do not call a concurrently started approval unstarted** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.interruptedApproval`): interruptedApproval returns without settling when a concurrent delivery may already have started the call, because that execution owner records the result or unknown outcome.
- **Approval revocation settles under a detached context** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.revokeApproval`): revokeApproval only records evidence and settles under its own bounded detached context, because the authority watcher can cancel the call after revalidation fails.
- **Approved connector calls revalidate their Connection** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.validateApprovalTool`): validateApprovalTool recomputes the Connection digest so a connector-backed call still acts under the Connection and injection configuration (Tool and policy) its approval was bound to.
- **Approval start is recorded under the epoch interlock** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.recordApprovalStart`): recordApprovalStart records authorization to start under the short task-data epoch interlock that cancellation uses, then releases it before any remote tool I/O.
- **Approval start retries only reads** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.recordApprovalStart`): recordApprovalStart retries only the preceding reads; once the start event write was attempted its result is reported without repeating the mutation.
- **Delivered approval denials are final** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.blockApproval`): blockApproval transitions the effect Pending to Failed so a delivered denial is final, even if the reviewer had approved and transiently revoked authority later becomes valid again.
- **Claimed approvals are not reported unstarted** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.blockApproval`): blockApproval returns a conflict when another delivery claimed the call, because that delivery owns the execution outcome and this waiter must not report the action as unstarted.
- **Revalidate approval lease right before execution** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.prepareApprovedCall`): prepareApprovedCall revalidates the live lease after tool validation and the running event, because those reads and writes may block on storage and authority can lapse before execution.
- **Approval execution budget follows review expiry** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalLeaseExpiry`): acpMCPApprovalLeaseExpiry reserves MCPApprovalExecutionTimeout after the original review expiry, the latest possible start, without renewing the claim, capped by the Task deadline, because preparation can wait until the review expires.
- **Unreadable approval input preserves the call** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.executeApprovedCall`): executeApprovedCall leaves an approved, unclaimed call intact when its private input Secret cannot be read, so exact redelivery rechecks the original authority instead of failing or executing.
- **Approved calls execute only the persisted request** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.executeApprovedCall`): executeApprovedCall executes only the private persisted request from the approval Secret, never the review preview or the redelivered caller's arguments.
- **Approval execution budget starts after preparation** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.executeApprovedCall`): executeApprovedCall keeps the acknowledged claim during preparation and starts MCPApprovalExecutionTimeout only after preparation succeeds; once execution can begin, a strict prompt-authority watcher stops it on any unreadable authority.
- **Forwarded write receipts keep a reduced result** (`controller.forwardedWriteKeptResult`): executeApprovedCall returns the full result to the caller but stores only forwardedWriteKeptResult in the receipt and event, so durable records keep only what they may.
- **Only no-effect errors or Orka refusals prove no effect** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.failCallWithoutEffect`): failCallWithoutEffect settles an approved call as a final failure only when it provably reached no remote system: a NoEffectError or Orka's own pre-send refusal such as a missing requester link, which keeps its code and fix. A tool response is never such proof; if the receipt cannot be recorded it reports false and the caller settles the outcome as unknown.
- **Approved call failure logs are redacted** (`controller.logApprovedCallFailure`): logApprovedCallFailure redacts and bounds the cause before logging because tool errors can quote remote responses.
- **Approval results are hashed in canonical JSON** (`controller.canonicalMCPApprovalResult`): canonicalMCPApprovalResult hashes and replays one canonical JSON form on both sides of storage because Kubernetes may change field order, number spelling, and escaping of stored responses, and a valid saved receipt must remain verifiable.
- **Abandoned pending denials bind to the reservation** (`controller.acpMCPAbandonedPendingReceipt`): acpMCPAbandonedPendingReceipt binds a pre-execution denial to its effect reservation when a crash prevented the approval request event from being saved; recovery may use it only after proving an older epoch or a terminal or deleting Task with the same run.
- **Pending CAS proves an approval never started** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.failMCPApprovalPending`): failMCPApprovalPending transitions with the Pending state, version, and digest preconditions because that CAS is the proof execution never began; a concurrent claim wins rather than being overwritten as an unstarted call.
- **Revoking unbound pending approvals needs a fresh Task read** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.mcpApprovalUnboundPendingAbandoned`): mcpApprovalUnboundPendingAbandoned never revokes on the discovery hint alone: a fresh Task read must bind the reservation's runtime session and epoch to a run the broker also refuses, covering a lost request event followed by Task cleanup without a restart or Secret access.
- **Transient failures do not revoke same-epoch reviews** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.acpMCPApprovalPendingDenial`): acpMCPApprovalPendingDenial returns no denial for a transport disconnect, missing local lease, or temporary read failure, because a same-epoch review can still reconnect.
- **Approval recovery checks the Task UID** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.mcpApprovalCurrentTask`): mcpApprovalCurrentTask returns nil when the Task is gone or its UID changed, so approval recovery never acts on a recreated Task that reuses the name.
- **Broker approval projections use the Task fence** (`controller.mcpApprovalTaskFence`): mcpApprovalTaskFence moves only with cleanup of the Task's own data because the broker writes the Session association from the call's credentials, not history, so cleanup of other Tasks in the namespace must not refuse a live call's projection.
- **Recovery approval projections use the namespace fence** (`controller.mcpApprovalNamespaceFence`): mcpApprovalNamespaceFence also moves with Session cleanup because recovery copies the request's Session association from history; cleanup that detaches it must refuse the append and make recovery read again.
- **Approval projections are fenced read-then-append** (`controller.withRetainedMCPApproval`): withRetainedMCPApproval reads the retained request and appends the projection as separate fenced calls: cleanup either removes the projection after the append or wins first and refuses it, so no evidence is projected onto a removed request. Appends are deduplicated, absence never proves cleanup or outcome, and terminal decisions are observed only after the projection commits.
- **Projections require an explicit request UID** (`controller.projectRetainedMCPApproval`): projectRetainedMCPApproval requires an explicit Task UID on the original approval request, not the legacy untagged-event fallback or a terminal-only derived approval, before projecting.
- **Projections derive decisions from the fenced read** (`controller.projectRetainedMCPApproval`): projectRetainedMCPApproval derives decisions and the observed history position from its own fenced read rather than authorizing appends with a cached projection.
- **Failed approval receipts prove no effect** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalReceiptOutcome`): acpMCPApprovalReceiptOutcome interprets a saved response without granting permission to repeat the call: Failed records prove no effect (a pre-execution denial, or an approved call that sent nothing or was refused), while a completed tool that returned an error has a Succeeded effect.
- **Abandoned pending denials bind to the exact effect** (`controller.acpMCPApprovalReceiptOutcome`): acpMCPApprovalReceiptOutcome accepts an abandoned-pending denial only when it names the exact effect ID and stale code, because a crash can precede the approval event and recovery must bind the denial to the effect instead of inventing an approval identity.
- **Approval recovery never loads executable Secrets** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions`): reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions joins already-listed effects with safe approval bindings and never loads executable Secret contents or asks a stale runtime to redeliver a call.
- **Recovery cache keys on pre-projection event sequence** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions`): reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions records the event sequence from before projection in its recovery cache, so a late stale event or its own append invalidates the cache on the next scan even when the terminal effect's version is unchanged.
- **Pending approvals are rechecked every scan** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions`): reconcileMCPApprovalExecutions never caches pending reviews because expiry and durable prompt termination can change without an effect mutation or Task event.
- **Approval effects are indexed by safe digests** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalTask`): reconcileMCPApprovalTask indexes effects by safe operation digests while retaining the exact durable identity for receipt verification and projection, because runtime operation IDs may be URLs.
- **Settle unrequested reservations only after authority ends** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalTask`): reconcileMCPApprovalTask settles a reservation that has no ApprovalRequested event only after proving its authority ended, since Reserve succeeds before the event is appended; a delayed request event can later join the receipt without loading its Secret.
- **Old-epoch in-flight approvals become OutcomeUnknown** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileMCPApprovalExecution`): reconcileMCPApprovalExecution seals an in-flight approval from an old epoch as OutcomeUnknown with the exact lease CAS, because an old epoch can no longer commit a result; a crash here is repaired by the next scan.
- **Recovery decision and outcome commit as one group** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.appendMCPApprovalRecoveryOutcome`): appendMCPApprovalRecoveryOutcome runs only inside withRetainedMCPApproval against its fresh fenced read and commits the decision and execution events in one grouped append; a terminal decision a reviewer recorded after that read refuses the group, the projection rereads, and the reviewer's decision remains authoritative.
- **Recovery dedupe keys include history position** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.appendMCPApprovalRecoveryOutcome`): appendMCPApprovalRecoveryOutcome includes the observed effect version and last event sequence in its dedupe key so a late stale writer cannot make an earlier key prevent the next scan from repairing its projection.
- **MCP authority read failures are marked at the read boundary** (`controller.acpMCPAuthorityReadError`): acpMCPAuthorityReadError marks failures where the backend is read, because only there can an unavailable backend be distinguished from identity and policy checks; a missing or invalid record is definitive, an unreadable one is not.
- **Only external runtimes freeze prompt MCP policy** (`controller.ACPMCPBrokerCredentials.ExpectedMCPConfiguration`): ExpectedMCPConfiguration freezes the exact prompt-scoped tool and approval policy for external runtimes. RuntimePool callers leave it nil because the controller-owned supervisor already receives the same policy.
- **ACP MCP broker authenticates before reading request bodies** (`controller.ACPMCPBrokerPreAuthenticator`): The ACP MCP broker resolves the controller bearer from non-secret pool identity headers and constant-time compares it before reading the request body, so an untrusted Pod without a bearer cannot occupy handlers by drip-feeding chunked bodies. Resolvers without ACPMCPBrokerPreAuthenticator fall through to the post-decode bearer check.
- **Nil broker connector dependencies fail closed** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor`): RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor dependencies SubstrateMCPRouter, AgentExecutionSnapshots, and Connections are optional, and leaving any nil makes the tools that need them (brokered Substrate MCP Tools, connector-backed tools, catalog built-ins with a frozen link) fail closed rather than run another way.
- **Custom Tools always bind transaction authority** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.EnforceTransactionCredentialAuth`): Brokered custom-Tool executions always bind the authenticated Task's transaction authority. EnforceTransactionCredentialAuth mirrors the controller-wide context-token enforcement mode and gates only Secret-credential authorization. ADR 0011. See [ADR 0011](0011-vendor-neutral-transaction-and-outbound-access.md).
- **Broker connector-read scopes match the API** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ConnectorReadScopes`): ConnectorReadScopes lists the transaction scopes that let a Task created by a delegated context token run list_connections, and must match the scopes the API's connector routes accept.
- **Broker credential scopes match worker Job scopes** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.TransactionCredentialReadScopes`): TransactionCredentialReadScopes authorizes Secret-backed outbound credentials and must equal the scopes the controller stamps into worker Jobs, so brokered and per-Task execution authorize identically.
- **Connection digest read failures are errors** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ConnectionDigest`): ConnectionDigest returns a non-secret digest of the frozen Connection behind a brokered tool, or empty when not connector-backed. A failed read is an error and never a silent 'not connector-backed', because the effect record must carry the binding it promises and its request digest must not depend on transient read availability.
- **Unlinked catalog built-ins fail before approval** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ConnectionDigest`): A catalog built-in runs in the ACP MCP broker only under the Connection frozen for it; without one, ConnectionDigest fails the call before any approval or effect record is created.
- **Connector classification drift is refused** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.refuseConnectorClassificationDrift`): If a Task froze a Connection for a Tool's policy but the policy is no longer connection mode, the policy was retargeted after dispatch and the call is refused, so drift never executes under the policy's new credentials. A Tool without a policy, or whose policy was never frozen, is simply not connector-backed.
- **Connector binding digest includes Tool spec** (`controller.connectorBindingDigest`): The connector binding digest combines the frozen Connection identity with the Tool spec and policy credential-output dispatch digest, so approvals and effect records are tied to the configuration in force and a changed policy is detected on revalidation.
- **Connector-read scope boundary for delegated Tasks** (`controller.brokerConnectorReadAuthorizer`): Under enforcement, a Task created by a delegated context token whose transaction lacks every connector-read scope may not list the requester's linked accounts. A Task without a transaction (created directly by a person or ServiceAccount) is not narrowed.
- **Transaction scopes fall back to legacy Scope** (`controller.brokerConnectorReadAuthorizer`): brokerConnectorReadAuthorizer reads TaskTransaction.Scopes and falls back to the space-separated Scope field, exactly as the transaction-authority binder does, so both agree on a Task's scopes.
- **Only sealed requesters reach tools** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.taskConnectionAuthority`): Only a requester whose provenance the controller key sealed for this Task is handed to tools; an unverified spec.requestedBy is treated as no requester.
- **Forwarded write effect records keep no answer** (`controller.forwardedWriteKeptResult`): ExternalEffect status is readable by anyone who can read the resource, and any forwarded write can answer with a secret (listKeys, listClusterUserCredential, a PUT of a function key). forwardedWriteKeptResult therefore keeps only a note with the status, and a redelivery joining the record gets that note. ADR 0040. See [ADR 0040](0040-forwarded-cli-requests.md).
- **Throttled forwarded writes keep status and delay** (`controller.forwardedWriteKeptResult`): A throttled or failing forwarded write keeps its status and retry delay in the effect record so a replay still tells the Azure client to retry, as Azure did; azureRetryableStatus mirrors the Azure SDK retry set.
- **Forwarded write approval previews are masked** (`controller.maskForwardedWriteArguments`): Approval previews of forwarded writes keep the body's shape and field names but mask every value, except allowlisted sizing fields; arguments that do not decode keep no body.
- **Forwarded query values are masked except api-version** (`controller.maskForwardedQuery`): maskForwardedQuery masks every query value of a forwarded write in approval previews except api-version, keeping parameter names, because values such as a SAS signature can be secrets.
- **Azure preview allowlist is per resource type** (`controller.azurePreviewSchemas`): azurePreviewSchemas lists, per Resource Manager resource type, body paths of a PUT or PATCH that only size, place, or version the resource, each with the JSON type it must have to be shown. Writes to other types and every action (POST) keep all values masked.
- **Azure resource path parity** (`controller.azureResourceType`): After the provider namespace, a Resource Manager path is type/name pairs; an odd-length tail is an action or a collection list, so azureResourceType returns no type for it and the body stays fully masked.
- **Preview strings limited to sizing characters** (`controller.azurePreviewText`): A shown approval-preview string may contain only the letters, digits, and separators of Azure locations, SKU names, and versions; anything else in an allowlisted field is masked like an unknown field.
- **Preview masking rejects wrong types and path-posing keys** (`controller.maskJSONValues`): maskJSONValues shows a value only at an allowlisted path with the expected type and a short string, so a secret sent in a sizing field of the wrong type stays hidden. Keys that are empty or contain a separator or wildcard could pose as another path, so they and everything under them never match.
- **Tool drift checked before approval and execution** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ValidateACPMCPTool`): ValidateACPMCPTool checks custom Tool configuration drift before an approval is spent, and ExecuteACPMCPTool repeats the check at the execution boundary.
- **Tool drift refuses newly approval-gated writes** (`controller.matchCurrentCustomACPMCPTool`): matchCurrentCustomACPMCPTool refuses a call whose Tool no longer matches the authorized descriptor, and a forwarded write the Tool now requires approval for while the prompt's policy does not.
- **Preparation failures are reported as no effect** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ExecuteACPMCPTool`): Preparation sends nothing, so a forwarded write refused during preparation is a NoEffectError, reported as refused and never as possibly applied.
- **Direct refusals return as tool results** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ExecuteACPMCPTool`): A call answered directly gets an Orka refusal as its tool result; only approval settlement consumes the no-effect classification.
- **Refused-without-effect is set only by Orka** (`controller.acpMCPRefusedWithoutEffect`): acpMCPRefusedWithoutEffect marks a call Orka refused before any request left (such as a missing requester link) and carries the agent's tool result plus code and reason for approval settlement. It is set only by Orka's own refusal, never inferred from a tool's response.
- **Prepare before execution budget, invoke once** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): prepareACPMCPTool performs all reads without invoking the tool. Approval calls prepare before starting their execution budget, then revalidate authority before invoking the returned call exactly once.
- **Linked bindings are read before the call** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): A catalog built-in runs under the requester's linked account when the Task froze one; the binding is read during preparation so an unloadable snapshot is a preparation failure, never a silent run on the Task's own credentials. ADR 0033. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Linked tokens are scoped by the authenticated Task** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): Linked-account tokens are scoped by the current Task's workspace, so preparation uses the authenticated ACP Task from context, never the name the factory supplied; otherwise no Task scope would be checked.
- **Linked built-ins require linked-account context** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): A catalog built-in reaches the broker only through its link; without a tool context carrying LinkedAccounts it would fall through to the tool's own credential path, so preparation refuses it.
- **Linked built-in calls are held to the catalog bound** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): The linked credential is refreshed to stay valid for the catalog's per-call bound, so the whole call is held to BuiltinConnectorToolTimeout; a longer multi-page read could outlive the token partway.
- **Only failed reads are recoverable preparation failures** (`controller.acpMCPAuthorityReader`): acpMCPAuthorityReader marks only failed Kubernetes reads as recoverable preparation failures; descriptor and Task authority mismatches remain definitive.
- **Forwarding write tools resolve credentials as writes** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.prepareACPMCPTool`): A forwarding write tool resolves its credential as a write, so a linked account must allow writes; preparation deep-copies the Tool to mark it.
- **Calls resolve under the policy they were classified under** (`controller.connectorPolicyPin`): A call resolves credentials under the same policy it was classified under: a policy retargeted after dispatch (no longer connection mode while the Task froze a Connection) is refused by connectorPolicyPin, and a policy replaced or edited before the request is refused by the resolver.
- **Custom Tool no-effect classification** (`controller.customToolNoEffectError`): customToolNoEffectError marks a custom Tool failure as no-effect only when provable: the executor marks every send, so an error without that mark stopped before the request left, and a 4xx other than 408 answering the original request (not a followed redirect) is a refusal. Upstream bodies stay out of the summary.
