- **Admission rejections are safe tool results** (`controller.acpMCPToolExecutionResult`): A definitive admission rejection is returned as a safe tool result, not an ambiguous external effect; post-call authority checks still apply.
- **Registry argument rejections are resendable** (`controller.acpMCPToolExecutionResult`): When the registry rejects arguments before the tool runs, the call had no effect and the agent may resend it; the message names only the field and JSON types.
- **Missing-link refusals are safe to relay** (`controller.acpMCPToolExecutionResult`): A refusal for a missing or unusable requester link sent nothing, and its reason is Orka's own text naming the provider and fix, safe for the agent and logs.
- **Read-only tool timeouts are definitive** (`controller.acpMCPToolExecutionResult`): A read-only Tool may hit its own request deadline while the prompt is active and that is reported as a failure; preparation failures never carry the attempt marker, and consequential timeouts keep unknown outcomes.
- **Upstream error bodies never reach the ACP process** (`controller.acpMCPToolExecutionResult`): Tool execution failures return a generic error to the ACP process to keep upstream bodies out; the broker still checks prompt cancellation and authority, including replay receipts, before committing the result.
- **Broker binds Task transaction authority like worker Jobs** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.bindTaskTransactionAuthority`): bindTaskTransactionAuthority gives the per-request custom-Tool executor the authenticated Task's transaction and credential authority, mirroring the worker Job env and transaction-token Secret mount. Tokens and scopes bind in every mode, only Secret-credential authorization is gated by EnforceTransactionCredentialAuth, and missing Task authority fails closed.
- **Broker body must match pre-authenticated pool** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): After header pre-authentication, the ACP MCP broker requires the decoded body's pool identity to match, so a caller cannot pre-authenticate as one pool and act as another.
- **Operation capability verified with a fresh timestamp** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): The broker verifies the operation capability with a timestamp taken after credential resolution, because that Kubernetes I/O can outlast the capability's expiry.
- **Unnamed Connection refused before reservation** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): A consequential connector-backed call that cannot name its Connection digest is refused before effect reservation, since the effect record must carry the binding it promises.
- **Forwarded writes run once with replay** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): Forwarded POST and PATCH writes need not be idempotent, so they run through runExternalEffectOnceWithReplay. ADR 0040. See [ADR 0040](0040-forwarded-cli-requests.md).
- **Concurrent delivery yields unknown outcome** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): When another delivery claimed a forwarded write, it may have sent it, so this delivery returns tool_outcome_unknown and leaves the record to that delivery or reconciliation.
- **Refused forwarded writes are definite failures** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.ServeHTTP`): A forwarded write refused before sending is recorded as a definite no-effect failure, as on the approval path, not an unknown outcome.
- **Early MCP calls park until prompt accepted** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.authorizeMCPPromptAdmission`): The runtime can issue its first tool call before the Accepted event reaches the durable store; authorizeMCPPromptAdmission parks that call under its live prompt lease and original deadline, and admits no review or execution until the ordinary authorizer observes the accepted attempt.
- **Broker watches prompt authority during tool calls** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.watchPromptAuthority`): Fiber's HTTP adapter cancels request contexts only on shutdown, so watchPromptAuthority cancels in-flight tools when the durable prompt settles or disappears. It checks the current attempt rather than the original lease expiry because prompts renew leases; approval waiters tolerate read failures, approved execution uses a strict watcher.
- **Join authority watcher before releasing context** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.watchPromptAuthority`): Fiber reuses the request context after the handler returns, so the authority watcher goroutine must be joined before release.
- **Local prompt revocation is definitive** (`controller.DurableACPMCPPromptAuthorizer.AuthorizeACPMCPPrompt`): Local prompt lease revocation is definitive even when durable authority cannot be read, and is checked again after I/O before granting authority.
- **Cancel request stops new MCP calls at once** (`controller.findACPMCPTaskExecution`): A recorded cancel-request annotation stops new MCP calls immediately; the Cancelled phase follows only on the next reconcile.
- **Provider-workspace pools bind auth Secret by name and UID** (`controller.resolveRuntimePoolAuthSecret`): Provider-workspace RuntimePools bind their unpredictable auth Secret name and immutable UID on the pool, and label-only discovery is forbidden for them. Deployment-backed pools keep epoch label selection because their deterministic mounted Secret predates the binding contract. ADR 0024. See [ADR 0024](0024-acp-execution-workspace-runtime-pools.md).
- **Select auth Secret of the active epoch** (`controller.resolveRuntimePoolAuthSecret`): During graceful epoch replacement both the draining and next epoch auth Secrets exist, so resolution selects the one mounted by the pool's exact active instance instead of requiring a single Secret.
- **Tool drift tests use production broker components** (`controller.TestProductionACPMCPBrokerRejectsLateCustomToolDrift`): The ACP MCP tool drift tests wrap only the scheduling barrier; authentication, snapshot resolution, prompt authorization, effect reservation, and the custom Tool executor stay production so the race is tested on the real path.
- **Forwarding Tool names exclude runtime built-ins** (`corev1alpha1.Tool`): The Tool CRD refuses forwarding Tools named like a runtime built-in tool, because the descriptor builder treats those names as provider-native and never forwards them; the CRD's name list must cover every built-in name a Tool can take.
- **Forwarding Tools need a credential-injecting policy** (`controller.forwardingPolicySupported`): A forwarding Tool backed by a Gateway policy is refused before the agent starts because the executor never forwards Gateway-policy requests; Direct, Connection, and AgentIdentity policies are accepted.
- **Forwarding Tools need the Bearer output** (`controller.forwardingPolicySupported`): A forwarding Tool whose policy would send the credential in another header or without the 'Bearer ' prefix is refused before the agent starts, because Azure Resource Manager would refuse every request.
- **Write tools count toward the tool limit** (`controller.adjustInputsForForwardingWrites`): A forwarding Tool's derived write tool counts toward the Task tool limit, and a Task already at the limit fails permanently instead of retrying a policy that can never be built.
- **Denying a write tool keeps codex planning** (`controller.adjustInputsForForwardingWrites`): A Task that denies only a forwarding write tool still plans on codex, whose built-in tools are enforced all or nothing, because the deny names no built-in tool; the Task keeps the Tool's reads and gets no write tool however the deny is spelled.
- **Forwarding writes need a readWrite link** (`controller.adjustInputsForForwardingWrites`): A forwarding Tool behind the requester's linked account offers its write tool only with a readWrite link, like write-class connector Tools, and hides it with a readOnly link or none rather than letting it fail after an approval. ADR 0033. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Read-only brokered tools leave state unchanged** (`controller.readOnlyBrokeredTools`): readOnlyBrokeredTools lists only tools whose every invocation leaves durable state unchanged; check_messages is consequential because its mark_read argument defaults to true.
- **Only provider-native tools are implicit** (`controller.normalizeACPProviderNativeToolPolicy`): normalizeACPProviderNativeToolPolicy expands only provider-native tools implied by an empty runtime allowlist. Brokered and custom tools are never implicit: when configured they already appear in the allowed list, so an explicit list is returned unchanged.
- **Planning inputs are adjusted before profile planning** (`controller.adjustInputsForConnectorTools`): adjustInputsForConnectorTools and adjustInputsForForwardingWrites apply connector visibility, forwarding writes, and approval defaults to copies of the planning inputs before the profile is planned; both the runtime plan and the MCP configuration are built from those copies so their digests agree and the frozen snapshot records the effective policy.
- **Connector write tools need approval and a readWrite link** (`controller.adjustInputsForConnectorTools`): Write-class connector tools are hidden when the requester's link is readOnly or the runtime cannot honor approvals, and visible connector write tools are added to the approval-required set; there is no connector write without approval. ADR 0033. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Provider-native tools shadow same-named Tools** (`controller.adjustInputsForConnectorTools`): Provider-native tool names take precedence over a Tool resource of the same name, so they are never judged connector-backed and such a Tool brings no forwarding write tool; admission also forbids those names.
- **Forwarded writes are approved by the broker on any runtime** (`controller.buildMCPPolicyConfigurationWithRegistry`): MCPApprovalPolicy.NeedsRuntimeApprovals ignores forwarded write tools because the broker gates their approval, so they are the one approval-required tool any runtime accepts; other approval-required tools need a qualified AgentKit or Foundry runtime.
- **Forwarding write tools derive from their Tool** (`controller.buildCanonicalMCPToolDescriptors`): A forwarding write tool is never a Tool itself: it is derived from its forwarding Tool, which must still allow writes when descriptors are built, and is never requested by name.
- **All policy paths honor Tool write approval** (`controller.buildCanonicalMCPToolDescriptors`): Every path that builds an MCP policy, including registered external runtimes, refuses a forwarding write the Tool requires approval for when the policy does not.
- **Approval-bound read timeouts are shortened** (`controller.buildCanonicalMCPToolDescriptors`): Approval-bound reads share the execution and receipt lease of approved writes, so their timeout is reduced below the broker deadline; equal timeouts would let the broker deadline expire first. Ungated reads keep their configured timeout.
- **Consequential Tool timeouts bounded by the effect ledger** (`controller.customACPMCPToolDescriptor`): Consequential calls run under the external-effect ledger, whose fixed lease covers only maxACPExternalEffectCallDuration; a longer configured timeout would be cut off and settled OutcomeUnknown, so it is rejected when the Tool is exposed. Forwarding write tools are held to the same bound.
- **Forwarding Tools are revalidated after admission** (`controller.forwardingToolDescriptor`): forwardingToolDescriptor rechecks a forwarding Tool after admission so a Tool stored before the rules existed cannot be exposed: it reads only, takes its credential from an outbound access policy, and declares nothing a call could use to leave its origin. ADR 0040. See [ADR 0040](0040-forwarded-cli-requests.md).
- **Disallowed tools normalized like policy construction** (`controller.adjustInputsForForwardingWrites`): adjustInputsForForwardingWrites normalizes DisallowedTools with sortedUnique as policy construction does, so a padded deny of a write tool removes both its approval and its descriptor.
- **Provider must grant forwarded writes** (`controller.providerGrantsForwardedWrites`): A linked-account forwarding write tool is offered only when the ConnectorProvider declares the tool as a forwarding tool that grants writes; otherwise every call would be refused.
- **Brokered tools do not affect codex read-only surface** (`controller.validateACPProviderNativePolicy`): validateACPProviderNativePolicy ignores brokered tool names, which the MCP broker enforces, so delegated children that add coordination tools keep the codex read-only surface.
- **Missing local prompt lease denies execution** (`controller.ACPMCPPromptLeaseRegistry`): ACPMCPPromptLeaseRegistry holds the controller-confirmed lease of each live approval-enabled prompt. Durable attempts and epoch fences stay authoritative, and a missing local lease denies execution, including after controller restart, because accepted prompts are not replayed.
- **Prompt lease renewal requires confirmed exact lease** (`controller.acpMCPPromptLease.renew`): renew consumes only a controller-sent renewal whose exact lease the runtime client confirmed, so an ambiguous or late response cannot revive prompt authority.
- **Lease authorization checks context directly with fresh clock** (`controller.ACPMCPPromptLeaseRegistry.authorize`): Prompt lease authorization checks the lease context directly, without waiting for a cancellation goroutine or remote cleanup, and reads the clock after the durable authorizer's I/O.
- **Task data guard callbacks stay short** (`controller.ACPMCPTaskDataGuard`): ACPMCPTaskDataGuard serializes brokered data access with authoritative prompt and Session transitions; its callback must finish Kubernetes reads before opening its data transaction and must never call a control-store mutation or a remote tool.
- **Terminal Task projections use the broker data interlock** (`controller.withACPTaskStatusGuard`): Terminal Task status projections take the same durable interlock as brokered data access, including cancellation that precedes the PromptAttempt's settlement.
- **Refresh authority after acquiring the data interlock** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.taskDataGuard`): Prompt cancellation, settlement, and Session generation changes share the durable interlock used for brokered data access; authority is refreshed after acquiring it and the interlock is held until the short SQLite transaction completes.
- **Stale fences stay definitive** (`controller.ACPMCPBroker.taskDataGuard`): In the task data guard only interlock contention and unreadable authority are recoverable; a confirmed stale fence keeps its definitive classification.
- **Runtime sessions are bound to their launch mode** (`controller.RuntimeSession`): A RuntimeSession is bound to its launch mode through the MCP binding digest, so a mode change recreates the session (from the Session transcript for a turn) instead of reusing a provider launched the other way, while a pre-approved launch keeps its digest.
- **Native settlement requires matching checkpoint** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.guardNativeSessionSettlement`): Appending a terminal result without the matching native state would make the previous checkpoint diverge from canonical history, so guardNativeSessionSettlement blocks it; a non-success marker settles with the existing checkpoint carried forward by carriedNativeCheckpoint.
- **Native capture intents hold no secrets** (`controller.nativeCaptureIntent`): nativeCaptureIntent retains only immutable, non-secret request identity on the Task; the client signs each send separately with the current controller credentials.
- **Native capture superseded only on proof** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.supersedeRetryableNativeCapture`): A fresh native capture supersedes the original (recorded in nativeCaptureIntent.Supersedes) only after the same runtime incarnation proves the original never started or failed with no surviving writer.
- **Non-success outcomes carry the previous checkpoint** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.carriedNativeCheckpoint`): A failed, cancelled, or lost prompt produces no native state because the supervisor poisons and retires that runtime, so waiting for a capture would wedge the Session; carriedNativeCheckpoint settles with the prior checkpoint, whose boundary advances with the marker. A pending capture intent from a successful prompt stays strict and must reconcile its exact receipt.
- **Incompatible native Tasks rejected before binding** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.validateTaskNativeSessionRuntime`): validateTaskNativeSessionRuntime rejects an incompatible Task before binding a staged import to a new Session control or acquiring its mutation lease; turn preparation checks again in case an import was staged after this read.
- **Native restore honors sessionRef cutoff** (`controller.nativeRestoreHonorsHistoryBoundary`): The provider reloads its whole saved conversation, so a native checkpoint may be restored only when the Task's sessionRef has no cutoff or the cutoff equals the checkpoint's own boundary.
- **Imported identities never become destination authority** (`controller.nativeRestoreForTask`): When restoring a native session, imported or captured runtime and policy identities never become destination authority; they are replaced with the destination fence, and the supervisor installs and loads with its actual fresh cwd.
- **Canonical bootstrap suppressed only after load proof** (`controller.verifyTaskNativeRestoration`): Canonical transcript bootstrap is suppressed only after the exact ACP load proof of the native session.
- **Operator-tainted runtimes are excluded from native continuity** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.skipNativeCaptureForOperatorTaint`): Operator access may change a child's private agent state, so a tainted runtime never becomes a Session's native continuity source: a first turn falls back to the canonical transcript, and a Session already depending on native continuity fails closed rather than continuing from an older checkpoint. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Capture receipt reconciliation is retrieval only** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.captureTaskNativeSession`): Native capture receipt reconciliation can only retrieve the immutable result of the original capture; it cannot stop a child or begin another capture.
- **Taint read after capture decides snapshot use** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.acceptNativeCapture`): An operator exec admitted before capture closed operator admission may have changed the private home; the capture barrier killed it but its effects are in the snapshot. orka-api persists the taint before forwarding any operator write, so acceptNativeCapture reads the authoritative taint after capture and discards a tainted snapshot. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Tainted fallback reconciles pending capture intent** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.fallBackFromOperatorTaintedCapture`): Before a tainted turn falls back to the canonical transcript, a persisted capture intent is reconciled to record that the turn has no native checkpoint, because settlement and runtime deletion otherwise keep waiting for one.
- **Capture-skipped events use a durable dedup key** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recordNativeCaptureSkipped`): recordNativeCaptureSkipped uses a durable deduplication key so the event survives annotation and event write ordering and controller restart without duplication.
- **Native capture recovery targets the surviving incarnation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverTaskNativeCapture`): Native capture recovery captures from the exact surviving built-in RuntimePool incarnation; before a checkpoint or capture intent exists, confirmed pool replacement or deletion lets the turn settle canonically through the existing cleanup gates.
- **Recovery bypasses closed admission** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverTaskNativeCapture`): Native capture recovery reaches an existing session even when the pool's admission for new work is closed.
- **Poisoned workspace validation has no native checkpoint** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.nativeCaptureWorkspaceUnsupported`): Poisoned workspace validation cannot produce a native checkpoint, but callers with an existing capture intent must still reconcile its exact receipt.
- **Native cancel tests stop the provider directly** (`controller.TestACPDispatcherNativeRecoveryRequestedCancellationKeepsItsMessage`): Native-session cancellation tests stop the simulated provider directly on cancel; requiring a TCP disconnect first would make settlement depend on HTTP transport timing and flake.
- **Crash test clients never use a proxy** (`controller.nativeProcessCrashRESTConfig`): The native process crash test's parent and child processes must reach the same local API server directly; a loopback URL alone does not exclude client-go's ambient HTTP(S)_PROXY, so nativeProcessCrashRESTConfig disables the proxy explicitly.
- **Operator taint reads are authoritative and fail closed** (`controller.acpTaskOperatorTainted`): acpTaskOperatorTainted treats a Task as tainted when the Task or its exact linked ExecutionWorkspace incarnation carries the operator marker. It reads the uncached API so a marker orka-api just wrote is never missed, and fails closed on any read error other than a vanished workspace. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Linked-Task taint uses the recorded incarnation** (`controller.acpTaskOperatorTainted`): Linked-Task taint is evaluated for the Task's recorded workspace incarnation UID, not whatever the name resolves to now, because a delayed or recovery publication still carries data from that incarnation after it is deleted or replaced.
- **orka-api refuses forge writes for tainted Tasks** (`controller.ACPTaskOperatorTainted`): ACPTaskOperatorTainted lets orka-api's credential broker refuse forge write credentials when operator access tainted the Task, its linked workspace incarnation, or any Task linked to that incarnation; the reader must be uncached. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Workspace taint also honors linked Task markers** (`controller.acpWorkspaceOperatorTainted`): orka-api marks both the workspace and the tainted Task. Task markers and workspace links are protected by Task provenance admission while the workspace marker has a separate policy, so a missing workspace marker never proves an incarnation clean; every publish, reuse, cold-resume, suspend, or checkpoint decision also honors the marker of any Task linked to its exact UID, and reads fail closed. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Unpersisted workspaces are untainted** (`controller.acpWorkspaceOperatorTainted`): A workspace without a UID was never stored, and Tasks link to incarnations only by UID, so acpWorkspaceOperatorTainted reports it untainted.
- **Taint refusals are distinguishable from cancellations** (`controller.operatorTaintedPublicationMessage`): operatorTaintedPublicationMessage is the bounded terminal message recorded on publication, delivery status, and Task for an operator-taint refusal, so it is distinguishable from a user cancellation, which has an empty terminal reason.
- **One pre-publish refusal decision for all paths** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskPublicationRefused`): taskPublicationRefused is the single pre-publishing decision for fresh and recovery publication: cancelled Tasks and operator-tainted sessions both settle as cancelled before publish so tainted workspace contents never reach a forge, and a user cancellation takes precedence over a taint reason. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Workspace operator taint is permanent** (`workspace.ExecutionWorkspace`): The shared core admission policy makes the workspace operator taint permanent for every identity, including holders of the core admit verb, while other core writes and the first taint write stay admitted. ADR 0044. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Admission policy tests probe with dry runs** (`controller.TestWorkspaceCoreAdmissionPolicyMakesTheOperatorTaintPermanent`): ValidatingAdmissionPolicy activation is asynchronous, so the taint-permanence test probes with dry-run updates; a real update admitted before activation would clear the fixture marker.
- **Only permanent outbox failures dead-letter** (`controller.permanentOutboxDeliveryError`): Only permanentOutboxDeliveryError failures (payload corruption, identity mismatch) may dead-letter a terminal Task projection. Infrastructure failures such as a control-plane outage stay pending, because recovery treats an existing projection as authoritative and Task deletion requires Delivered, so dead-lettering a transient failure would leave the Task nonterminal and undeletable. ADR 0012. See [ADR 0012](0012-gateway-inbox-outbox-semantics.md).
- **Outbox UID mismatch is permanent** (`controller.ACPOutboxProjector.deliver`): A Task terminal projection whose Task UID mismatches is a permanent delivery error so MaxAttempts applies: bounded retries cover an in-progress restore settlement, while a name recreated with a different UID can never deliver and must not be retried forever.
- **Restored Task projections deliver as a no-op** (`controller.ACPOutboxProjector.deliver`): Clean-cluster restore settlement already patched the new Task incarnation directly, and the projection stays immutable source history, so delivering it against the restored UID is an exact no-op.
- **Outbox messages are valid UTF-8 and rune-truncated** (`controller.sanitizeOutboxError`): The outbox store rejects invalid UTF-8, so terminal projection messages repair invalid sequences and truncate on a rune boundary; otherwise the projection could never settle.
- **Legacy projection test bypasses normalization** (`controller.legacyTaskTerminalProjection`): The outbox regression test encodes through a distinct legacyTaskTerminalProjection type so future encoding normalization cannot hide the rows persisted by the broken controller version.
- **Failed park read releases the runtime** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.turnRequestedPark`): turnRequestedPark reads the authoritative Task for a wait recorded by park_until during this exact attempt and prompt; a failed read counts as parked so the runtime is released and its process memory is never reused by a continuation, at the cost of a recreated RuntimeSession. ADR 0041. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Parked runtime releases are retried** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.releaseParkedRuntimeSession`): releaseParkedRuntimeSession deletes the exact resident RuntimeSession of a parked read-only turn after its Session result is final; a failed deletion is recorded and retried by retryParkedRuntimeRelease before the next turn claims a resident slot, so cleanup never waits for spare capacity. ADR 0041. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Forget only the retired binding** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.forgetRetiredRuntimeSessionBinding`): The Session lease is released when a parked result is finalized, so a later turn may already have installed a newer binding; forgetRetiredRuntimeSessionBinding removes the cached binding only while it names the exact retired RuntimeSession incarnation.
- **Parked release retries never delete usable sessions** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.retryParkedRuntimeRelease`): retryParkedRuntimeRelease retries each failed parked release as its own exact incarnation, forgetting it only after deletion succeeds or its runtime instance was replaced, and never deletes a RuntimeSession generation the current turn could use.
- **Parked release keys name exact incarnations** (`controller.acpParkedRuntimeReleaseKey`): acpParkedRuntimeReleaseKey names one exact RuntimeSession incarnation so releases of different generations or runtime boots never replace or clear each other.
- **Permission holds end before the runtime timer** (`controller.acpPermissionHoldMargin`): acpPermissionHoldMargin ends a permission hold before the runtime's own permission timer answers, so the controller's answer always reaches a still-pending request.
- **Permission cancel retries fit the settle timeout** (`controller.acpPermissionCancelBackoff`): acpPermissionCancelBackoff paces retries of recording a cancelled permission request and must stay within acpApprovalSettleTimeout.
- **Frozen tool policy decides permissions first** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.resolvePromptPermission`): resolvePromptPermission lets the frozen tool policy decide first; a Task that asks for its requester's decisions holds only requests that policy would allow, off the prompt stream, until the requester decides, the hold expires, or the prompt ends.
- **Permission decisions use the structured tool name** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.planPermissionDecision`): A Requester Task's frozen MCP configuration carries NativePermissionsAsk, so its RuntimeSession asks before every policy-allowed acting tool; planPermissionDecision still decides only on the request's structured tool name.
- **Brokered approvals are not asked twice** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.planPermissionDecision`): A brokered tool that requires approval already waits at the MCP broker, so the runtime permission prompt is not held for it too.
- **Permission hold IDs use their own domain** (`controller.acpPermissionApprovalID`): acpPermissionApprovalID uses a domain separate from brokered tool approvals so brokered approval recovery never adopts a held permission request.
- **Permission holds answer only recorded outcomes** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.runPermissionHold`): Exactly one terminal approval event wins in the store, and runPermissionHold answers the runtime only with an outcome read back from the store, never inferred from the timer; past the deadline it keeps recording expiry until that or another outcome wins, so the answer matches the approvals API.
- **Permission cancellations are retried** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.cancelPermissionHold`): No hold remains after cancelPermissionHold, so a transient store failure would leave the durable request pending until expiry; it retries within the settle time and treats a conflict as the request already ended.
- **Requester Tasks launch runtimes that ask** (`controller.taskNativePermissions`): A requester can decide only permission requests the runtime sends, so taskNativePermissions launches a Requester Task's runtime to ask before each policy-allowed acting native tool.
- **Unsupported permission runtimes fail before launch** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.rejectPermissionDecisionsRuntime`): A Requester Task whose runtime cannot launch a session that asks fails before any RuntimeSession exists instead of running its tools without asking.
- **Retained cleanup clients grant no execution authority** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.runtimePoolRetainedCleanupClient`): runtimePoolRetainedCleanupClient lets a new controller owner finish cleanup of an exact, still-authenticated old native boot, including validating an abandoned terminal turn, but grants no new execution authority: every mutation revalidates the leader, frozen Task, exact Pod and pool, immutable auth material, and old runtime fence. The ordinary runtimePoolClient stays current-epoch-only for admission.
- **Cleanup keeps the old supervisor's generation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.runtimePoolRetainedCleanupClient`): Retained pool cleanup uses the old supervisor's authenticated RuntimePool generation, never a newer desired Deployment generation, as execution authority.
- **Retained cleanup transport binds exact requests** (`controller.retainedPoolCleanupTransport`): Old-epoch runtime credentials are pool-wide, so retainedPoolCleanupTransport binds the actual encoded request rather than a client method name, preventing a cleanup client from targeting a peer Session or outliving its current owner.
- **Cleanup transport holds the recovery interlock** (`controller.retainedPoolCleanupTransport`): retainedPoolCleanupTransport holds the authoritative recovery interlock through transmission and the bounded response body, because the old runtime epoch alone cannot fence out a new leader.
- **Accepted prompt stream stays readable during cancellation** (`controller.streamACPPromptWithCancellation`): streamACPPromptWithCancellation keeps the accepted stream readable while cancellation settles, because a completed settlement alone cannot recover result text; only the stream, including a validated clean EOF, proves it. Lease renewal and permission decisions still use the execution context.
- **Cancellation uses a Task copy** (`controller.streamACPPromptWithCancellation`): Cancellation works on a deep copy of the Task because the stream callback may update Task status while cancellation is in flight.
- **Abort the stream unless completion is proven** (`controller.streamACPPromptWithCancellation`): During cancellation the stream transport is kept only when a proven completion still needs result text, and is aborted when revocation is unproven or no result remains.
- **No prompt submit after cancelled context** (`controller.streamACPPromptWithCancellation`): context.AfterFunc runs asynchronously even for an already-cancelled context, so streamACPPromptWithCancellation checks the context before submitting a new prompt.
- **Join cancellation watcher before session cleanup** (`controller.streamACPPromptWithCancellation`): Stream-error handling reuses this exact operation's cancellation response and joins the watcher before the RuntimeSession can be cleaned up.
- **Clean-room publication excludes the ACP child** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.publishWorkspaceDelta`): publishWorkspaceDelta runs the clean-room prepare/publish/verify transaction for one immutable, durable workspace delta; the ACP child never controls the repository, branch, commit, credential, or request identity it uses.
- **Publication windows budget every sequential stage** (`controller.publicationSettlementWindow`): publishWorkspaceDelta runs up to maxSequentialPublisherSettlementStages (3) publisher calls sequentially under each bounded context: the delivery context covers branch-claim refresh, preflight, and prepare; the detached settlement context created after the publishing CAS covers publish, verify, and PR reconciliation. publicationSettlementWindow = stages x (call timeout + margin) + margin (16m at the 4m default); budgeting one call would let a slow publish starve verification and misclassify a delivered push as outcome-unknown.
- **Publication pinned to verified revision** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.publishWorkspaceDelta`): Publication preparation is pinned to the verified immutable baseline revision; the original selector may be a bare branch or tag and is not durable enough to reconstruct later publish or verify requests.
- **Cancellation cannot abort a won publication** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.publishWorkspaceDelta`): Once publishing wins the durable CAS, Task cancellation must not abort exact-commit publication or independent remote verification, so settlement runs on a detached bounded context.
- **PR numbers parsed as 32-bit** (`controller.pullRequestNumberFromForgeID`): Pull-request numbers are parsed with bitSize 32 so they always fit a platform int; real forge numbers never exceed it and larger values are invalid forge identities.
- **Commit messages end in one newline** (`controller.taskPublicationCommitMessage`): taskPublicationCommitMessage returns the Task-authored message ending in exactly one newline, as the publisher requires, or the default title.
- **Cancel request stops publication at once** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskCancellationRequested`): A recorded cancel-request annotation stops publication immediately; the Cancelled phase follows only on the next reconcile.
- **Ambiguous publication keeps its branch claim** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reclaimStandaloneTaskBranchClaim`): An ambiguous remote publication outcome retains its branch claim as a durable fail-closed ownership barrier until explicit reconciliation.
- **Recovery never reclaims a replaced owner** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reclaimStandaloneTaskBranchClaim`): If the immutable Task-owned identity was deleted and replaced, recovery for the old terminal Task must never reclaim the new owner.
- **Harness 'empty' revision never enters Task status** (`controller.taskDeliveryStatusForKubernetes`): Harness v2 uses 'empty' as a protocol-only revision for Tasks without a repository workspace; it is not a Git object ID and must not reach schema-validated Task status. Every other value is preserved so malformed real revisions fail closed at the API; a workspace with intent or options but no repository counts as repository-less, matching emptyRuntimeWorkspace and prepareRuntimeWorkspace.
- **Terminal projections carry the full frozen identity** (`controller.terminalProjectionExecution`): Prompt attempt reclamation validates the terminal projection against the attempt's request digest and the Task's complete runtime identity (supervisor boot ID, profile, MCP, and workspace digests), so terminalProjectionExecution deep-copies the frozen execution identity; a sparse payload would make the PromptAttempt unretirable and the Task undeletable. The volatile transition time is cleared so retries enqueue byte-identical payloads.
- **PR metadata comes from the verified snapshot** (`controller.withTaskPullRequestMetadata`): withTaskPullRequestMetadata takes PR metadata from the verified execution snapshot's Task, never agent output, and the external-effect digest freezes it across retries.
- **PR text validated before truncation** (`controller.validateTaskPullRequestText`): validateTaskPullRequestText runs before SCM effects are reserved and inspects the full prompt line before truncation, so a truncated credential cannot escape detection.
- **PR presentation fields need peer capability** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.pullRequestPresentationCapability`): New PR presentation fields require a publisher that advertises them. During rollout ordinary Tasks keep the legacy wire shape, while explicit overrides wait before prompt admission and are checked again before any branch publication.
- **Publication recovery reuses the attached identity** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcilePersistedPublication`): reconcilePersistedPublication resumes only the immutable Publication attached to a successfully settled prompt and never allocates a new prompt or Publication identity.
- **Continuations read the verified publication target** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcilePersistedPublication`): Continuation preparation reads from the independently verified publication target rather than the original upstream or PR base.
- **Completed publication effects replay without the publisher** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverPublicationPullRequest`): An exact completed external effect is durable evidence, so replaying it must not depend on the publisher still being available or upgraded.
- **Legacy PR reconcile shapes proven by digest** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.loadPersistedPullRequestRequest`): Older controllers omitted Task presentation and, before that, sessionUid in PR reconcile requests; recovery accepts only the exact legacy shape proved by the original digest, and a same-branch PR never establishes legacy ownership.
- **Per-Task recovery errors vs startup errors** (`controller.acpTaskRecoveryErrors`): acpTaskRecoveryErrors marks failures isolated to surviving Tasks; store initialization, epoch acquisition, and listing failures remain startup errors.
- **Stale attempts past the write boundary become OutcomeUnknown** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverStaleAttempts`): recoverStaleAttempts classifies every old-epoch ACP attempt before the new leader admits work: it resumes only states that provably crossed no prompt request-write boundary, and makes every potentially accepted prompt terminally OutcomeUnknown without allocating a replacement attempt.
- **Stale recovery runs on one worker outside admission** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.scheduleStaleAttemptRecoveries`): scheduleStaleAttemptRecoveries retries the whole stale batch on one worker so bounded runtime and network waits never occupy prompt admission slots; the active-Task map still excludes simultaneous recovery owners.
- **Restored Task UIDs never rekey durable records** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverRestoredTaskIncarnation`): Kubernetes UIDs are incarnation fences, not portable identifiers. recoverRestoredTaskIncarnation settles a Task whose UID changed in a clean-cluster restore while every durable source record stays keyed by the UID frozen in AgentExecutionBinding and is never rewritten or replayed under the restored UID.
- **Restored Validating attempts settle without a Publication** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleRestoredTerminalDelivery`): Publication creation precedes the Validating to Preparing transition, so a restored Validating attempt may have no Publication and settles fail-closed without inventing one.
- **Deleting Tasks re-fenced only in two cases** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.deletingTaskRecoverable`): Stale recovery never replays deleting prompts or drops runtime cleanup authority a Task finalizer waits on. deletingTaskRecoverable re-fences a deleting Task only when attempt and Task execution are still before submission with no RuntimeSession binding (admission then cancels it before runtime contact), or when a standalone attempt already settled and only its terminal projection remains.
- **Re-fencing preserves later Task execution states** (`controller.acpTaskExecutionBeforeSubmission`): Re-fencing rewrites a Task's execution state, so only Tasks whose own execution status is still before prompt submission qualify; a terminal or later state must be kept.
- **Resident status fences unknown native creates** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverStaleTask`): If the unknown-create marker could not be persisted, authenticated resident runtime status still fences a native installation before stale-epoch pre-submission recovery re-admits or rotates the Task.
- **Recovered cancel requests stay OutcomeUnknown** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverStaleTask`): A cancel request recorded before leadership changed does not prove the prompt stopped, so recovery keeps the outcome unknown and carries the request in the terminal message.
- **Archived Session recovery requires proof** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverArchivedTerminalSession`): recoverArchivedTerminalSession recognizes completed Session deletion without recreating control, transcript, turn, or outbox rows; the Task epoch advances only after its delivered projection and runtime retirement proof both validate, and legacy deletions without that evidence stay blocked.
- **Recovery keeps the live failure classification** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRestoredJournaledPromptTerminal`): Recovery uses the same fixed status classification as the live path and never copies runtime diagnostics into another durable or public message; they stay in their canonical journal event.
- **Deleting Tasks of live read Sessions keep authority** (`controller.taskScopedRuntimeSessionCleanupCompleteForUID`): A live read Session keeps its conversation process across Tasks, so a deleting Task keeps its frozen authority until Session cleanup records an exact runtime cleanup receipt.
- **Rotated external runtimes clean up via frozen endpoint** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.externalRuntimeRotatedEndpointCleanupClient`): When a live AgentRuntime now points elsewhere, externalRuntimeRotatedEndpointCleanupClient rebuilds cleanup authority from the immutable binding: the frozen endpoint must authenticate the exact resident runtime fence, and the live object is authoritative only for the AgentRuntime UID.
- **Retained cleanup authority is native-only** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): Unknown terminal recovery on a native pool uses exact retained cleanup authority for every mutation, including deletion after validation, even before an epoch changes, and retained boots never get admission authority. Workspace pools keep their current-epoch backend-aware client and wait for a new epoch owner.
- **Replacement allows settlement, not retirement** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): Once a runtime is confirmed replaced, durable terminal settlement can proceed without contacting the replacement, but runtime retirement still requires its own proof.
- **Replacement requires complete status identity** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): Recovery waits for a complete authenticated status identity before deciding a runtime was replaced, because the same runtime may still own the session.
- **Boot drift never mints a cleanup receipt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): A different boot on the same AgentRuntime cannot prove the frozen boot deleted its RuntimeSession, so recovery waits for exact cleanup proof rather than minting a receipt from identity drift.
- **Session deletion is delete-only** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): Session deletion never runs validation; normal Task recovery must first finish its abandoned local validation barrier using native-only retained cleanup authority, so other backends keep waiting.
- **Validation responses are not retirement receipts** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): After validation or finalization, recovery re-observes the exact runtime and never treats a validation response as a runtime retirement receipt.
- **Lost validation responses resume the same receipt** (`controller.abandonedValidationFinalization`): A validation response can be lost after a delta was prepared, so recovery resumes the same canonical no-publication receipt via abandonedValidationFinalization rather than an abandonment identity derived from the Task's empty delivery.
- **External runtime epoch rechecked before deletion** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reconcileRecoveredRuntimeSession`): Runtime status can outlive controller leadership and pool clients lack the external-runtime mutation hook, so recovery rechecks the external runtime cleanup epoch before exact deletion.
- **Recovered Tasks keep uncleaned task-scoped bindings** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchRecoveredTaskReserved`): patchRecoveredTaskReserved clears the RuntimeSession binding when returning a Task to admission, except a task-scoped session the attempt released without a cleanup receipt (such as after a rate-limited prompt whose delete failed): the Task is then the only record of that cleanup obligation, and the next dispatch retires it first.
- **Identity-only recovered bindings are not reusable** (`controller.recoveredRuntimeSessionBinding`): recoveredRuntimeSessionBinding rebuilds a binding from durable execution status so recovery settlement carries the live generation and digests; a Task that never bound a RuntimeSession yields an identity-only binding callers must not reuse as live.
- **Recovery preserves an existing terminal projection** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchRecoveredTerminalExecution`): The live settlement may have committed its terminal projection before Task status reached recovery, so recovery preserves that record's message and delivery evidence instead of rebuilding a different payload.
- **Pre-push cancellation settles Cancelled** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchRecoveredTerminalExecution`): Matching the live settlement and restoredTaskPhase, a publication cancelled before push settles the Task as Cancelled, not Failed.
- **Workspace-backed pools never share plain pools** (`controller.ACPRuntimePlan.Workspace`): ACPRuntimePlan.Workspace binds a pool to an execution-workspace provider and changes PoolName, so workspace-backed sessions never share a plain pool and acpRuntimePoolWorkspaceMatchesPlan requires an exact binding match both ways. ADR 0024. See [ADR 0024](0024-acp-execution-workspace-runtime-pools.md).
- **ACP planning requires explicit v2 classification** (`controller.validateACPRuntimePlanningAgent`): validateACPRuntimePlanningAgent admits ACP planning only for a complete built-in runtime with an explicit orka.harness.v2 classification and a valid v2 OpenCode shape; a missing selector is never protocol evidence.
- **Delivery plan refresh preserves frozen profile** (`controller.currentACPRuntimeDeliveryPlan`): currentACPRuntimeDeliveryPlan validates controller-owned runtime artifacts and refreshes the plain-pool identity while preserving the Task's immutable runtime profile. Workspace-backed pools keep their frozen plan because rotating their workspace has separate lifecycle and lineage rules, and a retired workspace image is usable only through an exact preexisting pool.
- **Materialize native grant before injecting tools** (`controller.effectiveACPAllowedTools`): A nil allowlist means the implicit full native grant, so the native defaults are materialized before brokered coordination tools are appended; appending to nil would replace them with an allowlist of only the messaging tools.
- **Repository-monitor presets map to per-runtime read surfaces** (`controller.requestedACPAllowedTools`): Repository-monitor presets use Claude-style path-scoped names that are not canonical provider-native descriptors, so requestedACPAllowedTools translates them into the exact read-only surface each runtime can enforce.
- **OpenCode monitor surface omits Grep** (`controller.requestedACPAllowedTools`): OpenCode's Grep permission cannot carry the path-specific secret-file exclusions applied to Read, so the repository-monitor surface for OpenCode is Read and Glob only.
- **Copilot monitor surface is Glob, Grep, Read** (`controller.requestedACPAllowedTools`): Copilot has no LS tool, so the untranslated monitor preset would be an invalid runtime profile; its read-only surface is Glob, Grep, and Read.
- **Codex read-only surface is enforced by the session boundary** (`controller.requestedACPAllowedTools`): Codex has no per-tool switches; its Glob/Grep/Read monitor surface is enforced by the RuntimeSession boundary: elevation requests are rejected, file writes are supervisor-mediated, and read-intent workspace delta classification fails any modifying turn.
- **Kubernetes is authoritative for Session lineage** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuityConfig.Lineages`): Session lineage is established or verified in the Kubernetes SessionControl status CAS that records the mutation Lease; the SQLite lineage store only receives an idempotent projection afterwards, and on projection failure the lease is retained so no other owner proceeds before an idempotent retry repairs it.
- **Session continuity does not submit prompts** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity`): ACPSessionContinuity owns durable ACP Session identity, mutation fencing, canonical transcript bootstrap, and atomic SessionTurn completion, and deliberately never submits prompts.
- **Session continuity requires all stores** (`controller.NewACPSessionContinuity`): NewACPSessionContinuity requires every store because publication ambiguity and blocked-session recovery must be classified from durable receipts, never caller assertions.
- **Ensure session converges on durable UID** (`controller.ACPEnsureSessionRequest`): ACPEnsureSessionRequest.ExpectedSessionUID turns loading into an exact identity assertion; without it, creation reuses any already-bound durable identity before generating a UID, concurrent creators converge on the first durable UID, and an expected different UID is rejected.
- **Bind-before-control UIDs are never unbound** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.sessionUIDForCreate`): sessionUIDForCreate preserves the durable bind-before-control identity after a failed or lost Kubernetes create and never unbinds it, because the original create may have succeeded even when the caller saw an error.
- **Read after any transcript create error** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.ensureTranscriptSession`): SessionStore predates typed create conflicts, so after any create error the transcript Session is read back and concurrent creators converge without depending on driver errors.
- **Session leases bind the prompt digest** (`controller.ACPAcquireSessionLeaseRequest`): ACPAcquireSessionLeaseRequest acquires the next generation from an exact SessionControl snapshot, and PromptRequestDigest binds the lease to the immutable prompt attempt so a different prompt cannot reuse it.
- **Only Kubernetes decides lineage establishment** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.prepareSessionLineageClaim`): prepareSessionLineageClaim only decides whether an absent lineage may be established; Kubernetes stays the decision point, so a stale caller seeing a nonempty transcript can verify a lineage a concurrent first user committed but cannot establish one. Non-Gateway lineage is established only while the transcript is empty, and unclassified existing transcripts fail closed.
- **SessionTurn opens before any prompt write** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.OpenTurn`): The durable SessionTurn is opened after the PromptAttempt is bound to the lease but before any prompt request is written; prompt execution must not cross this barrier until OpenTurn succeeds.
- **Turns finalize only under their exact snapshot** (`controller.ACPSessionTurn`): ACPSessionTurn is the exact session and turn snapshot used for finalization; a newer lease or Session version cannot finalize it.
- **Finalization projections own their identity** (`controller.ACPFinalizationProjection`): ACPFinalizationProjection is committed with the SessionTurn, and the helper owns its aggregate identity and payload digest so callers cannot project a different turn.
- **Ambiguous publication blocks the Session** (`controller.ACPFinalizeAssistantRequest`): When publication is terminally ambiguous, the assistant result stays canonical but the Session and BranchClaim remain reconciliation-blocked.
- **OutcomeUnknown never carries assistant content** (`controller.ACPFinalizeOutcomeUnknownRequest`): ACPFinalizeOutcomeUnknownRequest intentionally has no assistant-result field and FinalizeOutcomeUnknown never accepts or synthesizes assistant content, preventing invented output when acceptance or settlement cannot be established.
- **Verified baseline derives only from Publication receipt** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.FinalizeAssistantResult`): Session finalization never sets VerifiedBaseline directly; the durable store advances it only by deriving it from the independently verified Publication receipt, and releases only the matching lease.
- **Cleanup auth requires a pre-takeover conformance snapshot** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionRuntimeCleanupAuthSnapshot`): sessionRuntimeCleanupAuthSnapshot proves a restored credential version was conformed before takeover, because the admission Ready bit cannot prove it once the surviving supervisor's startup epoch is older than the controller's.
- **Blocked Session recovery needs an independent receipt** (`controller.ACPIndependentBranchReceipt`, `controller.ACPSessionContinuity.RecoverBlockedSession`): ACPIndependentBranchReceipt records a fresh remote branch observation made outside the runtime Pod, with a digest over repository, ref, SHA, operation, and observation time. RecoverBlockedSession uses it to return a PublicationOutcomeUnknown or DeliveryConflict Session and BranchClaim to Available, cannot run without it, and loads store versions immediately before the cross-aggregate CAS.
- **Settlement writers never replace an authoritative completionTime** (`controller.TestACPSessionResultProjectionPreservesTerminalLifecycleMessage`): Neither the queued outbox projection delivery nor the direct terminal delivery helper may overwrite a Task completionTime already written by an interrupted prior settlement pass; the first authoritative value is kept.
- **Terminal recovery skips turns whose live finalization ran the activation tail** (`controller.TestACPSessionResultProjectionPreservesTerminalLifecycleMessage`): sessionTurnRequiresTerminalRecovery reports no recovery for a turn whose live finalization already ran the activation tail, so the recovery sweep never resettles the Task as if live settlement had been interrupted.
- **Recovered terminal sessions replay the immutable outbox projection** (`controller.TestACPSessionResultProjectionPreservesTerminalLifecycleMessage`): When finalizeRecoveredTerminalSession finds an already finalized SessionTurn, it replays the stored immutable outbox projection, including its message, rather than building a new one.
- **RuntimeSession binding generation advances only on recreation** (`controller.ACPRuntimeSessionBinding`): ACPRuntimeSessionBinding is the controller-owned durable identity projected into RuntimeSession status; its Generation increments whenever the provider session must be recreated instead of transparently recovered.
- **PromptDelivered is memory-only and errs toward repeating context** (`controller.ACPRuntimeSessionBinding`): ACPRuntimeSessionBinding.PromptDelivered lives only in controller memory: creating a RuntimeSession clears it and a binding rebuilt from Task status starts false. Once-per-process prompt context such as the toolbox note may therefore repeat after a controller restart, but is never skipped for a process that has not received a prompt.
- **Profile rotation or runtime loss bumps generation exactly once** (`controller.PlanACPRuntimeSession`): PlanACPRuntimeSession picks a monotonic RuntimeSession generation: a profile rotation or proven runtime loss increments it exactly once and always requires canonical bootstrap, while unchanged live state is reused.
- **Recreated RuntimeSessions respect the workspace generation floor** (`controller.enforceACPRuntimeSessionGenerationFloor`): enforceACPRuntimeSessionGenerationFloor prevents a recreated provider RuntimeSession from reusing a generation already committed on durable workspace data; only a reusable live binding exactly at the floor stays valid.
- **Live RuntimeSession reuse requires an exact workspace identity match** (`controller.bindACPRuntimeSessionWorkspace`): bindACPRuntimeSessionWorkspace reuses a live session only when repository, source ref, verified baseline, intent, and relative root all match. Initial or already-rotated sessions record the desired digest, and a mismatch on a reused live session advances the monotonic generation exactly once.
- **Session runtime cleanup fences are deletion-only** (`controller.sessionRuntimeCleanupFence`): sessionRuntimeCleanupFence may use the terminal execution's older runtime epoch but never grants admission. Session teardown fences permit only exact deletion; only a standalone terminal fence may also finalize the evidence-bound publication that must precede that deletion.
- **Only standalone terminal cleanup may finalize publication** (`controller.sessionRuntimeCleanupFence`): sessionRuntimeCleanupFence.allowPublicationFinalization lets standalone terminal cleanup finish an evidence-bound publication before exact deletion; Session teardown never sets it, so its authority remains delete-only.
- **Runtime retirement revalidates the cleanup owner from the authoritative reader** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.externalRuntimeCleanupEpoch`): Runtime retirement runs outside the epoch mutation lock, so externalRuntimeCleanupEpoch rereads the controller epoch fence from the authoritative reader instead of trusting the manager's cached fence before using the cleanup owner.
- **Session runtime cleanup derives authority only from finalized turns** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.CleanupSessionRuntime`): CleanupSessionRuntime runs only after the durable Session cleanup intent blocks new turns, and takes all runtime authority from finalized turns and verified immutable Task execution snapshots. It also replays interrupted Session deletions.
- **Continued RuntimeSessions are cleaned once by their newest turn** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.CleanupSessionRuntime`): CleanupSessionRuntime sorts targets by descending lease generation because a continued RuntimeSession belongs to its newest completed turn; each exact runtime incarnation is deleted once and proof is recorded for every Task that retained the same conversation process.
- **Write sessions pass a Task-scoped runtime cleanup barrier** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionRuntimeCleanupTarget`): Write sessions are Task-scoped and pass their runtime cleanup barrier independently of conversation deletion; Session cleanup only checks runtimeSessionCleanupCompleteForUID, and their Task finalizers retain frozen authority until Session cleanup completes.
- **Mutable Task status cannot authorize a prior-epoch runtime mutation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.sessionRuntimeCleanupTarget`): Projections may recover omitted identity from mutable Task status, but only a complete, explicitly frozen execution (RuntimeSessionUID and ControllerEpoch present) can supply the resident runtime's original epoch for a prior-epoch mutation.
- **Only the Task's current runtime incarnation receives a cleanup receipt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recordSessionRuntimeCleanupForTask`): Session runtime cleanup leaves an older attempt's immutable turn archived and writes the Task-scoped cleanup status receipt only for the Task's current runtime incarnation.
- **Two-dispatch cleanup test uses a one-minute fixture timeout** (`controller.newContinuedSessionCleanupFixture`): The two-turn session cleanup test raises the fixture context timeout to one minute because both prompt dispatches include event redaction, which needs more setup time under the race detector than the default.
- **Bootstrap transcripts omit structured tool arguments** (`controller.ACPBootstrapTranscript`): ACPBootstrapTranscript intentionally drops structured tool arguments: session continuity restores conversation text only and never replays provider-native or tool state.
- **Task transcript limit applies before continuity-wide caps** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.BuildBootstrapTranscriptWithLimit`): BuildBootstrapTranscriptWithLimit applies the Task-specific message limit before the continuity-wide byte and message caps so a caller's narrower context boundary is never widened during provider-session recreation.
- **Gateway bootstrap excludes the terminal prompt message from transcript** (`controller.ACPSessionContinuity.BuildBootstrapTranscriptThrough`): BuildBootstrapTranscriptThrough returns the bounded transcript ending at an exact message plus that untruncated message; gateway Tasks use the terminal user message as the durable SessionTurn prompt and exclude it from the transcript so provider input has no duplicate prompt block.
- **A replacement supervisor boot cannot certify old runtime retirement** (`controller.assertUnknownCleanupRequiresOriginalRuntime`): Unknown-session cleanup requires proof from the original supervisor boot; a runtime reporting a different SupervisorBootID cannot certify the old boot's retirement, even after the canonical conversation has continued.
- **Unsupported runtimes reject skills rather than ignore them** (`controller.acpSkillRuntimeUnsupportedReasons`): acpSkillRuntimeUnsupportedReasons gives a reason for each built-in runtime that cannot receive skills; such runtimes are rejected at admission and planning instead of silently ignoring the Agent's skills.
- **Copilot runtime does not support Agent Skills** (`controller`): Copilot CLI 1.0.96 in ACP mode loads repository skills from .github/skills, .agents/skills, and .claude/skills regardless of settings, so enabling its skill tool would hand the model untrusted repository skills whose allowed-tools can add permission rules; acpSkillRuntimeSupport therefore excludes Copilot.
- **Runtime skills resolve the default source path before freezing** (`controller.RuntimeSkillsFromAgent`): RuntimeSkillsFromAgent preserves declared order and resolves each skill's default source path so the frozen profile names the exact folder that is read.
- **Skill admission checks are repeated at planning time** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy.agentSkillPlanRejection`): agentSkillPlanRejection repeats the Agent skill admission checks during planning so a Task never reaches a RuntimePool with skills it cannot bind, even if the Agent was admitted under a different policy.
- **Editing an Agent's skills selects a new RuntimePool** (`controller.TestPlanACPRuntimeFreezesSkillsIntoTheProfile`): RuntimePool profiles are frozen at planning time: editing an Agent's skills produces a different profile and pool, and a planned Task's frozen projection keeps the original skills.
- **RuntimePool startup probe budgets for skill and toolbox checks** (`controller.TestRuntimePoolSkillStartupProbeOutlastsSkillCheck`): The supervisor runs a bounded skill check before serving, after any toolbox check, so the RuntimePool startup probe must allow each check's timeout on top of the normal allowance or kubelet restarts the container before a classified failure is reported.
- **Session storage grows with resident capacity and skill count** (`controller.TestRuntimePoolSkillSessionCopiesAreCountedInSessionStorage`): Every resident session receives private skill copies under /sessions, so the sessions volume and the container's ephemeral storage scale with the pool's resident capacity and skill count, or a pool at capacity exhausts the volume on skill copies alone.
- **Task cleanup readiness orders detach before runtime receipts** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskDeletionReady`): acpTaskCleanupReady always requires terminal control-plane settlement. Workspace detach can produce runtime retirement receipts, so its preparatory check runs before those receipts and Session archival, while final Task reclamation still requires both through acpTaskDeletionReady.
- **Dispatcher-settled queued timeouts use the normal projected barriers** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskCleanupReady`): A queued timeout settled by the dispatcher already has a durable terminal projection, so acpTaskCleanupReady applies the normal projected cleanup barriers to it instead of the no-attempt shortcut.
- **Cleanup must settle or disprove an unbound PromptAttempt** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskCleanupReady`): A PromptAttempt is persisted before Task.status.execution, so acpTaskCleanupReady continues to the authoritative reclamation preflight, which either settles that unbound attempt or durably proves no attempt ever existed.
- **Missing final attempt defers to the store's reclamation marker** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskCleanupReady`): The final PromptAttempt may already have been removed by a prepared reclamation, so acpTaskCleanupReady lets the store validate its durable reclamation marker on retry instead of treating absence as an error.
- **Task keeps frozen cleanup authority until Session archive is durable** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskSessionDeletionReady`): Runtime retirement precedes the Session archival transaction, so the Task's frozen cleanup authority is kept until the archive is durable; a crash between the steps can then still resume the Session cleanup intent.
- **Cleanup recognizes Tasks cancelled before execution projection** (`controller.acpTaskRequiresAuthoritativeAttemptDiscovery`): A queued timeout, or a cancellation while the class workspace was being attached, ends the Task before its execution status is projected; cleanup recognizes this Cancelled shape with no RuntimePool and discovers any unbound PromptAttempt authoritatively.
- **Archived cleanup receipt identifies the terminal projection after reclamation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpTaskTerminalProjectionID`): After reclamation removes the PromptAttempt, the Task's runtime incarnation generation cannot reconstruct its per-prompt mutation lease, so acpTaskTerminalProjectionID uses the archived Session cleanup receipt, which still binds the exact attempt to its finalized turn.
- **Publication cache reclamation precedes durable Publication removal** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpPublicationReclaimTargets`): Publisher cache reclamation is ordered before durable Publication removal, so a missing Publication record means no cache was created or an earlier reclaim completed; the schema's initial acpPublicationGeneration then suffices for the endpoint's already-absent idempotency check.
- **Artifact retirement does not reclaim attempts or Session authority** (`controller.TaskReconciler.retireACPTaskArtifactReferences`): retireACPTaskArtifactReferences releases artifact capabilities after durable settlement without reclaiming PromptAttempts or Session cleanup authority; earlier artifact retirement never waives deleting-Task reclamation.
- **Prepared reclamation marker is authoritative after attempt removal** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpPromptAttemptReclamationRequest`): Once the final PromptAttempt has been reclaimed, the prepared reclamation marker is authoritative because the Task projection alone cannot reconstruct the frozen SessionTurn identity.
- **Bound Tasks pass one configuration gate before any demand** (`controller.TaskReconciler.queueACPRuntimeTask`): Ordinary planning and bound-task recovery both flow through the same workspace configuration and ACP admission gate before any workspace or RuntimePool demand exists, so it is the single place configuration rejects bound Tasks.
- **Task status carries only provider-neutral workspace projection** (`controller.TaskReconciler.queueACPRuntimeTask`): Task.Status.ExecutionWorkspace is a provider-neutral projection; no claim, sandbox, or other provider-native identifier ever enters public Task status. See [ADR 0024](0024-acp-execution-workspace-runtime-pools.md).
- **Workspace subPath is validated before repository resolution** (`controller.validateACPWorkspacePreflight`): Workspace preflight applies the RuntimeSession relative-root rule (ValidateWorkspaceRelativeRoot) to subPath before repository resolution and archive preparation, so a Task session creation must reject never spends SCM and artifact work.
- **Workspace-backed pool runtime namespace is frozen at workspace creation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPRuntimePoolWithPolicy`): The pool's runtime namespace comes from the linked workspace's creation-time annotation, not the mutable controller flag: workspace and pool creation happen on different reconciles, and a flag change between them would put the SandboxClaim and PVC in a namespace the deletion proofs never probe, falsely reporting data deleted. A frozen namespace the controller no longer permits fails the pool visibly.
- **Workspace-backed RuntimePools host one session and one prompt** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPRuntimePoolWithPolicy`): A workspace-backed RuntimePool hosts exactly one logical RuntimeSession inside one provider-owned physical workspace, so its capacity is fixed at MaxResidentSessions 1 and MaxRunningPrompts 1.
- **Workspace adapter deletes only pools carrying both link label and UID pin** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPRuntimePoolWithPolicy`): Workspace-backed pools carry the ExecutionWorkspace name link label and the workspace-incarnation UID annotation; the ACP workspace adapter proves exact ownership and deletes only a pool carrying both before tearing it down.
- **Deterministic-name pool race winners are validated before binding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPRuntimePoolWithPolicy`): When another creator wins the deterministic RuntimePool name race, the observed object is validated and activated through the same path as a preexisting pool; a Task is never bound to an unchecked same-name winner.
- **Pool required features come only from the frozen class and profile** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpWorkspacePoolRequiredFeatures`): acpWorkspacePoolRequiredFeatures collects requirements only from the exact class and profile frozen in the plan, and the nonempty set also freezes whether the pool materializes a native process.
- **Draining providers serve only existing Session workspaces** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpWorkspacePoolRequiredFeatures`): Draining advances the provider registration generation without replacing an admitted Session workspace's frozen installation, so only that exact logical Session-reuse workspace may be reused at an older generation; new identities still require an Active provider.
- **Workspace admission is rechecked uncached after pool materialization** (`controller.TaskReconciler.verifyACPWorkspaceReadyForPool`): verifyACPWorkspaceReadyForPool repeats the full workspace admission and attachment handshake through the uncached reader after a RuntimePool materializes. Recovery waits keep the data-bearing pool without allowing prompt dispatch, and withdrawn authority deletes the pool before prompt demand can survive against an orphaned, quarantined, expired, or revoked workspace.
- **Core workspace re-admission is a two-write sequence** (`controller.acpWorkspacePoolReadinessFailure`): Core re-admits a workspace in two writes: the Admitted condition names the next generation before the coreAdmission patch lands, so that intermediate state counts as recovery pending rather than failure.
- **Prompt dispatch waits for the provider to observe the workspace generation** (`controller.acpWorkspacePoolReadinessFailure`): Publishing workload and admission each advance the workspace generation, and the provider acknowledges them asynchronously, so prompt dispatch waits until provider status observes the current generation.
- **Withdrawn enforced epoch pauses prompts but keeps the workspace** (`controller.acpWorkspacePoolReadinessFailure`): The workspace adapter deliberately withdraws the enforced epoch during a recoverable outage; the controller keeps the exact data lineage but queues no prompt until the adapter restores the attachment handshake.
- **Standalone runtime cleanup authority never comes from mutable Task status** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.standaloneRuntimeCleanupFence`): standaloneRuntimeCleanupFence grants exact session deletion, preceded when needed by evidence-bound publication finalization, under an older runtime epoch only when the terminal attempt, immutable projection, and frozen execution binding agree; every mutation revalidates them under the current owner.
- **Same-epoch cleanup without a terminal projection cannot address older epochs** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.standaloneRuntimeCleanupFence`): Ordinary same-epoch runtime cleanup does not need a terminal projection, but without one standaloneRuntimeCleanupFence returns nil and cleanup cannot address a previous runtime epoch.
- **Runtime cleanup validates snapshot integrity but not current readiness** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.validateStandaloneRuntimeCleanupProjection`): Standalone external runtime cleanup reuses snapshot integrity validation without trusting the current endpoint or admission readiness; the cleanup client authenticates the frozen supervisor boot instead.
- **Cleanup uses the frozen source Task UID, not the live UID** (`controller.standaloneRuntimeCleanupBinding`): Standalone runtime cleanup keys on the frozen source taskUID because a restored incarnation's live UID may differ; the live object is bound separately through validateTask.
- **ACP spans identify Agents by UID** (`controller.acpTaskSpanAttributes`): ACP span attributes use the Agent UID, not its reusable name, as the agent ID so spans join correctly with identity records and issuer logs.
- **Toolbox image volumes require Kubernetes 1.36** (`controller.ACPToolboxMountMethod`): ACPToolboxImageVolumeMinimumServerVersion is 1.36 because that is the first Kubernetes minor where image volumes with subPath are generally available; the copy method works on every supported version.
- **One toolbox policy is shared by every gate** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy`): ACPToolboxPolicy is shared by Agent admission, Task planning, and RuntimePool materialization so every gate rejects the same inputs with the same message.
- **Agent Skills reuse and require toolbox delivery** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy`): ACPToolboxPolicy.SkillsEnabled admits runtime.skills only when Enabled is also set, because skills reuse toolbox image delivery, the registry allowlist, pull secrets, node selector, and mount method.
- **Empty toolbox registry allowlist admits no image** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy`): ACPToolboxPolicy.AllowedRegistries lists registry hosts or host/path prefixes; an empty list allows no image.
- **Toolbox image pull secrets are never mounted** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy`): ACPToolboxPolicy.ImagePullSecrets are added only to the runtime Pod's imagePullSecrets and are never mounted into a container.
- **Toolbox registry allowlist matches exact hosts and whole path segments** (`controller.ToolboxImageAllowed`): ToolboxImageAllowed requires an exact registry host (with optional port) and whole repository path segments, so registry.example.com.evil.io never matches registry.example.com and registry.example.com/tools-evil never matches registry.example.com/tools. It makes no network calls.
- **Toolbox admission checks are repeated at planning time** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy.agentToolboxPlanRejection`): agentToolboxPlanRejection repeats the toolbox admission checks during planning so a Task never reaches a RuntimePool with toolboxes it cannot bind, even if the Agent was admitted under a different policy.
- **Toolbox prompt note is capped at 8192 bytes** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy.agentToolboxPlanRejection`): acpToolboxPromptNoteMaxBytes bounds the Orka-generated toolbox note at 8192 bytes; four toolboxes with eight 256-byte path entries each fit well inside it.
- **Toolbox prompt note never claims a mounted-only folder is on PATH** (`controller.ACPToolboxPolicy.agentToolboxPlanRejection`): acpToolboxPromptNote picks its PATH sentence from the listed folders and marks toolboxes without pathEntries as mounted, not on PATH, so the note never claims a mounted-only folder is on PATH.
- **Toolbox prompt note never lists file names from untrusted images** (`controller.acpToolboxPromptNote`): acpToolboxPromptNote lists folders from the frozen profile only and never file names from inside the untrusted toolbox image.
- **ToolboxUnavailable failure finalizes the recovered SessionTurn first** (`controller.TestACPDispatcherToolboxUnavailableFinalizesRecoveredSessionTurn`): A Reserved Session Task whose interrupted preparation still holds an open SessionTurn is failed with ToolboxUnavailable only after that turn is recovered and finalized, so the next Session Task is not blocked on the mutation lease.
- **RunACPTraceBoundary is test-only glue for the API trace test** (`controller.RunACPTraceBoundary`): RunACPTraceBoundary exists in controller test code for the external test package because the API package imports controller, so task creation cannot be imported into controller's own tests; it runs the production reserve/execute sequence and real supervisor.
- **ACP admission gate is rechecked inside the reservation CAS** (`controller.ACPAdmissionGate`): ACPAdmissionGate is the process-local first barrier for new ACP demand; a planned drain closes it before any RuntimePool mutation, and callers must also check it inside the final RuntimePool reservation CAS so an admission that raced an earlier queue scan cannot cross the shutdown boundary.
- **Upgrade drain trigger listener is loopback-only** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainOptions`): ACPUpgradeDrainOptions is shared by the controller and the same-binary preStop trigger mode; the trigger listener binds only to loopback because the preStop child shares the Pod network namespace.
