- **Upgrade drain outbox barrier counts records awaiting future retry** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainOutboxBarrier`): ACPUpgradeDrainOutboxBarrier must count every Pending or Delivering outbox record, including those whose retry availability is in the future.
- **Upgrade drain barrier observation is read-only** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainBarrierObserver`): ACPUpgradeDrainBarrierObserver implementations read the publication, external-effect, and transactional-outbox barriers without mutating or claiming them.
- **Drain observer samples the outbox around Kubernetes barrier reads** (`controller.KubernetesACPUpgradeDrainBarrierObserver`): KubernetesACPUpgradeDrainBarrierObserver treats Kubernetes PromptAttempt, RuntimeSessionControl, Publication, and ExternalEffect records as authoritative and samples the SQLite-backed outbox before and after reading them, so a Delivering record cannot disappear into newly projected work.
- **Empty boot-preparation reservations do not block upgrade drain** (`controller.KubernetesACPUpgradeDrainBarrierObserver.ObserveACPUpgradeDrainBarriers`): Boot-preparation ExternalEffects only record a local observation; an interrupted controller can leave the reservation empty for the writer to resume, so the drain observer never counts it as in-flight external work.
- **Queued Tasks do not block upgrade drain completion** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainSnapshot.Quiescent`): Durable Queued plus NotRequested PromptAttempts are safe to resume under the next controller epoch, so ACPUpgradeDrainSnapshot completion ignores QueuedTasks (diagnostic only) and is fenced by the authoritative PromptAttempt, SessionControl, Publication, ExternalEffect, and outbox barriers rather than a possibly stale pool summary.
- **Upgrade drain status is safe to expose** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainStatus`): ACPUpgradeDrainStatus is exposed through readiness and diagnostics, so it must carry only non-secret phase, epoch, timing, and barrier counts.
- **Only a Completed drain marker for the previous epoch means planned takeover** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainMarker`): ACPUpgradeDrainMarker is the next leader's durable proof: only a Completed marker for the exact previous epoch permits planned-takeover recovery, and missing, Intent, TimedOut, malformed, or stale markers are deliberately treated as unplanned takeover.
- **Upgrade drain coordinator holds leadership until barriers settle** (`controller.ACPUpgradeDrainCoordinator`): ACPUpgradeDrainCoordinator runs only on the elected leader, exposes a loopback trigger for the preStop child, and retains leadership while RuntimePools and durable finalization barriers settle.
- **Drain trigger mode exits before opening the store** (`controller.RunACPUpgradeDrainTriggerMode`): RunACPUpgradeDrainTriggerMode returns handled=true whenever TriggerURL is set so the preStop child process exits instead of opening the store or starting a second controller manager.
- **Upgrade drain preStop runs the manager binary in trigger mode** (`controller.RequestACPUpgradeDrain`): RequestACPUpgradeDrain lets the distroless image's preStop hook run a second /manager process in trigger-only mode, so no shell, curl, wget, Service, or externally reachable drain endpoint is needed.
- **Abandoned validation recovery always uses the epoch-guarded cleanup transport** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverAbandonedWorkspaceValidation`): recoverAbandonedWorkspaceValidation always builds the exact, epoch-guarded cleanup transport, even when runtime and owner epochs match; the ordinary admission client must never carry these recovery mutations.
- **Abandoned validation recovery never upgrades outcomes or publishes** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverAbandonedWorkspaceValidation`): finishAbandonedWorkspaceValidation finishes the validation barrier when a native prompt completed after its controller lost the result. It never upgrades the Task's durable terminal outcome, fetches or clones a repository, publishes a delta, or invents runtime retirement evidence; destructive cleanup still needs the normal exact-session DELETE and its authenticated receipt.
- **Only unexported local work is abandoned during validation recovery** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.finishAbandonedWorkspaceValidation`): Abandoned validation recovery handles only local, unexported work: a published or in-flight publication cannot reach it because its durable delivery state is not NotRequested and its runtime is no longer Validating.
- **Validation recovery reconstructs only the committed workspace baseline** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoveredValidationWorkspace`): recoveredValidationWorkspace rebuilds only a previously committed workspace baseline whose digest must equal the admitted runtime binding; a deleted branch, changed Agent, or expired Git credential is never consulted.
- **Task-scoped Tasks without a frozen workspace digest may still recover** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoveredValidationWorkspace`): recoveredValidationWorkspace accepts a Task-scoped Task with no recorded RuntimeSessionWorkspaceDigest, because earlier controllers never recorded it and recovery would otherwise retry forever. This is safe: the baseline still comes only from committed evidence for that exact Task and prompt, the runtime rejects any other VerifiedBaseline, and recovery never publishes.
- **Workspace preparation digest is verified by re-marshaling the closed schema** (`controller.readCommittedWorkspacePreparation`): The producer commits json.Marshal(WorkspacePrepareResponse), but Kubernetes stores the field as JSON and may reorder keys, so recovery decodes into that exact closed schema and re-marshals before checking ResponseDigest instead of dropping digest verification or accepting arbitrary normalization.
- **A read Session without publication is not proven retired** (`controller.TestUnknownReadSessionDoesNotSkipRuntimeSettlement`): A continued read Session may have completed natively just as the controller lost its result, so reconcileRecoveredTaskScopedRuntimeSession never treats it as settled or retired merely because it did not request publication; it needs the exact runtime authority read.
- **Retained cleanup authority applies only to native runtimes** (`controller.TestCurrentEpochAbandonedValidationKeepsWorkspacePoolsOffNativeCleanupAuthority`): The retained runtime cleanup client used by reconcileRecoveredTaskScopedRuntimeSession is native-only; a same-epoch workspace pool keeps its ordinary current-epoch path instead of failing on missing retained native authority.
- **RuntimePool prompt 429 keeps the Task Pending in Submitting** (`controller.desiredWaitingForCapacityCondition`): A built-in RuntimePool's prompt 429 is reconciled in place while the execution is still Submitting: the runtime proved it did not accept the prompt, so desiredWaitingForCapacityCondition reports WaitingForCapacity and the Task stays Pending until a prompt is accepted.
- **Prior repository identity authorizes a durable-resume checkpoint wipe** (`controller.preparedACPRuntimeWorkspace`): priorRepositoryIdentity records the canonical repository identity a session ran on before a verified publication transition moved its continuation to a new repository (for example a PR fork). Under an expected durable resume it authorizes the supervisor to wipe a checkpoint bound to exactly that identity and re-materialize from the new baseline.
- **Resumed workspaces require a committed checkpoint at or above the floor** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskExpectsDurableResume`): taskExpectsDurableResume reports when the linked execution workspace carries a resumed lineage: every session must then find a committed durable checkpoint and runtime creation fails if preserved data is missing rather than materializing fresh. The returned floor is the newest committed checkpoint generation recorded, and an older same-identity checkpoint is a stale provider restore.
- **Resume lineage read failures abort dispatch** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskExpectsDurableResume`): taskExpectsDurableResume fails closed: a read failure aborts and retries the dispatch, because silently omitting the resume expectation would let a lost checkpoint be replaced by a fresh materialization.
- **Resume asserts a checkpoint only after a RuntimeSession was created** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskExpectsDurableResume`): The resumed lineage asserts a committed checkpoint only when a RuntimeSession was actually created on the workspace before suspension; a first Task cancelled before session creation validly suspends an empty volume, and its continuation must materialize fresh instead of failing closed forever.
- **Checkpoint restores always expect committed data** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.taskExpectsDurableResume`): A fresh restoreFrom has no destination lineage yet but taskExpectsDurableResume still requires committed data, so it cannot report success from an empty newly materialized directory instead of the requested checkpoint.
- **Legacy durable-session records assert a checkpoint without a floor** (`controller.workspaceDurableSessionGeneration`): workspaceDurableSessionGeneration treats an annotation value of "true", stamped before generations were tracked, as asserting the checkpoint exists with no generation floor.
- **Corrupt checkpoint generation records fail closed** (`controller.workspaceDurableSessionGeneration`): workspaceDurableSessionGeneration returns an error for a corrupt controller-owned generation record so it cannot disable the stale-snapshot fence, and keeps the raw annotation out of the error because metadata can be modified outside the controller.
- **Durable checkpoint generation record only advances** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.markLinkedWorkspaceDurableSessionCommitted`): markLinkedWorkspaceDurableSessionCommitted records the newest committed checkpoint generation and never lowers it, so a stale provider restore presenting an older generation is rejected by the resume assertion.
- **Verified publication transitions capture the prior repository identity** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.prepareRuntimeWorkspace`): When a verified publication transition moves a session to a new repository, the prior canonical repository identity is captured to authorize the durable-resume checkpoint transition.
- **Repo-less workspace baseline is scoped to the Session** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.prepareRuntimeWorkspace`): The repo-less protocol baseline is keyed by the Session UID, not the Task UID, because the supervisor compares each delta's VerifiedBaseline against the session's creation baseline; a task-scoped identity would fail every repo-less continuation with a digest conflict.
- **Repo-less binding digest includes the Session-scoped baseline** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.prepareRuntimeWorkspace`): The repo-less binding digest keeps the stable Session-scoped baseline. Older controllers erased the task-scoped identity there, so making the Session identity explicit forces those live sessions through one generation rotation instead of reusing a supervisor with a different baseline.
- **Workspace artifact download operation IDs are unique per session creation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.prepareRuntimeWorkspace`): The workspace artifact download operation ID is bound to the execution attempt and session generation, because a RuntimeSession recreated after a completed or ambiguously failed first download needs a fresh ledger operation or it hits ErrReplay and never materializes its workspace.
- **Empty workspace branch asks the Publisher to freeze the default branch** (`controller.runtimeWorkspaceSourceRef`): An empty workspace branch is an explicit request for the Publisher to resolve and freeze the repository's advertised HEAD or default branch before execution, so it is passed through as empty rather than defaulted.
- **Workspace preflight mirrors the Publisher's IP-literal rejection** (`controller.canonicalWorkspaceRepositoryURL`): Workspace preflight rejects loopback, unspecified, and link-local IP-literal repository hosts, mirroring the Publisher's validateRepository rule, so a Task the Publisher would always reject fails preflight instead of settling as WorkspaceUnsupported after creation.
- **Default workspace slot name matches the API default** (`controller`): defaultWorkspaceSlotName is "default" to mirror the documented API default for Task.spec.execution.workspace.workspaceSlot; changing one without the other changes pool identity for unset slots.
- **Runtime workspace binding carries no provider-native IDs or secrets** (`controller.ACPRuntimeWorkspaceBinding`): ACPRuntimeWorkspaceBinding is the canonical execution-workspace binding for one RuntimePool. It carries no provider-native identifiers and no secrets, is frozen into the immutable execution snapshot, and is recomputed exactly during snapshot verification.
- **Workspace template references are set only for Substrate** (`controller.ACPRuntimeWorkspaceBinding`): ACPRuntimeWorkspaceBinding.TemplateNamespace and TemplateName reference the operator-owned Substrate ActorTemplate and are set exactly when Provider is substrate; agent-sandbox workloads run only controller-rendered templates.
- **Workspace request validation uses a never-persisted Session placeholder** (`controller.validateACPWorkspaceBindingRequestWithClass`): validateACPWorkspaceBindingRequestWithClass validates a class request with a placeholder Session identity that is never persisted or used for pool identity.
- **Workspace Session UID is proposed before and established after validation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.planACPWorkspaceSessionUID`): planACPWorkspaceSessionUID resolves an existing immutable Session identity or proposes a fresh one without creating transcript or SessionControl state; the proposal is established only after the complete execution candidate passes validation and canonical encoding.
- **Concurrent workspace Session creators converge on the first durable UID** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPWorkspaceSessionUID`): ensureACPWorkspaceSessionUID establishes the proposed Session identity after candidate resolution; concurrent creators converge on the first durable SessionControl UID and the caller rebuilds the still-unbound candidate when that winner differs from its proposal.
- **Workspace binding digest encoding must not change** (`controller.acpWorkspaceBindingDigest`): acpWorkspaceBindingDigest keeps its encoded fields unchanged so stored class bindings retain their digest.
- **Schema-v1 workspace binding digests are accepted only for snapshots** (`controller.legacyACPWorkspaceBindingDigest`): legacyACPWorkspaceBindingDigest reproduces schema-v1 class binding digests written before the effective detach action became attachment-scoped; validateSnapshotACPWorkspaceBindingValues accepts it only while re-verifying immutable snapshots, and new bindings never use it.
- **Effective detach action is not part of the pool binding digest** (`controller.acpWorkspaceBindingDigestWithClassOnDetach`): The effective detach action is attachment-scoped (a session suspended by one Task can be continued by a Task selecting Delete), so it is excluded from the immutable physical-pool binding digest; including it would fail pool validation for every continuation choosing a different allowed action.
- **Workspace bindings fold into RuntimePool identity** (`controller.applyACPWorkspaceBindingToPlan`): applyACPWorkspaceBindingToPlan folds a resolved workspace binding into the RuntimePool identity so workspace-backed sessions never share a plain pool.
- **Session workspace pool name depends only on Session UID and slot** (`controller.applyACPWorkspaceBindingToPlan`): A Session-reuse workspace pool's identity uses only the immutable Session UID and workspace slot, so its name stays stable across mutable runtime and workspace inputs and an incompatible continuation reaches the existing pool and fails closed instead of silently materializing a fresh filesystem.
- **Task workspace projection follows execution state and never overrides Failed** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPExecutionWorkspaceStatus`): projectACPExecutionWorkspaceStatus moves the provider-neutral Task workspace projection Pending to Ready once the prompt runs in the workspace-backed pool and Pending/Ready to Released once the attempt settles; it never writes provider-native identifiers or overrides a Failed projection.
- **Workspace projection transitions retry on identity read failure** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPExecutionWorkspaceStatus`): projectACPExecutionWorkspaceStatus returns the class-attachment read error instead of persisting the advanced phase, because the rebuilt projection lacks class identity yet and the phase gate would then skip it forever, dropping ClassRef, WorkspaceRef, State, and AttachedEpoch.
- **ACP projection is the only writer of class attachment identity** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPClassAttachmentIdentity`): projectACPClassAttachmentIdentity surfaces the selected class, workspace incarnation, observed state, and enforced attachment epoch in Task status. Session-reused workspaces carry no Task owner reference, so the generic workspace controller cannot project these fields and the ACP projection is their only writer.
- **Task status reports only the adapter-enforced attachment epoch** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPClassAttachmentIdentity`): The requested spec epoch and the adapter-enforced status epoch diverge while attachment is pending and after max-lifetime enforcement clears it, so Task AttachedEpoch is set only from the epoch the adapter actually enforces for that Task.
- **Idle pool reaper never destroys quarantine evidence** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapIdlePools`): reapIdlePools never deletes a workspace that detach-timeout settlement deliberately quarantined, even when its linked pool idles at Stopped past the TTL, so quarantine evidence survives.
- **Settlement refreshes the Released workspace projection after revocation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.refreshACPReleasedWorkspaceProjection`): refreshACPReleasedWorkspaceProjection runs on settlement completion because the terminal transition happens before revocation; without the refresh Task status would permanently claim state Attached and the pre-revocation epoch.
- **Released projection ignores cached state of deleting workspaces** (`controller.TaskReconciler.refreshACPReleasedWorkspaceProjection`): refreshACPReleasedWorkspaceProjection does not copy state from a workspace held only by its cleanup finalizer or already deleted, because the informer cache can still serve pre-delete status that would be frozen into the released Task.
- **Idle reaper reclaims pools of failed suspensions** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.reapIdlePools`): A failed suspension preserves no checkpoint, so reapIdlePools may reclaim its stopped pool, while a genuinely suspended or suspending workspace stays deliberately retained.
- **Class-selected ACP sessions require the reserved adapter controllerName** (`controller.acpWorkspaceSuspendDataOnly`): Only ExecutionWorkspaceProvider objects whose controllerName is acpWorkspaceProviderControllerName (acp.workspace.orka.ai/runtime-pool) may back class-selected ACP RuntimeSessions through the in-tree RuntimePool adapter.
- **Class deletion policy is honored per retained-data category** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassDeletionPolicy`): ACPWorkspaceClassDeletionPolicy freezes separate deletion dispositions for provider resources, persistent volumes, and checkpoints, and finalization honors each independently.
- **Durable volume StorageClass is pinned by UID** (`controller.ACPSandboxDurableVolume`): ACPSandboxDurableVolume.StorageClassUID pins the StorageClass whose Delete reclaim semantics were validated at class resolution; provisioning reverifies it so a same-name replacement class cannot silently retain volumes.
- **Frozen class bindings reject live drift instead of rebinding** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): Every ACPWorkspaceClassBinding field is immutable snapshot input: live class or provider drift is rejected against these exact values instead of silently rebinding.
- **External class bindings pin lifecycle contract and provider parameters** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): External ACPWorkspaceClassBinding values pin the installed lifecycle contract version and both opaque provider-owned parameter objects; an empty ControllerName identifies only the legacy in-tree snapshot format retained for migration.
- **RuntimeProviderConfig is pinned by UID** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.ProviderConfigUID pins the exact cluster-scoped RuntimeProviderConfig that selected the physical backend, so recreating the immutable config under the same name reads as drift, never as a silent backend swap.
- **Effective detach action rules** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.EffectiveOnDetach is the Task-requested detach action when the class allows it, otherwise the class default. Delete is always executable; Suspend only for session-reused workspaces whose backend profile permits DataOnly suspension.
- **Workspace suspension mode is DataOnly or none** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.SuspendMode freezes the operator-permitted suspension scope from the class profile: empty means suspension is not permitted and DataOnly is the only supported value. See [ADR 0024](0024-acp-execution-workspace-runtime-pools.md).
- **Uncapped suspended workspaces need an age bound** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.MaxSuspendedWorkspaces freezes the class retention cap; when nil, per-workspace age must be bounded by idleTimeout or maxLifetime.
- **Class lifecycle policy is frozen in class order** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding freezes DefaultOnDetach and AllowedOnDetach in class order so the materialized ExecutionWorkspace carries the exact class lifecycle and never drifts from it.
- **Frozen class lifecycle must match the live class exactly** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.Lifecycle`): ACPWorkspaceClassBinding.Lifecycle rebuilds the frozen class lifecycle, which must match the live class spec byte for byte or the workspace core controller rejects the materialized workspace as policy drift.
- **Only the class Binding enters the execution snapshot** (`controller.acpResolvedWorkspaceClass`): acpResolvedWorkspaceClass carries live-resolved class data for the pure binding resolver; only Binding is frozen, and the other fields validate the Task request against class policy without entering the snapshot.
- **Established sessions may continue through stricter class readiness** (`controller.mayResolveFrozenACPContinuation`): mayResolveFrozenACPContinuation lets an established session continue through a current-generation class condition stricter than its Task binding, such as a frozen pool volume after its StorageClass is retired or a Delete-bound continuation after the provider withdraws Suspend. All provider, profile-hash, and frozen-binding fences still run.
- **Delete-bound running continuations may omit the Suspend feature** (`controller.acpWorkspaceResolutionRequiredFeatures`): acpWorkspaceResolutionRequiredFeatures lets a Delete-bound continuation omit the class's implied Suspend feature only when its existing workspace is already running and needs no provider resume operation.
- **Continuation evidence requires the existing workspace and UID-linked pool** (`controller.TaskReconciler.frozenACPContinuationExists`): frozenACPContinuationExists proves the planned Session UID already owns the deterministic class workspace and its UID-linked RuntimePool, and reports separately whether it is running since a suspended or settling workspace still needs Suspend to resume. A planned Session UID alone is not evidence, because every new session-reused Task resolves one before class readiness is checked.
- **Workspace class resolution verifies identity, not caller authority** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveACPWorkspaceClass`): resolveACPWorkspaceClass pins classRef against the live class, provider, and adapter parameter objects and fails closed before any workspace or pool demand. Class use authorization is enforced at admission by the workspace-class-use webhook and policy; the resolver re-verifies object identity and policy only.
- **Transient workspace planning failures requeue the Task** (`controller.errACPWorkspacePlanningTransient`): errACPWorkspacePlanningTransient marks workspace planning failures caused by transient reads such as uncached quota lists or durable session-store lookups, so the Task requeues instead of being permanently rejected during a brief API-server or control-store outage.
- **Suspend quota is checked at admission and rechecked at settlement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota`): enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota rejects a Task whose prospective Suspend would exceed the class retention cap, and settlement re-checks the live count so racing admissions still cannot exceed the cap.
- **Suspend quota excludes the Task's own workspace by Session UID** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota`): enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota excludes the continuation's own suspended session workspace from the count, or the Task resuming it could never reach ensureACPClassWorkspace. The exclusion matches the immutable Session UID, not the reusable name, so a recreated same-name Session still counts the old incarnation's workspace; Session reuse admits only the default slot, so the UID identifies the only resumable workspace.
- **Suspend quota counts the own workspace without session stores** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota`): Without durable session stores (validation-only resolution) enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota skips the own-workspace exclusion, which is stricter, never looser.
- **Permanent Session planning errors are not requeued** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota`): In enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota, a nonexistent create:false Session or failed stored-Session validation is returned as permanent, matching the binding stage, because marking it transient would requeue the Task forever instead of surfacing the failure.
- **Session store outages during quota checks stay retryable** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota`): A session-store read outage in enforceACPWorkspaceSuspendQuota is wrapped as errACPWorkspacePlanningTransient because the primary binding resolution re-runs the lookup with full classification; here it only shapes the quota exclusion.
- **Ready session workspaces may continue while the suspend cap is full** (`controller.TaskReconciler.readySessionWorkspaceAwaitingSuspendQuota`): readySessionWorkspaceAwaitingSuspendQuota admits a continuation that reuses its exact Ready session workspace while the class cap is full, because that workspace already consumed the session's only materialization and admitting the continuation creates no additional retained workspace.
- **Provider profile identity encoding must not change** (`controller.workspaceProviderProfileIdentity`): workspaceProviderProfileIdentity's JSON encoding is part of profile hashes frozen into existing class bindings and execution snapshots, so its fields, order, and tags must not change.
- **Retention bounds gate only new class resolution** (`controller.validateACPWorkspaceClassLifecycleValues`): validateACPWorkspaceClassBindingValues does not require retention bounds on frozen snapshots: they gate new class resolution only, so an upgrade cannot wedge a Task whose immutable binding predates that requirement.
- **Only all-Delete class deletion policies are executable** (`controller.validateACPWorkspaceClassLifecycleValues`): validateACPWorkspaceClassBindingValues accepts only Delete deletion-policy actions: class admission rejects retained policies and settlement destroys workspace and pool, so a snapshot frozen by a newer controller with Retain semantics fails closed after a rollback instead of starting destructive cleanup under an unsupported retention contract.
- **Restore requests add the provider restore requirement** (`controller.acpWorkspaceResolutionRequiredFeatures`): DataOnly suspension does not itself require the public restore API, so acpWorkspaceResolutionRequiredFeatures adds the Restore and Checkpoint requirements from the Task's restoreFrom even when the class omits them.
- **Workspace class resolution rechecks live provider usage policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveACPWorkspaceClass`): resolveACPWorkspaceClass re-checks the provider's live namespace usage-policy selector because the class's cached Ready condition lags policy edits and the profile hash excludes usagePolicy; a live edit must fail resolution closed immediately.
- **Planning does not re-resolve classes for frozen bindings** (`controller.TaskReconciler.rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan`): Once the execution binding is frozen, rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan does not reapply new-allocation readiness: core deliberately admits Draining providers for already-admitted workspaces, and re-resolving the class would fail the bound Task and destroy its valid workspace.
- **Bound-Task configuration gates use the verified frozen plan** (`controller.TaskReconciler.frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason`): frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason enforces configuration gates for bound Tasks against the verified frozen plan at the queue chokepoint, never against the public status projection, which can still be nil after a restart between the binding patch and the first queue operation.
- **Losing a frozen class, provider, or profile fails the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): Deleting or replacing the frozen class, provider, or RuntimeWorkspaceProfile after the snapshot froze is irreversible for that incarnation, so ensureACPClassWorkspace fails the Task on ClassNotFound, ClassProfileMismatch, ClassPolicyMismatch, or provider loss instead of requeueing forever.
- **Session workspace link is persisted before creation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace makes the Task's settlement link label durable before creating the session workspace, even if Create then fails, because a crash between Create and a post-create link patch would orphan the deterministic session workspace forever.
- **Continuations never attach while a revocation stamp stands** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace never attaches a continuation while a revocation stamp stands, even for Suspend: the suspend settlement retires the stamp in the same optimistic patch that sets DesiredState=Suspended, and attaching earlier would reuse the workspace warm and let the old settlement treat the foreign attachment as completion, skipping the checkpoint.
- **Revised Session snapshots queue behind the attached predecessor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A revised class or provider snapshot keeps the same session workspace name while class and Session UIDs are stable; ensureACPClassWorkspace makes the successor wait for the attached predecessor's Delete settlement and rejects the stale workspace once no predecessor owns it.
- **Workspace adoption rejects provider identity or materialization drift** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace rejects, instead of reusing, a workspace whose recorded provider identity or controller-owned RuntimePool markers no longer match the frozen binding.
- **Settlement skips workspaces the Task does not own** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace revalidates the mutable link label and skips, never revokes or deletes, a workspace the Task neither owns nor shares a Session with, or one lacking the ACP controller label.
- **Workspace revocation fences the original workspace UID** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Settlement can race deterministic-name replacement after its uncached read, so both the revocation stamp patch and BeginRevocation fence the original workspace UID and an old Task cannot mark or detach the replacement incarnation.
- **Detach timeout quarantines the workspace and releases the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): When the adapter never releases the revoked epoch, settleACPClassWorkspace quarantines the workspace fail-closed once the frozen detachTimeout elapses and reports done so the Task finalizer releases.
- **Revocation stamps from a previous epoch are replaced** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): A revocation stamp bound to a previous attachment epoch never serves as the current revocation deadline; settleACPClassWorkspace replaces it with a fresh stamp instead of quarantining.
- **Settlement waits for the adapter to release the epoch** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace does not finalize or delete a workspace while the adapter still reports the attachment epoch as enforced.
- **Durable volumes require a Delete-reclaim StorageClass** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveACPWorkspaceClass`): Class resolution rejects a durable-volume profile bound to a StorageClass that retains volumes, because finalization would report the volume deleted while Kubernetes leaves the PV and repository data behind.
- **Class resolution requires the protected provider config status pin** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveACPWorkspaceClass`): The provider config metadata annotation is only a mirror; resolveACPWorkspaceClass waits until the adapter establishes the controller-owned status pin before authorizing a new workspace against the provider.
- **RuntimePool creation repeats the protected config pin check** (`controller.TaskReconciler.verifyFrozenACPProfile`): RuntimePool creation for a workspace repeats the resolver's protected provider config pin check, because the config object and its hash can stay unchanged while the provider reports it now operates against a different configuration.
- **Continuations queue behind a Failed workspace still held by a predecessor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace keeps a continuation queued when a terminally Failed workspace is still attached to its predecessor, because the predecessor's settlement removes that incarnation and the deterministic name is recreated fresh; a permanent WorkspaceFailed rejection would be wrong.
- **Attached-ready fast path rechecks the frozen maxLifetime** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace's attached-ready fast path rechecks the frozen maxLifetime so a deadline that elapsed between Attach and the adapter's Failed publication cannot admit RuntimePool demand from a stale Ready observation.
- **Expired attachments rotate before pool demand is recreated** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): When an attachment expires while a Task waits for its RuntimePool, ensureACPClassWorkspace rotates the epoch and bearer before recreating pool demand and deletes the superseded bearer only after the new epoch is enforced.
- **Attachment rotation rebinds the Task's frozen detach action** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): Credential rotation writes this Task's frozen detach action atomically with the replacement attachment, overwriting any stale detach-action metadata left by a predecessor.
- **Running ACP Tasks rotate expired workspace attachments in handleRunning** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleRunning`): Running ACP Tasks bypass the queue path, so handleRunning revisits their workspace attachment expiry; rotation keeps the same Task attached and the public projection advances only after the adapter acknowledges the new epoch.
- **Session Task workspaces are released before final reclamation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.prepareACPClassWorkspaceDeletion`): prepareACPClassWorkspaceDeletion releases a settled Session Task's workspace before final Task reclamation; DataOnly suspension retires its runtime and records the receipt Session archival needs, so waiting for archival first would leave the cancelled Task's Actor attached indefinitely.
- **Artifact capabilities retire before workspace detach** (`controller.TaskReconciler.prepareACPClassWorkspaceDeletion`): prepareACPClassWorkspaceDeletion retires artifact capabilities before detach can destroy their serving runtime, and keeps the Task finalizer plus all durable attempt and Session records until normal deletion observes runtime retirement and archival receipts.
- **External workspace parameters are read as unstructured objects** (`controller.resolveExternalWorkspaceParameters`): resolveExternalWorkspaceParameters keeps provider APIs out of Orka's scheme: the provider's installed schema and adapter validate native inputs, and core freezes the functional spec and exact object revision before admitting a workload.
- **Restore checkpoint authorization happens at Task admission** (`controller.TaskReconciler.validateACPWorkspaceRestoreCheckpoint`): Checkpoint use is authorized against the live caller by Task admission; validateACPWorkspaceRestoreCheckpoint only rechecks the public artifact identity for a fresh destination and need not read the source workspace, which may already be deleted.
- **Finalization uses the protected Task attachment epoch stamp** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): During workspace finalization the status projection can lag, so the protected Task attachment-epoch annotation supplies the actual epoch for the second finalization pass.
- **Workspace link label never carries provider-native identifiers** (`controller`): acpExecutionWorkspaceLinkLabel links a RuntimePool or Task to its controller-first ExecutionWorkspace by Orka-owned name and never carries a provider-native identifier.
- **Workspace detach action annotation records the attached Task's frozen choice** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceDetachActionAnnotation records the frozen effective detach action of the currently attached Task so settlement applies exactly that validated choice without reloading the execution snapshot.
- **Workspace settlement is marked on each Task, not the shared workspace** (`controller`): acpTaskWorkspaceSettledAnnotation marks each Task whose detach action has been applied. Terminal Tasks reconcile indefinitely and settlement must be once per Task; a per-Task marker (rather than one latest-settler annotation on the shared workspace) keeps every settled Task recognized, so an older terminal Task can never re-suspend a workspace a continuation asked to resume.
- **Task attachment epoch annotation lets later receipts supersede** (`controller`): acpTaskAttachmentEpochAnnotation records the workspace attachment epoch a Task held, so settlement can recognize that a later epoch's receipt supersedes this Task's displaced receipt.
- **Workspace-side settlement receipt is written with the suspension** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceLastSettledTaskAnnotation is written in the same patch as a suspension to cover the crash window before the Task-side marker lands; only the latest settler needs it because earlier settlers already carry Task markers, so a single overwritten value suffices.
- **Settlement acts only on the exact attached workspace incarnation** (`controller`): acpExecutionWorkspaceUIDAnnotation pins the ExecutionWorkspace incarnation a Task attached alongside the name link label; settlement acts only on that UID, because a Session recreated under the same name produces a new workspace UID that an old Task must never revoke or delete.
- **Workspace backend is recorded at materialization** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceBackendAnnotation records the resolved physical backend so a recreated same-name provider config can never silently re-serve an existing workspace through a different backend.
- **Workspace durability is recorded at materialization** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceDurableAnnotation records at materialization that the frozen class profile provisions durable artifacts (a data-only suspension checkpoint or durable PVC), so terminal dispositions report what actually existed instead of inferring it from allowed detach actions.
- **Substrate suspend mode is recorded on the workspace** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceSuspendModeAnnotation records the profile-frozen Substrate suspend mode because the terminal adapter still needs it after the linked RuntimePool and its rendered DurableDir policy are deleted.
- **Pool loss after cold resume is terminal data loss** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceResumedLineageAnnotation marks a workspace cold-resumed from a preserved data checkpoint; its linked pool then holds the only copy of session data, so a missing or foreign pool is terminal data loss even after the adapter projected Ready or Attached.
- **Failures proving no durable data free the suspension quota slot** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceDurableDataAbsentAnnotation records on a terminally failed workspace that the failure proved no durable data exists (missing, foreign, or non-suspend-capable pool, or settlement without a consensual checkpoint); retention frees its suspension-quota slot, while resume-loss failures that preserve a claim stay charged until deletion proves cleanup.
- **Pending cold resume counts as live demand for idle retention** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceResumeRequestedAnnotation marks a continuation that requested cold resume and has not attached; idle retention treats it as live demand because a cold boot can outlast idleTimeout, the next attachment clears it, and frozen maxLifetime remains the hard bound if the requester dies.
- **ACP workspace attachments last at most 24 hours** (`controller`): acpWorkspaceAttachmentTTL bounds one ACP Task attachment to 24 hours, clamped by class maxLifetime; the attachment Secret is not the RuntimePool data-plane credential, and the epoch enforces the one-writer rule.
- **Terminally failed workspaces fail the waiting Task** (`controller.errACPWorkspaceTerminalFailure`): errACPWorkspaceTerminalFailure marks a workspace whose adapter reported a terminal Failed state, such as an enforced maximum-lifetime expiry, so the waiting Task fails instead of requeueing forever.
- **Replacement workspaces wait for predecessor deletion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): When the existing workspace's desired state is Deleted, ensureACPClassWorkspace waits; the replacement is created only after the predecessor object deletion completes.
- **Operator taint on a linked Task forfeits workspace reuse** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): The workspace operator-access marker alone is not authoritative: ensureACPClassWorkspace also treats an incarnation as tainted when a Task linked to it was tainted by operator access, and such a workspace forfeits reuse.
- **Operator-tainted workspaces are never reattached or resumed** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A workspace incarnation tainted by operator access is never re-attached, cold-resumed, or reused warm. While its holder settles (which always deletes a tainted workspace) a continuation queues for the fresh replacement; a detached tainted incarnation not being settled fails the Task closed.
- **Task settlement link is persisted before core admission** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace persists the settlement link as soon as the deterministic workspace exists, so a Task deleted while core admission is pending can still settle and apply its frozen detach action to a session workspace that has no Task owner reference.
- **Cold resume waits until suspension has settled** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A continuation requests cold resume only once the workspace is actually Suspended: the adapter replaces the runtime with a fresh boot and credential bootstrap before attachment, and resuming while the backend still drains or checkpoints would lift the pool's suspend intent mid-flight and strand serving in Draining with no undrain transition.
- **Resume request preserves detach time and adopts the continuation's action** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): The cold-resume patch records pending demand without changing the last detach instant, so a dead requester does not earn a new retention interval. In the same patch the continuation's frozen detach action replaces the suspender's (a Delete continuation dying during cold boot settles with Delete), and the resumed-lineage marker makes later pool loss terminal.
- **Resume demand records identify the requesting Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): The resume-requested annotation carries the requesting Task's name and UID so retention can retire demand whose requester died before attaching instead of holding the workspace forever.
- **Failed suspensions without a checkpoint cannot be resumed** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): When a suspension failed closed and preserved no data checkpoint, ensureACPClassWorkspace fails the continuation rather than resuming, because resuming would fabricate a workspace the data-only contract cannot back.
- **Resume demand is recorded before the suspension settles** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A continuation records its resume demand while the suspension is still settling so that if idleTimeout expires while the checkpoint drains, retention sees outstanding demand instead of deleting the retained workspace about to be resumed.
- **Workspaces denied core admission for frozen identity fail the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): When core admission is denied because the frozen identity can never become admissible (class or provider generation moved, class or provider deleting, or the immutable profile replaced), ensureACPClassWorkspace fails the Task: holding the unadmitted reference would deadlock their finalizers and requeueing would leave the Task Pending forever.
- **Session workspaces queue behind the current attachment holder** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): When another Task holds the one-writer attachment, ensureACPClassWorkspace queues a session-reused continuation behind it; deterministic per-Task names make this unreachable for reusePolicy none.
- **Settlement link is persisted before attachment** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace persists the settlement link before attaching, because a crash between attachment and a later link patch would leave a session workspace attached to a Task cleanup cannot associate with it, and every later Task for that Session would queue forever.
- **Task attachment epoch is recorded immediately after Attach** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): ensureACPClassWorkspace records the attachment epoch on the Task right after Attach, because a Task cancelled before the attached-ready pass would otherwise settle with a zero epoch and the displaced-receipt check could let its stale detach action reapply over newer session state; a failed mark retries before readiness advances.
- **RuntimePool demand waits for re-admission and epoch acknowledgement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): Attachment bumps the workspace generation, so ensureACPClassWorkspace returns not-ready until core re-admits it and the adapter acknowledges the enforced epoch; RuntimePool demand can never precede them.
- **Tainted workspace handoff is refused using linked Task markers** (`controller.refuseOperatorTaintedACPWorkspaceHandoff`): refuseOperatorTaintedACPWorkspaceHandoff refuses every optimistic-lock attachment attempt that would hand an incarnation tainted through any linked Task to a different Task; validateACPClassWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt checks only the workspace marker, which cannot stand in for linked Task markers.
- **External providers may acknowledge attachment before physical demand** (`controller.validateACPClassWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt`): validateACPClassWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt allows the attach handshake for a fresh materialized workspace or a terminated cold-resume predecessor because an external provider needs an acknowledged attachment before core publishes physical demand; generic selection is not relaxed.
- **Attachment attempts recheck taint on the exact object version** (`controller.validateACPClassWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt`): The attachment manager calls validateACPClassWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt on the exact object version of every optimistic-lock attempt, so a taint orka-api committed after ensureACPClassWorkspace read the workspace still refuses the handoff.
- **Failed workspaces with a verified checkpoint are retained** (`controller.failedACPWorkspaceRetainsCheckpoint`): failedACPWorkspaceRetainsCheckpoint reports whether a failed incarnation still has a verified checkpoint: Core cold-resumes only from verified retained data, so the immutable request proves one exists, and deleting the workspace would release the provider-owned checkpoint.
- **Fresh attachment clears revocation stamp and resume demand** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recordACPWorkspaceDetachAction`): recordACPWorkspaceDetachAction removes any prior revocation stamp and pending resume demand on a fresh attachment, so the next detach measures detachTimeout from its own revocation and retention resumes ordinary idle handling.
- **Attachment rotation never opens a detached ownership gap** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh`): ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh keeps one Task's attachment enforced and rotates its bearer at the hard credential expiry without a detached ownership gap, reporting ready only after core re-admission and adapter acknowledgement of the current epoch.
- **Attachments are never renewed past the workspace hard lifetime** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh`): The adapter enforces the immutable maxLifetime by tearing down the linked pool, so ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh never renews attachment authority past that deadline.
- **Expired attachment bearers are deleted immediately and again after rotation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh`): An attachment bearer past its hard expiry is already unusable, so ensureACPWorkspaceAttachmentFresh deletes it immediately; later reconciles repeat the deletion after the new epoch is acknowledged to close the patch-before-delete crash window.
- **Superseded attachment Secret deletion is repeated after rotation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deleteSupersededACPWorkspaceAttachmentSecret`): deleteSupersededACPWorkspaceAttachmentSecret repeats deletion of the immediately prior bearer after the provider enforces the replacement epoch, closing the post-rotation crash window; epochs advance one at a time and deletion is idempotent across restarts.
- **Workspace link and cleanup finalizer land in one patch** (`controller.TaskReconciler.linkTaskToACPWorkspace`): linkTaskToACPWorkspace records the Orka-owned workspace name on the Task so settlement and finalization find it without reloading the snapshot, and installs the cleanup finalizer in the same metadata patch so a Task cannot disappear during deletion after acquiring settlement ownership.
- **Per-Task workspaces use a non-controller owner reference** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A per-Task workspace gets a Task owner reference so it is garbage-collected even if settle-time deletion never runs; it is deliberately not a controller reference because the ACP state machine projects Task workspace status and a controller owner would make the generic owner projection fight it.
- **Workspaces record the creator's detach action at materialization** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): The creator's frozen effective detach action is recorded when the workspace is materialized so retention honors the class default even if the Task never attaches; the attach path refreshes it with the attached Task's choice.
- **Initial workspace materialization counts as pending demand** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): A new workspace is stamped with resume-requested demand because slow first provisioning can outlast idleTimeout and retention must not suspend or delete it under the waiting Task; the record clears on attachment or suspension, and frozen maxLifetime bounds it if the creator dies.
- **Workspace runtime namespace is frozen after resolving an empty flag** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureACPClassWorkspace`): The workspace's runtime namespace annotation is written with the namespace actually used for provider children; callers resolve an empty --acp-runtime-namespace to the workspace namespace first so that choice is frozen too, otherwise a later non-empty flag would redirect deletion proofs to a namespace the original PVC never lived in.
- **Workspace settlement requires Task provenance admission** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Without Task provenance admission, the reserved workspace-link metadata is forgeable by any direct Task writer, so settleACPClassWorkspace skips the privileged revocation and detach actions; the Task releases and the workspace is cleaned through explicit workspace deletion, since adapter finalizers do not depend on Task metadata.
- **Settlement proves workspace absence through the uncached reader** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace proves workspace absence through the uncached API reader because a just-created session workspace can be invisible to the informer cache, and a cache miss must never release the Task finalizer while an ownerless orphan occupies the deterministic session name.
- **Settled Tasks do not act on newer workspace state** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Once a Task's detach action has settled the workspace, settleACPClassWorkspace returns done: later state such as a continuation's resume request belongs to the newer attachment.
- **Live attachment outranks an old workspace settlement receipt** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): In settleACPClassWorkspace a live attachment or UID-bound pending resume demand owned by this Task is stronger evidence than an old workspace receipt, so the current Task's frozen detach action is processed rather than mistaken for a predecessor's completed settlement.
- **Workspace receipt completes the Task marker instead of reapplying** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): When the workspace-side receipt proves this Task's suspension landed or an equal-or-later settlement displaced it, settleACPClassWorkspace only completes the Task marker and never applies its stale detach action to newer session state.
- **Privileged settlement requires the controller-written incarnation pin** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace requires the controller-written workspace UID pin before any privileged action, so a forged or stripped link cannot revoke or delete a workspace the Task did not attach, and a pin from another incarnation is skipped. The one recoverable case, a crash after the pre-creation label but before the pin, is resolved by proving the Task's Session identity matches the workspace's immutable Session binding via the durable stores.
- **Displaced Tasks record settlement durably on themselves** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): When another Task has attached the session-reused workspace, settleACPClassWorkspace leaves revocation to that Task and durably marks this Task settled, so a later reconcile seeing the workspace unattached never reapplies a stale detach action to the successor's state.
- **Settlement persists the live attachment epoch before revoking** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Attach and the Task epoch annotation are separate writes, so if the annotation failed and cancellation reached settlement first, settleACPClassWorkspace records the live epoch before revocation clears the only attachment copy, keeping displaced-receipt recovery fail-closed.
- **Revocation stamp is written before the attachment clears** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace makes the pending-detach stamp durable before clearing the attachment so a continuation that sees a nil attachment also sees the pending Delete settlement and waits for the fresh workspace.
- **Settlement stays pending after initiating revocation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): After initiating revocation settleACPClassWorkspace returns not-done and requeues, because an immediate cached re-read can still show the pre-patch attachment and would report the detach action applied without running it.
- **Quarantined workspaces only get credential cleanup** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Quarantine is terminal: a settlement retry after the adapter released the epoch only completes credential cleanup idempotently and never executes any detach action on the preserved evidence.
- **Unattached owners reassert their own detach action before settling** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): A failed settlement-ownership transfer can leave a candidate's detach action on the workspace before its Task link commits, so before an unattached owner settles, settleACPClassWorkspace reasserts that Task's own effective policy and a vanished candidate cannot flip Delete and Suspend.
- **Detach action writes use UID and resourceVersion preconditions** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace applies the attached Task's frozen detach action only after revocation and adapter release leave no prompt or writer, and writes with UID and resourceVersion preconditions so a concurrent re-attachment cannot lose its workspace.
- **Operator-tainted workspaces always settle with Delete** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Operator access forfeits retention: settleACPClassWorkspace never suspends, retains for recovery export, or hands off a tainted workspace and always applies Delete. Both the workspace marker and every linked Task's marker count, and an unreadable Task fails settlement closed.
- **Failed workspaces are kept for explicit recovery export** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): For a terminally failed workspace the provider stops the attempt without capturing or replaying it; settleACPClassWorkspace keeps the Failed source for explicit recovery export and leaves its deadline to idle/maxLifetime retention.
- **Workspace settlement receipt precedes the Task marker** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Because retention may have requested the suspension with no receipt, settleACPClassWorkspace stamps this Task's receipt on the workspace before the separate Task marker patch, so a restart completes the marker instead of re-suspending state a continuation has resumed.
- **Workspace settlement receipts never move backward** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace never overwrites a workspace receipt with one from an earlier attachment epoch; a queued continuation that died before attaching has epoch zero, and overwriting a later Task's receipt would let the older settlement reapply Suspend after a resume.
- **Suspension retention requires the durable-commit stamp** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): RuntimePool creation alone does not prove a durable session exists; settleACPClassWorkspace permits retention only with the synchronous durable-commit stamp, otherwise it deletes the empty incarnation and lets the adapter tear down any checkpoint-free pool.
- **Suspend quota exhaustion defers settlement instead of deleting** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Quota exhaustion never replaces a frozen Suspend action with Delete: a live queued continuation may take the still-Ready workspace directly, otherwise settlement stays pending until capacity opens or maxLifetime forces cleanup.
- **Destructive settlement revalidates the frozen detach policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): Before deleting, settleACPClassWorkspace revalidates the frozen policy: a detach action this controller cannot execute (written by a newer controller) or retained deletion categories fail closed after a rollback instead of deleting under a contract this version cannot honor.
- **Impossible suspensions delete the terminal incarnation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): When no retained native checkpoint remains or the hard lifetime expired, settleACPClassWorkspace deletes the terminal incarnation so the Session can create a clean workspace instead of repeatedly attempting an impossible suspension.
- **A requester that never executed defers Delete to a live successor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): A Task that terminated before executing against the workspace (cold-resume flip stored Delete but no RuntimePool demand was stamped) does not destroy the retained repository under another live queued continuation; the successor attaches and stamps its own action. A Task that executed keeps its frozen Delete, and a tainted workspace is never handed to a successor.
- **Settlement deferral transfers ownership to a verified successor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): deferACPSettlementToSuccessor completes a requester's settlement without destroying the workspace when another live continuation is queued for the same incarnation, transferring the successor's detach policy. Delete deferral requires a pre-attachment requester while quota-exhausted Suspend may hand off after execution, and an unlinked successor's Session UID must match the workspace's immutable Session identity so a recreated same-name Session never adopts the old cleanup.
- **Settlement deferral examines every queued candidate** (`controller.successor`): deferACPSettlementToSuccessor examines every candidate so one ineligible waiter (an out-of-policy override or a recreated Session) does not hide a later valid continuation queued behind it.
- **Unprovable successor Session identity retries settlement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deferACPSettlementToSuccessor`): When a candidate successor's Session identity cannot be proven, deferACPSettlementToSuccessor retries the settle instead of destroying the workspace under a possibly valid waiter or transferring ownership to a recreated Session.
- **Deferral publishes the successor's detach policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deferACPSettlementToSuccessor`): deferACPSettlementToSuccessor transfers the successor's policy onto the workspace because the waiter stamps its own action only at attachment; leaving the dead requester's Delete would destroy the retained workspace if the successor also terminates before attaching.
- **Predecessor keeps settlement until the successor's binding is durable** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deferACPSettlementToSuccessor`): A live waiter may still be in its first planning pass, so deferACPSettlementToSuccessor keeps the predecessor responsible until the waiter's immutable binding is durable; linking sooner can orphan the workspace if the waiter dies.
- **Successor Delete is published only after its link is durable** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deferACPSettlementToSuccessor`): deferACPSettlementToSuccessor may publish a successor's Suspend before the ownership link because a stale Suspend preserves data, but publishes a destructive Delete only after the successor's Task link is durable; until then the predecessor's Suspend stays in force.
- **Settlement ownership transfers durably before the predecessor releases** (`controller.TaskReconciler.deferACPSettlementToSuccessor`): deferACPSettlementToSuccessor links the successor durably before the predecessor releases, because an unlinked successor deleted before its first reconcile would leave a workspace nobody settles; a conflict or deleted successor retries and rescans.
- **Missing pool identity proves a Task never used its workspace** (`controller.taskNeverHeldACPWorkspaceAttachment`): taskNeverHeldACPWorkspaceAttachment relies on ACP dispatch stamping the pool identity into status.execution only after workspace attachment is admitted, so its absence proves the Task never executed against the linked workspace.
- **Revocation stamps are bound to one attachment epoch** (`controller.TaskReconciler.markACPWorkspaceRevocationStarted`): markACPWorkspaceRevocationStarted stamps "<epoch> <RFC3339Nano>" so detachTimeout is measured per revocation and a stale stamp from a previous epoch can never act as a later attachment's deadline and instantly quarantine a live workspace.
- **Detach instant is stamped when revocation begins** (`controller.TaskReconciler.markACPWorkspaceRevocationStarted`): markACPWorkspaceRevocationStarted also stamps the last-detached instant so idle retention never falls back to the creation timestamp while the epoch settles.
- **Quarantine removes attachment credentials before releasing the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.quarantineACPWorkspacePastDetachTimeout`): quarantineACPWorkspacePastDetachTimeout deletes the attachment credentials before releasing the Task; the adapter repeats this cleanup, but settlement cannot claim completion while an attachment bearer still exists.
- **Settlement receipts accept the legacy Task-UID-only form** (`controller.parseACPWorkspaceSettlementReceipt`): parseACPWorkspaceSettlementReceipt accepts the legacy single-field receipt carrying only the Task UID as epoch 0, alongside the current "<taskUID> <attachmentEpoch>" form, because existing workspaces may still carry it.
- **Workspace link label, not RuntimePoolName, triggers settlement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.reconcileACPClassWorkspaceSettlement`): reconcileACPClassWorkspaceSettlement uses the controller-owned workspace link as the settlement trigger because a terminal planning failure can happen after attachment but before RuntimePoolName is written, and that attachment must still be revoked.
- **Detach actions wait for the whole Task to settle** (`controller.TaskReconciler.reconcileACPClassWorkspaceSettlement`): reconcileACPClassWorkspaceSettlement waits for the Task phase to settle, not just the prompt, because SessionTurn finalization and artifact retirement still need the live runtime and a racing Suspend would kill the supervisor mid-finalization; deletion-driven settlement proceeds regardless.
- **Idle timeout counts from the last detach** (`controller.acpWorkspaceLastDetachedAnnotation`): acpWorkspaceLastDetachedAnnotation records when the last Task attachment was revoked; the class idleTimeout counts from that instant, or from creation for a workspace that never attached. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Retention cap is frozen on the workspace** (`controller.acpWorkspaceMaxSuspendedAnnotation`): acpWorkspaceMaxSuspendedAnnotation freezes the class retention cap on the materialized workspace so settlement can enforce it without reloading the execution snapshot. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Legacy retention deadline key stays stable** (`controller.acpWorkspaceLegacyRetentionDeadlineAnnotation`): acpWorkspaceLegacyRetentionDeadlineAnnotation stores the fixed fallback cleanup deadline used when the idle clock cannot safely bound a workspace. Its historical key must not be renamed because workspaces migrated by earlier controllers already carry it.
- **Suspend quota Lease holds one pending claim** (`controller.acpSuspendQuotaLeaseClaimsAnnotation`): One class-owned Lease stores at most one pending suspended-capacity claim; its resourceVersion serializes quota transactions across leader handoff. Settled occupancy is counted from live workspaces, so the claims annotation stays constant-size regardless of the configured cap. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Retention reconciler writes desired state only** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler enforces the frozen class idleTimeout and maxLifetime on class-backed ACP workspaces acting only in Orka core's role: it writes desired state and deletes objects, while the workspace adapter executes the transitions. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Quota counts bypass the informer cache** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.APIReader`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.APIReader bypasses the informer cache for suspended-capacity counts so a quota claim never trusts a stale list; it falls back to Client only when unset.
- **Retention without a control store fails closed** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.DurableControlStore`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.DurableControlStore resolves immutable Session identities for unlinked continuation Tasks; without it, retention treats such demand as outstanding (fail-closed).
- **Expiry waits for the cleanup finalizer** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile must not delete a workspace before the core controller installs the cleanup finalizer, because that finalizer runs the linked RuntimePool teardown and records the terminal disposition; deleting earlier would orphan the pool.
- **Inspect retention fence before lifetime returns** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile inspects the deterministic retention fence before either lifetime path can return, so a malformed reserved Lease cannot survive a maxLifetime-only workspace and keep future incarnations fail-closed forever.
- **maxLifetime only clamps the requeue** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): In ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile the maxLifetime deadline never bypasses idle evaluation: an already-elapsed idleTimeout still applies even when maxLifetime is nearer than the poll interval; the earliest deadline only clamps the requeue.
- **Idle evaluation ignores spec.attachmentEpoch** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile defers idle evaluation for a live attachment or a revoked one the adapter still enforces. It deliberately does not consult spec.attachmentEpoch, which is a monotonic high-water mark that stays positive forever after the first attachment.
- **Re-read after detach-instant refresh** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): After refreshing the detach instant, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile requeues and re-reads before applying idle policy, which also gives Task settlement a chance to execute the frozen detach action against the completed revocation.
- **Skip demand lookups without idleTimeout** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): When idle retention is disabled, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile skips the uncached requester lookup and namespace-wide continuation scan because demand cannot change any decision, avoiding O(workspaces x Tasks) work every requeue.
- **Missing detach stamp starts a fresh idle window** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): Older controllers could settle a suspension without recording the detach instant, so ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile stamps the first post-upgrade observation and grants one full idle interval rather than expiring from creation time. A present but malformed stamp stays fail-closed.
- **Retire stale idle-expiry fence on restart** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): A controller restart can leave an idle-expiry fence installed without its post-fence demand scan; ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile deletes that stale fence before returning for live demand so the requester can resume the preserved incarnation.
- **Observed Suspended state is not demand** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): In ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile a live continuation that requested cold resume but has not attached counts as demand, while an observed Suspended or Suspending state alone does not; if the requester dies, idle retention resumes from the actual detach timestamp.
- **Malformed detach stamp holds idle retention** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): A malformed last-detached-at stamp means controller-written metadata was corrupted; ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile holds idle retention and relies on the bounded maxLifetime path instead of falling back to creation time, which would wrongly destroy or suspend a long-lived workspace.
- **Registered demand defers suspended expiry** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): When a continuation has registered UID-bound demand on a suspended workspace, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile does not expire it because the retained checkpoint is about to resume; maxLifetime remains the hard bound if that requester dies.
- **Idle suspended workspaces are deleted** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): A suspended workspace past its idleTimeout has exhausted retention, so ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile admits only terminal deletion until richer retention dispositions are designed. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Idle Ready retention uses the class default** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): Task detach overrides govern settlement only; once an unattached Ready workspace exhausts idleTimeout, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile applies the frozen class default regardless of the last Task's choice.
- **Unknown recorded detach action holds cleanup** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): If the recorded detach action is corrupt or from a newer binary, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile holds idle cleanup even though idle retention uses the class default, because another controller path may still consume that action.
- **Idle Failed workspaces are deleted** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): The adapter treats Failed as terminal and will not retry resume or suspension, so ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile deletes an idle Failed workspace instead of suspending it, which would only refresh last-detached-at and extend retention past idleTimeout.
- **Invalid durable marker blocks idle Delete** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): The durable-workspace marker is controller-authenticated, so ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile treats invalid content as corruption and holds idle cleanup rather than as evidence that Delete is safe.
- **Operator taint covers linked Tasks** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile counts the operator-taint marker on the workspace and on every Task linked to this exact incarnation; an unreadable Task holds retention.
- **Operator-tainted workspaces are deleted on idle** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): Operator access forfeits retention: ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile deletes an idle tainted workspace instead of suspending it, so it is never captured into a data checkpoint for continuations or forks. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Uncommitted workspaces are deleted on idle** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): A materialized RuntimePool does not prove a durable session exists, so ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile matches Task settlement and deletes an idle workspace with no committed RuntimeSession checkpoint rather than suspending it.
- **Quota exhaustion keeps Suspend pending** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile`): When the suspend quota is exhausted, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.Reconcile keeps the workspace Ready and retries instead of replacing the class Suspend action with Delete; maxLifetime remains the independent hard bound. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Quarantined workspaces still idle-expire** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.reconcileQuarantinedWorkspaceRetention`): reconcileQuarantinedWorkspaceRetention applies idleTimeout from the revocation/detach instant to quarantined workspaces, which are terminal but may still hold the deterministic Session workspace name, so idle-only classes eventually reach finalizer-backed cleanup.
- **Fallback deadline bounds unclocked workspaces** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.ensureRetentionFallbackDeadline`): ensureRetentionFallbackDeadline gives workspaces whose idle clock is unavailable (legacy unbounded retention, corrupted idle metadata, quota-capped bindings lacking maxLifetime) one bounded migration window from first observation. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Refresh detach instant once epoch is released** (`controller.refreshACPWorkspaceDetachInstantAfterEpochRelease`): refreshACPWorkspaceDetachInstantAfterEpochRelease replaces the provisional revocation-start timestamp once the adapter stops enforcing the epoch; comparing the two stamps makes the update one-shot while keeping the revocation marker until Task settlement applies the frozen detach action.
- **Pre-marker workspaces remain suspendable** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspaceSuspendableAnnotationPresent`): runtimePoolWorkspaceSuspendableAnnotationPresent reports frozen evidence that the class permitted DataOnly suspension, but still accepts the recorded detach action for workspaces created before the durable marker existed.
- **Malformed durable marker implies possible data** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspaceMayContainDurableData`): runtimePoolWorkspaceMayContainDurableData is the fail-closed retention and quota predicate: a present but malformed durable marker counts as possible preserved data until the adapter proves absence or terminal cleanup.
- **Fence creation linearizes idle expiry** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace`): In ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace, creating the retention fence is the idle-expiry linearization point; an uncached re-scan afterward lets every continuation admitted before the fence cancel deletion, while later Tasks start a fresh incarnation. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Idle deletes use UID+resourceVersion fences** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace`): ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace fences idle-triggered deletions with UID and resourceVersion so a concurrent attachment or resume becomes a retried conflict, while maxLifetime deletion intentionally uses only the UID precondition. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **No retention event for foreign deletion** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace`): When the workspace is already gone, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace records no Event or action metric because retention applied no action.
- **Lost fenced delete cancels the fence** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace`): When a fenced idle deletion loses to a concurrent attach or resume, ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.expireWorkspace records no Event or metric and deletes the expiry fence so the winning Task remains admissible.
