- **Deleted Session needs no retention fence** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.acpWorkspaceRetentionControl`): acpWorkspaceRetentionControl returns no control when the Session is deleted, because a deleted Session cannot admit another continuation for that immutable UID.
- **Same-name replacement Session needs no fence** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.acpWorkspaceRetentionControl`): acpWorkspaceRetentionControl returns no control for a same-name replacement Session, because it cannot admit a continuation for the workspace's immutable Session UID.
- **Recreate the fence Lease per incarnation** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.activateACPWorkspaceRetentionFence`): activateACPWorkspaceRetentionFence keeps the deterministic logical-workspace Lease name but recreates the Lease per workspace incarnation, because its API-server creationTimestamp is the cutoff compared with Task creationTimestamp; patching an old Lease would keep an earlier cutoff and miss new demand.
- **Fence activation uses API-server time** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.ensureACPWorkspaceRetentionFenceActivation`): ensureACPWorkspaceRetentionFenceActivation canonicalizes old, missing, or controller-clock activation values to the Lease creationTimestamp so Tasks and the fence are compared on the API server's single clock.
- **Remove malformed fence with exact preconditions** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.currentACPWorkspaceRetentionFence`): currentACPWorkspaceRetentionFence deletes malformed or wrongly owned objects at the reserved fence name using exact object preconditions, so a concurrent valid replacement wins and the next reconcile re-reads it.
- **Tasks wait behind an active retention fence** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskBlockedByACPWorkspaceRetentionFence`): TaskReconciler.taskBlockedByACPWorkspaceRetentionFence makes Tasks created after the fence wait without mutating the expiring incarnation until cleanup exposes an absent or replacement UID; Tasks visible to the final demand scan cancel expiry instead. See [ADR 0029](0029-acp-workspace-retention.md).
- **Process-local quota locks only reduce contention** (`controller.acpSuspendQuotaLocks`): acpSuspendQuotaLocks only avoids redundant Lease contention within one process; the Kubernetes Lease is what fences claims across leader handoff. Entries live only while referenced so recreated classes do not leak locks.
- **Read quota Lease before listing workspaces** (`controller.claimACPSuspendQuotaSlot`): claimACPSuspendQuotaSlot reads the Lease transaction fence before listing workspaces, so any racing claim or release advances its resourceVersion and the final optimistic write retries instead of committing stale occupancy.
- **Replace malformed quota Lease conditionally** (`controller.claimACPSuspendQuotaSlot`): claimACPSuspendQuotaSlot replaces a malformed pre-upgrade or tenant-created object at the reserved quota Lease name only when its UID and resourceVersion still match, so a concurrent valid replacement wins and forces a retry.
- **Keep pending claim while its write may land** (`controller.releaseObsoleteACPSuspendQuotaLease`): releaseObsoleteACPSuspendQuotaLease keeps a pending claim reserved while its workspace write can still succeed, so a replacement leader can finish it.
- **Every suspension path rejects tainted workspaces** (`controller.suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota`): suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota is the backstop for every suspension path: a workspace tainted through its own or any linked Task's marker is never checkpointed, and an unreadable Task refuses suspension.
- **Stale settlement reserves no quota** (`controller.suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota`): suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota returns without reserving quota when the settlement already landed or a later attachment displaced it, so it never suspends newer Ready state.
- **Settlement receipt shares the suspension patch** (`controller.suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota`): suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota writes the settlement receipt in the same patch as the suspension, so if the controller dies before the Task-side marker patch, a restarted reconcile completes the marker instead of re-applying Suspend to newer session state.
- **Suspension clears pending resume demand** (`controller.suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota`): suspendACPWorkspaceWithinQuota deletes the resume-request annotation because suspension settles pending provisioning or resume demand; a later continuation stamps fresh demand when it resumes the workspace.
- **Legacy demand records fall back to Session scan** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.pendingWorkspaceDemandOutstanding`): pendingWorkspaceDemandOutstanding trusts current demand records bound to the requesting Task UID; legacy records without that identity fall through to the exact Session continuation scan.
- **Any live continuation keeps demand outstanding** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.pendingWorkspaceDemandOutstanding`): The UID-bound demand stamp records only the last writer, so pendingWorkspaceDemandOutstanding also treats any live non-terminal Task on the Session as demand; otherwise the recorded requester terminating would surrender a workspace another continuation waits on.
- **Malformed demand stamp counts as live** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.recordedWorkspaceDemandLive`): recordedWorkspaceDemandLive treats a malformed controller-owned demand stamp as live demand (fail-closed) while the persisted fallback deadline bounds the corruption when the class has no maxLifetime.
- **Legacy demand stamps prove no identity** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.recordedWorkspaceDemandLive`): recordedWorkspaceDemandLive does not trust legacy demand stamps holding only a timestamp or a mutable Task name; it resolves demand through the exact Session continuation scan instead.
- **Recycled Task names are not the requester** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.recordedWorkspaceDemandLive`): recordedWorkspaceDemandLive rejects a Task recreated under the same namespace/name as the recorded requester, so its unrelated lifetime cannot keep stale demand alive.
- **Terminal requester ends recorded demand** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.recordedWorkspaceDemandLive`): recordedWorkspaceDemandLive reports no demand for a requester that can never attach, because its settlement or deletion settlement owns remaining cleanup.
- **Session continuation scan uses a field selector** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.liveSessionContinuationExists`): liveSessionContinuationExists relies on the Task CRD exposing sessionRef.name as a selectable field so its uncached read stays bounded to one Session.
- **No control store keeps waiters as demand** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.liveSessionContinuationExists`): liveSessionContinuationExists counts an otherwise eligible waiter as demand when no DurableControlStore can resolve the immutable Session identity, preserving fail-closed behavior.
- **Return every continuation candidate** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations returns every live continuation for the exact incarnation and fails closed on list errors; returning all candidates lets settlement skip an ineligible waiter instead of concluding no successor exists.
- **Recreated classes are separate incarnations** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations resolves class UID verification once for unlinked candidates, because a class recreated under the same name has a new UID and its Tasks resolve a new workspace incarnation.
- **Demand binds to the workspace incarnation** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations binds demand to the workspace incarnation rather than the Session name: linked waiters must carry this workspace's name and UID, while not-yet-linked waiters count until their first reconcile links or fails them.
- **Only workspace-requesting Tasks are demand** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations counts an unlinked Task only if it requests a session-reused execution workspace of this class; plain transcript-backed Tasks sharing the Session name would otherwise suppress idle retention and hold quota.
- **Other classes are not demand** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations skips Tasks naming a different class or using the legacy enabled-workspace path, since they cannot resolve to this workspace and must not defer its settlement or retention.
- **Deleted class waiters are not demand** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations ignores name-matched waiters when the frozen class is gone, because they resolve nothing or a future recreation's new incarnation.
- **Recreated class waiters are not demand** (`controller.liveACPSessionContinuations`): liveACPSessionContinuations ignores waiters whose classRef resolves a replacement class with a different UID, since they target a different workspace incarnation.
- **Invalid frozen cap means zero** (`controller.acpWorkspaceSuspendedCapFromAnnotation`): acpWorkspaceSuspendedCapFromAnnotation treats a present but invalid frozen cap as zero (exhausted) instead of silently disabling the class hard quota.
- **Failed durable workspaces keep their quota slot** (`controller.workspaceConsumesSuspendedQuota`): workspaceConsumesSuspendedQuota keeps charging a terminally failed workspace that may still contain durable data, including one with a present but invalid protected marker; only an absent marker or an explicit durable-data-absent proof frees the slot.
- **Cold resumes in flight still consume quota** (`controller.workspaceConsumesSuspendedQuota`): A cold resume sets DesiredState to Ready before the preserved runtime serves, so workspaceConsumesSuspendedQuota keeps charging a workspace still observed Suspended or Suspending until the adapter projects Ready or Attached or proves the data absent.
- **Workspace lifetime bounds active prompts** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.newWorkspaceRuntimeContext`): ACPDispatcher.newWorkspaceRuntimeContext applies the workspace frozen lifetime as a prompt deadline. Deleting the RuntimePool closes admission but waits for controller work to settle, so the prompt must enter normal deadline cancellation for the pool drain to complete.
- **Ceiling check without a Task judges explicit defaults** (`controller.validateAgentRuntimeCeiling`): validateAgentRuntimeCeiling judges the Task override or else the Agent default; with a nil Task it checks only defaults the Agent sets explicitly, since Tasks may still supply their own policy within the ceiling. Orka-injected coordination tools are not counted. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Registered allowlists judged by exposed tools** (`controller.validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy`): validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy judges a Task allowlist that exactly matches the AgentRuntime registration by the tools that policy exposes after its deny list and Bash gate; any other allowlist (which dispatch rejects) is judged as requested. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Turn ceiling skips registered runtimes** (`controller.validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy`): validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy skips the maxTurns ceiling for registered external runtimes because their profile fixes the iteration budget and rejects maxTurns overrides. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Bash ceiling skips registered runtimes** (`controller.validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy`): validateAgentRuntimeCeilingWithRegisteredPolicy applies only the tool allowlist ceiling to registered external runtimes because their profile fixes the Bash gate. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Ceiling judges post-normalization tools** (`controller.resolvedCeilingToolRequest`): resolvedCeilingToolRequest returns the tools the Task can execute after the runtime's normalization, deny list, and Bash gate, starting from the same allowlist dispatch uses and dropping only injected coordination tools; a nil list means the unrestricted native set. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **External runtimes judged by registered policy** (`controller.resolvedCeilingToolRequest`): resolvedCeilingToolRequest judges an external runtime by what its registered policy exposes, matching buildAgentRuntimeMCPConfigurationWithRegistry, because those runtimes accept only the exact registered allowlist and reject allowBash and disallowedTools overrides.
- **Blank allowlist is judged unrestricted** (`controller.resolvedCeilingToolRequest`): resolvedCeilingToolRequest treats a nonempty all-blank allowlist, the legacy unrestricted sentinel, as the widest grant so it can never slip past a ceiling.
- **Task-requested coordination tools are counted** (`controller.resolvedCeilingToolRequest`): resolvedCeilingToolRequest exempts coordination tools only when Orka injected them; one the Task requested itself must still fit the ceiling. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Agent defaults judged at write intent** (`controller.resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools`): Without a Task, resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools judges Agent defaults with write intent, the widest case, because read intent narrows tools on some runtimes.
- **Codex sessions always count as Bash** (`controller.resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools`): resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools always counts Bash for Codex because every Codex session runs shell commands, even read-only ones; allowBash: false cannot remove that. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Deny list matches names exactly** (`controller.resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools`): resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools judges exactly what dispatch grants via MCPToolPolicy.Allows, whose deny list matches names exactly, so a differently cased deny entry does not narrow the request.
- **Ceiling tool matching rules** (`controller.ceilingAllowsTool`): ceilingAllowsTool matches tool names case-insensitively but scopes exactly because scopes carry command or path text. A bare entry covers scoped requests of the same tool, a scoped entry covers only its scope, and a forwarding Tool entry covers its derived write tool.
- **Only valid Tool names derive write tools** (`controller.ceilingAllowsTool`): ceilingAllowsTool derives a write tool only from a single suffix on a valid Kubernetes name with no scope or second suffix.
- **Delegated children judged on materialized native list** (`controller.resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools`): For delegated children, dispatch materializes the native tool list before injecting messaging tools, so resolvedCeilingRuntimeTools judges that list and an exact-match deny entry like "bash" leaves "Bash" granted.
- **Omitted built-in contract means the namespace mode** (`controller.agentOmitsBuiltInContract`): agentOmitsBuiltInContract identifies built-in Agents without contractVersion; admission allows that because each namespace is bound to exactly one execution mode, so the omission can only mean that mode.
- **Read contracts through the effective default** (`controller.withEffectiveBuiltInContract`): Every planning and binding path reads the Agent contract through withEffectiveBuiltInContract so an Agent created in the same apply as its Task is classified before the Agent reconciler persists the value; the stored object is never modified.
- **Agent contract selector may be cleared** (`controller.TestAgentContractSelectorMayBeClearedOnAPIServer`): The Agent CRD validation must allow reapplying a manifest that omits the only supported contractVersion, deleting the stored selector without an admission webhook; the controller writes the value back.
- **Built-in Agents get the namespace mode stamped** (`controller.AgentReconciler.Mode`): AgentReconciler.Mode is the controller static execution mode; a built-in Agent that omitted contractVersion is stamped with it on first reconcile so the stored object carries its classification.
- **Agent toolbox admission mirrors Task planning** (`controller.AgentReconciler.ToolboxPolicy`): AgentReconciler.ToolboxPolicy gates runtime.toolboxes at Agent admission with the same rules Task planning applies, so readiness and planning never disagree.
- **Identity claims read live state** (`controller.AgentReconciler.APIReader`): AgentReconciler.APIReader reads live state for identity claims so two Agents never claim one identity from a stale cache. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Nil verifier leaves identities declared** (`controller.AgentReconciler.IdentityVerifier`): When AgentReconciler.IdentityVerifier is nil, Agent identities stay declared but unverified rather than failing.
- **Issuer outage backoff is shared** (`controller.AgentReconciler.issuerBackoff`): AgentReconciler.issuerBackoff pauses identity verification for every Agent after the issuer fails to answer, so an outage costs one timeout rather than one per Agent on the single reconcile worker.
- **Cache issuer answers per Agent** (`controller.AgentReconciler.verifications`): AgentReconciler.verifications keeps each Agent's last issuer answer until it may be asked again, so requeues caused by the Agent's own status writes never query the issuer early.
- **Controller binds identities on behalf of checked callers** (`controller.AgentReconciler.Reconcile`): The API binds Agent identities only for callers it has checked for the bind verb and writes them as the controller, which is why the controller RBAC grants bind on agentidentities.
- **Contract stamp conflicts retry with backoff** (`controller.AgentReconciler.Reconcile`): When stamping the built-in contract hits a conflict, AgentReconciler.Reconcile returns the error so the requeue with backoff reads the fresh object.
- **Agent identities are reverified periodically** (`controller.AgentReconciler.Reconcile`): AgentReconciler.Reconcile requeues verified identities on agentIdentityReverifyInterval because identities can be disabled or deleted at the issuer at any time.
- **Identity release waits for bound Tasks** (`controller.AgentReconciler.Reconcile`): AgentReconciler.Reconcile rechecks a releasing identity claim later because the release completes only once Tasks bound to it are done.
- **Validate token limits for pre-bound Agents** (`controller.validateAgentModelLimits`): validateAgentModelLimits rejects token limits that Agents admitted before the CRD bounds existed can still carry, because workers would otherwise silently replace a non-positive maxTokens with their default.
- **runtimeRef readiness checks existence only** (`controller.AgentReconciler.validateRuntimeRef`): AgentReconciler.validateRuntimeRef only reports a missing AgentRuntime in the Agent namespace; dispatch still resolves the runtime and checks its live readiness in the Task namespace.
- **Provider watch matches every namespace** (`controller.AgentReconciler.agentsForProvider`): AgentReconciler.agentsForProvider lists Agents in every watched namespace because a providerRef may name another namespace; Agents are matched on the reference's effective namespace.
- **Missing Tool objects do not fail validation** (`controller.AgentReconciler.validateTools`): AgentReconciler.validateTools skips a referenced Tool that is not found because the name may be a built-in tool; it does not fail Agent validation.
- **Status decisions are fenced to one generation** (`controller.AgentReconciler.updateStatus`): AgentReconciler.updateStatus never records validation, claim, or verification results on a recreated or edited Agent, which could name another identity; it errors to retry, and the edit requeues the Agent.
- **Identity claim writes are compare-and-set** (`controller.AgentReconciler.updateStatus`): AgentReconciler.updateStatus writes the identity claim only if the stored claim still equals the one it was decided from.
- **Failed validation is rechecked periodically** (`controller.AgentReconciler.updateStatus`): AgentReconciler.updateStatus requeues failed validation on agentValidationRecheckInterval because missing dependencies are often created later and Secrets are not watched.
- **Non-positive TTL never deletes** (`controller.AgentReconciler.checkTTLExpiry`): AgentReconciler.checkTTLExpiry treats a zero TTL as permanent and a negative TTL as invalid; neither ever deletes the Agent.
- **Default instruction validation scope** (`controller.agentValidatesDefaultInstructions`): agentValidatesDefaultInstructions excludes external and legacy runtimes because their prompt delivery is not described by the built-in v2 configuration validators.
- **Readiness validates default instructions only** (`controller.AgentReconciler.validateDefaultInstructions`): AgentReconciler.validateDefaultInstructions checks the default role and soul against the execution delivery guards; Task-level overrides are not reflected in Agent readiness.
- **No-soul AI keeps legacy prompt delivery** (`controller.AgentReconciler.validateDefaultInstructions`): For AI Agents without a soul, AgentReconciler.validateDefaultInstructions keeps the legacy role-source precedence and raw environment delivery; literal dollar escaping applies only with a soul.
- **ACP prompt validation covers native envelopes** (`controller.AgentReconciler.validateDefaultInstructions`): validateACPProviderSystemPrompt checks Codex's JSON-escaped native environment envelope and Copilot's literal-instruction policy, not just the raw ACP configuration bound.
- **Soul index covers role sources** (`controller.agentSoulConfigMapDependencyIndex`): agentSoulConfigMapDependencyIndex is cache-only and indexes every role source read by Agent readiness validation in addition to soul sources.
- **ConfigMap mapping uses declared references** (`controller.AgentReconciler.agentsForSoulConfigMap`): AgentReconciler.agentsForSoulConfigMap maps declared references without fetching the ConfigMap, so creation and deletion events also requeue dependent Agents.
- **AgentRuntime updates are ignored** (`controller.AgentReconciler.SetupWithManager`): AgentReconciler.SetupWithManager ignores AgentRuntime update events because Agent readiness depends only on whether the referenced AgentRuntime exists, and its status updates are frequent.
- **Agents watch other identity claimants** (`controller.AgentReconciler.SetupWithManager`): AgentReconciler.SetupWithManager watches Agents sharing an identity because only one Agent may hold it, so a claimant appearing, changing, or leaving changes the others' readiness.
- **Agent reconciles run with one worker** (`controller.AgentReconciler.SetupWithManager`): AgentReconciler runs with MaxConcurrentReconciles 1 because identity claims are decided one Agent at a time in reconcileIdentityClaim.
- **Discover prompt boundary via the planner validator** (`controller.TestAgentDefaultInstructionsCodexNativeEnvelope`): The default-instructions boundary test binary-searches the native limit through the planner validator instead of copying harnessv2.MaxAgentSystemPromptBytes or the JSON/native-envelope estimate, so the test tracks the real guard.
- **Agent Tasks freeze a write-once execution binding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureAgentExecutionBinding`): Every executable agent Task freezes its protocol, backend, and an immutable content-addressed execution snapshot in a write-once binding before any executor side effect. The snapshot store and revisioned backend control are mandatory; a controller that cannot prove either never queues executor demand.
- **Candidate resolution performs reads only** (`controller.agentExecutionCandidate`): agentExecutionCandidate is the pure resolution product (prospective binding plus plaintext snapshot body); producing it performs reads only, with no durable writes or runtime side effects.
- **Execution snapshot holds no secrets** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotBody`): agentExecutionSnapshotBody is the canonical non-secret executable input frozen into the immutable snapshot; credentials stay references only and raw credential values or TxTokens never enter it.
- **Frozen Connections must still match at call time** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotBody.Connections`): agentExecutionSnapshotBody.Connections freezes, per reachable connection-mode OutboundAccessPolicy, the identity of the requester Connection at dispatch; call-time resolution fails closed unless the live Connection still matches, and no token material is recorded.
- **Re-linking invalidates frozen Connection authority** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotConnection.GrantSequence`): agentExecutionSnapshotConnection.GrantSequence records the Connection consent count when frozen; a later re-link raises it and invalidates the snapshot's authority to use the account.
- **Pin the connection-mode policy identity** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotConnection.PolicyUID`): agentExecutionSnapshotConnection pins PolicyUID and PolicyGeneration because the policy's credential output semantics are part of what the Task was dispatched with.
- **External runtime status fence is re-read** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotExternalRuntime`): agentExecutionSnapshotExternalRuntime freezes the non-secret registration and authentication authority, but the runtime live status fence is re-read just before dispatch because controller epochs and supervisor boots change without changing the endpoint or profile.
- **Workspace binding stays provider-neutral** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceBinding`): agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceBinding freezes the canonical provider-neutral binding and never carries provider-native identifiers, physical workspace names, or secrets. See [ADR 0026](0026-acp-class-backed-execution-workspaces.md).
- **Class binding carries Orka-owned identity only** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceClass`): agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceClass carries only Orka-owned class identity and policy, never provider-native identifiers or secrets. See [ADR 0026](0026-acp-class-backed-execution-workspaces.md).
- **Preserve omitted versus empty allowlist** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotToolPolicy`): agentExecutionSnapshotToolPolicy keeps AllowedToolsOmitted separate from an explicit empty allowlist because the two mean different tool grants under Agent defaults.
- **Connector adjustments feed plan, policy, and snapshot** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID`): Connector-backed tool visibility and approval defaults depend on the requester's links, so resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID applies them to copies used for the plan, the MCP policy, and the frozen snapshot alike.
- **Forwarding write tools require approval** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID`): A forwarding Tool that allows writes adds its write tool to the Task, requiring approval unless the Tool waives it.
- **Resolve Session UID before live storage checks** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID`): A class-backed continuation resolves its immutable Session UID before the class resolver checks live storage, because the linked RuntimePool carries the frozen volume identity and requiring the original or default StorageClass would reject a valid continuation after that class was retired.
- **Native launch mode is frozen with tool policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID`): The native permission launch mode is frozen with the tool policy so the RuntimeSession binding, session create, and every prompt carry the same value.
- **Connector classification must not change during binding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveAgentExecutionCandidateWithWorkspaceSessionUID`): Visibility and approvals are decided from the first connector classification; connectorClassificationUnchanged refuses a snapshot whose frozen tools now classify differently.
- **Ceiling judged on the frozen registration** (`controller.resolveExternalBindingTargets`): resolveExternalBindingTargets re-judges the Agent ceiling against the AgentRuntime registration being frozen, because planning judged an earlier read and a policy change in between could widen tools.
- **Linked built-in loss during binding retries** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveExternalAgentExecutionCandidate`): If a linked built-in's Ready link disappears between the visibility check and freezing, resolveExternalAgentExecutionCandidate treats it as transient and retries; the snapshot is never written without it.
- **Binding digest is stable across reconciles** (`controller.canonicalAgentExecutionBindingDigest`): canonicalAgentExecutionBindingDigest hashes the binding with its digest and timestamp cleared so identical inputs re-resolve to the same digest across reconciles.
- **Binding conflicts are permanent** (`controller.errAgentExecutionBindingConflict`): errAgentExecutionBindingConflict marks a permanent conflict: an existing binding is never overwritten and the Task never dispatches under a mismatched candidate.
- **Binding writes are uncached compare-if-absent** (`controller.TaskReconciler.persistAgentExecutionBinding`): persistAgentExecutionBinding is an uncached compare-if-absent write-once CAS and never overwrites an existing binding.
- **Matching persisted binding wins** (`controller.TaskReconciler.persistAgentExecutionBinding`): persistAgentExecutionBinding treats a persisted binding with the same digest as authoritative, which also recovers from an ambiguous status-patch response.
- **Re-read after ambiguous status patch errors** (`controller.TaskReconciler.persistAgentExecutionBinding`): A transport error can arrive after the API server committed the status patch, so persistAgentExecutionBinding re-reads before classifying the failure and recognizes an exact binding instead of calling it a conflict.
- **Ask launch mode only for RuntimePool sessions** (`controller.validateFrozenNativePermissions`): validateFrozenNativePermissions allows the ask launch mode only for RuntimePool sessions and requires the mode to be exactly what the frozen permission decisions need.
- **Settlement reads the binding without dispatch gates** (`controller.TaskReconciler.loadVerifiedACPWorkspaceBindingForSettlement`): loadVerifiedACPWorkspaceBindingForSettlement reads the encrypted snapshot without dispatch-only deletion or generation gates, because the immutable binding remains the settlement authority after a Task terminates or begins deleting.
- **Re-verify binding uncached before demand** (`controller.TaskReconciler.loadVerifiedBoundExecution`): loadVerifiedBoundExecution re-reads the Task, backend control, and encrypted snapshot uncached immediately before executor demand is persisted.
- **Admitted work survives runtime drain** (`controller.TaskReconciler.loadVerifiedBoundExecutionForActiveSession`): loadVerifiedBoundExecutionForActiveSession keeps the immutable binding checks for already-admitted work without requiring the runtime to still accept new sessions while draining.
- **Binding waits for the live Agent generation** (`controller.agentCacheBehind`): agentCacheBehind delays binding while the cached Agent differs from the live one, because the binding freezes that generation and a stale copy must never bind past a tightened ceiling. Deleted Agents are left to deletion paths; recreated ones differ by UID. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Bound Tasks re-check the live ceiling** (`controller.TaskReconciler.requireBoundAgentCeiling`): requireBoundAgentCeiling re-judges a bound Task against the live Agent ceiling until it starts, catching a ceiling tightened between the live check and the binding write; later changes follow the binding as for running Tasks. See [ADR 0048](0048-agent-runtime-ceilings.md).
- **Linked built-ins must not alter external profiles** (`controller.checkExternalLinkedBuiltins`): checkExternalLinkedBuiltins admits GitHub built-ins on an external v2 runtime only when the requester's link leaves the registered policy unchanged (tools visible, writes already approval-required); anything else is a permanent refusal, never a per-person narrowing.
- **External runtimes refuse connection-mode tools** (`controller.TaskReconciler.checkExternalConnectorTools`): checkExternalConnectorTools fails closed for tools behind connection-mode policies on external v2 runtimes, whose registered MCP policy cannot be narrowed per requester, rather than advertising tools whose every call would fail.
- **Task denies of registered tools are refused** (`controller.TaskReconciler.checkExternalConnectorTools`): Because external session descriptors come from the registered policy alone, checkExternalConnectorTools refuses a Task that denies a linked built-in the policy still exposes, rather than binding a tool it denied.
- **Built-ins shadow same-named Tool CRs** (`controller.brokeredCustomCandidates`): brokeredCustomCandidates mirrors buildCanonicalMCPToolDescriptors: a registered built-in or provider-native tool takes precedence over a same-named Tool CR, which is then never exposed and cannot make a Task connector-backed.
- **Settings are reported via events, digests in binding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recordAgentExecutionSettings`): recordAgentExecutionSettings reports the model and reasoning effort as an event because the binding stores only their digests.
- **Transient planning errors requeue** (`controller.agentExecutionPlan.transientError`): agentExecutionPlan.transientError defers planning instead of rejecting, so a brief read outage requeues rather than permanently failing the Task.
- **Agent Task routing rules** (`controller.TaskReconciler.planAgentExecution`): planAgentExecution routes built-in Codex, Claude, Copilot, and OpenCode only through the harness v2 RuntimePool path; an execution workspace request binds a workspace-provider-backed pool when enabled and fails closed otherwise. External runtimeRef registrations share the ACP state machines without managed pools, and unsupported contracts fail before work starts.
- **Requester permission decisions need the v2 pool path** (`controller.requesterPermissionDecisionsRejection`): requesterPermissionDecisionsRejection fails closed on every route except a built-in runtime on the harness v2 RuntimePool path, the only one that can ask before each policy-allowed native tool; other routes would run those tools without asking.
- **Any present workspace is a request** (`controller.taskRequestsExecutionWorkspace`): taskRequestsExecutionWorkspace treats every present workspace field as a request so malformed or pruned requests fail validation instead of running normally.
- **Frozen bindings skip new-allocation readiness** (`controller.TaskReconciler.rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan`): Once the execution binding is frozen, rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan skips live class resolution and provider Active checks (Draining providers stay admitted for existing workspaces); dispatch gates are enforced on the verified frozen plan at queueACPRuntimeTask, never on the public status projection.
- **Workspace planning outages requeue** (`controller.TaskReconciler.rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan`): rejectUnsupportedACPWorkspacePlan requeues API, provider-authorization, and control-store outages, while real quota exhaustion and validation failures remain rejections.
- **External profile intent mismatch is permanent** (`controller.externalAgentRuntimeConformanceError`): externalAgentRuntimeConformanceError classifies a workspace-intent mismatch with the conformed profile as permanent so binding fails with the reason instead of retrying as readiness until Task timeout.
- **Dispatch gates apply to the frozen plan** (`controller.TaskReconciler.frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason`): frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason is the single authority enforcing configuration-level workspace dispatch gates against the verified frozen plan; ordinary planning and bound-task recovery both reach it, so a flag disabled after binding can never create new RuntimePool demand.
- **Class workspaces need the provider API** (`controller.TaskReconciler.frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason`): frozenWorkspaceDispatchDisabledReason rejects class-backed workspaces without --enable-workspace-provider-api because in cleanup-only mode the core workspace reconciler never admits a new workspace and the Task would stay Pending forever.
- **Snapshots retained 30 days unreferenced** (`controller.DefaultAgentExecutionSnapshotRetention`): DefaultAgentExecutionSnapshotRetention is the minimum audit and backup retention period (30 days) before an unreferenced execution snapshot may be collected.
- **Snapshot collection clock restarts conservatively** (`controller.AgentExecutionSnapshotRetentionManager`): AgentExecutionSnapshotRetentionManager deletes a snapshot only after Kubernetes and durable-store references are absent for a full window; its in-memory clock restarts on controller restart, delaying collection rather than risking premature deletion.
- **Re-check references before each snapshot delete** (`controller.AgentExecutionSnapshotRetentionManager.collectOnce`): AgentExecutionSnapshotRetentionManager.collectOnce re-lists Kubernetes references and re-counts durable references at the deletion boundary; any uncertainty aborts the pass without deleting this or later candidates.
- **Pending identity claims retry binding** (`controller.errIdentityClaimPending`): errIdentityClaimPending marks a Task waiting for its Agent to hold the identity it declares; binding retries instead of failing. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Claims cover only Tasks created strictly after the grant** (`controller.identityClaimCoversTask`): identityClaimCoversTask admits only Tasks created strictly after the claim grant, the only ones checked for Agent use while the Agent named the identity. Both timestamps have one-second precision, so a Task created in the grant's second is refused. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Pre-enforcement grants count from period start** (`controller.enforcedIdentityClaim`): enforcedIdentityClaim treats a grant from before the current Agent use enforcement period as starting at that period, so Tasks or sources created while the check was off are never covered; it returns errEnforcementPeriodPending until the start is known. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Identity claim grant and release protocol** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): reconcileIdentityClaim grants a claim only when no other Agent holds it, with one-at-a-time live-read reconciles. Release takes two passes: mark releasing (refused by new bindings and unstarted Tasks), then release once a live scan finds no unfinished bound Task. Grant time is recorded afterward from the API server's latest managedFields write time, so the controller clock never decides coverage. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Identity claims start from the live Agent** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): reconcileIdentityClaim reads the live Agent because a cached copy can still show a claim this Agent already released to another Agent.
- **Claims exist only under enforcement** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): Identity claims exist only while Agent use authorization is enforced and the issuer is configured; reconcileIdentityClaim releases a held claim when either is missing, as if the spec no longer named it. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Re-named identity is granted again** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): When the spec names a releasing identity again or enforcement returns, reconcileIdentityClaim regrants it with a new grant time because Tasks created while it was leaving were not checked for it.
- **Identity ownership is per namespace** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): reconcileIdentityClaim decides identity ownership within one namespace because the controller watches and holds Agent RBAC only there.
- **Deleted Agents hold identity until Tasks finish** (`controller.AgentReconciler.reconcileIdentityClaim`): A deleted Agent's claim goes with it, but reconcileIdentityClaim keeps the identity taken while its bound Tasks are unfinished.
- **Grant time from managedFields** (`controller.apiServerWriteTime`): apiServerWriteTime returns the latest managedFields write time at one-second precision; since the granting write set its manager's time, the result is never earlier than that write.
- **Identity verification timeout is short** (`controller.agentIdentityVerifyTimeout`): agentIdentityVerifyTimeout is shorter than a Tool call's token requests because Agents are verified one at a time.
- **Verification answers are per generation** (`controller.agentIdentityVerification`): agentIdentityVerification caches an issuer answer for one generation of one Agent for agentIdentityReverifyInterval; an edit asks the issuer again at once.
- **Identity verification skips invalid configs** (`controller.AgentReconciler.verifyAgentIdentity`): verifyAgentIdentity never sends a configuration that failed validation to the issuer; after the issuer fails to answer no Agent asks until backoff ends, and an issuer refusal is not retried early.
- **Issuer refusals are sticky** (`controller.AgentReconciler.verifyAgentIdentity`): verifyAgentIdentity keeps an issuer refusal until the issuer verifies the identity again; a validation failure or issuer outage must not downgrade it to merely declared, whose cached tokens Tools could still receive.
- **Requeue at cached answer expiry** (`controller.AgentReconciler.verifyAgentIdentity`): verifyAgentIdentity requeues when the cached answer expires rather than a full interval later, so an answer is never trusted past its lifetime.
- **Recorded refusals survive restarts** (`controller.AgentReconciler.lastIdentityRefusal`): lastIdentityRefusal prefers this controller's own last answer; without one, as after restart or leader change, the refusal recorded in Agent status stands, so only a fresh verification clears it.
- **Refusal messages share a parsable prefix** (`controller.identityRefusalPrefix`): identityRefusalPrefix starts every refusal message with the refused identity so lastIdentityRefusal can recognize the recorded refusal for that identity.
- **Released claims pass to waiting Agents** (`controller.AgentReconciler.agentsSharingIdentity`): agentsSharingIdentity requeues every other Agent declaring or holding an identity the changed Agent declares or holds, so a released claim is picked up by the next waiting Agent.
- **Parked continuations cannot use identity-bound Agents** (`controller.TaskReconciler.agentExecutionIdentity`): agentExecutionIdentity refuses parked-turn continuations for identity-bound Agents because the controller created them without an identity check and a Delegated identity would restart its maximum age at the wake. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Bind against the live identity claim** (`controller.TaskReconciler.agentExecutionIdentity`): agentExecutionIdentity judges the Agent's live claim, not the copy the Task resolved earlier.
- **Automation Tasks need a covered source** (`controller.requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim`): requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim refuses an Orka-created Task unless its controlling RepositoryScan or RepositoryMonitor (matched by UID) was created after the claim grant, because Orka creates those Tasks under its broad Agent use grant. GatewayBinding Tasks reference the binding only by name and are refused. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Orphaned automation Tasks are refused** (`controller.requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim`): requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim refuses Tasks carrying scan or monitor markers once their owner reference is gone, so an orphaned automation Task cannot pass as a root Task.
- **Foreign owners do not vouch for automation Tasks** (`controller.requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim`): requireTaskSourceCoveredByClaim refuses automation Tasks controlled by any other Orka owner, such as one attached after an orphaning delete.
- **Delegated children need a parent bound to the identity** (`controller.requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent`): requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent lets a delegated child use an identity-bound Agent only when its controlling parent is bound to that exact Agent and identity. A scheduled run uses its agent Task's own immutable Agent and needs that Task created after the grant; any other parent must be bound, since a worker could add a schedule to its own native Task. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Fork annotation only shapes the message** (`controller.requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent`): In requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent the fork annotation only explains the refusal message; the decision never depends on it.
- **Scheduled Tasks from automation judged by source** (`controller.requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent`): requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent also judges a scheduled Task created by automation or a GatewayBinding by that source, since its schedule may have been added later.
- **Worker-scheduled Tasks require the delegator to own identity** (`controller.requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent`): A scheduled Task a worker delegated was created under the worker's broad Agent use, so requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent requires the delegating Task to own the identity itself.
- **Parents must hold the identity now** (`controller.requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent`): requireParentOwnsIdentityAgent refuses a child when its parent was bound under an earlier grant or before the current enforcement period, because a child borrows its parent's current authority.
- **Only delegated bindings walk the parent chain** (`controller.agentIdentityBindingFromTask`): agentIdentityBindingFromTask computes the delegation start only for bindings that act for the requester; Self-mode Tasks never walk parents, and a Delegated call without a start is refused as expired.
- **Delegation start is the root requester action** (`controller.delegationStart`): delegationStart returns the creation of the chain's root Task, which child Tasks, retries, or wakes never move. An unprovable chain (including a fork without a UID pin) or one passing through another requester returns zero time, which Delegated mode refuses.
- **Parent links need an immutable UID pin** (`controller.delegatingTask`): delegatingTask accepts a parent link only when pinned by the brokered parent UID or controller owner reference; unpinned, missing, or recreated parents fail with errDelegationChainBroken.
- **Bound identity is rechecked until queued** (`controller.TaskReconciler.requireBoundIdentityClaim`): requireBoundIdentityClaim re-checks after binding and until queueing that the Agent still holds the frozen identity, since a release between decision and write could leave two Agents acting as one identity. Queued Tasks are checked by ACPDispatcher before every runtime admission. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **Frozen identities need the same controller config** (`controller.boundIdentityUnavailable`): boundIdentityUnavailable refuses a frozen identity unless Agent use enforcement is still on and the same issuer instance is configured.
- **Delegated calls need a live Task and Session** (`controller.DelegationLiveness`): DelegationLiveness requires a Delegated call's Task to be running without cancellation and its Session to exist, both read live; stopping a Session turn cancels its Task, so the Task check covers it.
- **Stopped ancestors stop delegated calls** (`controller.DelegationLiveness.requireLiveAncestors`): requireLiveAncestors refuses a child's delegated call once any ancestor was cancelled, is deleting, is gone, or lost its Session; normal completion does not stop children, and links without a UID proof fail closed.
- **Chat Sessions are pinned by epoch** (`controller.DelegationLiveness.requireLiveSessions`): requireLiveSessions refuses when the Task's Session or chat Session is gone or deleting, and requires the chat Session incarnation pinned in orka.ai/chat-session-epoch so a recreated chat cannot keep acting for the requester. It reads metadata only, never the transcript.
- **Chat Session namespace comes from the label** (`controller.DelegationLiveness.requireLiveSessions`): requireLiveSessions looks up chat Sessions in orka.ai/chat-session-namespace because a chat may create Tasks in another namespace while its Session stays in the chat's own.
- **Session deletion intent ends delegation** (`controller.DelegationLiveness.requireNoSessionCleanup`): requireNoSessionCleanup refuses once a Session's deletion records cleanup intent, even though the record stays until runtime retirement, and fails closed when the store cannot tell.
- **Delegated liveness check reads Session metadata only** (`controller delegated Session liveness check`): The Session liveness check that runs before every Delegated identity call must read only Session metadata, never load the Session with its transcript, because it runs on every call and the transcript can be large.
- **Identity checks list Agents and Tasks per namespace** (`controller identity claim listing`): The Helm install grants the controller Agent and Task RBAC only in its watch namespace, so identity-claim and binding checks must list Agents and Tasks with a namespace scope; a cluster-scoped list is forbidden there and would fail every check.
- **An unavailable issuer starts one shared back-off** (`controller.AgentReconciler.issuerBackoff`): When the identity issuer is unavailable, the first Agent verification starts a back-off shared by all Agents, so the rest of the reconcile queue is not each held for an issuer timeout, and every Agent reports the issuer unavailable rather than its identity refused. A definitive refusal is the identity's own answer and starts no back-off. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **An issuer refusal stands until a fresh verification** (`controller.AgentReconciler identity verification`): Once the issuer refuses an Agent's identity, the refusal recorded in the Agent's status stands across later invalid edits, issuer outages, controller restarts, and leadership changes; only a new successful verification clears it, so none of these turn a refused identity back into a merely declared one. A refusal recorded for a different identity does not carry over. See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **An unconfirmable identity check retries the prompt** (`controller ACP dispatcher identity check before submission`): When the dispatcher cannot read the Agent's identity claim, or the enforcement period is not yet recorded, the pre-submission identity check returns a retryable error and never fails the Task as IdentityUnavailable. A Planned attempt, or a resubmission after a rate-limited rejection, provably was not accepted, so it returns to Reserved and the Task stays nonterminal until the claim can be read.
- **Test proxy enables full duplex before flushing** (`controller.newBackendExistingWorkFixture proxy`): The backend conformance test proxy enables HTTP full duplex because the upstream may flush acceptance before its transport finishes reading the forwarded body, and HTTP/1 would otherwise close the request body during that flush.
- **Existing-work clients recheck admission before new sessions** (`controller.ACPDispatcher v2 client`): A harness v2 client constructed for existing work must still recheck admission conformance when asked to create a new session; conformance proven for existing prompts does not carry over to new admissions.
- **A timed-out cleanup write may still apply later** (`controller AgentRuntime cleanup`): An API timeout does not prove a submitted write stopped, so AgentRuntime cleanup must stay correct when an old serialized request lands after another controller completed cleanup and a new object owns the same name; the cleanup ownership test replays the exact request to prove it.
- **Refresh the AgentRuntime after a retention fence write** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.Reconcile`): Retention recovery may fence owner metadata through an immutable historical boot snapshot, so AgentRuntimeReconciler.Reconcile re-reads the AgentRuntime to refresh its resourceVersion before updating status, without carrying an observation over to another owner.
- **Pre-conformance cleanup snapshots cannot admit Service work** (`controller agentRuntimeCleanupSnapshot.ServiceBackendDigest`): ServiceBackendDigest is optional for cleanup snapshots created before physical Service conformance was recorded. Such snapshots keep cleanup authority but cannot admit work through a Service until the current backend passes deep conformance.
- **A drain certifies only the boot that answered it** (`controller AgentRuntime drain cleanup proof`): A drain certifies cleanup only for the supervisor boot that answered it. After an epoch rotation a different live boot cannot prove cleanup for Tasks still bound to the frozen boot; only an existing exact receipt can.
- **Cleanup metadata patches carry UID and resourceVersion** (`controller.agentRuntimeCleanupMetadataPatch`): agentRuntimeCleanupMetadataPatch includes the immutable UID and the resourceVersion in the merge patch, because ordinary MergeFrom omits an unchanged UID; a delayed metadata write must neither affect a new object with the same name nor overwrite a newer finalizer set.
- **Poisoned session retry does not remove the finalizer** (`controller.retryPoisonedAgentRuntimeSession`): retryPoisonedAgentRuntimeSession resumes only one failed cleanup after drain has closed admission; active and already-deleting residents keep their current cleanup owners. Even when that exact deletion succeeds, a later authenticated quiescent Status still gates AgentRuntime finalizer removal.
- **Poisoned cleanup uses a stable per-resident DELETE operation** (`controller.retryPoisonedAgentRuntimeSession`): Retrying a poisoned AgentRuntime session uses a stable per-resident operation: failed cleanup records no completed DELETE, and success records it and removes the resident, so repeated polling does not accumulate pool-wide drain operation records.
- **Managed RuntimePool UIDs are reserved from external registrations** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.conflictingManagedRuntimePoolIdentityOwner`): Every managed RuntimePool UID is reserved for that pool regardless of its lifecycle state, so an external AgentRuntime registration can never make shared authorization lookups ambiguous for an existing managed pool.
- **Incumbent AgentRuntime keeps its pool identity while NotReady** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.conflictingAgentRuntimePoolIdentityOwner`): A single published AgentRuntime registration stays the owner of an authenticated pool identity even while NotReady, because active sessions retain its broker authority. If an upgrade left several published owners, stable object identity ordering picks one so reconciling the others repairs the ambiguity.
- **Conformance dials target pinned backend Pods** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler conformance`): Verified Service backend pins are resolved right after the endpoint policy passes, so every AgentRuntime conformance dial targets a proven backend Pod rather than the mutable Service ClusterIP. Non-Service endpoints return no pins and use the public-address dial control instead.
- **Each Service backend Pod is conformance-checked separately** (`controller.checkAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): checkAgentRuntimeServiceBackends checks every pinned backend endpoint under one overall probe deadline, because a pooled HTTP connection to one Pod cannot certify another Pod the Service may select.
- **Each backend check derives its deadline from the caller** (`controller.checkAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): Each backend conformance check derives its timeout from the caller's context rather than a shared shallow deadline, so an identity change discovered near the shallow deadline still gets one bounded lifecycle check.
- **Endpoint-policy reads use the uncached APIReader** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.endpointReader`): AgentRuntime endpoint-policy reads prefer the uncached APIReader so a Service, EndpointSlice, or backend Pod mutated just before dispatch is observed exactly, not at manager-cache latency; dispatch-time revalidation must use an uncached reader.
- **ExternalName Services are refused as AgentRuntime endpoints** (`controller AgentRuntime endpoint validation`): An ExternalName Service is a CNAME alias to an arbitrary hostname and would let a namespace-scoped caller steer conformance traffic, which exempts recognized .svc hostnames from the public-address dial policy, at cross-namespace or internal targets, so AgentRuntime endpoint validation refuses it.
- **AgentRuntime endpoint Services must have a selector** (`controller AgentRuntime endpoint validation`): The same-namespace exemption from the public-address dial policy is justified only when the Service routes to same-namespace Pods Kubernetes selected; a selectorless Service or a manually managed EndpointSlice/Endpoints backend can route the ClusterIP anywhere, so it is refused.
- **External AgentRuntime endpoints refuse non-public IP literals** (`controller AgentRuntime endpoint validation`): A non-Service AgentRuntime endpoint is probed from the controller's privileged network position, so a private, link-local, or otherwise non-public IP literal is refused; otherwise a namespace-scoped caller could name a ClusterIP directly and aim controller traffic at internal addresses.
- **Service backends are proven by the Pod, not the EndpointSlice** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.validateAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): Every EndpointSlice address behind an AgentRuntime endpoint Service must resolve to a live same-namespace Pod the Service selector selects and whose PodIPs contain the address. EndpointSlice fields (managed-by label, TargetRef, Addresses) are writable by a namespace caller, so only the Pod's own status and labels count, checked on every reconcile right before probing.
- **Only the endpoint's Service port is pinned** (`controller.agentRuntimeEndpointServicePort`): The AgentRuntime endpoint resolves to one Service port, the URL port or the scheme default, and only the matching EndpointSlice port is pinned, so a Service exposing extra ports (metrics, sidecars) never receives controller bearer or capability traffic; an endpoint naming a port the Service does not expose is rejected.
- **Target port resolution mirrors Kubernetes** (`controller.agentRuntimeServiceTargetPort`): agentRuntimeServiceTargetPort resolves ServicePort targets as Kubernetes does: numeric targets need no container-port declaration, and named targets search regular containers, then restartable init sidecars.
- **Pod digest hashes only physical identity** (`controller.agentRuntimeServicePodDigest`): agentRuntimeServicePodDigest hashes only physical identity, never resource versions or readiness timestamps, so ordinary EndpointSlice and status refreshes do not trigger AgentRuntime lifecycle mutations. Sidecar and ephemeral container statuses are included because a Service can target them and their process replacement must invalidate the Pod's conformance.
- **Dispatch pins the connection to verified backend IPs** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.verifiedAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): Dispatch pins the authenticated AgentRuntime connection to a verified backend Pod IP, through PinnedBackendDialTransport, because the validate-then-dial TOCTOU cannot be closed while routing through the still-mutable Service ClusterIP; a Service or EndpointSlice swapped after the check cannot reroute the request.
- **Only serving backends enter the pinned set** (`controller.verifiedAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): Every endpoint's advertised address is validated against its Pod, but only a currently serving backend is pinned, because ApplyPinnedBackendDial round-robins the pins without health fallback and an unready, terminating, or deleting Pod would fail dispatch while healthy backends remain.
- **Zero verified backends fails closed** (`controller.verifiedAgentRuntimeServiceBackends`): A Service endpoint with no verified backend for the selected port is denied rather than dialed through the unpinned Service ClusterIP, which .svc endpoints exempt from the public-address control; treating zero pins as not pinned would let a later EndpointSlice divert authenticated status or mutation traffic to an unverified address.
- **Pinnable endpoints need an independently Ready Pod** (`controller.agentRuntimeEndpointPinnable`): agentRuntimeEndpointPinnable tolerates a nil EndpointSlice Ready condition for older controllers but requires the backing Pod to be Ready itself, so publishNotReadyAddresses or a forged slice cannot put an unready Pod in the authenticated dial set.
- **New work needs conformed backends; cleanup does not** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.AgentRuntimeConformedServiceBackendPins`): AgentRuntimeConformedServiceBackendPins additionally requires every current v2 Service backend to belong to the last successful conformance check and is used for new work. Cleanup keeps its original frozen authority and uses AgentRuntimeServiceBackendPins even after the serving backend changes.
- **Conformance dials are public-only except Service DNS** (`controller.agentRuntimeEndpointRequiresPublicDial`): agentRuntimeEndpointRequiresPublicDial restricts conformance dials to public addresses for every endpoint except recognized same-namespace Service DNS forms, which legitimately resolve to cluster-internal addresses, and the loopback test escape hatch.
- **AgentRuntime auth Secrets must not carry surrounding whitespace** (`controller AgentRuntime auth material`): Runtimes that project the AgentRuntime auth Secrets as files trim whitespace before verifying while the controller signs with the raw bytes, so a trailing newline would invalidate every signed capability and leave the runtime permanently unready; the controller refuses such values with a clear message.
- **Bearer token and capability key must be distinct** (`controller AgentRuntime auth material`): The controller bearer token is sent on every request, while the operation capability secret is a signing key that must never transit; identical values would let a bearer holder mint valid status and exact-fence capabilities, so they must resolve to distinct bytes.
- **Auth Secrets are revalidated after conformance** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.requireCurrentAgentRuntimeAuthMaterial`): requireCurrentAgentRuntimeAuthMaterial revalidates both v2 auth Secrets after conformance, reading through the uncached APIReader when available, so readiness fails closed when either Secret was replaced or rotated while the probe ran.
- **Observed capabilities record the registered provider and model** (`controller AgentRuntime observed capabilities`): Conformance proves the registered provider and model are members of the runtime's advertised capability sets, so observed status records exactly that selected identity even when the runtime advertises additional supported values.
- **Auth Secret rotation forces a deep conformance probe** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.Reconcile`): When an AgentRuntime's operation capability Secret rotates, reconciliation forces a deep conformance probe and fails closed if the runtime still holds the old key, rather than leaving the prior Ready status in place.
- **A Foundry replacement boot is only a retirement relay** (`controller.agentRuntimeFoundryRetirement`): After a rollout, the replacement Foundry boot is independently authenticated and revalidated only as a retirement relay; it never inherits the old boot's admission or execution authority, and its observation is kept with the proof but cannot become an admitted boot until the old boot's retirement has committed. Container death alone never proves remote Foundry retirement.
- **Saved Foundry retirement proof validates at its issuance time** (`controller agentRuntimeFoundryRetirement validation`): Durable Foundry retirement proof outlives its transport capability, so a saved retirement request is validated at its exact original IssuedAt rather than required to be unexpired now.
- **Boot retirement commit is epoch-guarded and claims no work** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.persistBootRetirement`): The control store guards both the retirement reservation and the immutable proof commit by the current controller epoch, and committing retirement proof never claims a prompt or an execution result.
- **Test proxy delays EOF until the response flushes** (`controller.runtimeProxyEOFBody`): runtimeProxyEOFBody holds the transport's post-Content-Length EOF check until the downstream response has flushed, reproducing the ordering that once disconnected the recovery fixture's original prompt stream, so tests keep covering that race.
- **A pending boot observation commits before admission** (`controller AgentRuntime recovery boot observation`): A pending external-effect observation of an AgentRuntime boot cannot have admitted work, and its exact response must commit before conformance or public Session creation can start.
- **Termination witness checks topology plus full spec digest** (`controller.witnessedContainerTerminationWithTopology`): witnessedContainerTerminationWithTopology compares against a topology view of the Pod spec that may omit controller-rendered RuntimePool toolbox init containers, while the spec digest still covers the complete Pod spec.
- **Retained boot auth stays until proven retirement** (`controller AgentRuntime retained boot authentication`): A pending AgentRuntime DELETE keeps the original immutable boot authentication Secret, with its cleanup finalizer, until boot retirement is proven; a retained Secret that is not immutable or lost its finalizer is refused.
- **Interrupted boot enrollment resumes before drain** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.resumeRecoveryBootRetention`): A witness owns its boot before retention writes complete, so takeover and deletion resume that interrupted enrollment before attempting drain. Only the originally observed credentials and physical backend may complete it; changed source auth cannot replace missing retained boot authority.
- **Unqualified Foundry boots grant no retirement authority** (`controller AgentRuntime Foundry recovery qualification`): Foundry boots whose runtime does not advertise SupportsFoundryRecovery keep their existing enrollment and live drain contract but cannot grant remote retirement authority.
- **Foundry broker partition must match the registered profile** (`controller AgentRuntime Foundry broker validation`): The Foundry broker ledger must partition sessions under the registered agent configuration digest; a different partition could certify retirement of sessions that never belonged to this runtime profile.
- **Retention preparation is skipped for existing witnesses** (`controller AgentRuntime boot retention preparation`): Recovery reserves a preparation recording the original Pod identity before an ambiguous retention PATCH, but not when a witness already records that identity, because adding a preparation would hide the witness's legacy retention format.
- **No new boot is adopted before older boots retire** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.reconcileKubernetesRuntimeRecovery`): reconcileKubernetesRuntimeRecovery runs before conformance and never adopts a new runtime boot until every older enrolled boot has positive retirement evidence.
- **Session exposure records possible admission immutably** (`controller.agentRuntimeSessionExposure`): agentRuntimeSessionExposure records possible runtime admission, not prompt success. A controller restart may clear a Planned attempt's mutable binding, so this immutable record is what still identifies every runtime boot that could own the Task's work.
- **Persist the process binding before creating the runtime Session** (`controller ACP dispatcher external Session creation`): The dispatcher persists the complete process binding, including the runtime boot, before creating the runtime Session; stamping the boot only after create returns leaves a crash window in which admitted work has no durable identity.
- **Recovery dispatch tests use a one-minute deadline** (`controller agent runtime recovery dispatch tests`): The recovery dispatch tests span enrollment, several full event streams, and takeover; under race instrumentation the event redaction work exceeds the ordinary single-dispatch fixture deadline, so they use a one-minute context timeout.
- **Controller epoch is not original prompt authority** (`controller.verifiedKubernetesRuntimeRetirement`): The Task's mutable controller epoch is deliberately not the original prompt authority: verifiedKubernetesRuntimeRetirement lets the current owner read the original boot's retirement proof after takeover advances the epoch.
- **Pod metadata alone cannot create a recovery owner** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.preparedRecoveryPodWitness`): preparedRecoveryPodWitness reads only an observation whose exact digest was reserved before its atomic Pod retention patch, so Pod metadata alone cannot create a recovery owner or alter the authenticated observation.
- **Pod retention follows preparation publication** (`controller AgentRuntime recovery enrollment`): Recovery enrollment starts Pod retention only after the boot preparation has been published successfully, so every retained Pod has a durable preparation.
- **Discharging a preparation is not retirement proof** (`controller AgentRuntime recovery enrollment`): Abandoning a boot preparation only discharges an unpublished preparation; a committed boot still needs its own independent positive retirement proof.
- **Legacy witness-first retention may fill annotations** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.requireLegacyRecoveryPodRetention`): Older controllers enrolled recovery witness-first with no preparation record. requireLegacyRecoveryPodRetention lets an interrupted rollover fill annotations that format never wrote; an exact retired witness without any preparation distinguishes it from missing metadata on a newly retained Pod.
- **Final owner removal rechecks preparations** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.requireSettledRecoveryPreparations`): requireSettledRecoveryPreparations is read-only and runs inside the final runtime-owner removal guard, so a preparation added after the earlier scan cannot leave a late Pod PATCH able to install an orphaned finalizer.
- **Only unretained unpublished preparations are abandoned** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.requireSettledRecoveryPreparations`): Owner removal may win before a delayed preparation is published and fences every new retention write; only a preparation that is still unretained and unpublished may be abandoned at that point.
- **Recovery envtest listens on a non-loopback interface** (`controller.recoveryAPITestListenAddress`): The real EndpointSlice API rejects loopback addresses, so recoveryAPITestListenAddress listens on a concrete local IPv4 interface, letting enrollment exercise the real HTTP dial pins and unmodified physical identity records.
- **Runtime identity validation precedes old-boot drain** (`controller AgentRuntime retained-secret drain`): Even with retained boot authentication, AgentRuntime recovery validates the runtime's physical identity before any authorized drain of an old boot; a replaced container cannot be drained with the retained credentials.
- **Recovery supervisor Secret references are resolved** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.validateAgentRuntimeRecoveryPodSpec`): validateAgentRuntimeRecoveryPodSpec resolves the supervisor's Secret references in addition to the pure PodSpec check, because disabling ServiceAccount token automount does not stop a legacy service-account-token Secret from being mounted or supplied through an explicit environment variable.
- **Recovery Secret admission inspects only the Secret type** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.validateAgentRuntimeRecoveryPodSpec`): Recovery supervisor Secret admission is decided by the Secret's type alone, and the controller never inspects or exposes the Secret's data.
- **Projected ServiceAccount tokens are refused regardless of automount** (`controller recovery supervisor PodSpec validation`): A projected volume with a ServiceAccountToken source mounted into the recovery supervisor is refused even when the Pod disables automount, because explicit token projections are independent of the automount setting.
- **Topology fixtures place the broker container first** (`controller agent runtime recovery topology tests`): The recovery topology fixtures put the broker container before the supervisor so tests prove recovery patches and selects the supervisor by its configured name rather than by being the first container.
- **Boot witnesses are immutable pre-admission observations** (`controller.agentRuntimeBootWitness`): agentRuntimeBootWitness is an immutable observation made before runtime admission, observing the authenticated boot and the Kubernetes container in one bracketed read; a later endpoint or Pod absence cannot replace this proof.
- **Recovery template digest excludes only the epoch** (`controller.recoveryTemplateDigest`): recoveryTemplateDigest excludes only the controller-owned epoch value, so any image, command, volume, auth source, selector, or security change is a distinct physical authority that recovery cannot silently adopt.
- **Recovery Pods must keep the template's executable configuration** (`controller.validateRecoveryPodTemplate`): validateRecoveryPodTemplate verifies the execution configuration as well as the owner chain: Kubernetes may assign scheduling fields and default the ServiceAccount name, but must not inject or replace executable configuration.
- **Recovery requires exactly one Service backend address** (`controller AgentRuntime recovery backend`): Recovery witnesses retain a single dial address, so recovery rejects Service backends with any other address set before committing a witness that could not later be read or retired.
- **Recovery binds EndpointSlice UIDs** (`controller AgentRuntime recovery backend`): Recovery binds the exact EndpointSlice UIDs behind the runtime Service, which is deliberately stricter than the portable dialing policy used for ordinary dispatch.
- **Fence the owner before retaining a recovery Pod** (`controller.AgentRuntimeReconciler.fenceRecoveryPodRetention`): A timed-out owner-removal PATCH may still be in flight after its epoch guard returns, so recovery changes the owner's resourceVersion before retaining the Pod; either this write or the original removal must then conflict.
- **AgentHost uses the uncached reader for absence and cleanup** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.APIReader`): AgentHostReconciler reads through the uncached APIReader to confirm that an object it created is really gone, since the cache may lag a recent create, and to clean up in a host namespace the manager cache may no longer cover.
- **AgentHost finalizer and namespace are persisted first** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.Reconcile`): AgentHostReconciler persists its finalizer and the host namespace in status before creating any object, so cleanup always knows where to look.
- **A wake request counts only once its time has come** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.Reconcile`): An AgentHost wake request counts only once its time has come, allowing agentHostWakeClockSkew of one minute, so it wakes the host at most once; a request dated in the future would otherwise be later than every idle suspension until that date and defeat them all.
- **Connection link check gates waking but not suspension** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.Reconcile`): A host fed by a Connection stops when its link does, suspended or not, so waking never bypasses the check. A link check that could not run still lets a suspension proceed, because stopping needs no link; a running host in that state retries instead. See [ADR 0036](0036-agent-hosts-from-connections.md).
- **Suspension proceeds without template or token** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.suspend`): AgentHost suspension needs nothing but the Pod's receipt, so it is honored even when the host's template or token is gone.
- **Host NetworkPolicy is re-synced to current controller labels** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.Reconcile`): The controller's namespace or Pod labels may change after a host's NetworkPolicy was created, so reconcile updates the policy to the current desired rules; without them the controller cannot read the host's status.
- **Connection-fed hosts requeue before token refresh** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.Reconcile`): A host fed by a Connection requeues before its token is due for refresh and at least every agentHostResync, so a token rotation whose watch notification was lost is still noticed.
- **Token grant observation is persisted after delivery** (`controller.agentHostToken`): The grant observation carried by agentHostToken is persisted only after the token has been delivered and the host Pod reconciled, so status never records a grant the host did not receive.
- **AgentHost token sources rely on admission checks** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.githubToken`): AgentHostReconciler.githubToken trusts admission to have checked that whoever set githubTokenSecretRef may read that Secret and that only the controller sets githubTokenFrom; the reconciler reads either source with its own privileges.
- **A host decline is checked before the shared link check** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.checkHostLink`): A person who declines a host after it was provisioned stops it, as the provisioner never starts one for such a link. The link still serves their other uses, so the decline check is the host's own, and it runs first because it needs nothing the shared link check may be waiting on.
- **The first Connection token pins the host's GitHub account** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.connectionToken`): A token an AgentHost does not hold yet must belong to the GitHub account the host's volume belongs to; the first token pins that account before it reaches the host, so a re-linked Connection cannot serve the volume to another account.
- **Hosts get only tokens with a GitHub-set expiry** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.connectionToken`): Code in an AgentHost can read its token, so the host gets only a token that expires and a leaked copy stops working on its own. Only a grant that credential custody can refresh carries an expiry GitHub set; a token Orka was handed without one holds only Orka's own deadline and is refused. See [ADR 0036](0036-agent-hosts-from-connections.md).
- **A lost connection token is reminted with a new Pod** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.ensureConnectionToken`): ensureConnectionToken creates the host's connection token once; if its Secret is deleted, a new token is minted and the Pod replaced so the host and its clients agree on it again.
- **A token from a new source waits for the old Pod to stop** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.retirePodOfAnotherToken`): The host's token copy is mounted into the running Pod and follows its Secret, so a GitHub token from a new Secret, key, or Connection is written only once no Pod started with the old source remains; otherwise the old Pod's processes could read it before they stop.
