- **API server refusals are reported, not mirrored** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.ensureObject`): AgentHost object creation does not mirror the API server's own rules (RuntimeClasses, huge pages, Pod Security, quotas, admission policies); an Invalid or Forbidden response is reported as ObjectRejected with the server's message and the next resync retries.
- **Standalone connection Secret owner reference is a fallback** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.reconcileConnectionSecret`): The Standalone connection Secret carries a controller reference only as a garbage-collection fallback; the AgentHost finalizer deletes it first under its receipt.
- **A failed host outside the managed namespace is left for deletion** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.stopFailedHost`): stopFailedHost does nothing for a host whose objects live outside the namespace this controller manages; only deletion of the AgentHost cleans them up.
- **Host agent updates wait for idle, bounded by a deadline** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.reconcileAgentUpdate`): The Orka host agent is copied into an AgentHost Pod when it starts, so moving to the controller's current agent means a new Pod and loses a running turn. reconcileAgentUpdate waits until the host reports idle, but no longer than agentHostAgentUpdateDeadline (24h) counted from when the HostAgentUpdatePending condition first became True, so a host that never reports idle or never becomes ready still moves.
- **Any terminated regular host container fails the Pod** (`controller AgentHost Pod failure detection`): AgentHost Pods use restartPolicy Never, so nothing restarts a regular container that exits: the host leaves the Pod running only until its sidecar stops and a template helper leaves it running but never Ready. The controller treats any terminated regular container as failure without waiting; the Orka sidecar is an init container and restarts on its own.
- **Host NetworkPolicies are watched for immediate repair** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.SetupWithManager`): A host's NetworkPolicy is its confinement, so AgentHostReconciler watches NetworkPolicies and repairs a deleted or edited one at once rather than at the next resync.
- **ConnectorProvider changes reach fed hosts directly** (`controller.AgentHostReconciler.connectionFedHosts`): A ConnectorProvider that stops serving hosts or no longer authorizes their links leaves its Connections' status unchanged, so AgentHostReconciler watches providers and enqueues every Connection-fed host in the namespace; at most one provider there serves hosts, so no finer index is kept.
- **A token from another source replaces the host Pod** (`controller.staleAgentHostPod`): The host daemon reads its GitHub identity from the token once, at start, so staleAgentHostPod replaces the Pod when the token comes from another Secret or key, while a new value in the same Secret and key reaches the running host through its mounted copy without a restart.
- **Only the canonical link provisions a person's host** (`controller.AgentHostProvisioner.Reconcile`): AgentHostProvisioner acts only on a Connection whose name is the canonical connectors.ConnectionName for its provider and subject, so a differently named Connection can neither provision nor identify a person's host.
- **Retained hosts record their provider before the link goes** (`controller.AgentHostProvisioner.recordHostProvider`): AgentHostProvisioner records the full provider name on every retained host, including hosts created before the annotation existed, before their Connection is removed, even when the link or provider no longer qualifies for a host.
- **A link blocked by another host's name requeues** (`controller.AgentHostProvisioner.Reconcile`): When another host already holds a link's name, the provisioner requeues after agentHostResync, because deleting that other host enqueues nothing for this link.
- **Provider annotation patch uses an optimistic lock** (`controller.AgentHostProvisioner.recordHostProvider`): recordHostProvider patches with an optimistic lock so it never patches a replacement host of the same name or overwrites concurrent metadata changes.
- **Conflicting host providers provision nothing** (`controller.AgentHostProvisioner.providerConflict`): When more than one ConnectorProvider in a namespace serves agent hosts, none of them provisions and each reports the conflict, so a person's host never depends on which provider reconciled first.
- **Host names map login underscores to double hyphens** (`controller.agentHostNameFor`): agentHostNameFor names a host gh-<login>; managed user logins carry an underscore, which a Kubernetes name cannot, so it becomes a double hyphen, which no GitHub login contains, keeping names valid and unique.
- **The AI soul is a prepared input, not worker environment** (`controller.resolvedAISoul`): resolvedAISoul is prepared by the controller from the Agent's soul source and bound to the Task; it is never taken from a worker-controlled environment value.
- **Memory consolidation runs never bind the Agent's soul** (`controller.memoryConsolidationRun`): A memory consolidation run uses Orka's fixed consolidation prompt and sends none of its Agent's prompts, so resolveAISoul neither reads nor binds the Agent's soul and a missing soul ConfigMap cannot block the run.
- **Soul prompt size is checked on the escaped envelope** (`controller.resolveAISoul`): Kubernetes expands $(VAR) references in EnvVar.Value, so resolveAISoul measures the escaped literal envelope against maxContainerDeliveredPromptBytes before persisting a binding; the worker still receives the original literal bytes.
- **Control-field edits keep the recorded soul binding** (`controller.resolveAISoul`): A control-field edit such as spec.suspend advances the Task generation without changing its execution inputs, so resolveAISoul keeps the recorded soul binding when the inputs digest still matches; an input edit does not match. A binding recorded before the inputs digest existed keeps the exact generation check.
- **A started Task cannot acquire its first soul** (`controller.validateAISoulIntroduction`): validateAISoulIntroduction lets a Task that never had a soul keep running without one, but refuses an already-started Task (attempts, start time, Job, or iteration recorded) that would acquire its first persona on retry or iteration.
- **Session soul is pinned before Job creation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.pinAISoulSession`): pinAISoulSession pins the soul digest to the Session before the Job is created, so later transcript, result, or lock-release failures cannot make a used Session look unestablished or let its next Task change persona.
- **Gateway Sessions skip controller soul pinning** (`controller.TaskReconciler.pinAISoulSession`): pinAISoulSession does not pin a soul for Gateway Sessions because the canonical Gateway projection owns their soul revision and lock.
- **Only invalid souls or pin mismatches fail a Task** (`controller.isPermanentAISoulConfigurationError`): isPermanentAISoulConfigurationError treats only invalid soul declarations and pinned-identity mismatches as terminal for a Task; reader, Session-store, and status-write failures stay ordinary reconciliation errors that retry.
- **ACP built-in links come only from the frozen snapshot** (`controller.linkedBuiltinAccounts`): linkedBuiltinAccounts resolves a requester's linked account for catalog built-in tools the controller runs for an ACP Task using only bindings frozen into the Task's execution snapshot. A built-in with no frozen link keeps the Task's own credentials, and a frozen link that cannot be used now fails the call rather than falling back.
- **Link-only built-ins refuse calls without a frozen link** (`controller.linkedBuiltinAccounts.required`): When linkedBuiltinAccounts.required is set, a catalog built-in with no frozen link is refused instead of reported unbound, because the broker offers such tools only through the link and a call without one must never run on other credentials.
- **The catalog timeout rides with the built-in binding** (`controller.linkedBuiltinAccounts.BuiltinToolCredential`): BuiltinToolCredential puts the catalog's per-tool timeout into the ToolBinding so the credential source refreshes any token that would expire before the call could finish, regardless of the caller's own polling arguments.
- **Refusals are copied before adding the provider** (`controller.linkedBuiltinAccounts.BuiltinToolCredential`): BuiltinToolCredential names the provider on a link refusal so the agent can tell the person how to relink, but describes a copy because the credential source may share the error value among callers.
- **Live linked built-ins refuse write-class tools** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts`): liveLinkedAccounts resolves a signed-in person's linked account for built-ins the API runs directly (chat and the compatibility proxies), reading the Connection live because there is no dispatch snapshot. Only a never-linked built-in keeps the tool's own credential path; an unusable existing link fails the call, and so does any write-class built-in, because these surfaces have no approval gate and linked writes are available only through a dispatched Task.
- **Live link resolution reads points fresh and scans from cache** (`controller.LiveLinkedAccountsWithCache`): LiveLinkedAccountsWithCache serves the person's Connection point reads fresh from the API reader and namespace-wide scans (providers, the authoritative Connection listing) from the cache, so a call with no link never lists every Connection against the API server.
- **An unaccepted declaring provider still binds the link** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.unacceptedProviderLink`): A provider that declares a built-in but is not accepted right now (its spec changed and conditions lag) still owns the person's link; that link is bound but unusable and never a reason to fall back to other credentials.
- **Live link lookup accepts any owned Connection name** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.BuiltinToolCredential`): The API accepts any owned Connection name and the reconciler adopts Connections created outside it, so live link lookup considers all of a person's links to the declaring provider, not just the canonical name. Exactly one link is used, read fresh for readiness; several are refused outright; none means another provider's link may still keep the call bound.
- **Canonical Connection is read fresh before fallback** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.BuiltinToolCredential`): The cached Connection listing can trail a Connection the API just created, always under the canonical name, so live link lookup reads that name fresh before any fallback and informer lag never looks like an unlinked account.
- **A link that vanished mid-lookup is being removed** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.BuiltinToolCredential`): A Connection listed a moment ago that is gone on the fresh read is treated as being removed, failing the call, rather than as unlinked, which would fall back to other credentials.
- **A withdrawn built-in stays bound to the person's link** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.unacceptedProviderLink`): A GitHub provider that is accepted but no longer declares a built-in still owns the person's link, so dropping a write tool from the catalog never hands the call to operator credentials.
- **Orphaned links stay bound and unusable** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.unacceptedProviderLink`): A link whose provider was removed keeps its tokens until the person disconnects it, and whether that provider declared the tool can no longer be known, so the link is bound and unusable. The lookup uses the authoritative unlabeled listing so a link created outside the API or with its index label stripped still keeps the call bound.
- **A retargeted provider keeps the link bound until consent** (`controller.liveLinkedAccounts.unacceptedProviderLink`): When a provider's authority digest moved since the person consented (endpoints or catalog changed), it may have been the GitHub provider the link was granted for, so the link stays bound and unusable until the person consents again or disconnects.
- **Built-in connector class must match broker effect** (`connectors.BuiltinConnectorToolClass and the brokered GitHub registry`): Every catalog built-in's connector class must agree with the broker registry's effect: a write-class built-in judged read-only would run without the approval its class promises, and a read built-in judged consequential would be ledgered and replay-blocked like a mutation for what is only a fetch.
- **A link changed after planning retries the binding** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnections`): If the requester's link disappears or is narrowed (readWrite to readOnly) between planning the tool policy and freezing it, freezeRequesterConnections returns ErrLinkedBuiltinChanged so binding retries and plans again, rather than freezing a tool no call can use.
- **Native workers never bind linked built-ins** (`controller native connector tool filter and freeze`): The native-worker connector filter and freeze never touch catalog built-ins, because a native worker Pod runs them itself on the Task's own credentials; linked-account binding applies only to tools the ACP broker executes.
- **Linked built-ins bind the authenticated Task** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor`): The linked token for a brokered built-in is scoped by the current Task's workspace, so RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor binds the authenticated Task's name and refuses a tool-context factory that omits it or names another Task.
- **Without an ACP broker nothing runs under a linked account** (`controller.brokeredLinkedBuiltins`): When the controller has no ACP broker registry, as for a harness-v1-only controller, no tool is classified or run as a linked built-in, so GitHub tools in the native registry keep their Task credentials and v1 Tasks that allow them are not refused.
- **Linked repository scope is checked against the verified parent** (`controller.linkedRepositoryScopeInherited`): linkedRepositoryScopeInherited trusts only the exact parent a delegated child records: a replaced parent (same name, other UID), an orphaned child whose owner reference is gone, or a child controlled by a Task other than the recorded parent fails with ErrLinkedRepositoryScope, while a parent that cannot be read is a retryable error, not a verdict. The chain is walked only when a linked built-in is in play.
- **Transaction scopes gate linked connector listing** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor connector listing`): A Task created by a delegated context token carries that token's scopes; with EnforceTransactionCredentialAuth on, a transaction missing the connector-read scope refuses the linked listing, a legacy space-separated Scope is read the same way, and audit mode records but never narrows. An unverified requester without a sealed stamp is treated as nobody.
- **Connection status writes compare against the status as read** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile snapshots the status before any pass mutates it and decides whether to write by comparing against that snapshot, so a change made only to ScopesGranted or by a recovered completion is persisted rather than mistaken for no change.
- **Connection index labels are repaired** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): The API lists a person's Connections by index labels, so ConnectionReconciler repairs missing or stripped labels on Connections created outside the API, and the person's listing never misses a link they own.
- **An impossible provider name is a stable invalid reference** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): A providerRef name that is not a valid DNS-1123 subdomain can never exist, so ConnectionReconciler treats it as NotFound, a stable invalid reference, rather than a read failure retried forever.
- **Committed completions outlive the status write** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): A committed connector completion is the durable record that lets a lost status write be repaired, so ConnectionReconciler deletes it only after the recovered status has been persisted.
- **An expired unrefreshable link stops advertising Ready** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.expireLinkedCredential`): expireLinkedCredential withdraws Ready from a link whose only material is an access token past its expiry that cannot be refreshed. The credential source records the same verdict when a call hits it, but a link nothing calls must not advertise itself as usable forever.
- **Committed completions repair a lost Connection status** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.applyCommittedCompletions`): applyCommittedCompletions finishes a completion whose custody write succeeded but whose status write never did. The committed marker is authoritative: while custody still holds exactly that material the link is recorded from it and the row is removed only after status persists; a completion that custody or the provider no longer matches is dropped at once, while a failed custody read proves no mismatch and keeps the row for the next pass.
- **Recovered completions use the API's authority fence** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.applyCommittedCompletions`): A recovered completion is applied only if its material was issued by the provider's current OAuth client and consented under its current authority (client plus tool destinations), the same fence the API applies to a retried completion; otherwise the link stays Pending until the person consents again. A mode change since the completion does not discard it, since recording it under the current mode projects Pending when scopes fall short.
- **ScopesGranted is Unknown while the provider is unresolved** (`controller.scopesUnknownCondition`): While a Connection's provider is not resolved, scopesUnknownCondition replaces the ScopesGranted verdict with Unknown so the conditions never pair ProviderResolved=False with a stale ScopesGranted=True.
- **ScopesGranted is independent of consent-owned Ready** (`controller.scopesGrantedCondition`): scopesGrantedCondition compares the scopes granted at the last consent with what the current mode and provider require and never touches the consent-owned Ready condition, so widening the mode projects Pending while the read-only consent stays valid, narrowing restores readiness, and a provider requiring more scopes asks for consent again.
- **Tokens from a replaced OAuth client require new consent** (`controller.scopesGrantedCondition`): When the held token belongs to another OAuth client (the provider was replaced, or its client or endpoints changed), the Connection requires consent again and the token must never be refreshed against the new authority.
- **Connection finalize deletes custody before releasing** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): ConnectionReconciler.finalize deletes sealed custody, drops pending consents, and revokes the provider token before removing the finalizer. Custody deletion must succeed first; a provider that refuses revocation does not block disconnect, but missing revocation material (client Secret or key) keeps custody and the finalizer until an operator restores it, because custody is the only copy of the tokens.
- **Custody tombstone commits before revocation** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): Connection finalize commits the custody tombstone first, so a completion that loaded the Connection just before deletion cannot land new material while the provider revocation calls are in flight; otherwise that material would be neither revoked nor kept.
- **Only committed connector material is ever revoked** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): Parked completions nobody committed are deleted and their tokens left to expire, never revoked, because Orka cannot prove whose grant they belong to: a forwarded consent link or a provider re-issuing a long-lived token could make it another person's live credential. revokeTokens revokes only committed credentials, and sends them only to the OAuth revocation authority sealed with them.
- **Disconnect also revokes retired credentials** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeBestEffort`): On disconnect the current grant may already be gone (revoked by the provider and custody shredded), but credentials that later commits replaced were committed by the same owner and may still be live upstream, so revokeBestEffort revokes them regardless. A custody row that cannot be opened is not deleted; the disconnect retries rather than discarding the only copy.
- **Committed completions are never reaped** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.reapExpiredCompletions`): reapExpiredCompletions skips committed completions: applyCommittedCompletions finishes them, because their marker is what lets the link be recorded from material custody already holds.
- **Revocation reads the provider uncached** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): revokeTokens reads the ConnectorProvider uncached, because tokens are disclosed to the endpoint its spec names and an operator's change to the client or revocation URL must be seen even before the informer catches up. revokeTokens reads the ConnectorProvider through the uncached APIReader before disclosing tokens, so an operator who retargets the revocation endpoint and disconnects before the informer catches up never sends tokens to the stale endpoint.
- **Namespace teardown lets tokens expire instead of blocking** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): A missing client Secret keeps a disconnect waiting while the namespace stays, since an operator can restore it even while only the provider is deleted. During namespace teardown the Secret is deleted first and nothing can be recreated, so the tokens are left to expire rather than holding the namespace forever.
- **Client secrets are opaque bytes** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): The provider client secret is treated as opaque bytes: revokeTokens judges only whether it is empty or whitespace and never trims the value it sends.
- **Each token revocation gets its own deadline** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): revokeTokens gives each token its own connectionRevokeTimeout so a stalled first revocation call cannot consume the second's deadline.
- **Connections requeue only at time-driven state changes** (`controller.connectionNextPass`): ConnectionReconciler relies on Connection and provider watches and the manager resync; it requeues only at the next moment state changes with time alone, just past a credential's expiry or the earliest parked completion's expiry, because a fixed periodic requeue per linked account does not scale.
- **Connection state derives from conditions, not prior state** (`controller.projectConnectionState`): projectConnectionState derives the coarse state from conditions: an unresolved provider is Error, otherwise the consent-owned Ready condition decides, qualified by ScopesGranted. Expired and Revoked come from the Ready reason rather than the previously stored state, so a provider outage that briefly projects Error cannot erase them.
- **Completion listings return a copy callers may mutate** (`store.ConnectorConsents.ListConnectorCompletionsForConnection`): ListConnectorCompletionsForConnection returns a fresh slice, not a view of stored rows, because ConnectionReconciler.reapExpiredCompletions deletes completions while iterating the listing. Fakes must copy the same way.
- **Disconnect tombstones custody before provider calls** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): ConnectionReconciler.finalize tombstones connector custody before any revocation call, so a completion that loaded the Connection just before deletion cannot commit new material while the slow provider calls run; such material would be neither revoked nor kept. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Failed completion deletes schedule their own retry** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.reapExpiredCompletions`): The Connection controller has no periodic requeue. When reapExpiredCompletions cannot delete an expired completion it returns a deadline connectionRevocationRetry away, so the failure itself schedules the next pass.
- **Tokens are revoked only at the authority sealed with them** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): When a ConnectorProvider is replaced under the same name or its OAuth client changes, disconnect deletes custody but revokeTokens never sends the stored tokens to the new revocation endpoint; tokens go only to the authority sealed with them. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Disconnect revokes only committed credentials** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.revokeTokens`): Disconnect revokes the committed credential, while parked completions nobody committed are deleted unrevoked and left to expire. Such a token may be another person's live grant (a forwarded consent link or a provider re-issuing a long-lived token), so Orka must not revoke it. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Committed completions repair a lost status write** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.applyCommittedCompletions`): A completion whose custody write succeeded but whose status write was lost is finished by applyCommittedCompletions: the controller records the link from the committed material and then removes the row. The row stays when the status write fails, because it is the durable record the next pass repairs from; a committed row that custody no longer matches is only removed.
- **Committed completions under a changed authority are dropped** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.applyCommittedCompletions`): A committed completion consented under an authority the provider no longer has, such as a retargeted tool destination, is dropped without linking, the same fence the API applies to a retried completion.
- **Expired refreshless links withdraw Ready** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.expireLinkedCredential`): When a linked access token has no refresh token and has expired, expireLinkedCredential withdraws Ready instead of advertising an account the agent cannot use.
- **Recovered status is written before the completion row is removed** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): On an already settled Connection, a recovered completion is the only status change. Reconcile must write that status before deleting the completion row, or a lost write leaves the link Pending forever.
- **Revocation authority covers client and revocation endpoint only** (`connectors.ProviderRevocationDigest`): The revocation digest covers the OAuth client and revocation endpoint but not the token endpoint, so an operator who moves only the token endpoint can still disconnect with revocation; a changed client cannot.
- **Custody is retained while revocation material is missing** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): Custody is the only copy of the tokens. When the provider's client Secret is gone, finalize keeps custody and the finalizer, requeues after connectionRevocationRetry, and revokes and finishes once the Secret is restored. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Namespace teardown finishes disconnect without revocation material** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): When the client Secret is gone and the ConnectorProvider itself is being deleted (namespace teardown), nothing can restore the revocation material, so finalize finishes the disconnect and leaves the tokens to expire instead of holding the namespace.
- **A deleted provider outside teardown keeps custody** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.finalize`): A ConnectorProvider deleted outside namespace teardown while its client Secret is missing does not release custody: the Secret can still be restored, so custody and the finalizer stay rather than leaving live tokens unrevoked.
- **A ScopesGranted-only change is persisted** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.updateStatus`): The consent owner may already have written Ready=True and state Ready, so a pass whose only change is the ScopesGranted condition must still write status; change detection cannot key on Ready or state alone.
- **Unreadable custody keeps the committed completion** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.applyCommittedCompletions`): A transient custody read failure proves no grant mismatch, so applyCommittedCompletions keeps a committed completion, the only record that can repair a lost status write, for the next pass.
- **Invalid provider references are terminal** (`controller.ConnectionReconciler.Reconcile`): A provider reference no object could have (for example containing a slash) is reported as a stable invalid reference, not a read failure retried forever.
- **Connector tool classification pins the policy object** (`controller.connectorToolInfo`): connectorToolInfo records PolicyUID and PolicyGeneration of the OutboundAccessPolicy it was classified from, so execution can refuse a different policy object or revision than the one dispatch decided against.
- **Linked built-ins carry no policy** (`controller.connectorToolInfo.Builtin`): An Orka built-in tool declared by a ConnectorProvider has no OutboundAccessPolicy; its connectorToolInfo uses the BuiltinConnectionKey as PolicyName and the controller resolves the person's credential for it directly.
- **Built-ins declared by several providers are refused** (`controller.ErrBuiltinToolProviderAmbiguous`): A linked account can serve a built-in tool from only one ConnectorProvider, so when several accepted providers declare the same built-in, builtinToolProvider refuses the configuration with ErrBuiltinToolProviderAmbiguous instead of picking one.
- **Repository scopes use canonical owner/repo** (`controller.taskRepositories`): taskRepositories reduces a Task's workspace repositories to canonical owner/repo pairs (raw value when not a GitHub URL) so repository scope checks compare repositories the same way the GitHub tools do.
- **Delegated children stay within their parent's repositories** (`controller.linkedRepositoryScopeInherited`): A coordination child inherits its parent's verified requester, and delegate_task lets a coordinator name any repository, so linkedRepositoryScopeInherited checks every child's workspace against its parents' repositories up the controller-owner chain to the Task the person created; otherwise the person's linked account could be pointed anywhere by delegating. A Task with no controlling Task is its own root unless the controller-managed parent annotation shows it was delegated, in which case the orphaned child is refused. Read failures are returned so the caller retries.
- **Only the ACP broker classifies linked built-ins** (`controller.connectorScope.builtins`): connectorScope.builtins is set only by paths where the controller executes built-ins (the ACP broker). A native worker runs built-ins itself and never holds a linked account, so for native Tasks built-ins stay on the Task's own credentials.
- **Native connector dispatch identity includes object UIDs** (`controller.NativeConnectorToolDispatchDigest`): NativeConnectorToolDispatchDigest binds the Tool and policy spec digest to both objects' UIDs, so a Tool or policy deleted and recreated with the same spec is refused by a running native Job like a changed one.
- **Classification uses the native worker's real built-in set** (`controller.NativeWorkerToolRegistry`): NativeWorkerToolRegistry is the registry a native AI worker resolves tools against: the default registry, the memory tools every worker registers, and coordination tools when the worker registers them for the Task. A Tool resource shadowed by any of these is never what the worker runs, so it is never classified as connector-backed; nativeBuiltinName placeholders exist only to make those names shadow and are never executed.
- **Proxy pull request tools are not worker built-ins** (`controller.nativeWorkerToolRegistry`): The controller's default registry holds pull request tools only to serve the compatibility proxies; a native worker has them only as coordination tools, so nativeWorkerToolRegistry drops them and adds them back only when coordination applies.
- **Connector dispatch digest covers Tool and policy specs** (`controller.ConnectorToolDispatchDigest`): ConnectorToolDispatchDigest covers everything that shapes a connector-backed call besides the person's Connection: the Tool spec (URL, method, headers, schema) and the connection-mode policy that injects the credential. The Job builder freezes it at dispatch and the controller executes only a definition that still matches.
- **Classification uses the policy's registry** (`controller.classificationRegistry`): classificationRegistry returns the same built-in registry the runtime's tool policy was built against (or the default). Using a different registry would treat a Tool resource shadowed by a built-in in one registry but not the other inconsistently.
- **Freezes retry on a missing connection policy** (`controller.classifyConnectorTools`): classifyConnectorTools treats a Tool whose policy is missing as not connector-backed for plain classification (its execution fails on its own), but with strictPolicies (snapshot freezes and bindings that refuse connector Tools) it returns an error so the caller retries. Otherwise a policy of the same name recreated later in another mode could pass the adapter-change guard through an omitted entry.
- **Linked built-ins need a configured broker registry** (`controller.classifyConnectorTools`): In classifyConnectorTools only a configured broker registry executes linked built-ins; the fallback default registry is used only to decide that a built-in shadows a Tool resource.
- **Built-ins win over same-named Tool resources** (`controller.classifyConnectorTools`): A built-in tool wins over a Tool resource of the same name in every runtime, so classifyConnectorTools never treats such a Tool resource as the implementation.
- **No broker, no linked built-ins** (`controller.brokeredLinkedBuiltins`): brokeredLinkedBuiltins returns nothing without a broker registry: when the ACP broker is not configured nothing runs under a linked account, so the native registry's GitHub tools keep their Task-credential path.
- **Child requester seals are fenced on resourceVersion** (`controller.SealChildRequesterStamp`): SealChildRequesterStamp seals a child only when the parent has a valid stamp, the child is controller-owned by the parent, and the requesters match. The stamp and source annotation are written in one patch fenced on the child's resourceVersion, so a requester swapped between validation and sealing makes the write conflict instead of being signed.
- **Brokered child seals retry transient failures** (`controller.ACPChildTaskSealer`): ACPChildTaskSealer seals children the controller creates for brokered coordination tools and scheduled runs directly against the parent Task. Nothing repairs a seal later and an unsealed child fails closed for connector tools, so transient read or patch failures and resourceVersion conflicts are retried a bounded number of times; a refusal is final.
- **No stamp key or requester means no seal** (`controller.ACPChildTaskSealer`): ACPChildTaskSealer does nothing when the requester stamp key is absent or too short or the child has no requester, matching the API and worker sealers.
- **Undecodable frozen bindings fail closed** (`controller.FrozenConnectionBindingsFromJob`): FrozenConnectionBindingsFromJob returns an error for a Job whose frozen Connection bindings cannot be decoded rather than yielding an empty binding set.
- **No stamp key, no trusted requester** (`controller.requesterStampKey`): requesterStampKey verifies the stamp the API seals onto Tasks it created for a verified person; without the key no requester is ever trusted for connector use.
- **Unsealed stamps are pending for two minutes** (`controller.requesterStampGrace`): The API seals a requester stamp in a second write after create, so for requesterStampGrace (2 minutes) after creation an unsealed stamp is transient and dispatch retries instead of committing a write-once binding with no Connections; after it, the stamp is treated as unverified.
- **Seal-pending applies only when a seal can arrive** (`controller.requesterStampPending`): requesterStampPending covers Tasks the API stamped and coordination children a worker created (its parent's worker asks the controller to seal right after). Without a configured key no seal can arrive, so nothing is pending and dispatch is never delayed.
- **Requester provenance requires a UID-bound stamp** (`controller.requesterProvenanceVerified`): Trusted workers may set spec.requestedBy on Tasks they create, and admission is not retroactive, so neither the field nor the controller-only source annotation proves anything. requesterProvenanceVerified trusts only a stamp the controller key sealed for the Task's own UID; a coordination child is sealed only through its parent's authenticated worker, so an owner reference never lets a child inherit another person's authority.
- **Executors carry only the verified requester** (`controller.verifiedTaskRequester`): verifiedTaskRequester returns the requester only when the controller key sealed it for the Task (a pending stamp counts as none), so a requestedBy claimed by a worker or planted Task never reaches a credential lookup or another person's identity-use records.
- **Ambiguous Connection links bind nothing** (`controller.requesterConnection`): requesterConnection lists the person's links to a provider under any name, because the reconciler adopts Connections created outside the API. Exactly one link binds; several are ambiguous and bind nothing, as the live resolver and list_connections also refuse them.
- **Canonical Connection is read uncached** (`controller.requesterConnection`): A cached listing can trail a Connection the API just created under the canonical name, so requesterConnection reads that name fresh. An object at that name that is not the person's link is ignored rather than allowed to hide the link they do hold.
- **Links are judged against the current provider** (`controller.requesterConnection`): Connection Ready is only the controller's last projection. requesterConnection re-reads the ConnectorProvider and judges the link against it, because an unreconciled provider update (new authority, wider scopes) would make every call fail while the frozen snapshot, which re-consent cannot repair, keeps the stale grant.
- **readOnly links hide connector write tools** (`controller.FilterConnectorToolsForRequester`): FilterConnectorToolsForRequester hides write-class tools behind a connection-mode policy when the requester's Connection to that provider is readOnly; connector write tools that remain visible default into the approval-required set.
- **Brokered built-ins follow connector visibility rules** (`controller.FilterBrokeredConnectorToolsForRequester`): Catalog built-ins that a ConnectorProvider declares and the controller broker executes follow the same readOnly and approval rules as Tools behind connection-mode policies whenever the requester holds a Ready link to that provider.
- **Brokered built-ins without a link are hidden** (`controller.filterConnectorToolsForRequester`): A catalog built-in the broker could execute reaches the requester only through a linked account, so without one it is hidden rather than run on other credentials.
- **Classification drift is checked per tool** (`controller.connectorClassificationUnchanged`): connectorClassificationUnchanged compares the planning and freeze classifications tool by tool, not by policy: a Tool retargeted onto a connection-mode policy another Tool already uses would otherwise pass on that Tool's entry although its visibility and approval default were decided as a plain tool.
- **Unlinked policy-backed tools stay visible and fail closed** (`controller.filterClassifiedConnectorTools`): A brokered built-in without a Ready link is hidden because the link is its only credential path, while a Tool behind a connection-mode policy stays visible and fails closed at call time, so a policy retargeted later still cannot serve it.
- **Native dispatch uses one classification and frozen modes** (`controller.nativeConnectorDispatch`): nativeConnectorDispatch classifies a native Job's tools once and derives visibility, approvals, and dispatch digests from it. With frozen bindings, link modes come from the bindings rather than a second Connection read, and a connector tool with no binding for the same policy object is drift: the build fails and dispatch retries instead of mixing revisions in one Job.
- **Frozen connection policies must still classify as connector** (`controller.nativeConnectorDispatch`): A policy frozen as connection-backed must still classify as one at Job build; one that left connection mode, or a Tool retargeted off it, would otherwise run as a plain local tool with no controller route and no connector approval default.
- **Native Job build compares each tool to the freeze** (`controller.nativeConnectorDispatch`): nativeConnectorDispatch compares every tool's digest against the freeze: a Tool retargeted off a policy another Tool still uses would run as a plain local tool, and one retargeted onto a frozen policy would reach the person's credential undecided.
- **Freeze records only Ready links and never partial views** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnectionsForTools`): freezeRequesterConnectionsForTools records which requester Connection was Ready for each reachable connection-mode policy. A missing or unready Connection is not frozen so later calls fail closed, and read failures are returned so the caller retries rather than freezing a partial view.
- **Linked built-ins freeze only with a usable link** (`controller.freezeClassifiedRequesterConnections`): A built-in is frozen only with a usable link: without one the broker never offers it, and a link made after dispatch is never picked up, so the dispatch-time approval set stays true for the run. Planning already hid built-ins without a usable link, so a link missing or narrowed at freeze time changed between reads and binding retries with ErrLinkedBuiltinChanged.
- **Every reachable connection policy is frozen** (`controller.freezeClassifiedRequesterConnections`): Every connection-mode policy the Task can reach is frozen, with or without a usable link. An entry without a Connection keeps calls failing closed even if the policy is later retargeted to a service credential, because the resolver refuses a frozen policy whose adapter changed.
- **Forwarding write tools share their Tool's binding** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnectionsClassified`): A forwarding write tool is not a Tool of its own; it acts under its forwarding Tool's Connection binding, so the freeze skips it and covers the forwarding Tool.
- **Tools removed mid-binding force a retry** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnectionsClassified`): Every brokered custom descriptor was built from an existing Tool, so freezeRequesterConnectionsClassified re-reads each one and retries binding if it disappeared; otherwise a Tool recreated under a policy in another mode could run without a frozen-policy entry.
- **Frozen bindings carry the provider** (`controller.FrozenConnectionsFromTaskStatus`): FrozenConnectionsFromTaskStatus carries each binding's provider, so a policy retargeted to another provider after dispatch is refused on the native path too.
- **Ready links require a grant sequence** (`controller.connectionReadyFor`): connectionReadyFor requires a positive GrantSequence because a linked Connection always carries the grant that linked it; without one there is nothing to bind the snapshot's authority to.
- **Missing snapshot binds nothing** (`controller.bindFrozenConnections`): bindFrozenConnections hands the executor the requester and frozen bindings from the execution snapshot; without a snapshot store or binding the executor keeps none, so connection-mode policies fail closed.
- **Connector refusals consider only exposed tools** (`controller.connectorCandidateTools`): connectorCandidateTools removes every name the effective MCP policy denies (Task disallowed tools plus extras such as an external runtime's registered policy), so a connector refusal never triggers on a tool the policy would not expose.
- **Only connector-reaching Tasks wait for a seal** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnections`): A Task whose tools reach no connection-mode policy is never held for a pending requester seal; ErrRequesterStampPending applies only when there is something to freeze.
- **Scheduled runs are sealed against their scheduled Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled`): Each run of a scheduled Task the API sealed for a person acts for the same person, so the controller seals the run against the scheduled Task right after creating it; the run's binding neither waits out the grace window nor loses the person's Connections.
- **Built-in and provider-native tools shadow same-named Tool CRs** (`controller.freezeRequesterConnectionsClassified`): A Tool CR sharing a name with a registered built-in or a provider-native runtime tool is never exposed as a brokered custom tool, so it cannot make the Task connector-backed; descriptor precedence decides which implementation runs.
- **Unestablished connector bindings are errors** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ConnectionDigest`): RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.ConnectionDigest returns an error when a binding cannot be established instead of reporting the tool as not connector-backed, because the effect record must carry the digest it promises.
- **Connector write tools are hidden where approvals are unsupported** (`controller.adjustInputsForConnectorTools`): On a runtime without approval support, adjustInputsForConnectorTools hides a connector write tool even behind a readWrite link, rather than exposing it unapproved or failing the session.
- **Missing Tool policies are bounded by a grace period** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): When an Agent's Tool references an OutboundAccessPolicy that does not exist, native dispatch retries shortly while the policy may still be applied, and after the grace period fails the Task naming the policy instead of leaving it Pending with no reason.
- **Provider reconciler never exposes the client secret** (`controller.ConnectorProviderReconciler`): ConnectorProviderReconciler validates OAuth settings, tool declarations, and the client Secret reference, but never reads the client secret value into status or logs.
- **Providers outlive the Connections that reference them** (`controller.ConnectorProviderConnectionsFinalizer`): ConnectorProviderConnectionsFinalizer holds a ConnectorProvider while any Connection in its namespace references it, because the provider's OAuth client is what revokes those tokens; releasing it first would let disconnect delete the only sealed copies without revoking anything. The remaining Connections are named in the Accepted condition. See [ADR 0033](0033-user-connectors.md).
- **Provider release checks references uncached** (`controller.ConnectorProviderReconciler.finalize`): Releasing a provider is irreversible, so ConnectorProviderReconciler.finalize lists referencing Connections through the APIReader rather than the cache, which can trail a Connection created moments ago.
- **Epoch acquisition outlasts an abandoned mutation lease** (`controller.defaultEpochAcquisitionTimeout`): A crashed process can leave a two-minute control-store mutation lease, so defaultEpochAcquisitionTimeout is three minutes: acquisition waits past the lease's expiry instead of weakening its fencing checks.
- **Controller epoch fences every ACP control-store mutation** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager`): ControllerEpochManager acquires one durable epoch after leader election, and every ACP control-store mutation must use CurrentFence so a stale leader cannot commit authoritative state after takeover. It advances each registered storage dataset to the exact Kubernetes-granted epoch, bound to the installation, and opens each contract's write fence once its dataset advanced. Datasets open independently so an artifact store outage at takeover does not hold back state writes; CurrentFence opens with the state contract.
- **Contract fence fields are guarded by storageMu** (`controller.contractFence`): contractFence.datasetID and fence are set when opened becomes true, and all three fields are read and written only under ControllerEpochManager.storageMu.
- **Uncommitted epoch CAS retries reuse the exact payload** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.acquire`): When the epoch compare-and-swap did not commit, the manager keeps the exact pending payload, including its digest and timestamp, so a retry stays idempotent.
- **Contracts on one dataset share one advance** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.openContract`): Several storage contracts can be served by one dataset; datasetAdvances runs a single controller-epoch advance per dataset and every contract on it opens its fence from that one advance.
- **Terminal storage errors at takeover** (`controller.definiteEpochStorageError`): definiteEpochStorageError stops takeover retries for a dataset bound to another installation or holding a newer or conflicting epoch (ErrFenced, which also matches ErrConflict), a binding naming another dataset, an invalid request, an incompatible schema, or a missing installation record; other failures are retried with backoff.
- **Crash recovery waits out an abandoned mutation interlock** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager`): After process death leaves a durable control-store mutation interlock, a new ControllerEpochManager recovers in the same startup by waiting for the lease to expire; it must not steal or renew the abandoned lock.
- **Epoch acquisition keeps retrying without a restart** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager`): An epoch acquisition blocked by an expired-but-present interlock recovers on its next capped retry (defaultEpochCASMaxRetryDelay) without restarting the manager.
- **Acquisition timeout does not bound the acquired epoch** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager`): defaultEpochAcquisitionTimeout bounds only acquisition; once the epoch is acquired the manager keeps running past that deadline.
- **Pre-epoch attachment Leases get a one-time epoch marker** (`controller.deleteWorkspaceOwnedAttachmentLeaseForEpoch`): Attachment Leases created before epoch fencing carry no epoch annotation, so deleteWorkspaceOwnedAttachmentLeaseForEpoch first patches the marker with an optimistic lock. Every new publisher updates the same Lease before writing attachment intent, so the Lease resourceVersion fence closes a concurrent handoff.
- **Attachment bearer text lives only in its Secret** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.Attach`): WorkspaceAttachmentManager.Attach rotates authority and writes attachment intent; the bearer token text exists only in the created Secret and the function's short-lived buffer, which is zeroed, and the result passed to a worker Job carries only references.
- **Attachment revalidates admission at every ownership fence** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.attach`): attach revalidates the caller's admission policy at every ownership fence. The public Attach path always requires a reusable workspace; ACP may also attach its exact materialized workspace before provider compute starts.
- **Detach action binds atomically with the attachment** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.attach`): Caller-supplied annotations such as the attached Task's frozen detach action are written in the same optimistic workspace update that installs the attachment, so no crash window leaves the attachment observable with a stale prior action.
- **Recovered orphan ownership is decided by fences, not task UID** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.recoverOrphanedAttachmentSecret`): An orphaned attachment Secret records the Task attempt that created it, which can differ after an expired Lease transfers to a successor Task. The workspace owner, epoch, renewed Lease fence, and optimistic workspace patch decide which Task may publish the recovered authority.
- **Attachment revocation is two-phase** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.BeginRevocation`): BeginRevocation only clears attachment intent; the provider adapter must revoke the active epoch, and FinalizeRevocation deletes the token Secret and Lease only after provider status no longer reports that epoch as attached.
- **Expired same-Task attachments re-attach in place** (`controller.validateWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt`): validateWorkspaceAttachmentAttempt admits a detached reusable workspace or an expired, fully enforced attachment held by the same Task. The latter is a new attempt: Attach advances the epoch, renews the Lease, and rotates the bearer without opening a detached ownership gap.
- **Failed-attempt cleanup preserves an attached bearer** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.cleanupFailedAttachmentAttempt`): A handed-off orphan Secret can still race the original paused attempt. Once either Task attaches that exact Secret, cleanupFailedAttachmentAttempt must keep the bearer; the workspace's optimistic patch decides which attachment won.
- **Successors adopt next-epoch orphan Secrets** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager.recoverOrphanedAttachmentSecret`): A controller restart after creating the attachment Secret but before the workspace patch leaves an exact next-epoch orphan. Once the old holder's Lease expires, a successor adopts that credential instead of failing forever on AlreadyExists.
- **Workspace class use is checked as the live admission caller** (`controller.WorkspaceClassAuthorizer`): WorkspaceClassAuthorizer requires the AdmissionRequest.UserInfo of the live Kubernetes admission request for its use SubjectAccessReview, never an identity copied from a Task spec.
- **AgentHosts carry only Secrets their creator can read** (`controller.AgentHostTemplateAuthorizer.AuthorizeSecretRead`): AuthorizeSecretRead checks that the admission caller may get the Secret an AgentHost names as its GitHub token source, so a host can only carry a credential its creator could read.
- **Class finalizers change only through optimistic metadata patches** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceClassReconciler.Reconcile`): ExecutionWorkspaceClassReconciler adds and removes its finalizer with a finalizer-only merge patch, never a full Update: an Update re-serializes the user-authored spec through Go types (for example 2m becomes 2m0s), which the CRD's functional-spec immutability rule rejects, leaving a legal class unreconcilable. The patch carries an optimistic lock so a concurrent finalizer write by another controller conflicts and retries instead of being erased by a merge from a stale read.
- **Unsuspendable Substrate Actors are stopped by deletion** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceRequest.TemplateSuspendUnsupported`): Template validation sets TemplateSuspendUnsupported when Substrate cannot suspend an Actor of the template because it snapshots volumes only and mounts no durable directory; such an Actor is stopped by deleting it.
- **Core workspace reconciler stays provider-neutral** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler`): ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler owns generic workspace identity, immutable binding validation, desired lifecycle, and finalization, and never writes provider-native fields or makes provider lifecycle calls itself. See [ADR 0026](0026-acp-class-backed-execution-workspaces.md).
- **Cleanup-only mode installs the finalizer on ACP-owned workspaces** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.Reconcile`): Cleanup-only mode admits nothing new but still installs the cleanup finalizer on ACP-owned workspaces (identified by the protected materialization markers): a workspace created just before the API was disabled would otherwise never gain it, retention would wait forever, and idleTimeout and maxLifetime could never reclaim the workspace and its pool. Generic provider-routed workspaces keep adapter-owned cleanup, because a finalizer no running adapter can settle with StateDeleted would make the object undeletable.
- **Deleted RuntimePool deletes its workspace** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.Reconcile`): Deleting a linked RuntimePool makes the workspace's Deleted state terminal without deleting the object, so the reconciler deletes the workspace too; finalization then frees the deterministic Session name instead of leaving a workspace no Task can attach.
- **Quarantine precedes provider-visible denial** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.reconcileWorkspaceAdmissionDenial`): The reconciler invalidates the protected admitted generation with the quarantine spec update before publishing any provider-visible denial status; the quarantined generation must be observed first, and existing maintenance intents converge idempotently over the same two passes.
- **Missing core admission markers mean new allocations** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.validateWorkspaceBindings`): ExecutionWorkspace and spec.coreAdmission shipped in the same API version, so there is no legacy marker backfill path: a workspace without core admission markers is a new allocation.
- **Core admission records the generation adapters will see** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.patchWorkspaceCoreAdmission`): Updating spec.coreAdmission is itself a spec change, so the API server advances the workspace generation once; core writes AdmittedGeneration as that next generation, the one adapters will observe.
- **External provider disposition excludes core credentials** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.reconcileWorkspaceDeletion`): External providers never held core's credentials: their disposition covers provider resources, while core separately proves the RuntimePool and attachment credentials are gone. The protected materialization markers identify this ownership before the pool publishes its first workload request.
- **Core status patches use an optimistic lock** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceReconciler.patchWorkspaceCondition`): A merge patch replaces the whole conditions array and carries no precondition, so core status patches use MergeFromWithOptimisticLock; without it a stale core write silently erases adapter-owned conditions and RetryOnConflict never sees the race.
- **ACP routing identities are reserved controller names** (`controller.workspaceProviderControllerReserved`): The shared checkpoint policy authorizes the exact registration named by its routing label, which core cannot replace with a hash or annotation. Workspaces and checkpoints labeled with the legacy ACP routing identity are legacy-owned, and legacy registrations under the original route must retire under the previous release, so no provider registration may claim either form as controllerName.
- **Checkpoint scans are skipped only on proven absence** (`controller.ExecutionWorkspaceProviderReconciler`): Cleanup-only installations may serve only the four base workspace APIs, so the provider reconciler skips the checkpoint scan only when discovery returns a no-match error; any other discovery failure is returned.
- **Overlapping bindings reuse the current Gateway's capabilities** (`controller.GatewayBindingReconciler`): Two GatewayBindings overlap only when they name the same GatewayRef, so the reconciler judges a peer binding's programmability with the current Gateway's observed capabilities instead of reading them again.
- **Gateway reply freshness ignores metadata and status changes** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveProvider`): The Task, Agent, and provider tuple a gateway reply Job is planned from is invalidated only by spec changes; an Agent metadata or status update that bumps resourceVersion does not force a replan.
- **Existing gateway Jobs are never replanned** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): Once a gateway reply Job exists, createTaskJob does not replan it, even when the live Agent is unavailable; freshness checks apply only before the Job is frozen.
- **Edited Tasks cannot reuse a stale Agent resolution** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): createTaskJob reads the Task uncached before freezing a gateway reply Job; if the Task was edited since the Agent and provider were resolved, it returns a conflict and leaves the Task Pending instead of using the previous revision's resolution.
- **Gateway readiness is not a planning-time origin check** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): Gateway admission availability does not decide whether a Task has gateway origin at planning time; createTaskJob freezes reply_in_conversation into the Job even while Gateway readiness or capability recovery is pending.
- **Delegated children inherit a bounded parent grant** (`controller.TaskReconciler.inheritGatewayTaskGrant`): inheritGatewayTaskGrant gives a delegated child its parent's frozen gateway grant, bounded by every waiting ancestor's deadline and linked to that parent, and requires Task provenance admission.
- **Children of unbound parents fail permanently** (`controller.TaskReconciler.inheritGatewayTaskGrant`): A parent binds before it runs, so a parent that could delegate but has no agent execution binding (such as a native AI-worker Task) never will; its child's gateway pass fails as a permanent configuration error instead of retrying.
- **Gateway pass reacquisition is idempotent** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.bindGatewayTaskGrant`): A reconstructed executor reacquires the exact gateway pass binding it held, without a new parent tool effect; the gateway refuses conflicting reacquisition, wider children, and account relinking.
- **Cross-namespace children keep the original gateway namespace** (`controller.gatewayPassChain`): A delegated child running in another namespace keeps the original gateway namespace UID in its pass, so cross-namespace delegation never rebinds the conversation's origin.
- **Terminal reply projection leaves child delegation intact** (`gatewayruntime.Service.ProjectTerminals`): Projecting a parent's final reply completes the parent's gateway event but must not consume the separate delegation its waiting child still uses.
- **Cancellation during pass issuance stops the tool send** (`controller.RegistryACPMCPToolExecutor.bindGatewayTaskGrant`): A delegated-task cancellation that lands while a gateway pass is being issued must prevent the tool send that would follow; liveness is checked after issuance.
- **Deletion stops a child pass independently** (`controller.gatewayPassActive`): Deleting a child Task stops its gateway pass even while its parent is live and waiting for it.
- **Gateway pass parents come from protected provenance** (`controller.gatewayPassParent`): gatewayPassParent identifies a cross-namespace parent only by the protected parent UID and namespace annotations, as stoppedAncestor does; a controller owner reference always identifies a local parent.
- **Ancestors keep pass authority only while waiting on that child** (`controller.gatewayPassActive`): gatewayPassActive denies cancelled or deleting Tasks. After a successful turn only an ancestor may retain gateway authority, and only while it is waiting for that exact child UID; the caller itself (child nil) gets no such grace.
- **Gateway pass chains use frozen authority** (`controller.gatewayPassChain`): gatewayPassChain walks only frozen parent references and compares each against live protected provenance; live Agent policies never replace a parent's frozen gateway authority.
- **Continuations cannot anchor a gateway pass chain** (`controller.gatewayPassChain`): A continuation Task has its own UID but no gateway event or child grant, so gatewayPassChain refuses a chain that passes through one.
- **Parked parents retain delegation origin only** (`controller.gatewayPassChain`): At freeze time there is no tool call, and a parent that is not Running (for example parked after a successful turn) retains only its delegation origin, validated with ValidateParentTaskGrantOrigin; tool-scoped grant validation applies only to Running ancestors with a tool call.
- **Gateway credential endpoints come only from operator configuration** (`gatewayruntime credential configuration`): The live Gateway and the frozen snapshot can agree on an attacker-chosen origin, so where gateway credentials are sent is decided only by operator-owned controller configuration (the pinned endpoint file), never by Gateway or snapshot fields.
- **Job names are capped at 63 characters** (`controller.maxJobNameLength`): Kubernetes mirrors Job names into Pod labels such as job-name, which are capped at 63 characters, so maxJobNameLength truncates generated Job names to 63.
- **Validation workers run under a high Task-derived UID** (`controller.applyRepositoryMonitorValidationProcessLimit`): The shipped worker enforces RLIMIT_NPROC, which Linux accounts per real UID. applyRepositoryMonitorValidationProcessLimit gives repository validation Pods a high Task-derived UID so their process accounting is isolated from ordinary worker Pods; a hash collision only shares the same bound and cannot raise it.
- **Validation Pods render the QoS memory-pressure toleration** (`controller.applyRepositoryMonitorValidationDefaultTolerations`): Validation workers use non-BestEffort resources, for which Kubernetes adds a node memory-pressure toleration; the builder renders it explicitly so the admitted Pod still matches the expected spec exactly.
- **Gateway reply eligibility needs an exact durable binding** (`controller.JobBuildOptions.GatewayReplyEligible`): GatewayReplyEligible is set only from an exact durable gateway event bound to the Task UID; nothing else may enable reply_in_conversation for a Job.
- **Jobs carry their own frozen Connection bindings** (`controller.JobBuildOptions.ConnectionBindings`): The requester's Connections frozen for a dispatch are carried on the Job, so recovery and the controller's connector endpoint judge the Job's own bindings, never a later freeze.
- **Connector data is read through the freeze's reader** (`controller.JobBuilder.connectorReader`): When a dispatch froze bindings through an uncached reader, connector visibility and dispatch digests are read through that same reader so one Job never mixes revisions.
- **Task Jobs never retry at the Job level** (`controller.JobBuilder.BuildWithOptions`): Task Jobs set BackoffLimit 0 because retries are owned by the Task controller, not by the Job controller.
- **Agent name labels only for same-namespace Agents** (`controller.JobBuilder.BuildWithOptions`): Policy name selectors can be installed before the Agent exists, so the Job's agent-name label is set only for an Agent in the Task's namespace; the policy namespace then supplies the scope.
- **Validation containers keep the default termination message path** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildContainerWithOptions`): Kubelet mounts a regular file at the termination message path; pointing it at /dev/null replaces the device and the container runtime fails to start, so repository validation containers keep the default path.
- **Default worker memory fits real test suites** (`controller.defaultTaskResourceRequirements`): Default worker resources are sized for real Go, Node, and Python test suites; a 512Mi limit silently OOMKilled workers running go test on medium repositories. Agents and Tasks can still override via spec.resources.
- **Controller-owned env vars override Task env** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): Task-level env vars are appended first and controller-owned values are then forced: AI worker telemetry variables are reserved so workload authors cannot bypass default-off telemetry or redirect GenAI metadata, code_exec sandbox settings are reserved so the backend, image, NetworkPolicy, runtime class, AppArmor profile and limits keep their defaults, provider endpoint settings are reserved so the Provider and Agent alone choose where provider requests go, and identity and approval variables are restored so Task authors cannot spoof execution identity or approval state.
- **code_exec settings are reserved by prefix** (`controller.isReservedAIWorkerCodeExecEnv`): The AI worker reads its code_exec sandbox settings from its own environment, so isReservedAIWorkerCodeExecEnv reserves every name starting with workerenv.CodeExecEnvPrefix, including names added later, rather than listing today's settings.
- **Provider endpoint settings are reserved from Task env** (`controller.isReservedAIWorkerProviderConnectionEnv`): The controller omits provider endpoint settings the Provider and Agent leave unset, and the AI worker and provider SDKs then read them from the environment, so a Task value would choose where the Provider credential is sent. isReservedAIWorkerProviderConnectionEnv reserves them, and reserves fallback provider settings, including the fallback count, by the workerenv.AIFallbackEnvPrefix prefix.
- **Only scalar OTLP settings reach workers** (`controller.JobBuilder.addTelemetryEnvVars`): addTelemetryEnvVars copies only non-secret scalar OTLP settings: header variables carry credentials, and certificate variables are file paths whose files the controller does not mount into worker Pods.
- **Container Task env sources need the direct-mount opt-in** (`controller.JobBuilder.validateContainerTaskEnvSources`): The kubelet resolves secretKeyRef and configMapKeyRef with node credentials, so passing them through would give a container Task any Secret in its namespace that the controller refuses to mount from spec.secretRef. Only sources that read the Pod's own metadata are always allowed; every other source, including ones added to the API later, requires the direct Secret mount opt-in.
- **Provider CRD type is authoritative** (`controller.resolveAIConfig`): When a Provider CRD is resolved via providerRef its type wins; model.provider and the Task AI provider are only hints used when no Provider CRD exists.
- **The worker container name is a controller contract** (`controller.workerContainerName`): The controller reads a Job's frozen dispatch policy from the environment of the container named worker, so the native worker container must keep that name.
- **Connector tool resolution failures are transient** (`controller.ErrConnectorToolResolution`): ErrConnectorToolResolution marks a Job build that could not determine which tools are connector-backed; the Task controller requeues instead of failing the Task.
- **Model settings are reserved even when omitted** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): addAIEnvVars sets every model configuration variable, including the Azure deployment name, even when empty, so Task env and Agent Secret EnvFrom cannot supply values outside the Agent's model configuration or override Provider routing and the logical model default.
- **Connector write tools fail the build on read errors** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): Connector-backed write tools are hidden from readOnly links and otherwise always require approval. A classification read failure fails the Job build so dispatch retries, because starting a worker with a write tool advertised but missing from the approval set would let it run without approval. Visibility, approvals, and digests come from one classification against the native worker's registry.
- **Only autonomous coordinators see connector write tools** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): Only an autonomous coordination Task can park on an approval and resume to execute the approved call; any other native Task would request approval and finish without it, so such Tasks never receive connector write tools.
- **Memory consolidation runs get no tools or memory** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): A memory consolidation run calls no tools and reads no injected memory; it receives inputs from the controller and submits one structured result. The mode is controller-owned, so Task env cannot start or stop a run. See [ADR 0051](0051-offline-memory-consolidation.md).
- **Tool list and deprecation metadata are controller-owned** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): The AI tool list and deprecated memory tool metadata are always set by the controller, even when empty, so Task env and Agent Secret EnvFrom cannot supply tools or suppress compatibility warnings.
- **Withdrawn tools keep no dispatch digest** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): The controller executes a connector-backed tool only as defined when the worker was dispatched. A write tool withdrawn from the list keeps no digest, so it can never be executed for that Job.
- **Child identity enables the coordination registry** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): When a Task registers coordination tools by child identity without coordination configured, the coordination registry is still enabled so explicitly selected coordination tools resolve to platform implementations rather than Tool CRs.
- **Unresolvable fallbacks clear their deployment slot** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): Every configured fallback slot has its Azure deployment variable reset before resolution, so an unresolvable fallback cannot retain a spoofed deployment from Task env.
- **Child TxToken is mounted for every workload container** (`controller.JobBuilder.addTransactionTokenSecret`): The child transaction token is delegation authority held in an owner-referenced Secret; it is mounted as a Pod volume and its file path exposed to every workload container so secondary containers can make TTS-mediated calls.
- **Transcript token is projected only into the init container** (`controller.JobBuilder.addSessionVolume`): A short-lived projected token is always mounted only into the trusted fetch-session init container, keeping transcript loading available even when the main Pod disables ServiceAccount token automount.
- **Required transcripts retry through Task startup** (`controller.sessionTranscriptMaxAttempts`): Worker authorization waits for the controller-persisted Job name and UID, so required session transcripts retry (up to 300 attempts, bounded by the Task timeout) through the startup window; this preserves that boundary without a pre-status identity fallback.
- **Workspace init gets only proxy env and patch digest** (`controller.workspaceInitPassthroughEnv`): The workspace preparation init container holds the clone credential, and Git honours variables such as GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS, GIT_ASKPASS, and GIT_SSH_COMMAND that would run a caller-chosen command with it. workspaceInitPassthroughEnv passes only HTTP proxy variables and the prior-Task patch digest a repository mutation Task pins.
- **Only PROMPT.md enters the system prompt** (`controller.JobBuilder.addSkillVolumes`): Of a Skill's files, only PROMPT.md is injected into the model system prompt; other files are mounted under /workspace/.skills only.
- **Prompts are capped below MAX_ARG_STRLEN** (`controller.maxContainerDeliveredPromptBytes`): AI worker prompts and system prompts travel as environment variables, and Linux rejects any single environment string over MAX_ARG_STRLEN (128 KiB) with an opaque exec failure. validateContainerDeliveredPromptSize caps them at 110 KiB so the Task fails with an actionable message; it applies only to AI worker Jobs, and it measures the values that actually reach the environment by mirroring resolveAIConfig precedence and resolving ConfigMap-backed system prompts.
- **Repository-less AI tasks get a writable /workspace** (`controller.JobBuilder.Build`): JobBuilder.Build mounts a writable emptyDir at /workspace for AI tasks without a repository because the worker file tools default to /workspace. Files written there are uploaded as task artifacts, so the artifact directory is the workspace root for these tasks.
- **Disabling coordination tool injection keeps the coordination marker** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): Disabling implicit coordination tool injection only stops messaging tools from being exposed to the model; JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars still sets ORKA_COORDINATION_ENABLED for child tasks so the worker keeps its coordination mode.
- **Connector tool read failures fail the Job build** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): A read failure while resolving connector tools makes JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions fail so the Task controller requeues. Starting the worker anyway could advertise a write tool that is missing from the approval set.
- **Jobs carry connector tool spec digests from dispatch** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): The Job carries the spec digest of every dispatched connector tool in ORKA_CONNECTOR_TOOL_DIGESTS so the controller executes each tool only as it was defined at dispatch, never a later edit.
- **Controller owns the connection-bindings env var** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions drops any Task-supplied value for the connection-bindings env var and writes its own frozen bindings, so a Task cannot forge a connection binding.
- **Codex read-only tasks are accepted via kernel-enforced sandbox** (`controller.validateReadOnlyBuiltInAgentRuntime`): validateReadOnlyBuiltInAgentRuntime accepts Codex read-only tasks because they run in Codex's native read-only agent mode with a kernel-enforced read-only sandbox; runtimes without such enforcement are rejected.
- **Prompt size is measured after ConfigMap resolution** (`controller.JobBuilder.validateContainerDeliveredPromptSize`): validateContainerDeliveredPromptSize measures an Agent system prompt after resolving its ConfigMap source, because the resolved text is what gets exported to the container; measuring the reference would miss oversized prompts.
- **Connector dispatch builds from the freeze** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): For native dispatch, connector link modes come from the bindings frozen at dispatch, not a second Connection read. A policy that changed since the freeze fails the build so dispatch retries, and a Tool shadowed by a coordination built-in the worker registers is never classified as connector-backed.
- **Stale frozen connection policy fails the Job build** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): If the frozen OutboundAccessPolicy generation no longer matches, the Job build fails with ErrConnectorToolResolution so dispatch retries instead of mixing policy revisions.
- **Retargeted connector Tool fails the Job build** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): When one of several Tools sharing a frozen policy is retargeted to a plain policy after the freeze, the build fails even though the policy is still classified through the other Tool, because the retargeted Tool would run locally with no connector route or approval default.
- **Policy leaving connection mode fails the Job build** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): If the frozen policy is no longer a connection policy, its tools would run as plain local tools with no connector route or approval default, so the build fails and dispatch re-freezes.
- **AI worker ServiceAccount name comes from the Downward API** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): The AI worker learns its ServiceAccount name from the Downward API and Task env cannot replace it, so a Task cannot make the worker claim another identity.
- **Task env cannot control memory consolidation** (`controller.JobBuilder.buildEnvVarsWithOptions`): Controller-owned memory consolidation env vars (mode, context enablement, AI tools) override Task env, so Task env cannot start, stop, or widen a consolidation run.
