- **Unfinished consolidation runs leave no transcripts** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseMemoryConsolidationRun`): When a memory consolidation Task ends or is deleted before finishing its run, TaskReconciler.releaseMemoryConsolidationRun clears the run's transcripts. Finished runs and unrelated Tasks are left untouched.
- **Task env cannot spoof deprecated memory tool markers** (`controller.JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars`): JobBuilder.addAIEnvVars overrides any Task-supplied ORKA deprecated-memory-tools value, so user env cannot hide deprecation warnings or falsely mark a tool as opted in.
- **OpenCode validation applies to the effective classified Agent** (`controller.ValidateOpenCodeAgentSpec`): ValidateOpenCodeAgentSpec validates Agents classified orka.harness.v2. An Agent that omits the runtime selector is first classified by the controller's execution mode before planning, and these rules are then applied to that effective Agent.
- **Policies can never request tokens for the controller ServiceAccount** (`controller.OutboundAccessPolicyReconciler.ReservedServiceAccount`): OutboundAccessPolicyReconciler.ReservedServiceAccount names the controller's own ServiceAccount; no OutboundAccessPolicy that requests tokens for it is accepted or granted TokenRequest RBAC, so a tenant policy cannot borrow controller identity.
- **The AI worker's own ServiceAccount never gets a TokenRequest grant** (`controller.OutboundAccessPolicyReconciler`): A TokenRequest for the AI worker's ServiceAccount can bind the token to any Pod that runs as it, including another Task's worker Pod, so the reconciler never grants TokenRequest RBAC for that account, refusing it by configured name even when it is externally managed and unlabeled, and revokes a grant left from earlier releases.
- **Stale TokenRequest grants are revoked only after the desired one is ready** (`controller.revokeStaleOutboundTokenRequestObjects`): revokeStaleOutboundTokenRequestObjects deletes policy-controlled grant objects other than the desired one only once the desired one is ready, so access is not interrupted, and it never touches objects the policy does not control.
- **Substrate MCP Tools reject outboundAccessPolicyRef** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPAuth`): Substrate MCP actors are reached only through the Orka MCP proxy, so ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPAuth rejects outboundAccessPolicyRef on them regardless of endpoint: a caller-resolved outbound credential or gateway route can never apply.
- **Controller rejection revokes remote MCP acceptance** (`controller.ToolReconciler.Reconcile`): Admission keeps accepting schemaless remote MCP Tools for compatibility, so when ToolReconciler later rejects one it must revoke the Accepted condition rather than leave a stale acceptance.
- **Actor ownership is recorded before health status** (`controller.ToolReconciler.Reconcile`): For actor-backed Tools, ToolReconciler.Reconcile records actor ownership in one pass before the first health or status update, so availability appears only on a later reconcile.
- **Temporary artifact storage refusals are retried, not blocked** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): When the artifact store has not yet accepted an implementation's patch artifacts (for example while the controller's storage fence is closed), the RepositoryMonitor retries instead of recording the item as blocked, because a blocked record skips the action permanently. A delivered implementation whose summary artifact the store has not accepted yet is retried rather than recorded as blocked, so a temporary storage refusal never permanently skips the delivery.
- **Automatic repair authorization comes from the administrator** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): Repair.Enabled is the repository administrator's authorization for bounded automatic repair, and a controller policy action is never recorded as a human. Repair labels narrow repair to labelled pull requests, and tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair re-checks the account that added the label before each new repair.
- **Repair labels run a review-fix loop** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): A repair label runs a review-fix loop: each head is reviewed, its findings are fixed first, and CI is fixed only once the review has nothing left to fix.
- **Only a fresh review of the head drives repair** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): Review-first repair requires that the review is current for the head (repositoryMonitorReviewedHeadFresh); a stale or pre-validation review of the head is not a reason to change it.
- **requireGreenCI repairs fix CI before review** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): When Review.RequireGreenCI holds the review until CI passes, waiting for the review would never end, so tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair fixes the CI the pull request broke first.
- **Repair attempt bounds count durable attempts** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy`): repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy counts durable attempts, including commands exhausted before a Task was created, before another repair command identity can be created, so the attempt bound cannot be bypassed.
- **Automatic repairs use the durable command queue** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): Automatic repair enqueues through the same durable command queue as label and API requests. Executing inline under the inventory run would race command recovery with a different run identity and could reject the already-created Task as a metadata mismatch.
- **Automatic repair intent selection** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorAutomaticRepairIntent`): repositoryMonitorAutomaticRepairIntent picks one repair for the head or none: a full repair of a behind head updates the branch through GitHub's update-branch API, while a conflicted (dirty) head cannot be updated by GitHub and needs a human; otherwise a repair label fixes review findings before CI and automatic repair fixes CI first, and ciFirst allows only a CI fix for a head whose review waits for green CI.
- **Exhaustive review passes finish before repair moves the head** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorAutomaticRepairIntent`): No automatic repair is chosen while an exhaustive review of the current head still has pending passes, because the repair would move the head out from under the review.
- **Workflow polling advances outcomes without resubmitting implements** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.queueRepositoryMonitorWorkflowPoll`): queueRepositoryMonitorWorkflowPoll advances CI and review/repair outcomes even when GitHub emits no new pull_request event, but it never repeatedly submits implement commands.
- **Only pull-request inventory runs throttle workflow polling** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.queueRepositoryMonitorWorkflowPoll`): Only runs that inventory pull requests count toward the workflow poll cooldown, and the run scan is bounded to the cooldown window, including full inventory runs.
- **Exhaustive passes are queued idempotently for review-only monitors** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.queueRepositoryMonitorExhaustivePass`): queueRepositoryMonitorExhaustivePass queues the next exhaustive pass when no workflow poll would, since a review-only monitor otherwise waits for an unrelated event or schedule. The run ID is derived from the review record so repeated ingestion queues it once.
- **Repair refusal does not block review selection** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.refuseRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): A repair refusal starts no work, so a reviewed pull request whose head has no current review proceeds to review selection instead of repeating the refusal every poll; otherwise the refusal handles the pull request for this poll.
- **Repair refusals are recorded once per head and reason** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.refuseRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): Review selection may overwrite the item's skip reason, so refuseRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair also records the refusal as an event, deduplicated per head and reason rather than every poll.
- **Each same-head repair attempt gets its own command identity** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): A failed repair Task consumes one attempt, and a later attempt on the same head gets a new command identity rather than reusing one that would collide on the legacy SQL unique key.
- **Automerge checks for workflow runs awaiting approval** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCheckCI`): A workflow run held for maintainer approval reports no check run, so reported checks can look pending or green while CI never ran. repositoryMonitorCheckCI therefore also asks whether a run is awaiting approval before treating CI as green.
- **Awaiting-approval check degrades without Actions access** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCIAwaitingApproval`): repositoryMonitorCIAwaitingApproval reports whether GitHub holds a workflow run for the SHA until a maintainer approves it, as for fork pull requests. A token without Actions access cannot tell and reports false.
- **Rate-limit 403s are errors, not missing Actions access** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCIAwaitingApproval`): GitHub also uses 403 for rate limiting, which says nothing about Actions access, so repositoryMonitorCIAwaitingApproval falls back to false only on 404 or a non-rate-limit 403 and returns other errors.
- **CI evaluation reads both check runs and commit statuses** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCheckReportedCI`): Check runs and commit statuses come from different CI systems, so repositoryMonitorCheckReportedCI reads both; a passing check-run set never hides a failing status context.
- **Combined status is recomputed without Orka's own contexts** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCheckCommitStatus`): GitHub's combined status is paginated, holds only the latest status per context, and its aggregate includes Orka's own status, so the controller recomputes the result after excluding audited IDs instead of trusting the aggregate state.
- **CI evidence is untrusted, sanitized, and bounded** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIEvidence`): CI evidence gives a CI repair the failing checks' diagnostics (check output, annotations, commit status descriptions) for the exact head. It is untrusted CI output, so it is sanitized and bounded before entering the repair prompt, and a failed lookup degrades to the check names plus a reason instead of failing the repair.
- **CI evidence availability values** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceAvailable`): Evidence availability values form part of the orka.prRepair.ciEvidence.v1 schema: available means complete, truncated names bounded fields, unavailable carries a reason, rate_limited means GitHub throttled the lookup, unsupported means the source has no such detail (commit statuses have no output or annotations), and stale means GitHub reported the check for another commit.
- **CI evidence limit units** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIEvidenceMaxChecks`): CI evidence text limits are UTF-8 bytes measured after sanitizing, while encoded limits are bytes of an entry as the repair prompt's indented JSON encodes it, measured by repositoryMonitorEvidenceEncodedSize.
- **Evidence size accounting mirrors the prompt's JSON layout** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIEvidenceEntryPrefix`): The repair prompt encodes its payload with json.MarshalIndent(payload, "", " "), placing each failingCheckEvidence.checks or pendingChecks entry three levels deep. repositoryMonitorCIEvidenceEntryPrefix and the separator size mirror that layout, so changing the prompt encoding requires changing these constants.
- **CI free text is opt-in** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIEvidence.TextOmitted`): Unless the monitor opts in with spec.repair.ciEvidence.text, check output, annotation titles and messages, and commit status descriptions are left out of CI evidence and TextOmitted is set.
- **A repair Task's prompt freezes its CI input** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRepairCIInput`): The CI part of an orka.prRepair.input.v1 payload is frozen into the repair Task's prompt; rebuilding the prompt for an existing Task reuses the input parsed back from that prompt rather than re-reading CI.
- **Building CI repair input never fails** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorRepairCIInput`): buildRepositoryMonitorRepairCIInput never returns an error: a failed lookup yields evidence naming the reason, so a CI repair still starts with whatever is known.
- **CI repair names only the failures the head introduced** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorRepairCIInput`): The CI input names the checks to fix separately from checks that also fail on the base, so the repairer leaves inherited failures alone.
- **Annotation reads share one budget and stop at rate limits** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCIEvidenceFor`): repositoryMonitorCIEvidenceFor gives all annotation reads for one head a single time budget and stops at the first rate limit; each entry records what it could not include.
- **Evidence entry fitting drop order** (`controller.fitRepositoryMonitorCheckEvidence`): fitRepositoryMonitorCheckEvidence keeps an entry within its encoded byte budget by dropping annotations from the least severe end, then the summary, title, and URL, then shortening the description and finally the name; the caller omits an entry that still does not fit.
- **Evidence URLs are dropped whole, never shortened** (`controller.fitRepositoryMonitorCheckEvidence`): fitRepositoryMonitorCheckEvidence drops an evidence URL entirely instead of truncating it, because a shortened link would point somewhere else.
- **Evidence read failures are classified without error text** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceErrorAvailability`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceErrorAvailability classifies a failed GitHub read into an availability and reason without copying its error text, which can carry URLs or response bodies, into the repair prompt.
- **Evidence URLs drop credentials, query, and fragment** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceURL`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceURL keeps only http(s) links without userinfo, query, or fragment, since signed URLs carry their signature in the query string.
- **Only monitored-repo GitHub check links survive** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceGitHubCheckURL`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceGitHubCheckURL keeps a link only when it is a github.com check run or Actions page of the monitored repository whose path holds only the repository and numeric IDs. Other links, such as an external CI target_url, are dropped whole because their path can carry a credential no check recognizes.
- **CI text sanitization order** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines strips ANSI escapes, then control and format runes, redacts credentials per line, and withholds lines that only look like a credential once joined. Raw text past the limit is cut at a line or word boundary first so no partial credential survives the cut.
- **Joined-secret checks use unredacted lines** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines`): Redaction can replace part of a credential wrapped across lines and hide the rest from the joined check, so repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines runs the joined check on the stripped but unredacted lines.
- **Line count mismatch after redaction withholds everything** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines`): Redaction must never join or split lines; if the redacted and original line counts differ, repositoryMonitorEvidenceSanitizedLines keeps nothing but a redacted marker.
- **Wrapped credentials in CI text are withheld through their continuation chain** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdJoinedSecrets`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdJoinedSecrets withholds lines that look like credentials, PEM blocks, multi-line credential values, and credentials wrapped across line breaks. A continuation chain runs from a line's last word through single-word lines (a trailing backslash always continues); up to repositoryMonitorEvidenceSecretJoinBytes of joined words are checked after each line's own text, and a joined credential withholds the line through the chain's end. A line ending inside a credential header value withholds its chain unchecked, checks are capped by repositoryMonitorEvidenceSecretCheckBudget beyond which chains are withheld unchecked, unredacted lines are joined, and a credential followed by another word is withheld because it cannot be told from a longer one.
- **Joined-secret check budget fails closed** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdJoinedSecrets`): Text split into many tiny lines would make the joined-secret check quadratic, so past repositoryMonitorEvidenceSecretCheckBudget chains are withheld unchecked. A credential header whose value starts after the line break is also withheld through its chain unchecked, since the value can be any opaque word.
- **Continuation check targets only joined-only credentials** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceContinuesSecret`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceContinuesSecret reports a credential formed only when a line is joined with its chain's continuation; credentials the continuation forms alone are caught when checking from the next line, and those the line forms alone by per-line redaction.
- **PEM blocks in CI text are withheld whole** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdPEMBlocks`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdPEMBlocks withholds every line from a BEGIN marker through its END marker, or through the last line if unterminated, because PEM bodies are base64 no line check recognizes and their headers break continuation chains. An END before any BEGIN means the text starts inside a block, so earlier lines are withheld too; the scan is linear and independent of the check budget.
- **Detecting an open credential header value** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceOpensHeaderValue`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceOpensHeaderValue treats a line as ending inside a credential header value when, after the last credential header name, it has at most one word (such as Bearer) and no closing quote; a quote opening the value, as in Authorization:"Bearer, does not close it.
- **Escaped line breaks always continue a chain** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceLineWords`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceLineWords treats a trailing backslash as an escaped line break that continues into the next line regardless of surrounding characters, dropping the backslash from the words.
- **Raw CI text limit is a multiple of the kept size** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceRawLimit`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceRawLimit sanitizes a multiple of the kept byte budget (with a floor) so redaction rarely shortens text below the budget while the sanitizer's work stays bounded.
- **CI-evidence credential heuristics withhold whole lines** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceCredentialLine`): The shared redactor recognizes only unambiguous credentials, but CI text also carries credentials that are credential-like only in context (a password option in a shell trace, a registry config auth field, a workflow mask command). The CI-evidence heuristics accept losing some ordinary CI text and withhold the whole line rather than decoding or partly keeping the value.
- **Credential long-option matching rules** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceOptionPattern`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceOptionPattern matches long options whose value is a credential (docker login --password, wget --http-password, vault --token) with the value after = or whitespace; --password-stdin-style options take no value, a quoted value is taken through its closing quote, and docker build --secret is deliberately excluded because it takes a file reference.
- **Short -p password option is case-sensitive** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceShortPasswordPattern`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceShortPasswordPattern matches -p only after login or database client commands (docker login, mysql, sshpass), and the option is case-sensitive because -P is a port.
- **Lines with ::add-mask:: are withheld** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceAddMaskCommand`): A shell trace (set -x) prints a GitHub Actions ::add-mask:: command with its value before the runner masks it, so CI evidence withholds lines carrying that command.
- **Attached option values are always literal** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceOptionValue`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceOptionValue treats a value attached to its option, directly or after =, as literal even if it starts with a hyphen, while a value after whitespace may be the next option.
- **Literal credential values exclude masks and variable references** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceLiteralValue`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceLiteralValue rejects empty values, masks such as ***, and unexpanded references such as $PASSWORD, ${{ secrets.X }}, or %PASSWORD%, but a single-quoted value is literal because a shell trace prints '$PASSWORD' as the value itself.
- **Opaque credential value heuristic keeps ordinary words** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceOpaqueValue`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceOpaqueValue treats a credential field value as a credential only if it is at least minLen bytes after trimming quotes and punctuation and has a digit, a base64 or token symbol, or at least 20 bytes, so ordinary words such as required or disabled are kept.
- **Mixed-case base64 counts as an encoded credential** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceEncodedValue`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceEncodedValue also accepts mixed-case base64 of at least 12 bytes, because encoded credentials need no digit or padding (dXNlcjpzZWNyZXQh encodes user:secret!).
- **Multi-line credential values are tracked across lines and diffs** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker follows credential keys whose continuation lines no check would recognize: YAML block scalars or empty-value keys through deeper-indented lines, and quoted values through the closing quote or at most repositoryMonitorEvidenceQuotedScalarMaxLines lines. Keys behind unified diff markers are recognized because CI prints config diffs; the tracker is stateful per stream and its scan is linear and independent of the check budget.
- **Diff sides are tracked independently** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker`): The multiline tracker keeps separate open-value state for the removed and added sides of a diff, and non-diff text is on both sides, so a removed line that ends a value cannot expose the added lines that replace it.
- **Multiline withholding rules per diff side** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker.withhold`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker.withhold updates each diff side a line is on independently, since a context line can continue a value on one side and open another on the other; a blank line inside a block is not withheld but does not end the block.
- **A line opening a new quoted credential restarts tracking** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceMultilineTracker.withhold`): When a line opens another credential's quoted value, such as the added diff line replacing a value's first line, the tracker starts over with the new quote instead of treating the line as closing the old value.
- **Test credentials are assembled at run time** (`controller (CI evidence tests)`): CI-evidence tests build their synthetic credential values by concatenation at run time so the source tree never contains a literal credential that secret scanners would flag or that could be mistaken for a real one.
- **Retried repair handoffs adopt the stored prompt** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.createRepositoryMonitorRepairTaskWithEvidence`, `controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairTaskInput`): A retried repair handoff adopts the existing repair Task's stored prompt with its frozen evidence even if other prompt inputs moved, because the first handoff clears the item's skip reason after writing it into the prompt and rebuilding would lose it. repositoryMonitorRepairTaskInput reuses the stored prompt of an attempt's existing repair Task, because a rebuilt prompt would differ whenever inputs moved (such as a cleared skip reason or the item's verdict). It adopts the prompt only from a monitor-controlled Task whose prompt is a repair prompt for this intent and head with exactly the controller-rendered instructions; otherwise it rebuilds, so a stale or edited Task fails recovery validation. A fix_ci prompt freezes its CI input once the Task exists.
- **Pre-evidence fix_ci Tasks are recovered without fresh evidence** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.createRepositoryMonitorRepairTaskWithEvidence`): A fix_ci repair Task created by a controller version before repair prompts carried CI evidence is recovered with its stored prompt, and no fresh evidence is reported as having informed it; a pre-evidence prompt for a different head is not adopted.
- **Joined-secret withholding must stay linear** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdJoinedSecrets`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceWithholdJoinedSecrets once rewrote every line of a long continuation chain from each of its lines, which was quadratic (about 40s on 256 KiB of tiny lines); each line must be withheld at most once so the sanitizer stays linear.
- **Command work actions are restored before readiness publishes** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.queueRepositoryMonitorCommandWorkAction`): queueRepositoryMonitorCommandWorkAction records the queued work action for a partial or legacy handoff, even when its command run was already queued, so inventory cannot publish readiness without it.
- **Coalesced commands record intake's outcome** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): When command intake coalesced a command into an identical active action after the reconciler listed it, the work action is Completed; the reconciler then records the same coalesced outcome intake would, marking the command completed with the dependency error.
- **Retired plan approval maps to the approve action kind** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCommandActionKind`): repositoryMonitorCommandActionKind maps the retired approve-plan intent to the approve action kind so persisted work action IDs derived from it stay stable.
- **Running runs absent from activeRuns are abandoned** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.activeRuns`): activeRuns (guarded by activeRunsMu) holds the monitor runs this process is executing. A running run that is absent while this process holds the current controller epoch has no live executor: its claimant was an earlier epoch, which the storage write fence now rejects, or a local execution that returned before recording completion.
- **RepositoryMonitor declares its own Secret RBAC** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): The RepositoryMonitor reconciler keeps an explicit Secret get/list/create/delete RBAC marker even though Task reconciliation already grants those verbs, because RepositoryMonitor manages per-task runtime auth snapshot Secrets itself.
- **Monitor agent references enforce namespace isolation** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.resolveRepositoryMonitorAgent`): resolveRepositoryMonitorAgent resolves spec.agents.<role> to an Agent while enforcing namespace isolation; a non-empty reason is a user-facing condition, while a non-nil error is a transient read failure to retry.
- **Review publication retries continue while suspended** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.Reconcile`): Completed reviews still need publication retries while scheduled runs are suspended, so pending or published reviews requeue at the validation retry interval regardless of suspension.
- **Only reconcile-stopping errors block queued runs** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorRunsBlockedCondition`): RepositoryMonitorRunsBlockedCondition returns the Ready condition only when it reports an Error that stops reconciliation before queued runs are processed, meaning a new manual run would stay queued; a failed run or invalid schedule still lets queued runs proceed.
- **Runs are marked active before they are claimed** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): A monitor run is registered in activeRuns before it is claimed, so a concurrent reconcile in the same process never mistakes its own claim for an abandoned run.
- **Interrupted runs are terminalized or reclaimed** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordInterruptedMonitorRun`): When a run's execution returns before recording completion, for example on controller shutdown, the reconciler records a terminal state where safe; otherwise the next reconcile by the controller epoch holder reclaims the run.
- **executeClaimedMonitorRun returns nil only after recording state** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.executeClaimedMonitorRun`): executeClaimedMonitorRun returns a nil error only after the run's resulting state has been recorded; callers treat any error as an interrupted run.
- **Read the controller epoch before listing runs** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): Abandoned-run detection reads the controller epoch before listing runs: once this process holds the current epoch the storage write fence rejects earlier epochs, so a running run read afterwards can no longer be completed by an earlier-epoch claimant.
- **Interrupted-run records are detached but epoch-fenced** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordInterruptedMonitorRun`): recordInterruptedMonitorRun writes the terminal state with a detached, bounded context; the write still carries this process's controller epoch fence so it cannot land after a newer epoch owns storage. It returns nil when the run is no longer running here or the write fails, leaving the run to the epoch holder.
- **Incomplete CI results retry the run** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRunFailureState`): errRepositoryMonitorCIIncomplete schedules a retry rather than terminalizing the run, because partial CI results settle on their own.
- **Retention keeps a retained workflow's snapshot** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): Item retention keeps the issue snapshot a retained workflow was started from instead of advancing it, so the next uncapped run still sees a later edit as a snapshot digest change.
- **Runs stop when their command was coalesced after queuing** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): Intake can coalesce a command after recovery already queued its run; execution must then stop as for a cancelled action so the command's work never runs twice.
- **Halted issue commands leave the shared issue alone** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): A halted issue command, whether its phase is disabled or the transition is not allowed, must not block the shared issue item or emit an event for it, because the issue is driven by the action that still owns the work.
- **Halted fix commands leave the shared pull request item alone** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): A halted fix command (cancelled, or coalesced into another action) must not mark the shared pull request item failed even when the PR carries a blocked label, because the item belongs to the action still doing the work.
- **Created-PR discovery uses a fresh read** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): Discovering the pull request created by a delivered implementation uses a fresh read rather than cached state, so it still finds the PR when the head changed after delivery.
- **Terminal failed updates get a new bounded command** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryRepositoryMonitorAutomaticRepair`): After a branch update action fails terminally, a later automatic repair gets a new bounded command instead of resurrecting the terminal action as permanently queued.
- **Fixed-finding thread marking is limited to Orka's own reviews** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.markRepositoryMonitorFixedFindingThreads`): markRepositoryMonitorFixedFindingThreads adds an Addressed note and resolves threads only on inline comments of reviews Orka published, so a comment copying Orka's marker is ignored, and each finding is marked once. Because marking links a finding to its fix, security-sensitive reviews or findings are never marked unless the monitor posts security-sensitive reviews.
- **Only accepted reviews mark findings fixed on GitHub** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.markRepositoryMonitorFixedFindingThreads`): Only an accepted review updates the findings record, so a skipped or failed review result must not mark any finding fixed on GitHub either.
- **Thread marking re-checks the live pull request like publishing** (`controller.repositoryMonitorPullRequestThreadsMarkable`): Before marking fixed-finding threads, the controller re-reads the pull request and applies the same live checks that gate publishing (open, expected base, unchanged head), because a head that moved since the review may have reintroduced the defect.
- **Fixed-finding lookup reads every publication page** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorFixedFindingComments`): repositoryMonitorFixedFindingComments reads every page of review publication records, because a finding fixed long after it was reported sits on an old publication.
- **Thread-marking failures never block review ingestion** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.markRepositoryMonitorFixedFindingThreadsBestEffort`): Marking fixed-finding threads only tidies the pull request, so a GitHub failure must not block review ingestion; the failure is recorded and inventory polls retry it within repositoryMonitorFindingThreadRetryWindow.
- **Addressed-finding mutations are keyed by head** (`controller.repositoryMonitorFindingAddressedMutationID`): repositoryMonitorFindingAddressedMutationID keys the mutation by pull request head, not review, so a re-review of the same head does not resolve again a thread a maintainer reopened.
- **Review records carry a cumulative findings ledger** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): A review record's findings are the pull request's ledger at that review: every finding Orka has tracked, open or fixed. A follow-up review starts from the latest accepted record so findings keep their IDs and threads across heads instead of being rediscovered and reworded.
- **Same-head reviews can add but never fix findings** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewModeSameHead`): A same_head review re-reviews an already reviewed head; since nothing changed, it may add findings but must not mark any fixed.
- **Only the last exhaustive pass publishes** (`controller.repositoryMonitorPublishSkipExhaustivePassPending`): Publishing is skipped for an exhaustive-review pass that another pass will extend; the final pass publishes all findings together.
- **Incremental reviews cover only the pull request's own files** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.planRepositoryMonitorReview`): planRepositoryMonitorReview picks the review mode from the latest accepted review; an incremental review covers only paths the pull request changes, so changes merged in from the base branch are not reviewed again.
- **Unreadable findings records fall back to a full review** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.planRepositoryMonitorReview`): If the latest accepted review's findings record cannot be read, planRepositoryMonitorReview plans a full review instead of failing, because a corrupt earlier record only costs a full review.
- **Follow-up reviews require a base that saw every change** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.planRepositoryMonitorReview`): A follow-up review checks only changes since its base, so the base review must have seen every change it covers (repositoryMonitorReviewSawEveryChange); otherwise the gap would never be reviewed and a full review is planned.
- **Rebased or truncated deltas get a full review** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.planRepositoryMonitorReview`): A rebase, force-push, unreachable base, or a compare GitHub may have cut off at its file limit gets a full review, because without a provable, complete delta a full review is the safe plan.
- **Reviews whose Task is gone are not built on** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReviewSawEveryChange`): repositoryMonitorReviewSawEveryChange requires the review Task to prove it showed the whole pull request and, for a follow-up, every change since its base; a review whose Task is gone cannot prove that and is never used as a base.
- **Restored review plans fall back to full on base mismatch** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReviewPlanForTask`): repositoryMonitorReviewPlanForTask restores a review Task's plan from its annotations; a missing or mismatched base falls back to a full review so a review never resolves findings it was not shown.
- **Findings ledger merge rules** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger lets only an incremental review mark the base's open findings fixed; other modes carry them unchanged. New findings repeating an open finding are dropped, and a nil changed-lines map means the whole pull request was covered.
- **Only findings shown to the reviewer can be marked fixed** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): An incremental review may mark fixed only the open findings its prompt listed; findings left out of the prompt for size stay open.
- **Adjacent new findings are not repeats** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): A review may report adjacent defects of its own; repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger drops a new finding only when it restates an earlier open finding.
- **Repeats inside reviewed changes clear OutsideChanges** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): When a repeat of an open finding is reported inside the reviewed changes, the open finding's OutsideChanges flag is cleared so it may now block the pull request.
- **Security-sensitive reviews restrict all affected findings** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): A security-sensitive verdict may concern an open finding the review did not repeat or one it marked fixed, and which is unknown, so every open finding and every finding fixed at that head is marked security-sensitive.
- **Finding repeat matching rule** (`controller.repositoryMonitorFindingRepeats`): repositoryMonitorFindingRepeats treats a finding as a repeat only with the same file and the same normalized title; nearness does not count because unrelated defects can sit lines apart and lines move between heads. A finding more severe than the open one is never a repeat, so a new blocker is not dropped for an earlier minor finding.
- **Legacy findings get deterministic IDs** (`controller.repositoryMonitorLedgerFromRecord`): repositoryMonitorLedgerFromRecord assigns findings from records written before findings had IDs deterministic IDs derived from the record, so planning and ingestion agree and a follow-up review can report each finding separately.
- **Findings outside reviewed changes block only at P1 or above** (`controller.repositoryMonitorFindingBlocks`): repositoryMonitorFindingBlocks keeps a pull request at needs_changes for an open finding outside the changes a follow-up review covered only at P1 or above; an unrecognized priority counts as P1.
- **Review verdicts are derived from open findings** (`controller.repositoryMonitorDerivedReviewVerdict`): repositoryMonitorDerivedReviewVerdict decides passed or needs_changes from the open findings; an open security-sensitive finding keeps the review security_sensitive, and otherwise the reviewer's needs_human, security_sensitive, and skipped verdicts stand.
- **Files without a patch count as fully covered** (`controller.repositoryMonitorChangedLinesFromFiles`): repositoryMonitorChangedLinesFromFiles treats a compare file without a patch, such as a large or binary file, as covered in full.
- **Listed open findings are sanitized and deterministic** (`controller.repositoryMonitorListOpenFindings`): repositoryMonitorListOpenFindings sanitizes finding text like a published review because it is model output about untrusted code and Task readers see the prompt, and its result depends only on the ledger so ingestion can recompute exactly what a Task was shown.
- **Incremental review coverage comes from the prompt's delta** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewChangedLines`): repositoryMonitorReviewChangedLines reads covered lines from the delta embedded in the review prompt so coverage matches what the reviewer was shown; for other reviews or an incomplete delta it returns nil so every finding counts as inside the changes.
- **Open-trigger monitors still review automatic repair pushes** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewsEveryPush`): With the open review trigger a pull request is reviewed once, except when automatic repair may fix it: pushed fixes must be reviewed, so repositoryMonitorReviewsEveryPush returns true then.
- **Exhaustive review pass chain conditions** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorExhaustivePassPending`): Another exhaustive pass is due only when exhaustive review is on, the record is the head's latest accepted review, the head's first accepted review was full, the record added a finding, and fewer than repositoryMonitorExhaustiveMaxPasses passes ran; a review whose Task is gone ends the chain.
- **Reviewers cannot set Orka's ledger fields** (`controller.repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger`): repositoryMonitorMergeFindingLedger clears ID, status, first/resolved head, first review, and OutsideChanges on every new reviewer finding, so model output cannot forge Orka-owned ledger fields such as a fixed status or an existing ID.
- **Legacy security-sensitive records restrict their findings** (`controller.repositoryMonitorLedgerFromRecord`): A review record written before findings carried a SecuritySensitive flag is still treated as restricting its findings when the record's verdict or security status is security_sensitive, so an unpublished sensitive pass stays unpublished.
- **Security-sensitive findings are never named in published reviews** (`controller.renderRepositoryMonitorReviewBody`): renderRepositoryMonitorReviewBody never names a security-sensitive finding in a published review, even after it is fixed.
- **Fork repair receipts name the fork** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorRepairTasks`): For a pull request from a fork, the repair Task's delivery receipt must name the fork as its publication repository; ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorRepairTasks does not accept a receipt for the base repository.
- **Include-label removal revokes readiness on targeted runs** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`, `controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequest`): The include-label removal webhook queues a targeted run that never reaches missing-item cleanup, so that run must itself revoke readiness and settle the item's outstanding work actions. A targeted run never reaches missing-item cleanup, so retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequest revokes the readiness status itself, while heads shared with in-scope peers keep their peers' outcome.
- **Without the controller epoch only the timeout reclaims runs** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.failStaleRunningMonitorRun`): failStaleRunningMonitorRun reclaims a running run immediately only when this process holds the current controller epoch and is not executing it; without the epoch it cannot rule out a live executor elsewhere, so only repositoryMonitorRunningRunTimeout applies.
- **Compare responses at 300 files may be truncated** (`controller.repositoryMonitorGitHubCompareMaxFiles`): repositoryMonitorGitHubCompareMaxFiles is 300 because that is GitHub's documented ceiling for the compare endpoint's unpaginated files array; a compare reaching it may be cut off and cannot prove a complete delta.
- **ChangedFiles and MaintainerCanModify need a single PR read** (`controller.repositoryMonitorPullRequest.ChangedFiles`): GitHub returns ChangedFiles (the authoritative changed-file total) and MaintainerCanModify only on a single pull request read; list responses leave them zero, so code relying on them must fetch the pull request directly.
- **Excluded pull requests are retired before readiness** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): Excluded pull requests leave the inventory before readiness so they get no status, and a pull request that loses its include label is retired with its outstanding work cancelled because a repair Task could otherwise still push to it.
- **Automatic repair fetches pull request detail** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): Pull request lists omit mergeability, so when automatic repair is enabled but readiness publication is not, untargeted inventory runs fetch each pull request's detail; targeted runs already fetch it.
- **No work starts from a stale inventory** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): If readiness observes a newer head or a pull request leaving the monitor's scope, processPullRequestInventoryRun starts no work from the old inventory and leaves the next poll to rebuild it.
- **Commands for de-scoped targets are blocked** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): A command whose target pull request lost its include label since the first read is blocked with target_not_included rather than left pending for an item that is now retired.
- **Late label removal retires the pull request immediately** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): When the fresh pull request read shows an include label removed after the retirement pass listed pull requests, the pull request is retired immediately so its running work cannot push before the next poll.
- **Only newly started repairs consume inventory slots** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): Only newly started work consumes a run slot; active or exhausted repairs must not starve later pull requests on every workflow poll, and a GitHub update-branch counts as queued work even though it creates no Task.
- **Exhaustive continuation passes do not wait for CI again** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processPullRequestInventoryRun`): A head's first exhaustive pass already waited for green CI and the pass fence holds CI repair until the chain ends, so later passes on the same head skip the green-CI wait.
- **Control commands require a verified pull request** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): An unknown control target never gets a synthesized open inventory item; the command falls through to the normal targeted GitHub lookup so it is applied only after the pull request has been verified.
- **Pre-existing review Tasks are adopted only if byte-identical** (`controller.validateRepositoryMonitorReviewTaskMatchesExpected`): Task provenance admission does not reserve monitor labels, annotations, or owner references, so any principal with Task create access could pre-create a Task under the predictable review name and metadata proves nothing. validateRepositoryMonitorReviewTaskMatchesExpected adopts an existing Task only if its spec is byte-identical to what the controller renders now (diff context is deterministic for a fixed base/head/head-repo); the only tolerated difference is the controller-shaped GitHub-unavailable envelope, which carries no untrusted content, and unreproducible diff context fails closed.
- **Follow-up review content shares one prompt budget** (`controller.buildRepositoryMonitorReviewPrompt`): The pull request context, delta, and open findings share repositoryMonitorReviewContextMaxBytes, so a follow-up review prompt is never larger than a full review's.
- **Shared heads keep an in-scope peer's readiness** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): When retiring a missing item, readiness for its head is revoked only if no in-scope peer pull request with the same head already reconciled the shared commit outcome.
- **Explicit stops survive retirement** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): Retiring a missing item keeps a stopped-by-command skip reason, so a reopened pull request stays stopped.
- **Excluded pull requests have their Tasks cancelled** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequests`): retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequests retires open items whose pull request no longer carries an include label and cancels their unfinished review and repair Tasks, so no work started in scope publishes after the pull request leaves it.
- **Readiness peers are listed at most once per run** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequests`): A full run reconciles every excluded pull request's shared head against the fresh open inventory it already holds; a targeted run lists peers at most once, and only when a readiness status needs reconciling.
- **Each excluded pull request is retired independently** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequests`): One pull request's retirement failure, such as a rejected status write, must not leave the others in scope, so retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequests attempts each and joins every failure.
- **Retirement settles cancelled work like a stop** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.retireExcludedRepositoryMonitorPullRequest`): Retiring an excluded pull request cancels its work actions the way a stop does, so a returning include label does not find an orphaned running action holding readiness pending.
- **Pull request repair scope precedence** (`controller.repositoryMonitorPullRequestRepairScope`): repositoryMonitorPullRequestRepairScope grants full repair (branch updates, finding fixes, CI fixes) or CI-only repair; a full-repair label wins over a CI-only label, labelled requires checking the account that added the label before work starts, and without repair include labels other reviewed pull requests keep full repair.
- **Running reviews keep queued bookkeeping until ingestion** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): A review already running for the head keeps its queued bookkeeping until ingestion settles it, even if the pull request has since become CI-only; publishing still skips CI-only pull requests.
- **A head with a pending exhaustive pass is not yet reviewed** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler`): The head's latest accepted review decides whether another exhaustive pass is due; until that pass runs, review selection treats the head as not reviewed.
- **Implement commands are idempotent while implementation is active** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processIssueCommandRun`): When an issue is already in an implementation phase (queued, implementing, patch ready, or turning into a PR), processIssueCommandRun records a repeated implement command as succeeded without acting. Restarting planning would discard the ready plan, and starting another job would duplicate work.
- **Halted commands do not block or emit events for the issue** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processIssueCommandRun`): processIssueCommandRun returns early for a command whose work action is halted (cancelled or coalesced into another action). The issue is shared, and the action that still owns the work drives its state and events, so a halted command must not block it or emit events for it.
- **Paused items settle their completed attempt before retirement** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.settleRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue`): A paused monitor item keeps a completed ActionRecord. settleRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue settles that attempt before inventory retires the item; if settling fails, the attempt identity stays available so the next pass can retry.
- **Paused settlement also resolves implement commands waiting on a plan** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.settleRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue`): settleRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue looks up the work action derived from the completed record's action kind because an implement command can be waiting on its completed plan action. Skipping that lookup would leave the implement command unsettled.
- **Retained results without a Task receipt block the item** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.resumeRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue`): A retained paused result cannot advance when its Task is gone. resumeRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue releases the completed attempt and blocks the item with a task-missing reason, while the blocked item keeps the attempt's audit identity.
- **Resume applies the retained result before new commands** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.resumeRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue`): On resume, resumeRepositoryMonitorPausedIssue ingests the retained completed Task result before any new command can restart the item's workflow, so finished work is never discarded by a restart.
- **Runtime auth snapshots are swept once their result settles** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.cleanupRepositoryMonitorOrphanedRuntimeAuthSnapshots`): Once a Task's result has settled, its runtime auth snapshot credential is no longer needed. cleanupRepositoryMonitorOrphanedRuntimeAuthSnapshots deletes it even while the Task itself is retained, and repeats the sweep after transient cleanup failures.
- **Workspace policy rejections publish only the policy class** (`controller.repositoryMonitorWorkspaceValidationGuidance`): repositoryMonitorWorkspaceValidationGuidance turns a workspace content-policy rejection into public guidance that names only the policy class. The Task status message, which names the offending file, stays private to the namespace.
- **Plan action is persisted before implementation handoff** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.applyIssueActionRecord`): applyIssueActionRecord records the completed plan action before changing the item's active Task. If the implementation handoff fails before it is durable, the item still points at the completed plan Task and ingestion can retry safely.
- **Accepted implementation work continues through a workflow run** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.queueRepositoryMonitorLinkedPullRequest`): queueRepositoryMonitorLinkedPullRequest creates a workflow-triggered monitor run so accepted implementation work continues without waiting for an opened webhook or a schedule. The targeted inventory run verifies the current PR scope and head itself.
- **Monitor write Tasks share one publish path policy** (`controller.repositoryMonitorApplyPatchPathLimits`): repositoryMonitorApplyPatchPathLimits applies one path policy to implementation and repair Tasks: spec.issueWorkflow.implementation.allowedPaths when set, and repository control paths are always denied. The runtime enforces both limits before any publication.
- **PR creation receipt is persisted before usage projection** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.finishIssueMutation`): finishIssueMutation persists the confirmed PR creation outcome before recording the separately fallible usage link. A retry reuses that receipt, so an Orka-created PR is never reclassified as assistance when its usage link was not stored.
- **Research actions update the issue status comment** (`controller.repositoryMonitorIssueActionUpdatesStatusComment`): repositoryMonitorIssueActionUpdatesStatusComment returns true for research actions. Research is requested through the API, so without a status-comment update the result would only be stored as internal planning context and the requester would see nothing on the issue.
- **Issue run cap bounds only newly selected issues** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processIssueInventoryRun`): The per-run selection cap in processIssueInventoryRun limits newly selected issues only. An issue already past discovery keeps its recorded workflow (plan approval, open PR, verdict bookkeeping) even when its content yields a new snapshot digest, and that retained item neither consumes a selection slot nor counts as selected.
- **Paused issues keep their Task, result, and snapshot** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.processIssueInventoryRun`): While a monitor is paused, processIssueInventoryRun holds the next action and preserves the current Task or its completed result, including failures that release job budgets. It also keeps the old snapshot so edits are rediscovered on unpause instead of attaching the old result to new requirements.
- **Retained workflows keep the snapshot they started from** (`controller.repositoryMonitorIssueRetainWorkflowUnderRunLimit`): repositoryMonitorIssueRetainWorkflowUnderRunLimit keeps the stored snapshot digest, title, body, and labels from the existing item. Persisting the new digest beside the old workflow would make later runs treat the edited issue as unchanged and let an in-flight implementation settle against requirements it never saw.
- **Older monitor Tasks keep their immutable auth binding** (`controller.newRepositoryMonitorPausedAuthFixture`): Builtin ACP Tasks persisted before monitor Task creation became credential-free carry an immutable runtime auth Secret binding that ingestion still requires. newRepositoryMonitorPausedAuthFixture models such a Task so tests cover replay of those older Tasks.
- **Review findings have a byte budget below the body limit** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewFindingsBudgetBytes`): repositoryMonitorReviewFindingsBudgetBytes caps the review body through its findings lists so the tests section and the review marker always fit afterwards within GitHub's body limit.
- **Recovered reviews follow the same-head update policy** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.publishRepositoryMonitorReview`): When publishRepositoryMonitorReview recovers an existing Orka review for a head from its marker, that review is the head's publication, so the same-head update policy applies to it exactly as it would to a recorded publication.
- **Review bodies degrade findings to fit GitHub's limit** (`controller.renderRepositoryMonitorReviewBody`): renderRepositoryMonitorReviewBody keeps the body under GitHub's size limit when many findings are combined: past the budget, findings shrink to titles and then are only counted, while the review record keeps every finding. Room is reserved for every later finding's title so detail goes to the first findings without crowding out the rest.
- **Inline comments only for findings first reported at this head** (`controller.repositoryMonitorNewInlineFindings`): repositoryMonitorNewInlineFindings selects open findings first reported at the record's head inside the reviewed changes. Earlier findings already have inline threads, so including them would duplicate threads on the pull request.
- **Inline review comments target only RIGHT-side lines** (`controller.parseRepositoryMonitorPatchRightLines`): parseRepositoryMonitorPatchRightLines collects only added and context lines. Removed lines are LEFT-side lines in GitHub's review API and inline review comments on them are not supported by the monitor.
- **Agent text is redacted before it is bounded and published** (`controller.sanitizeRepositoryMonitorReviewText`): sanitizeRepositoryMonitorReviewText strips control and format runes and redacts credential shapes from agent-produced text (research summaries, review verdicts, plan excerpts) before truncating it. Text is published on GitHub, so a secret found in the repository must never reach a public comment, and truncation must not split a token past the redactor.
- **Line-wrapped credentials withhold the whole field** (`controller.sanitizeRepositoryMonitorReviewText`): A model can wrap one credential across lines, so sanitizeRepositoryMonitorReviewText checks a newline-joined shadow of both the original and the sanitized text and replaces the whole field with [REDACTED] when either is credential-shaped. Line-by-line redaction cannot tell which fragments belong to the secret, and line sanitization may remove the recognizable fragment while leaving a tail behind.
- **Publish authentication failures are retryable** (`controller.repositoryMonitorGitHubPublishFailureReason`): Installation tokens can expire between review completion and publication, so repositoryMonitorGitHubPublishFailureReason classifies 401 as a retryable authentication failure. The retry uses the current forge Secret after the normal publish cooldown, and the full PR safety and duplicate checks run again before another POST.
- **Changed files come from the immutable compare range** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.listRepositoryMonitorCompareFiles`): listRepositoryMonitorCompareFiles reads changed files and patches from GitHub's compare endpoint for the exact base...head SHAs, so the file set is bound to immutable commits rather than the current PR branch. GitHub does not paginate the compare files array and caps it at repositoryMonitorGitHubCompareMaxFiles, so callers must reconcile the result against the pull request's changed-file total.
- **One Orka review per head; verdict changes rewrite its summary** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview`): When a later review of the same head reaches a different verdict, updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview rewrites the summary of the review Orka already published instead of posting another, so GitHub shows the current verdict with one Orka review per head. Inline comments stay from the first review.
- **Unrecorded summary updates force a rewrite** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview`): If a summary update may have been applied by GitHub without Orka recording it, the shown summary is unknown, so updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview rewrites it instead of trusting the records.
- **Review rewrites get their own terminal publish record** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview`): updatePublishedRepositoryMonitorReview leaves the original publish record bound to its review and writes a separate skipped record for the rewriting review that points at the GitHub review it rewrote.
- **Shown-review lookup reads every publish record page** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorShownReviewRecordID`): Same-head skip records accumulate with every later review, so repositoryMonitorShownReviewRecordID pages through all publish records; reading only the first page could miss the newest summary update.
- **Attempted but unrecorded summary updates are ambiguous** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReviewUpdateUnrecorded`): A summary update's terminal record is written only after GitHub answers, so an update mutation attempted after the shown review was recorded either failed with an unknown outcome or succeeded unrecorded. repositoryMonitorReviewUpdateUnrecorded reports that case because GitHub may show a summary Orka's records do not.
- **CI failures are keyed by source and name** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIFailure`): repositoryMonitorCIFailure identifies a failure by source and name because a check run and a commit status context can share a name. Its other fields are raw, unsanitized GitHub details used only to build repair evidence and never to classify the failure.
- **Only persisted status IDs are excluded from CI evaluation** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorOwnedReadinessStatuses`): repositoryMonitorOwnedReadinessStatuses returns only status IDs GitHub returned and Orka persisted in its audit, across all peer monitors and namespaces, to avoid cyclic CI dependencies. The caller intersects them with GitHub's statuses for the exact repository and head; a context name alone never establishes ownership.
- **Accepted commands revoke readiness before their Tasks start** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReadyOutcome`): repositoryMonitorReadyOutcome checks active work actions for the current head so an accepted command revokes earlier success before its Task starts. At that point LastVerdict still describes the prior review and cannot be trusted alone.
- **Only full-repair pull requests repair review findings** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorNeedsChangesRepairEligible`): repositoryMonitorNeedsChangesRepairEligible returns false unless the pull request has full repair scope. Review-only or CI-only pull requests keep their findings until a person changes them.
- **Automatic repair prefers CI the pull request broke** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorNeedsChangesRepairEligible`): Automatic repair selection prefers CI failures the pull request introduced, even when review findings cannot be repaired, and leaves failures inherited from the base branch alone. CI and review repair intents share one repair policy and budget.
- **Readiness is published only for reviewed pull requests** (`controller.repositoryMonitorPullRequestInScope`): repositoryMonitorPullRequestInScope limits readiness to open, reviewed pull requests on the monitor's base branch. CI-only pull requests get no readiness status because no review vouches for them.
- **Readiness aggregates every open PR sharing a head** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorSharedHeadReadiness`): GitHub commit statuses belong to commits, not pull requests. repositoryMonitorSharedHeadReadiness aggregates outcomes across all open PRs on the same head so peers never overwrite a blocker with success or alternate blocking descriptions on every inventory poll.
- **Shared-head readiness precedence is failure, pending, success** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorSharedHeadReadiness`): When aggregating peer outcomes, repositoryMonitorSharedHeadReadiness lets failure beat pending and pending beat success, and breaks ties by sorting descriptions so the result does not depend on inventory order.
- **Readiness refreshes each peer PR from its detail response** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.refreshRepositoryMonitorReadinessPullRequest`): GitHub's pull request list omits mergeable_state, so refreshRepositoryMonitorReadinessPullRequest fetches every evaluated peer. Otherwise a known conflict could be treated as ready when another PR sharing its commit moves.
- **Push target and maintainer-edit permission come from one response** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.refreshRepositoryMonitorReadinessPullRequest`): The maintainer-can-modify permission describes a specific head repository, so refreshRepositoryMonitorReadinessPullRequest takes the head branch and repository from the same detail response. A fork can be renamed or transferred without its head SHA changing.
- **Readiness is a commit status; GitHub owns approval and merge** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness`): reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness publishes readiness as a commit status so existing repository credentials can publish it without a GitHub App. Approvals and merging remain controlled solely by GitHub. Inventory callers may pass the full open-PR list to reuse for peers.
- **Readiness refresh result feeds back into inventory selection** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness`): reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness writes the refreshed pull request snapshot back to the caller so a refresh that blocks readiness also blocks the repair and review stages later in the same poll.
- **Persisted prior head stays authoritative for readiness cleanup** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness`): On retry, GitHub's list may already show a new head. reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness keeps the persisted previous head authoritative for departed-head cleanup until the replacement item has been saved.
- **Stop carries across head changes; other outcomes wait** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness`): After a head change, reconcileRepositoryMonitorReadiness applies only a maintainer stop immediately, without using positive review evidence from the previous head. Every other outcome waits for the next inventory snapshot.
- **Departed heads without a readiness status skip peer listing** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorDepartedHead`): reconcileRepositoryMonitorDepartedHead returns early when no readiness mutation exists for the head: nothing stale is shown, and an in-scope peer publishes its own status when processed. This avoids a peer listing that CI-only pull requests would otherwise repeat every poll.
- **Departed-head cleanup lists peers freshly** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorDepartedHead`): reconcileRepositoryMonitorDepartedHead fetches a fresh peer list because the old inventory can still contain the departed pull request.
- **Readiness is revoked when a PR leaves scope** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.revokeRepositoryMonitorReadiness`): A pull request can leave the base-filtered inventory without changing its commit. revokeRepositoryMonitorReadiness revokes any status Orka already published before retiring the item, including retained items from earlier controller versions, and never creates statuses for untracked heads.
- **Uncertain readiness POSTs recover through pending** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ensureRepositoryMonitorReadinessStatus`): Commit statuses are append-only. ensureRepositoryMonitorReadinessStatus retries an uncertain POST by publishing pending, never by trusting a matching context or replaying a stale success, and persists the returned status ID before evaluating CI so Orka's own pending status is excluded.
- **Readiness status writes are recorded as submitting first** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.publishRepositoryMonitorReadinessStatus`): publishRepositoryMonitorReadinessStatus marks the mutation as submitting before calling GitHub, so a crash or failed audit update recovers through pending rather than leaving a stale successful status.
- **PR stop actions stay retryable until the stop settles** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun`): tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun keeps a pull request stop command's work action retryable until cancelling the target work actions and recording the stopped state has settled, so a partial stop is completed on the next pass.
- **Running reviews stay queued until ingestion settles them** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun`): tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun leaves an item whose review is already queued untouched, because completed review Tasks are ingested only while the item still reports the review as queued; overwriting the verdict would orphan the review.
- **Halted commands do not mark the shared PR item** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun`): tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun returns early for halted update-branch and repair commands. The pull request item is shared, and the action the command was cancelled for or coalesced into still owns it.
- **Repair commands recheck until their Task exists** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun`): tryProcessPullRequestCommandRun rechecks repair preconditions on every pass until the repair Task exists, including retries after a failed create. Once the Task exists the repair has started and is recovered instead of re-evaluated.
- **Merged PRs set both item state and automerge state** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.completeRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranch`): completeRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranch sets both State and AutomergeState to merged for a merged pull request, so retirement never leaves a stale merge_ready automerge state behind.
- **Update-branch success event precedes the terminal action** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.completeRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranch`): The terminal work action stops command reconciliation, so completeRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranch persists the success event first under an ID derived from the mutation. Retries stay safe even if the event committed but its response or the next write failed.
- **Branch updates count against repair budgets** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy`): Branch updates mutate GitHub directly and have no RepairJob, so repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy counts their durable update-branch mutation records against the same finite repair budgets.
- **Policy commands exhausted before a Task count once** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy`): A policy repair command can exhaust its retries before any Task exists. repositoryMonitorRepairPolicy counts that terminal attempt once so the next poll gets a new bounded command identity, while accepted commands keep their existing run and retry budget.
- **Repairs push to forks only with opt-in and maintainer edits** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRepairRepository`): repositoryMonitorRepairRepository allows a repair to push to a fork head only when the monitor opts in and the PR author allows maintainer edits; otherwise it returns the reason it cannot push. GitHub still authorizes the push itself.
- **Fork repair session keys use repo:branch** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.createRepositoryMonitorRepairTaskWithEvidence`): Forks commonly reuse branch names such as main, so createRepositoryMonitorRepairTaskWithEvidence keys fork repair sessions as lowercased head repository, a colon, and the branch. A colon cannot appear in a Git ref, so fork keys never collide with monitored branch keys.
- **Pre-evidence fix_ci prompts are adopted without CI evidence** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRepairPromptPredatesCIEvidence`): repositoryMonitorRepairPromptPredatesCIEvidence recognizes fix_ci repair prompts written by older controllers before prompts carried failingCheckEvidence. Such a prompt is adopted for its head with no CI input, so no evidence is recorded as having informed it; prompts for another head, intent, or schema are not adopted and fail recovery validation.
- **Repair commit findings are sanitized list lines** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage`): Finding titles in repair commit messages are model output about untrusted code, so repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage sanitizes each onto its own list line where it cannot form a Git trailer.
- **Repair commit messages reserve room within the publisher limit** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage`): The workspace publisher limits the whole commit message in bytes, so repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage reserves room for the subject, trailer, list header, and a closing 'and N more' line before adding findings.
- **Security-sensitive findings stay out of repair commits** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage`): A pushed commit is public, so repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage lists a security-sensitive review's findings only when the monitor is configured to post security-sensitive reviews.
- **Only known finding priorities appear in commits** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage`): Finding priority is model output, so repositoryMonitorRepairCommitMessage prefixes a title with its priority only when repositoryMonitorPriorityRank recognizes it.
- **Repair prompts name the checks to fix** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorRepairPromptWithCI`): buildRepositoryMonitorRepairPromptWithCI names the failing checks and those that also fail on the base so the repairer leaves base failures alone. The list is guidance; when the lookup fails it is omitted and failingCheckEvidence explains why.
- **Only findings repairs fix review findings** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRepairPromptScope`): repositoryMonitorRepairPromptScope gives CI and branch-update repairs a scope sentence that treats review findings as context only, so they do not widen into fixing findings; only a findings repair fixes them.
- **Stored repair prompts must match rendered instructions exactly** (`controller.repositoryMonitorRepairPromptInstructionsMatch`): repositoryMonitorRepairPromptInstructionsMatch requires a stored prompt's instructions to equal exactly what the controller renders for the intent and the prompt's own input, so only frozen input data is reused. A Task whose instructions were edited after creation is rebuilt and fails recovery validation.
- **CI check names are sanitized before entering prompts** (`controller.boundRepositoryMonitorCheckNames`): CI check names are chosen by pull request authors, so boundRepositoryMonitorCheckNames sanitizes them and caps their count and length before they enter a repair prompt.
- **Repair pushes keep a review already queued for the new head** (`controller.repositoryMonitorResetItemAfterRepairPush`): repositoryMonitorResetItemAfterRepairPush clears review state invalidated by a repair push so the new head is reviewed afresh, but keeps a review the synchronize event already queued for the pushed head. Clearing its queued verdict would orphan the completed review Task, which is only ingested while the item reports it as queued.
- **Repair state projects only current-head evidence** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairStateForHead`): A branch can move while an older repair finishes, so repositoryMonitorRepairStateForHead lets only work or terminal evidence for the current head affect current readiness.
- **Pre-Task repair failures come from terminal work actions** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairStateForHead`): Repair failures before a Task exists have no terminal job or branch mutation, so repositoryMonitorRepairStateForHead reads command terminal actions, which distinguish failures and policy blocks from successful or cancelled commands whose Error may also contain a reason.
- **Issue event scans fail closed past a page limit** (`controller.repositoryMonitorIssueEventPageLimit`): repositoryMonitorIssueEventPageLimit bounds how many issue event pages the controller reads to find who added a repair label. A pull request with a longer history fails closed rather than trusting a partial event log.
- **Only CI failures absent on the base count as introduced** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorPullRequestBrokeCI`): repositoryMonitorPullRequestBrokeCI treats a failing check as the pull request's own only when it does not also fail on the base commit; failures inherited from the base are left alone. With no base result to compare, every head failure counts as introduced.
- **Only base-commit CI results are cached, per inventory run** (`controller.withRepositoryMonitorBaseCICache`): withRepositoryMonitorBaseCICache lets pull requests in one inventory run share a base commit's CI result. Base results only classify head failures as introduced or inherited; head results are never cached because they gate readiness and repair.
