- **Repair prompt check names are qualified when ambiguous** (`controller.repositoryMonitorCIFailureNames`): repositoryMonitorCIFailureNames qualifies a failure name with its source when a failure in the other list carries the same name, so a check run and a status context sharing a name stay distinguishable in the repair prompt.
- **Incomplete base CI results defer classification** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorPullRequestCIFailures`): repositoryMonitorPullRequestCIFailures reports incomplete when the base CI result is partial, because a hidden base failure would make an inherited head failure count as introduced. Work waits for complete results.
- **Repair label actors are resolved from the issue event log** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted`): Inventory sees labels but not who added them, so repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted reads the issue event log to find the account behind each current repair label and checks it has the permission label commands require. An unresolved account is refused.
- **The last labeled event names the current label's actor** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted`): GitHub returns issue events oldest first, so repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted takes the actor of the last labeled event for a label as the account that added the current label.
- **Label removals revoke the earlier labeller** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted`): An unlabeled event clears the recorded actor in repositoryMonitorRepairLabelActorPermitted, so a repair label removed after the inventory snapshot fails closed instead of crediting an earlier labeller.
- **Custom GitHub roles fall back to their base permission** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorCollaboratorPermission`): GitHub's collaborator permission API reports a custom role in role_name and its base permission in permission, so repositoryMonitorCollaboratorPermission uses role_name only for built-in roles and otherwise falls back to permission.
- **Queued policy repairs recheck their grant before acting** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorPolicyRepairWithdrawnReason`): A queued controller policy repair's grant can change after acceptance, so repositoryMonitorPolicyRepairWithdrawnReason rechecks it before creating a Task or updating the branch: the repair scope must still cover the intent, the account behind the current repair label must still be permitted, and a CI fix must still have a failure the pull request introduced. Explicit commands keep their own authorization.
- **CI lookup failures defer policy repairs** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorPolicyRepairWithdrawnReason`): When repositoryMonitorPolicyRepairWithdrawnReason cannot read CI or gets partial results, it returns an error so the command is deferred, rather than repairing blind or dropping the command.
- **Proved branch updates keep retrying projections** (`controller.pendingMutationProjectionError`): Once a GitHub update-branch mutation is proved, failures projecting its success (mutation record, monitor item, work action, event) are reported through pendingMutationProjectionError and retried even after the normal command retry budget is spent, without submitting the mutation again.
- **Review context budget stays far below the etcd object limit** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextMaxBytes`): The orka.prReview.context.v1 payload is embedded in Task.spec.prompt, so its 700 KiB total budget leaves margin under etcd's roughly 1.5 MiB object limit for the rest of the prompt and Task; the value matches the contract documented in website/docs/guides/repository-monitors.md. Patches are carried for at most repositoryMonitorReviewContextMaxFiles files and identities (path, status, counts) for up to repositoryMonitorReviewContextMaxFileEntries files, so the reviewer can discover every changed file in the Git-metadata-free checkout; identities take precedence over patches inside the budget.
- **Follow-up deltas share the review context budget** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewDeltaMaxBytes`): repositoryMonitorReviewDeltaMaxBytes bounds a follow-up review's delta, which is counted within the same repositoryMonitorReviewContextMaxBytes budget as the full pull request context.
- **Follow-up deltas use their own markers** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewDeltaBeginMarker`): The follow-up delta is a second orka.prReview.context.v1 payload framed by its own begin and end markers, so the pull request context block still parses on its own.
- **Review context fails closed on PR drift** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorReviewContext`): buildRepositoryMonitorReviewContext lists changed files and then refetches the pull request, failing closed if the base, head, or head repository drifted; the drift check runs even when the listing failed, so a moved head is never reviewed against stale identity. GitHub read failures do not fail the review: the context is marked unavailable with only a sanitized error class and the reviewer inspects the checkout instead.
- **Review files are bound to exact SHAs via compare** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorReviewContext`): buildRepositoryMonitorReviewContext reads the file set from the compare endpoint for the exact base and head SHAs rather than the mutable pull request file listing. With the listing, a force-push A to B to A between two PR reads would pass the drift check while labelling B's patches as A.
- **Compare listings are reconciled with the PR's changed-file total** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextBindChangedFileCount`): GitHub's compare endpoint silently caps its file array, so repositoryMonitorReviewContextBindChangedFileCount checks the listing against the authoritative changed-file total from the pull request read. A shorter listing, or a cap-sized listing when the total is unknown, marks the change set incomplete.
- **Review context errors are reduced to a class label** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextErrorClass`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextErrorClass reduces a GitHub read failure to a short class label and never includes the error text, which may carry request URLs or response bodies.
- **Review context places identities before patches** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextFromFiles`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextFromFiles renders file identities first, adds patches only while the byte budget allows, and drops identities only when the path list alone does not fit. A set truncated.files flag therefore means the change set is not fully represented, which the review prompt turns into a mandatory non-passing verdict.
- **Altered paths or deleted lines mark the change set incomplete** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextFromFilesWithin`): When a path or previous path is clipped, redacted, or control-stripped, or a deleted patch line is rewritten by sanitization, repositoryMonitorReviewContextFromFilesWithin marks the change set incomplete. The checkout has no Git metadata and may lack deleted or renamed-from paths, and deleted content exists nowhere else, so the reviewer cannot recover it.
- **Omitted patches fail closed except for pure additions** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextMarkUnreviewableOmissions`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextMarkUnreviewableOmissions marks the change set incomplete whenever an omitted patch hides what changed. The only exemption is a wholly added or copied file with no deletions, whose full content is in the head checkout. Removed files, patchless renames (a pure rename and a content-changing binary rename look identical without the base blob), deleted lines, and modified files all fail closed because the checkout cannot show which lines changed.
- **Complete patches must match GitHub's line counts** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextMarkUnreviewableOmissions`): A patch claimed complete must agree with GitHub's reported additions and deletions; repositoryMonitorReviewContextMarkUnreviewableOmissions marks the change set incomplete otherwise, because a short-served or inconsistent patch would hide part of the change while claiming completeness.
- **Review context budget is verified on the final encoding** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextTrimToBudget`): Per-entry size accounting is only an estimate, so repositoryMonitorReviewContextTrimToBudget measures the final encoding and trims conservatively: first the last remaining patch, then the last identity entry.
- **Capped entries never render or sanitize patches** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextIdentityEntry`): Files beyond the patch cap get identity-only entries in repositoryMonitorReviewContextIdentityEntry; their patch is never rendered, so it is not sanitized either, which keeps identity-only entries cheap.
- **Review context text is control-stripped before redaction** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize drops invalid UTF-8 and control characters other than newline and tab, then redacts credential shapes. Patches are untrusted pull request content persisted in Task.spec.prompt and readable through the Tasks API, and stripping first prevents a control byte inside a token from splitting it past the redactor. repositoryMonitorReviewContextDeletedLinesAltered reports when sanitization would rewrite a deleted line, because the reviewer cannot recover deleted content from the Git-free checkout; the original text is never retained.
- **Every '-' line in a GitHub patch is deleted content** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextDeletedLinesAltered`): GitHub file patches are hunk fragments without '--- a/' file headers, so repositoryMonitorReviewContextDeletedLinesAltered treats every '-'-prefixed line as deleted content, including one whose original text begins with '--'.
- **Deleted-line checks sanitize the whole patch** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextDeletedLinesAltered`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextDeletedLinesAltered sanitizes the whole patch rather than each deleted line so a credential spanning adjacent deleted lines is detected the way the rendered review context sees it.
- **Multi-line credential detection uses an 8-line window** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextSecretWindowLines`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextSecretWindowLines bounds how many adjacent patch lines are joined when looking for a credential that only becomes recognizable across lines, such as a folded or escaped YAML scalar or a value continued on the next line.
- **Unicode format runes are stripped before redaction** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize drops Unicode format runes (zero-width spaces, joiners, directional marks) before redaction because, like C0/C1 controls, they are invisible and can split a credential past the redactor.
- **Credentials split by tabs or newlines are withheld** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize`): Newlines and tabs survive sanitization because they carry diff and code structure, yet both can split a credential. repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize detects on a shadow of each line without its diff marker and tabs, withholds any line the secret policy recognizes, and withholds every line of a short adjacent window that the policy recognizes only once joined.
- **A final whole-text redaction pass follows line checks** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize`): After per-line and windowed checks, repositoryMonitorReviewContextSanitize runs redact.SensitiveText over the joined text to keep any cross-line reassembly the redactor itself performs.
- **Only continuation-shaped lines seed multi-line secret checks** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextLineMayContinue`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextLineMayContinue limits multi-line credential reconstruction to lines with a key separator that end in an escaped line break, open quote, bare key, or YAML block-scalar indicator. This keeps secret-policy cost proportional to the patch size rather than the patch size times the window.
- **Review context markers are line-anchored** (`controller.renderRepositoryMonitorReviewContext`): renderRepositoryMonitorReviewContext wraps the indented JSON payload in line-anchored markers. JSON strings cannot contain raw newlines, so a marker at the start of a line can only come from the renderer, never from an embedded patch or path.
- **Review context parsing uses first begin and last end marker** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewPromptContextBounds`): repositoryMonitorReviewPromptContextBounds locates the context block from the first line-anchored begin marker to the last line-anchored end marker after it. Untrusted payload text is JSON-encoded and can never start a line with a marker, so only the renderer's markers match.
- **Only the exact unavailable envelope is trusted as content-free** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextIsUnavailableEnvelope`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextIsUnavailableEnvelope accepts a context as the controller-shaped GitHub-unavailable envelope only when it matches the expected pull request with no files, no truncation, and a well-formed error class; such an envelope carries no untrusted content.
- **Passed verdicts require a complete change set** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewVerdictGateReason`): repositoryMonitorReviewVerdictGateReason enforces controller-side the rule the review prompt states: a passed verdict counts only when the reviewer saw the complete change set. A review whose embedded context is missing, unavailable, or file-truncated cannot have verified every changed file in a checkout without Git history, so its passed verdict never becomes the automerge gate.
- **Follow-up reviews also require a complete delta** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewContextGap`): repositoryMonitorReviewContextGap reports a gap when a follow-up review's delta since the last review is unavailable or truncated, because a small final diff can hide a large truncated delta.
- **Credential test fixtures are assembled at runtime** (`controller.TestRepositoryMonitorReviewContextRedactsCredentialsFromPatchesAndPaths`): Credential-shaped test fixtures such as the userinfo URL in TestRepositoryMonitorReviewContextRedactsCredentialsFromPatchesAndPaths are concatenated at runtime so the source never contains a literal credential URL that secret scanners or push protection would flag.
- **Finding ledger fields are set only by Orka** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewFinding`): The ID, Status, FirstHead, FirstReview, ResolvedHead, OutsideChanges, and SecuritySensitive fields of repositoryMonitorReviewFinding form Orka's ledger of findings for a pull request. Orka sets them when it ingests a review and ignores any values the reviewer model supplies.
- **Security-sensitive findings keep later reviews unpublished** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewFinding.SecuritySensitive`): A finding reported by a security-sensitive review carries SecuritySensitive, and every later review that still carries the finding stays security-sensitive so the finding's details remain unpublished.
- **Reviews shown a sensitive finding stay unpublished** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask`): ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask marks a review security-sensitive when its ledger or plan contained an open security-sensitive finding, because the review may describe that finding in its own prose even after fixing it.
- **Passed verdicts with blocking ledger findings become repairable** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask`): When a reviewer reports passed while Orka's ledger still counts findings as blocking, ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask derives needs_changes and marks the review repairable, since those findings are concrete code defects.
- **Passed verdicts without full context are downgraded** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask`): ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask does not rely on the model honoring the prompt's safety rule: a passed verdict without complete diff context is downgraded to needs_human before it can mark the head merge-ready.
- **Exceeding review rounds escalates to needs_human** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask`): When the review round limit is reached, ingestCompletedRepositoryMonitorReviewTask sets needs_human: the findings stay open and published, but automatic repair stops so a person can rethink the change.
- **Review results are selected from ACP output by schema** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewJSONPayload`): ACP results can include earlier commentary with code braces or diagnostic objects, so repositoryMonitorReviewJSONPayload scans top-level JSON objects and selects the review by its schema. It refuses to choose between multiple review results and never accepts nested example objects.
- **Review rounds count distinct accepted heads since a held pass** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReviewRoundsFor`): repositoryMonitorReviewRoundsFor counts distinct reviewed heads before a new review is recorded. Only accepted reviews count (failed, stale, and skipped do not), and several reviews of one head count once. A passed review ends the count only while it is its head's latest review, so a pass superseded by a later review of the same head, or an exhaustive pass whose chain never finished because a new head was pushed, does not reset it.
- **Review round counting relies on newest-first records** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorReviewRoundsFor`): repositoryMonitorReviewRoundsFor walks review records newest first, as the store returns them, so the first record seen for a head is that head's latest review.
- **Review metrics are recorded once, right after record creation** (`controller.recordRepositoryMonitorReviewMetrics`): recordRepositoryMonitorReviewMetrics is called exactly once, immediately after a review record is created, so a retried ingestion does not count the review or its findings again.
- **Rounds-to-pass is sampled once per PR and never fails ingestion** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordRepositoryMonitorRoundsToPassBestEffort`): recordRepositoryMonitorRoundsToPassBestEffort records how many heads a pull request needed before its first passed review. The review_first_passed event marks the sample as recorded, and an earlier pass the round count ended at (including one recorded before the event existed) also means this is not the first pass; a passed exhaustive pass that another pass will extend has not passed yet. Metrics never fail ingestion, so a lookup error skips the sample.
- **Review metric labels use bounded vocabularies** (`controller.repositoryMonitorReviewMetricMode`): A rejected review's mode is read from Task metadata, so repositoryMonitorReviewMetricMode maps it onto the review modes Orka creates, and repositoryMonitorFindingMetricPriority maps reviewer-supplied priorities onto P0-P3, keeping metric label values bounded and safe.
- **Finding priority metric labels are bounded to P0-P3** (`controller.repositoryMonitorFindingMetricPriority`): repositoryMonitorFindingMetricPriority accepts only priorities repositoryMonitorPriorityRank recognizes, because the priority is reviewer model output and an unbounded metric label would be unsafe.
- **Legacy plan phases normalize to a replanning hold** (`controller.repositoryMonitorNormalizeLegacyIssuePhase`): The legacy issue phase also covered plans that needed human input, so repositoryMonitorNormalizeLegacyIssuePhase maps it to blocked with repositoryMonitorLegacyPlanRequiresReplanning, preserving the hold until a fresh command requests a plan under current policy.
- **Usage refresh requeues while unmerged links exist** (`controller.usageOutcomeRequeueAfter`): usageOutcomeRequeueAfter includes recently observed usage PR links when deciding whether to requeue, because they still need a timer for their next refresh; the store already excludes links whose merge is confirmed.
- **Usage outcomes refresh each linked PR directly** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.refreshMonitorUsageOutcomes`): refreshMonitorUsageOutcomes keeps refreshing after the producing Tasks finish and queries each linked pull request directly, including closed pull requests that no longer appear in the open-PR inventory.
- **Usage PR state comes from one GraphQL observation** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.fetchUsagePullRequest`): fetchUsagePullRequest uses GraphQL because it exposes GitHub's rules-aware merge state and review decision for the same head in a single observation. Unknown review decisions fail closed for readiness.
- **Validation starts only after egress block is proven** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.reconcileRepositoryMonitorValidationConfinement`): reconcileRepositoryMonitorValidationConfinement waits for a later reconcile to mark the validation NetworkPolicy ready; the Pod gate then proves the repository endpoint is unreachable from the Pod network namespace before the validation container can start.
- **Validation container normalization mirrors API defaults** (`controller.defaultRepositoryMonitorValidationContainer`): defaultRepositoryMonitorValidationContainer sets an empty env fieldRef apiVersion to v1 because the API server stores it that way; exact-spec comparison against the stored Job would otherwise fail.
- **Only command intake writes the completed work action status** (`controller.repositoryMonitorWorkActionStatusCompleted`): The completed work action status is written only by command intake when it coalesces an action into an identical active action; the controller never writes it, and treats it as halted.
- **Coalesced work actions are never overwritten** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordRepositoryMonitorWorkActionStateWithMetadata`): recordRepositoryMonitorWorkActionStateWithMetadata leaves a coalesced (completed) action unchanged because it is final and its outcome is the action it joined.
- **Queue recovery never rewinds work action state** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordRepositoryMonitorWorkActionStateWithMetadata`): When recording a queued status for an action that already exists, recordRepositoryMonitorWorkActionStateWithMetadata does nothing: queue recovery only fills missing actions and must not rewind state.
- **Validation error differs from validation failed** (`controller.findingValidationStatusError`): findingValidationStatusError marks a finding validation that produced no verdict, whereas findingValidationStatusFailed means the validator refuted the finding; the two must not be conflated.
- **Scan condition messages are capped at 32 KiB** (`controller.repositoryScanConditionMessageLimit`): Kubernetes rejects condition messages longer than 32 KiB and scan summaries can exceed that, so the full summary is kept in storage and only a capped status message, ending in a truncation suffix, is published on the RepositoryScan.
- **Scan reconcile rereads live status after ingestion** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile`): Ingestion can publish a new run binding or completion, so RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile rereads the RepositoryScan through the uncached reader because those writes may not have reached the informer cache yet.
- **Suspension lets an admitted scan run finish** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile`): Suspending a RepositoryScan pauses new runs only; a run already admitted keeps starting its next stage and queued review slices so it can finish.
- **Uncloneable scan sources are terminal errors** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile`): A repository source the provider cannot clone would fail every run, so RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile reports it as a terminal configuration error instead of retrying with new Tasks.
- **Scan schedules are validated before any run starts** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile`): RepositoryScanReconciler.Reconcile parses the schedule before starting a run, so an invalid schedule reports InvalidSchedule without creating the initial scan Tasks; a run already in progress still finishes.
- **Missing analysis Agents pause new scan runs** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reconcileAnalysisAgentPresence`): A new scan run's Task needs the analysis Agent. While it is missing the scan reports so and rechecks every repositoryScanAgentRecheckInterval instead of failing Task creation on every reconcile, and results of earlier runs are still ingested.
- **Repository schedules reject sub-minute @every intervals** (`controller.parseRepositorySchedule`): The cron parser clamps @every intervals below one second (including zero and negative) to one second, so parseRepositorySchedule rejects any @every interval shorter than five-field cron's one-minute granularity; otherwise every reconcile of a scan or monitor would start another run.
- **Every admitted scan run advances the schedule cursor** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.scheduleCursor`): scheduleCursor advances for every admitted run, failed ones included, so a scan that never succeeds still fires at most once per schedule slot.
- **Scan run records precede their Tasks** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.scheduleCursor`): The newest scan run record is written before its Task, so scheduleCursor can rely on its start time even when a failed run's completion has not yet reached status.
- **Only full scans request cross-component mapper slices** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.mapperCrossComponentEnv`): mapperCrossComponentEnv asks the mapper for slices not bound to one component (threat model investigations and an independent audit) only for full scans; change-focused runs with a base commit keep to the slices their changes touch.
- **Model-written env values escape dollar signs** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.mapperCrossComponentEnv`): Kubernetes expands $(VAR) references in container env values, so mapperCrossComponentEnv doubles every dollar sign in model-written investigation text to keep it literal.
- **Review Task counts read past the informer cache** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.createReviewTasks`): createReviewTasks lists current scan Tasks through the uncached reader because the informer cache can miss a retry Task created earlier in the same reconcile, which would otherwise be created twice.
- **Terminal stage Tasks are ingested before progression** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestTerminalStageTasks`): ingestTerminalStageTasks ingests terminal threat-model and mapper Tasks from the same Task list that progression reads, because the cache can show a stage Task terminal even though ingestOwnedTasks saw it running earlier in the reconcile. Otherwise the next stage would start from unvalidated output, and a later ingestion of a malformed result would be ignored because the run had moved on. Ingestion is idempotent, so already-recorded Tasks cost one store read.
- **Scan review Tasks are created in bounded batches** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.topUpReviewTasks`): Task deadlines include time spent waiting for a runtime, so topUpReviewTasks refills a run's review queue as earlier reviews finish rather than creating every review Task at once.
- **Review Task window is four per executable prompt** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskWindow`): reviewTaskWindow bounds a run's queued and running review Tasks at four per prompt the run can execute at once: the smaller of the controller's ACP dispatch capacity and the prompt capacity of the run's RuntimePool, or one prompt before any is queued. Task deadlines include runtime wait time, so lower capacity gets a smaller window.
- **Unknown review pools assume one prompt** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskWindow`): Until a run's threat model or a review is queued to a RuntimePool, reviewTaskWindow cannot know the pool's capacity and sizes the window as if it had one prompt.
- **Review RuntimePool is learned from the threat model Task** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewRuntimePool`): The threat model runs on the same Agent as the reviews, so reviewRuntimePool learns the run's RuntimePool and prompt capacity before the first review batch. An existing pool keeps the capacity it was created with.
- **Review Task budgets are shared across scans** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskBudget`): ACP dispatch capacity is controller-wide and scans can share a RuntimePool, so reviewTaskBudget limits a run's new review Tasks by its own window and by budgets all scans share. A scan whose reviews do not fit waits instead of queueing Tasks that would outlive their deadline.
- **Recently created review Tasks count against budgets** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskBudget`): The informer cache can lag review Tasks created moments ago by this or another scan, and a review names its pool only once queued, so reviewTaskBudget adds unlisted and not-yet-queued Tasks from the created-task ledger.
- **Created Tasks are remembered until the cache shows them queued** (`controller.createdTasks`): createdTasks records Tasks a reconciler created, with their run's RuntimePool, until the informer cache lists them queued to that pool, so budget counts taken from the cache include them meanwhile. Entries expire after createdTaskCacheGrace.
- **Failed first review attempts are ingested first** (`controller.compareScanTasksForIngestion`): compareScanTasksForIngestion orders Tasks by creation but puts failed first review attempts first, so their automatic retries exist before ingesting another Task refreshes run progress; otherwise the run could end on a failure a retry replaces.
- **Unreadable review retries keep the run active** (`controller.collectScanRunProgress`): If collectScanRunProgress cannot read whether a retry replaced a failed review, it keeps the run active so the next reconcile reads it again rather than ending the run on that failure.
- **Slices failing their retry are unreviewable, not fatal** (`controller.unreviewableAfterRetry`): unreviewableAfterRetry identifies a review slice whose retry prompt also failed, for example because the provider stopped the response. The slice stays unreviewed and is reported, but does not discard the rest of the run; results that fail validation still fail it.
- **Review retries are read past the informer cache** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.uncachedReviewRetry`): uncachedReviewRetry reads the retry of a failed first review attempt through the uncached reader, because the retry is created just before progress is refreshed and a stale cache would end the run on the failure the retry replaces.
- **Scan runs that reviewed nothing fail** (`controller.failScanRunWithoutReviews`): failScanRunWithoutReviews fails a finished run in which every review slice failed its retry, since it has nothing to report, while unreviewable slices do not fail a run that reviewed others. It runs after pending slices reopen the run, so first-batch failures do not end a run whose other slices have not started.
- **Failed scan runs are dated when observed** (`controller.stampFailedScanRunCompletion`): stampFailedScanRunCompletion sets CompletedAt on a failed run that no Task completion time dated, so status.lastScanAt still records the attempt.
- **Scan runs stay running until review slices are ingested** (`controller.keepScanRunningForPendingReviewSlices`): Kubernetes reports Task success before the controller ingests it; in particular the mapper may not have persisted any slices when all Tasks first appear terminal, so keepScanRunningForPendingReviewSlices keeps the run open for pending slices.
- **Existing validation Tasks are adopted under identity checks** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ensureValidationTask`): ensureValidationTask recovers older timestamp-named validation Tasks and manually requested validations for the same finding occurrence, applying the same identity and spec checks used for deterministic Tasks before adopting them.
- **Existing auto-validation Tasks are recovered past the cap** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.enqueueAutoValidationTasks`): Creating a validation Task may have succeeded before its response or the finding update was lost, so enqueueAutoValidationTasks recovers an existing Task in any phase even if it already consumed the auto-validation cap.
- **Incremental threat models keep saved investigations** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.keepThreatModelInvestigations`): Incremental scans do not ask for threat model investigations, so keepThreatModelInvestigations carries the saved ones into an incremental threat model that omits them; the next full scan reviews them.
- **Failed review prompts get one automatic retry** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): A failed review prompt is often a transient provider error, so ingestReviewTask gives the slice exactly one automatic retry before the failure counts against the run.
- **Review slice ingestion rechecks ownership in its transaction** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): ingestReviewTask rechecks slice ownership inside the store transaction, and a failed or missing slice update rolls back the findings, diagnostics, counters, and receipt together.
- **Duplicate findings do not consume the run cap** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): In ingestReviewTask a repeat of a finding the run keeps is stored as a duplicate and does not take a place under the run's finding cap. Duplicates are linked only after their canonical finding survives the cap and is stored under its final ID.
- **Findings reported by overlapping slices are kept once** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): Review slices overlap, so another slice may already have reported the exact same finding ID; ingestReviewTask keeps it once.
- **Every stored finding is reconsidered for validation** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): ingestReviewTask adds every stored finding to the ingestion's validation candidates, because merging into an existing finding can raise its severity or confidence past the validation threshold.
- **Duplicate findings alias through the store** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestReviewTask`): ingestReviewTask joins a duplicate to the finding history it continues, stores the observation, then aliases it through the store so a decision already recorded there (dismissed, suppressed, or false positive) carries over to its canonical finding.
- **Review retries bypass the budget wait but are counted** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.retryReviewSlice`): A review retry replaces its failed first attempt, so retryReviewSlice creates it without waiting for the shared review budget, but records it in the created-task ledger so it counts there like any new review.
- **Conflicting retry Tasks fail only their slice** (`controller.reviewRetryIdentityConflictError`): When a Task already exists under the deterministic retry name but is not the retry this controller would render (for example one from an older build), it is never adopted. reviewRetryIdentityConflictError makes that slice fail closed instead of aborting ingestion for every run of the scan on each reconcile.
- **Retry conflicts fail the slice instead of returning an error** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.retryReviewSlice`): retryReviewSlice fails a slice on a retry identity conflict rather than returning the error, because a returned error re-runs on every reconcile and blocks ingestion for every other run of the scan.
- **Retry mismatch diagnostics name components, not contents** (`controller.reviewRetryTaskMismatch`): reviewRetryTaskMismatch names the first identity component that differs so a conflict is diagnosable from the controller log without dumping either Task; prompts are compared by digest and length only.
- **Retry mismatch reports spec field paths only** (`controller.reviewRetryTaskMismatch`): The retry mismatch message is persisted into the scan run and the RepositoryScan condition, and a pre-created conflicting Task can carry inline env values, system prompts, or credential-bearing URLs, so reviewRetryTaskMismatch reports only differing field paths, never values.
- **Retry mismatch diagnostics name at most 8 paths** (`controller.reviewRetryMismatchPathLimit`): reviewRetryMismatchPathLimit caps how many differing spec field paths a retry conflict names so a very different Task cannot bloat the persisted run error.
- **Scan Task identity normalizes API server defaults** (`controller.comparableRepositoryScanTaskSpec`): comparableRepositoryScanTaskSpec applies API server defaults so stored and freshly rendered Tasks compare equal, while the deterministic prompt remains part of the identity.
- **Scan Task identity ignores admission-owned provenance** (`controller.comparableRepositoryScanTaskSpec`): comparableRepositoryScanTaskSpec clears RequestedBy and Transaction because admission owns provenance, and clears TimeZone only for unscheduled Tasks, which scheduler defaults do not affect; every execution field stays in the comparison.
- **Findings merge when they share a broken-control anchor** (`controller.findingWithSharedAnchor`): findingWithSharedAnchor treats two findings as the same broken control when both cite an overlapping root_control location or, when neither marks one, an overlapping sink. Findings without such roles never match, so reviews that do not mark roles keep the previous behavior.
- **Shared sinks match only without a named root control** (`controller.findingWithSharedAnchor`): findingWithSharedAnchor skips sink matching for a candidate that names a root_control location, because a shared sink identifies the same issue only when neither finding names its broken control.
- **Errored revalidation keeps the earlier verdict** (`controller.recordValidationError`): recordValidationError marks a validation that produced no verdict, but a revalidation that errors keeps the verdict it was meant to refresh, read from the finding's existing validation JSON, so a refuted finding stays refuted.
- **Validator-calibrated ratings stick to the finding** (`controller.applyValidatedRatings`): applyValidatedRatings adopts the severity and confidence a validator calibrated for a validated finding, so a merged or re-reported finding keeps those ratings instead of the reviewer's originals.
- **Validation status merge ranks verdicts above errors** (`controller.findingValidationStatusRank`): findingValidationStatusRank orders validation results when a duplicate merges into its canonical finding. An error ends a pending validation without a verdict, so it outranks pending but never outranks a verdict.
- **Uploaded patch summaries get terminal-result validation** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskArtifacts`): A pre-existing patch summary artifact is worker-supplied through the upload API, so verifyPatchTaskArtifacts normalizes it with the same bounded, credential-rejecting validation as a harness-v2 terminal result before it can become durable evidence.
- **Patch publication compares canonical clone URLs** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifiedSecurityPatchPublication`): Patch Task workspaces are built from the canonical HTTPS clone URL, so verifiedSecurityPatchPublication compares workspace and scan repositories in canonical form, keeping SSH-specified scans bound to the same repository identity.
- **Patch publication is verified before evidence is derived** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.updatePatchProposalFromSucceededTask`): updatePatchProposalFromSucceededTask verifies the patch publication first, because the harness-v2 evidence path derives the reviewable diff from the exact commit that verification proves.
- **Patch proposal failure reasons are sanitized** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.failPatchProposal`): Failure reasons can embed agent-echoed text such as a parser error quoting the supplied result kind, so failPatchProposal strips controls and redacts credential shapes before the reason is persisted and logged.
- **Legacy patch Tasks need occurrence identity to be adopted** (`controller.patchTaskMatchesCurrentFindingOccurrence`): Older patch Tasks stored the proposal ID in the scan-ID label. patchTaskMatchesCurrentFindingOccurrence keeps an explicitly current proposal replayable but requires finding occurrence identity before adopting a proposal onto a finding with no current patch work.
- **Review Task ledger is created before reconciles copy the reconciler** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.SetupWithManager`): SetupWithManager initializes the created-review-Task ledger before the controller starts, because reconciles work on copies of the reconciler and must share one ledger.
- **Patch artifacts bind to commit content** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): verifyPatchTaskEvidence accepts a diff artifact only when its change content matches the published commit, not merely its list of file names, so a stale or fabricated diff with the right paths fails closed.
- **Ingestion conflicts fail the slice, not the reconcile** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestScanTask`): ingestScanTask never adopts a conflicting Task and does not return the conflict as a reconcile error, because that would retry on every reconcile and block ingestion for every run of the scan; the slice fails closed with a diagnostic instead.
- **GitHub outages during patch verification retry** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskArtifacts`): verifyPatchTaskArtifacts returns transport errors and server-side GitHub statuses as reconcile errors so controller-runtime retries, because nothing else reconciles an unscheduled completed scan again; only client-side statuses settle the proposal as failed.
- **Uploaded summary artifacts are validated like results** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): A pre-existing summary artifact is worker-supplied through the upload API, so verifyPatchTaskEvidence applies the same bounded, credential-rejecting validation as a harness-v2 terminal result before it becomes durable evidence.
- **Durable diff evidence is redacted** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): Once a worker-written diff artifact is bound to the published commit, its durable copy carries the same credential redaction as the result-contract path, so a remediation that removed a checked-in secret does not preserve it in referenced evidence.
- **Consistent artifact pairs still need commit match** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): A diff/summary pair can be internally consistent yet stale or namespace-seeded, so verifyPatchTaskEvidence rejects it unless it matches the exact published commit's file set.
- **PR decoration keeps intent marker last** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.decorateSecurityPatchPullRequest`): decorateSecurityPatchPullRequest rewrites the publisher's generic pull request with finding details but keeps the publisher's intent marker as the final body line, which the publisher relies on to recognize its pull request.
- **In-hunk +++ and --- lines are content** (`controller.samePatchHunks`): samePatchHunks treats lines inside a hunk that begin with +++ or --- as change content, not file headers, so they participate in the comparison and cannot hide a change.
- **Truncated commit patches fail closed** (`controller.repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit`): repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit rejects a nonempty file patch whose added/deleted line totals disagree with GitHub's reported counts, because that marks a truncated fragment that cannot serve as evidence.
- **Commit paths are not normalized** (`controller.repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit`): repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit must not trim or otherwise normalize file names, because a legal Git path such as one with a leading space would silently change identity.
- **Repeated diff blocks invalidate a diff** (`controller.samePatchHunks`): samePatchHunks rejects an artifact that repeats a diff --git block for the same path, with or without hunks, so extra hunks or arbitrary lines cannot hide behind a second block that matches the commit.
- **No content before the first hunk** (`controller.samePatchHunks`): samePatchHunks rejects a block containing reviewer-visible lines such as +fake between the diff header and its first hunk, since those lines are shown to reviewers but not compared.
- **Redacted stored diffs stay verified** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): The result-contract path persists the commit diff credential-redacted, so on a later reconcile the pre-existing-artifact branch must compare against redaction and not fail an already verified proposal because [REDACTED] differs from the removed secret.
- **Investigation env escapes Kubernetes expansion** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.mapperCrossComponentEnv`): mapperCrossComponentEnv doubles $ in investigation text because the kubelet expands $(VAR) references and reduces $$ to $, so the worker receives the text literally.
- **Stale cache does not fail a retried slice** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.collectScanRunProgress`): collectScanRunProgress keeps a slice active when the cache has not yet seen its retry, so a run never ends on the failure the retry replaces.
- **Review top-up counts from an authoritative read** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.topUpReviewTasks`): topUpReviewTasks counts outstanding reviews from an authoritative API read rather than the cache, because the cache can miss a retry created earlier in the same reconcile and the bound would be exceeded.
- **First review batch assumes one prompt** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskWindow`): Before any review is queued and without a known pool, reviewTaskWindow sizes the first batch as if the pool serves one prompt, regardless of configured dispatch limits.
- **Review window follows the smaller limit** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskWindow`): Once reviews are queued, reviewTaskWindow uses the smaller of the dispatch limit and the pool's capacity, and an existing pool keeps the capacity it was created with.
- **Recent reviews count before the cache sees them** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskLedger`): reviewTaskBudget counts reviews recorded in reviewTaskLedger moments ago, including another scan's review on the same pool, so concurrent scans cannot oversubscribe the shared dispatcher while the cache lags.
- **Re-reported findings keep calibrated ratings** (`controller.mergeFindingValidationState`): mergeFindingValidationState keeps the ratings an earlier successful validation calibrated when a finding is reported again; a refuted validation leaves the reviewer's ratings in place.
- **First batch uses the threat model's pool** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reviewTaskWindow`): When the threat model ran on a known pool, reviewTaskWindow sizes the first review batch from that pool, since reviews run on the same pool.
- **Identity-conflict cancellation runs after rollback** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.cancelScanRunAfterIdentityConflict`): cancelScanRunAfterIdentityConflict cancels the scan run only after the ingestion transaction has rolled back, so the cancellation persists without keeping any stale result writes from the conflicting ingestion.
- **Ingestion commits before Kubernetes follow-up** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.finishScanTaskIngestion`): Scan Task results commit to SQLite before validation Tasks or diagnostic artifacts are published; finishScanTaskIngestion uses a pending ingestion receipt to retry only that follow-up work, never the result writes.
- **Follow-up only for this run's findings** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.finishScanTaskIngestion`): finishScanTaskIngestion only schedules follow-up for findings still owned by the ingesting scan and run, because a later scan may already have observed and taken over the canonical finding.
- **Task cleanup keeps ingestion receipts** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestScanTask`): Task history cleanup may delete old scan Tasks and their artifacts, but ingestion receipts and completed run history are kept so retained runs are never re-ingested or recounted.
- **Progression ingests stages before using them** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestOwnedTasks`): A threat-model or mapper Task can finish after ingestOwnedTasks listed it, so scan progression, the first step to see it terminal, must ingest that Task before trusting its output.
- **Ingestion recovery uses the receipt** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.ingestScanTask`): ingestScanTask recovers pending follow-up from the ingestion receipt even when the original Task results were cleaned up or the source slice now belongs to a later run.
- **Validation Task names are deterministic** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.finishScanTaskIngestion`): Validation Task names must not depend on timestamps: lists can lag a successful creation, and a retry must reuse the same name so Create returns AlreadyExists instead of duplicating the Task.
- **Remediation commits are capped at 300 files** (`controller.repositoryScanPublishedCommitMaxFiles`): repositoryScanPublishedCommitMaxFiles is 300 because GitHub's commit endpoint returns at most 300 files; a larger remediation cannot be fully verified and fails closed.
- **Two patch evidence contracts** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): verifyPatchTaskEvidence accepts either pre-existing diff and summary artifacts that verify against each other and the published commit, or an identity-bound orka.security.patch.v1 harness-v2 result. In the result contract the reviewable diff is never taken from agent output: it is derived from the exact commit the governed publication proved on the remote, fetched with the forge credential, changedFiles must match it exactly, and both artifacts are persisted under the standard names so the API and dashboard keep one contract.
- **Pre-existing artifacts bind to the published commit** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): Pre-existing patch artifacts are internally consistent but unproven, because the namespace-scoped internal upload API or a stale earlier attempt could seed a consistent pair for unrelated content; verifyPatchTaskEvidence binds them to the exact published commit before accepting them as evidence.
- **Worker artifacts are rewritten sanitized** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.persistSanitizedPatchArtifacts`): Worker-written patch artifacts are raw, so after validation they are rewritten with the normalized summary and the credential-redacted diff; a remediation that removed a checked-in credential must not keep it in referenced evidence, and later reconciles still bind the sanitized diff through the dual comparison in verifyArtifactDiffMatchesPublishedCommit.
- **Published diffs are redacted after checks** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyPatchTaskEvidence`): A published diff can legitimately carry a removed credential on a deleted line, so the durable diff artifact is credential-redacted and control-stripped, while path and changed-file checks run on the unmodified diff first.
- **GitHub reads use a bounded HTTP client** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.repositoryScanHTTPClient`): repositoryScanHTTPClient falls back to a client with a timeout instead of http.DefaultClient, which has none, because a stalled GitHub response would block the scan reconcile worker indefinitely.
- **Diff artifacts bind to commit hunk content** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.verifyArtifactDiffMatchesPublishedCommit`): Filenames alone are spoofable, so verifyArtifactDiffMatchesPublishedCommit requires each file's complete hunk body, path headers, positions, context, and changed lines to equal the published commit's, ignoring only index headers that vary between diff generators. File-mode transitions must match the full Git diff; rename, copy, similarity, and binary metadata are unsupported. The stored diff may match either the raw or the deterministically sanitized commit diff, because the result-contract path stores it redacted and a verified proposal must not fail on its second reconcile.
- **Repeated file blocks invalidate a diff** (`controller.patchHunksByFile`): patchHunksByFile marks a diff invalid when any path has a second file block, with or without hunks, because a second header could hide content the comparison never sees.
- **Only diff metadata may precede hunks** (`controller.patchHunksByFile`): patchHunksByFile rejects any non-metadata line outside hunks, because a fabricated reviewer-facing line before the first hunk would render in the artifact yet escape comparison.
- **Forge token never leaves the controller** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.repositoryScanForgeToken`): repositoryScanForgeToken reads the scan's forge credential only to authenticate the read of the published commit; the token is never persisted, logged, or passed to workers.
- **Transient GitHub failures retry verification** (`controller.errRepositoryScanPublishedCommitTransient`): errRepositoryScanPublishedCommitTransient marks transport errors, request timeouts, throttling, and server-side GitHub errors; it is returned as a reconcile error so patch verification retries instead of settling the proposal as failed, while other non-2xx statuses such as 401, 403, 404, and 422 are terminal.
- **Full diff binds file modes** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.fetchRepositoryScanPublishedDiff`): fetchRepositoryScanPublishedDiff binds GitHub's full diff, which carries file modes, to the complete paginated JSON file list and line counts for the same SHA, because JSON file patches alone cannot prove executable-bit changes.
- **Commit file pages are followed explicitly** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.fetchRepositoryScanPublishedCommit`): The commit endpoint paginates files with a documented per_page maximum of 100, so fetchRepositoryScanPublishedCommit follows pages explicitly up to repositoryScanPublishedCommitMaxFiles and fails closed on anything beyond.
- **Transport errors are logged, not returned** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.fetchRepositoryScanPublishedCommit`): A transport failure reading the published commit returns errRepositoryScanPublishedCommitTransient so the verification retries; the underlying error may carry the request URL, so it is logged rather than returned into status.
- **Unreviewable commit files fail closed** (`controller.repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit`): repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit renders the commit's file patches as one canonical git diff and fails closed on renames, copies, and files without a textual patch such as binary or oversized files, since they cannot be reviewed as a patch.
- **Whitespace-altered paths fail closed** (`controller.repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit`): repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit rejects a file whose name changes when trimmed, because a legal filename with leading or trailing whitespace would otherwise verify as a different path.
- **Line counts detect truncated patches** (`controller.repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit`): GitHub can serve a nonempty but truncated patch for a large change, so repositoryScanDiffFromPublishedCommit fails closed when a file patch's line totals disagree with the reported additions and deletions.
- **PR decoration is optional and idempotent** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.decorateSecurityPatchPullRequest`): decorateSecurityPatchPullRequest updates only a PR that still carries the exact publisher-created title and body, keeps the publisher's reconciliation markers at the end so the clean-room publisher still recognizes it, and logs failures without affecting the proposal, because decoration is cosmetic.
- **Decoration recognizes legacy publisher text** (`controller.repositoryScanPublisherPresentation`): repositoryScanPublisherPresentation recognizes only unedited publisher text, including PRs published before Task-authored presentation was supported, so older remediation PRs are still decorated.
- **Task-authored PR text is not overwritten** (`controller.repositoryScanPublisherPresentation`): An explicit Task PR title belongs to its author, so repositoryScanPublisherPresentation skips decoration; a body-only override still gets the finding title, but its body is left untouched.
- **Only the publisher footer is disposable** (`controller.repositoryScanPublisherMarkersValid`): repositoryScanPublisherMarkersValid accepts only the publisher's footer with its optional Session ownership marker last; any additional text is treated as a human edit and blocks decoration.
- **Post-create run validation fences late Tasks** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.createOrValidateScanStageTask`): createOrValidateScanStageTask revalidates run ownership after Create because retirement may have listed Tasks between preflight and Create; on loss it requests cancellation, which fences that cleanup and makes the late Task retryable.
- **Unscheduled scans replace stuck runs** (`controller.RepositoryScanReconciler.reconcileScanRunIdentity`): An unscheduled scan whose run lost identity starts a replacement run instead of staying in Scanning, and late output from the old run is never consumed by the new one.
- **Native retirement gate is read-only and fail-closed** (`controller.verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement is a read-only gate for the dispatcher: missing evidence returns false and never yields an absence-based cleanup receipt. It works after the pool or Pod is gone, never consults a replacement instance, and callers must keep their frozen-turn checks and epoch-guarded writes.
- **Retired-boot receipts use cached candidates only** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordRetiredRuntimePoolTasks`): recordRetiredRuntimePoolTasks uses one cached Task list per call only to select candidates; each cleanup receipt is written after an authoritative read under the epoch guard for the execution identity the witness matched, a candidate the cache misses is replayed later, and receipted Tasks are skipped so history costs one in-memory scan instead of an uncached list per retired boot.
- **Epoch interlocks stay short** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordRetiredRuntimePoolTasks`): recordRetiredRuntimePoolTasks takes a separate agentRuntimeRecoveryGuard per Task so a namespace's historical Task list cannot turn one mutation guard into an unbounded cleanup loop.
- **Boot evidence only for enrolled native instances** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain adds durable boot evidence only for enrolled native instances; workspace pools, provider and legacy instances, and a marked boot that never retained its Pod or committed a witness keep using the authenticated drained-receipt path.
- **Unenrolled boots retire by quiescent drain** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): Admission requires a committed witness, written only after the Pod is retained, and retention is released only after retirement proof; so a marked boot with neither witness nor retention barrier never admitted work, for example when it became Ready after scale-down began, and its exact authenticated quiescent drain suffices, as for unmarked Pods.
- **Drain proof drops status text** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): Drained-boot retirement records keep only structured protocol, fence, lifecycle, and drain fields, never supervisor status reason or message payloads.
- **Witnesses are reloaded before retirement** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): Boot witnesses are reloaded before retirement decisions because resumed boot preparations can commit new witnesses after the first read.
- **Termination proof drops message and reason** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): Terminated-boot retirement records keep only container ID and start and finish times, because Kubernetes termination Message and Reason may contain application text.
- **Only the owning boot releases a Pod** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.releaseRuntimePoolBootPod`): When a historical boot already released retention, a later committed boot may own the same Pod, so releaseRuntimePoolBootPod leaves the Pod retained and only that later boot's own replay may release it.
- **Finalizer removal fences delayed enrollment** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.removeRuntimePoolFinalizer`): removeRuntimePoolFinalizer guards only new enrolled native owners, leaving provider and legacy finalization unchanged, and uses an optimistic owner PATCH so a delayed enrollment cannot race finalizer removal.
- **Pod deletion is not retirement proof** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): A normal API deletion of a native runtime Pod must keep the exact Pod retained long enough to observe the container's terminal state; absence from the API alone is never retirement proof.
- **Start-failed Tasks may lack a boot ID** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordRetiredRuntimePoolTasks`): Dispatch planning records the instance and RuntimeSession identity on the Task, but the supervisor boot is recorded only after the RuntimeSession is created, so retirement must match a start-failed Task whose boot ID is empty.
- **Retirement gate does not need the pool receipt** (`controller.verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement, used by a deleting Task's own cleanup path, proves retirement from boot evidence and must not depend on a cleanup receipt the pool already wrote.
- **Unenrolled boots drain through rollout too** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): After a leader change or controller restart renders a new per-epoch template, the rollout path adopts a Ready, never-enrolled boot and drains it; that drain must complete so the pool moves to the new epoch and the new template can start.
- **Unenrolled path needs all evidence absent** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): The never-enrolled drain path applies only when both the witness and the retention barrier are absent; either half of the barrier, or a reserved but uncommitted witness, shows enrollment reached retention, so the drain fails closed and writes no cleanup receipt.
- **Enrolled boots still require a witness** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): An enrolled boot keeps its retention barrier until retirement is proven, so if its witness is lost the drain keeps failing closed with no receipt, no retirement record, and no release of the retained Pod.
- **Boot witnesses gate native admission** (`controller.runtimePoolBootWitness`): runtimePoolBootWitness carries no credentials or Pod environment payloads. Its immutable preparation is written before the Pod is retained, only a committed witness may admit work, and the evidence lives in the pool namespace independently of the cross-namespace Pod and the mutable ActiveInstance pointer.
- **Empty evidence reservations are skipped** (`controller.emptyRuntimePoolBootEvidenceReservation`): A boot evidence record that was reserved but never written, as an interrupted controller leaves it, cannot have retained a Pod or admitted work, so retirement and recovery skip it.
- **Native boot admission is template opt-in** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.observeNativeRuntimePoolBoot`): observeNativeRuntimePoolBoot brackets the authenticated probe with uncached physical reads, and only templates carrying the native marker use this admission path; legacy templates use the separate authenticated-drain rollout path.
- **Template revision is compared verbatim** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.nativeRuntimePoolBootOwners`): The runtime template revision annotation is hashed before API server defaulting, so nativeRuntimePoolBootOwners compares the Pod's inherited annotation to the Deployment's exactly instead of rehashing the server-defaulted PodTemplateSpec.
- **Native boots pin init containers** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.nativeRuntimePoolBootOwners`): The shared recovery template check omits init containers because AgentRuntime recovery forbids them, but toolbox pools render them, so nativeRuntimePoolBootOwners requires the Pod's init containers to equal the Deployment's.
- **Topology check ignores rendered init containers** (`controller.runtimePoolBootTopologySpec`): runtimePoolBootTopologySpec removes controller-rendered toolbox and skill init containers and their read-only image volumes before the shared topology check, because they run to completion before the runtime starts and never share its process namespace; any other init container, including a restartable sidecar, fails closed.
- **Owner is fenced before Pod retention** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.retainRuntimePoolBoot`): Before retaining a boot's Pod, the controller patches its owner so a delayed owner-finalizer removal is fenced and cannot race the new retention.
- **Retirement proof, not finalizer release, gates completion** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.enrollRuntimePoolBoot`): A retired boot's durable proof, not a released Pod finalizer, authorizes only completing the already-quiescent rotation or scale-down path.
- **Discarding a preparation proves nothing** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation`): discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation withdraws only a never-enrolled, unretained source; it does not certify process death, publish a witness, remove a finalizer, or create a cleanup receipt. The immutable preparation is kept for audit, and if a witness publication wins the race normal retained recovery applies. A newer pool generation or a different container boot is never evidence that an admitted process died.
- **Stop the obsolete template first** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation`): discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation scales the exact obsolete Deployment template to zero before deleting the unadmitted Pod, so its ReplicaSet cannot race the rollout with another unadmitted boot.
- **Preconditioned delete fences retention** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation`): The unadmitted Pod is deleted with resourceVersion preconditions, so a delayed retention PATCH either changes the version and defeats deletion, or loses to deletion and cannot add a finalizer.
- **A changed container is not death proof** (`controller.runtimePoolPreparedContainerChanged`): runtimePoolPreparedContainerChanged proves only that an unadmitted preparation cannot enroll the currently observed container; it deliberately supplies no termination evidence for the original container lifetime.
- **Task boot match tolerates unstarted sessions** (`controller.runtimePoolTaskBootMatches`): The dispatcher records instance and RuntimeSession identity before creating the RuntimeSession and the supervisor boot only after creation succeeds, so runtimePoolTaskBootMatches accepts an empty boot when the instance ID matches, since the instance ID is derived from the Pod UID and boot ID; a recorded boot must match exactly.
- **Witness match ignores replacement pools** (`controller.runtimePoolTaskWitnessMatches`): runtimePoolTaskWitnessMatches, shared by read-only historical cleanup and eager Task receipts, matches a Task only against its own boot witness and never compares old proof with the replacement pool.
- **Unenrolled historical boots stay unproven** (`controller.verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): verifiedNativeRuntimePoolRetirement returns false for a Task on a historical unenrolled boot even with a complete frozen identity, because a replacement's empty status cannot supply proof.
- **Retirement works without a live runtime** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): Native boot retirement must not require provider proxy admission configuration or a healthy runtime, because it runs after the old runtime is gone.
- **Boot retirement is permanent** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileNativeRuntimePoolRetirement`): Once a boot is retired it never becomes accepting again, even if a later supervisor status claims to accept work.
- **Legacy diagnostic Pods drain unenrolled** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordNativeRuntimePoolDrain`): Existing boots with diagnostic history that is inadmissible for new container-death witnesses still retire through their positive authenticated drain, without enrollment or a native retention finalizer.
- **Superseded preparations do not block rollout** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.discardSupersededRuntimePoolBootPreparation`): A superseded, unretained boot preparation is discarded rather than retried, so an obsolete unadmitted Pod cannot monopolize the pre-rollout replay and block the pool lifecycle.
- **Bootstrap nonce is public** (`controller.runtimePoolBootstrapNonceKey`): The bootstrap-nonce key holds a public per-instance nonce used by provider-hosted supervisors that boot credential-free; it binds a signed credential bootstrap request to the exact workload and grants nothing by itself.
- **Pool supervisor client has no dispatch** (`controller.RuntimePoolSupervisorClient`): RuntimePoolSupervisorClient exposes only probe and drain control to the Kubernetes reconciler and deliberately excludes Task and session dispatch, which stay with the dispatcher.
- **Provider proxy token stays out of Pod env** (`controller.RuntimePoolProviderProxyConfig`): RuntimePoolProviderProxyConfig binds managed pools to the authenticated cluster-local provider proxy; its BearerToken is copied into an epoch-scoped RuntimePool Secret and never placed in Pod environment variables.
- **Service host check accepts any cluster domain** (`controller.runtimePoolServiceHostInNamespace`): runtimePoolServiceHostInNamespace requires <service>.<namespace>.svc and accepts any DNS name after it, because Core does not know the cluster domain.
- **Provider token in sandbox is opt-in** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.AllowProviderTokenInSandbox`): AllowProviderTokenInSandbox lets a gateway-enforced native workspace whose provider cannot inject the provider-proxy token at its egress gateway receive the token through its sealed bootstrap; it is off by default, and such workspaces are refused admission.
- **Collector egress only extends gateway allowlists** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.TelemetryCollectorEgress`): TelemetryCollectorEgress admits projected OTLP collector origins only to the egress allowlist of gateway-enforced native runtimes; it has no effect without EnableTelemetry and never changes NetworkPolicy shapes.
- **Pool images come from the controller allowlist** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.AllowedImages`): AllowedImages is the controller-configured immutable image allowlist for built-in pools, because RuntimePool is controller-owned and its public spec must not select an arbitrary privileged supervisor image.
- **Routed runtimes need the Substrate router** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.SubstrateRouter`): Routed native runtimes on Agent Substrate v1 are reached only through Core's configured router with the controller's pod-certificate mTLS identity; without SubstrateRouter routed startup evidence fails closed.
- **Scale-down waits for authenticated quiescence** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.Reconcile`): A live in-memory pool keeps one replica until an authenticated drain and a separately observed Quiescent status form the scale-down barrier.
- **Drain config skips the current image allowlist** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolConfigForDrain`): runtimePoolConfigForDrain reconstructs the exact pool configuration without the current image allowlist so an old instance can still drain; callers outside deletion must first prove the historical image was authorized for this exact pool object.
- **Image provenance survives controller-owned changes** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.historicalRuntimePoolImageAuthorized`): historicalRuntimePoolImageAuthorized trusts the image-provenance condition across generations because the RuntimePool UID and the CRD's immutable image, profile, trust domain, runtime namespace, and workspace binding fix the workload identity, while controller-owned replica and capacity changes may advance generation without invalidating it.
- **Session workspaces prove provenance by lineage** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.historicalWorkspaceRuntimePoolSessionLineageMatches`): Session workspaces may outlive every Task that used them, so historical image authorization uses the core admission marker and condition plus the immutable Session UID, deterministic pool name, and append-once lineage digest to bind the workspace to this runtime image, profile, and workspace configuration.
- **runtimeNamespace cannot steer controller resources** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolNamespace`): The controller creates the pool's Deployment, Secrets, Service, NetworkPolicy, and PDB on the caller's behalf, so runtimePoolNamespace accepts an explicit spec.runtimeNamespace only when it equals the controller-configured runtime namespace or the pool's own namespace and rejects anything else, preventing a namespaced RuntimePool creator from placing them elsewhere.
- **Toolbox failure surfaces on restarted Pods** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileRuntimePoolServingWithPostProbeFence`): A restarted active Pod that fails its toolbox checks still sets ToolboxUnavailable so waiting Tasks fail instead of retrying against a fence that can never serve again.
- **Recycling rereads the Pod after enrollment** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileRuntimePoolServingWithPostProbeFence`): Enrollment patches Pod retention, so exact recycling rereads the Pod and uses its new resourceVersion instead of the cached pre-enrollment deletion precondition.
- **Evictions preempt capacity drains** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileRuntimePoolServingWithPostProbeFence`): A worker eviction starts its deadline-bound retirement even during a capacity drain, because the rotation path would otherwise keep handling the Pod without the eviction deadline.
- **Substrate identity rotation drops the checkpoint** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileRuntimePoolIdentityCapacityRotation`): Data-only Substrate pools store the child UID/GID allocator high-water with the DurableDir data; cold-booting the same actor keeps identity capacity exhausted, and resetting it would reuse a prior child UID/GID, so recycleSubstrateActor records checkpoint loss before teardown. Safe rollover would need a separate durable-ownership migration contract.
- **Workspace bootstrap Secrets stay private** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensurePrivateWorkspaceRuntimePoolSecrets`): ensurePrivateWorkspaceRuntimePoolSecrets gives provider-workspace bootstrap credentials unpredictable names that never enter provider-visible templates, publishes the auth Secret's exact name and immutable UID only after creation, and seeds credentials only after exact workload checks.
- **Consumed auth Secrets rotate in steps** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.rotateConsumedWorkspaceRuntimePoolAuthSecret`): After a bound physical instance is gone, rotateConsumedWorkspaceRuntimePoolAuthSecret first unpublishes the consumed Secret binding, lets the create-before-publish path delete the unbound Secret and create fresh credentials, and finally clears the old instance binding before any replacement workload exists.
- **Secret ownership without ownerReferences** (`controller.runtimePoolManagedCredentialSecret`): Pools may manage a dedicated runtime namespace where cross-namespace ownerReferences are forbidden, so runtimePoolManagedCredentialSecret treats exact immutable ownership labels, generated-name reconstruction, and data shape as the ownership boundary.
- **Pool Secrets use uncached reads** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureRuntimePoolSecret`): Pool Secrets always live in the runtime namespace while the manager cache is scoped independently, so ensureRuntimePoolSecret and ensureRuntimePoolProviderSecret read them directly to stay correct regardless of cache scope drift.
- **PID limit is an annotation** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolPodTemplate`): Kubernetes has no portable PodSpec PID limit, so runtimePoolPodTemplate sets a bounded 4096 PID annotation consumed by supported runtime and admission integrations when available.
- **Toolbox admission fails closed** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxAdmission`): runtimePoolToolboxAdmission rejects a pool whose frozen profile declares toolboxes or skills the controller cannot bind: the feature is off, mounting is unavailable, the mount method is invalid, the runtime has no native skills folder, or the pool is workspace-backed; such pools report ToolboxUnavailable.
- **Toolbox images are rechecked against today's allowlist** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxAdmission`): A frozen profile was admitted under the toolbox registry allowlist of its day, so runtimePoolToolboxAdmission rechecks it against the current allowlist and removing a registry stops existing pools from recreating Pods with its images.
- **Toolbox failure drains in two phases** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.finishRuntimePoolToolboxFailure`): finishRuntimePoolToolboxFailure first persists an admission-closed barrier with ToolboxUnavailable while keeping the active-instance fence so the dispatcher cannot reserve; later reconciles stop the Deployment only after authenticated quiescence or confirmed Pod absence and clear the fence only once stopped, so no Pod keeps a rejected image and in-flight work is never interrupted.
- **Toolbox idle check probes live state** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxWorkloadIdle`): runtimePoolToolboxWorkloadIdle runs before the rollout probe refreshes capacity counters, so it observes live state: no controller reservation or finalization work, and either a confirmed-gone active Pod or every Ready supervisor that may hold work reporting the full drained-and-quiescent invariant. A NotReady or unreachable active supervisor is never assumed idle.
- **Unrecorded Ready Pods must drain** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxWorkloadIdle`): A Pod can become Ready after admission closed but before any instance was recorded, so runtimePoolToolboxWorkloadIdle requires each Ready Pod to pass the authenticated drain before the workload stops.
- **Drain authenticates the deployed instance** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained`): Admission may change across a controller epoch, so runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained authenticates the exact deployed instance using its deployed configuration, not the credentials rendered for its replacement.
