- **In-place restarts are recycled** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained`): When a supervisor restarted in place its old session tree is no longer trustworthy, so runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained stops the Deployment through the UID-fenced recycle path instead of repeatedly comparing the old boot with the new one.
- **Drain needs request then quiescent probe** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained`): The toolbox drain uses the rollout two-step barrier: the supervisor itself must stop accepting sessions, closing the window between a dispatcher's stale pool read and scale-down, and only a later probe showing it fully quiescent allows the stop.
- **Task receipts precede scale-down** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolToolboxPodDrained`): Task-scoped cleanup receipts are written while the authenticated retirement fence still exists, and a failed write prevents scale-down and fence removal.
- **Status conflicts requeue quietly** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.finishRuntimePoolStatus`): When another writer such as the drain, session, or probe path advanced the pool first, finishRuntimePoolStatus requeues to recompute from the fresh object instead of returning the optimistic-lock conflict as a reconcile error.
- **Probe pressure needs an active fence** (`controller.clearRuntimePoolUnfencedProbePressure`): Supervisor pressure belongs to one exact authenticated runtime instance, so clearRuntimePoolUnfencedProbePressure drops it when no active instance fence exists, rather than publishing stale sessions and prompts for a lost, rejected, or recycled Pod.
- **Cross-namespace children skip ownerReferences** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.setRuntimePoolControllerReference`): Namespaced ownerReferences cannot cross namespaces, so setRuntimePoolControllerReference skips them for children in another namespace and relies on the pool finalizer and immutable ownership labels for cleanup.
- **Supervisor probes bypass env proxies** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.supervisorClient`): Supervisor probes and drains send authenticated headers to exact Pod endpoints, so supervisorClient uses a proxyless transport and environment proxies never carry them.
- **Routed pools are reached only via the router** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.supervisorClientForPool`): supervisorClientForPool reaches a pool bound to a routed native process only through Core's configured Substrate router with its exact target header; every other pool dials its exact bound endpoint directly.
- **Routed endpoints come only from Core bindings** (`controller.runtimePoolInstanceEndpoint`): runtimePoolInstanceEndpoint takes a routed endpoint only from Core's exact native-process binding; real runtime Pods always use their independently observed address.
- **Status probes prove the capability secret** (`controller.runtimePoolHTTPSupervisorClient.Probe`): Probe requires proof of the pool's capability secret in addition to the bearer token, bound to the profile digest and carrying a single-use nonce, while the boot ID and instance ID remain the values status discovers.
- **Old epoch Secrets outlive old ReplicaSets** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureRuntimePoolSecret`): Epoch rotation keeps the previous auth and provider Secrets while any old ReplicaSet can still create Pods referencing them through projected or envFrom volumes, and prunes them only after a later reconcile observes no live workload reference.
- **Service match ignores API-allocated fields** (`controller.externalCoreServiceMatches`): externalCoreServiceMatches clears IP families, IP family policy, internal traffic policy, and default session affinity before comparing, because the API server defaults or allocates them and they are not Core permissions.
- **Same-name replacements are foreign** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCoreDiscoveryResources`): deleteExternalCoreDiscoveryResources deletes only the exact recorded UID; once it disappears, a same-name replacement is foreign and is neither deleted nor allowed to hold up the Core allocation's finalizer.
- **Unissued credential intents own nothing** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recoverExternalCredential`): recoverExternalCredential treats a credential intent whose create was never issued as granting no authority over an existing same-name Secret.
- **Rejected creates record a no-effect receipt** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.createExternalCredential`): When the API affirmatively rejects a credential Secret create without creating the object, createExternalCredential persists that no-effect receipt before any retry or name reselection.
- **Legacy bootstrap credentials use saved UIDs** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordLegacyBootstrapCredential`): A legacy bootstrap binding may outlive the epoch's name binding during cold resume, so recordLegacyBootstrapCredential treats its saved Secret UID, never a public label, as the authority.
- **Dropping a receipt never claims a replacement** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCredentialIntent`): A rejected or never-issued create, or absence of the exact recorded UID, removes only that credential receipt and never grants ownership of a same-name foreign replacement.
- **Legacy auth bindings are UID authority** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalPrivateCredentials`): deleteExternalPrivateCredentials treats legacy auth bindings as exact Core UID authority; legacy provider and unbound auth inventory has no such authority and is left untouched.
- **Discovery receipts survive write conflicts** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.saveExternalDiscoveryIntents`): saveExternalDiscoveryIntents compares only the receipt, not the whole pool; after a conflict or lost response it re-reads, rewrites an unchanged receipt, and accepts one already written, so an unrelated pool write cannot drop an acknowledged create's UID that no read could recover.
- **Receipt retries patch only their key** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.saveExternalDiscoveryIntents`): On retry saveExternalDiscoveryIntents rebases on the current annotations so the patch diffs only the receipt key; a stale map could null out a concurrent annotation.
- **Discovery objects are owned by saved UID** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureExternalDiscoveryResource`): Cross-namespace discovery objects cannot carry ownerReferences, so only a durably saved UID from an acknowledged create authorizes reuse or deletion; an unknown creation outcome stays closed because public labels and specs cannot recover a UID.
- **Egress allowlist providers reject NetworkPolicy** (`controller.externalProviderRequiresEgressAllowlist`): externalProviderRequiresEgressAllowlist identifies providers, such as Agent Substrate v1, whose gateway-enforced backend accepts only the host-name egress allowlist and rejects a Kubernetes NetworkPolicy; they advertise the runtime.egress-allowlist feature.
- **Workspace network shape is frozen** (`controller.externalRuntimeUsesEgressAllowlist`): externalRuntimeUsesEgressAllowlist gives the egress allowlist only to native processes on gateway-enforcing backends while Pod-backed runtimes keep NetworkPolicy, and refuses a successor whose shape differs from its admitted predecessor rather than silently dropping policy.
- **Token injection or sealed bootstrap** (`controller.externalProviderTokenInjection`): externalProviderTokenInjection names the Secret key the egress gateway injects as the provider-proxy bearer; when it is nil Core delivers the token through the runtime's sealed bootstrap instead.
- **Injected-token placeholder grants nothing** (`controller.externalProviderTokenEgressPlaceholder`): A runtime whose provider-proxy bearer is gateway-injected sends a public placeholder in Authorization; the gateway discards it and the proxy never accepts it.
- **Runtime egress allowlist compilation** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgress`): externalRuntimeEgress compiles Core's egress intent into the host-name allowlist: the in-cluster provider proxy and controller broker API, plus projected OTLP collectors when collector egress and telemetry are enabled. Cleartext origins become HTTP rules and TLS origins TLSPassthrough so the runtime verifies the origin certificate; unexpressible origins fail admission. With injection the provider-proxy origin gets its own rule replacing Authorization with Core's provider Secret token so the token never enters the runtime, which requires a cleartext proxy origin not shared with another admitted origin because the gateway re-originates TLS trusting only public roots.
- **Collector egress follows exported endpoints** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgressForCollectors`): externalRuntimeEgressForCollectors admits exactly the collector endpoints the runtime environment exports to, so a signal dropped to fit the native environment leaves no collector origin behind.
- **Egress rules are sorted** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgressForCollectors`): Egress rules are sorted by host and port because the workload request revision hashes the request and must be stable.
- **Only the provider token may be injected** (`controller.externalRuntimeProviderTokenInjection`): externalRuntimeProviderTokenInjection accepts an admitted request's credential injection only when it injects Core's provider Secret token into Authorization.
- **Gateway runtimes need fully qualified Service names** (`controller.externalEgressHostResolvable`): externalEgressHostResolvable refuses a <service>.<namespace>.svc origin for gateway-enforced runtimes, because a Substrate Actor's resolver tries search suffixes its DNS relay refuses and cluster DNS answers only the fully qualified form; Core does not know the cluster domain, so it names the form to use instead of rewriting the host. Pod-backed runtimes resolve through their search path and skip the check.
- **Bootstrap must match the admitted provider Secret** (`controller.externalNativeCredentialBootstrap`): externalNativeCredentialBootstrap refuses a bootstrap for a different provider Secret than the admitted injection rule names, treating it as stale instead of seeding the placeholder.
- **Injection requires the egress allowlist** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgress`): Credential injection is a gateway feature; a native provider claiming it without the egress allowlist would get a NetworkPolicy-shaped request whose bootstrap carries the real token, so admission refuses it.
- **Eviction records bind one exact instance** (`controller.externalRuntimeEvictionAnnotation`): The external-runtime-eviction annotation records the observed eviction of the exact current instance, bound to workspace UID, sequence, and instance identity; a record that does not match authorizes nothing.
- **DisruptionTarget starts the eviction window** (`controller.podConditionDisruptionTarget`): Kubernetes sets the DisruptionTarget Pod condition before a disruptive termination that may not delete the Pod first, such as graceful node shutdown, so eviction detection treats it as a termination signal.
- **No suspension after the eviction deadline** (`controller.externalRuntimeEviction`): externalRuntimeEviction.Deadline ends the eviction window, and Suspend is never authorized at or after it.
- **Eviction outcome is acknowledged per instance** (`controller.externalRuntimeEviction`): externalRuntimeEviction.Outcome is written only after the matching status write is confirmed, so a WorkerEviction condition with the same reason left by an earlier instance is never mistaken for this instance's outcome.
- **Malformed eviction records are expired** (`controller.currentExternalRuntimeEviction`): currentExternalRuntimeEviction returns a record only when it binds the workspace's exact current instance, and treats a malformed record as an eviction whose window already ended.
- **Evicted workers are stale for admission** (`controller.externalWorkerEvictionError`): externalWorkerEvictionError reports that the exact attested native worker began graceful termination, and admission treats it as a stale identity.
- **Eviction window never extends** (`controller.externalWorkerEvictionSignal`): externalWorkerEvictionSignal starts the window at the kubelet signal (deletion time less grace) or an earlier DisruptionTarget condition, and ends it at the earlier of the worker shutdown budget and the Pod's grace; with both signals the earlier start and earlier deadline win, so a deletion after a disruption never reopens or extends the window, and every signal is clamped to Core's clock so a clock ahead of Core never extends it.
- **Drain requests carry the retirement action** (`controller.externalRetirementAction`): externalRetirementAction is the provider action Core will authorize for the exact current instance once its drain settles, and drain requests carry it so a supervisor whose worker is later terminated knows whether its data must remain suspendable.
- **Quarantine never earns Suspend** (`controller.externalRetirementAction`): A quarantined workspace is terminal and never reusable, so externalRetirementAction never grants it the data-preserving Suspend authorization; providers act on quarantine only through Stop or Delete.
- **Failed instances are not suspended** (`controller.externalRetirementAction`): A failed instance has no new data to capture, so externalRetirementAction does not suspend it; its last verified checkpoint stays with the provider for explicit recovery.
- **Supervisor eviction signal ignores drain reason** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.supervisorWorkerEvictionSignal`): supervisorWorkerEvictionSignal reads the worker-termination timestamp independently of the drain reason, because the signal can arrive during a suspension or capacity drain that keeps its own reason; a worker_eviction drain without a timestamp counts from its request time, clamped to Core's clock.
- **Eviction reuses the authenticated drain** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.beginExternalRuntimeEviction`): beginExternalRuntimeEviction records the eviction and, for a data-only suspendable workspace, requests exact retirement through the authenticated drain: admission stays closed, accepted prompts settle, and only a quiescent runtime earns Suspend before the deadline. A non-suspendable workspace keeps the failure semantics of a lost instance.
- **Eviction records only shrink** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordExternalRuntimeEviction`): recordExternalRuntimeEviction merges a new signal with any earlier record so the earliest signal and shortest window win, and a later observation never extends the time left to suspend.
- **Eviction signals are collected during drains** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.observeExternalRetiringWorkerEviction`): observeExternalRetiringWorkerEviction keeps collecting eviction signals while a retirement drain runs, because termination can start during a drain begun for another reason and the Pod can reveal a shorter window than an earlier supervisor signal.
- **Only Suspend drains for suspension** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.markExternalRetiringEviction`): markExternalRetiringEviction reports draining for suspension only for a Suspend retirement, since no other retirement preserves data; a condition already reporting this eviction, even with an expired window, is kept, and one from an earlier instance is replaced.
- **Late snapshots are not trusted** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalEvictionDeadlineExceeded`): externalEvictionDeadlineExceeded refuses Suspend at or after the eviction deadline, because the worker has killed or is about to kill the instance and a late snapshot cannot be trusted to hold settled work.
- **Suspend authorizations carry notAfter** (`controller.externalSuspendNotAfter`): Core cannot withdraw a written Suspend authorization, so externalSuspendNotAfter bounds it by the instance's eviction deadline and the provider never starts a suspension after notAfter, stopping the instance instead. A written bound is kept or moved earlier, never removed or extended; other actions carry no bound, which clears it when deletion supersedes Suspend.
- **notAfter truncation is safe** (`controller.externalSuspendNotAfter`): The API stores notAfter in whole seconds by truncation, so the stored bound never extends the eviction window and then takes precedence over the in-memory record.
- **Retirement markers wait for eviction outcome** (`controller.externalEvictionSettlementPending`): externalEvictionSettlementPending keeps retirement markers while the condition reports draining, or while the record binds the exact instance without an acknowledged outcome, since a lost draining report leaves the condition absent or showing an earlier instance's outcome with possibly the same reason; a record for any other instance never makes this one pending.
- **Provider deadline report wins** (`controller.externalEvictionOutcome`): When the provider reports the Suspend notAfter passed before it started, externalEvictionOutcome reports EvictionDeadlineExceeded even if retained data also appears, because the instance stopped without preserving its data.
- **Eviction outcome is a new transition** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalEvictionSettledCondition`): Until the instance's record acknowledges an outcome, externalEvictionSettledCondition treats any non-draining condition as another instance's outcome, even with the same reason, and records the outcome as a new transition with the current time.
- **Terminating workers admit no new work** (`controller.externalWorkerEvictionError`): Admission never reaches a terminating native worker, but settlement of already accepted work and exact retirement still can.
- **Deadline is rechecked before Suspend** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalEvictionDeadlineExceeded`): The authenticated drain probe can outlast a short eviction window, so the deadline is rechecked immediately before Suspend is authorized.
- **Eviction outcome precedes cold boot** (`controller.externalEvictionSettlementPending`): An evicted instance that stopped with retained data has its outcome recorded before the pool can cold-boot a replacement, even across a status conflict or a controller restart after retirement markers were removed.
- **Suspend deadline breaches are terminal** (`controller.externalEvictionOutcome`): When a provider sees an eviction-bounded Suspend only after its deadline and stops the instance without data, Core records the lost eviction and keeps the stopped instance terminal, never publishing a successor that a data-only class would reject.
- **Native runtimes have a 32-entry env budget** (`controller.nativeRuntimeEnvironmentLimit`): The native backend admits at most 32 environment entries (nativeRuntimeEnvironmentLimit), and the in-tree native runtime, including its live conformance flags, must stay within that budget.
- **Telemetry fits the remaining env budget** (`controller.fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry`): Suspendable native runtimes add durable-workspace entries to the same 32-entry budget, so fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry fits supervisor telemetry into what remains, dropping signals if needed, and the pool reports when telemetry cannot fully fit instead of publishing a request the provider refuses.
- **TelemetryReady is cleared without telemetry** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deriveExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady`): A workload request without supervisor telemetry clears TelemetryReady left by an earlier request rather than describing a runtime that no longer exists.
- **TelemetryReady derives from the frozen request** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deriveExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady`): Only the publishing reconcile fits telemetry, so when its status write loses the optimistic lock, or a request is republished, the next reconcile re-derives TelemetryReady from the frozen request and never keeps the predecessor's condition. deriveExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady re-derives TelemetryReady from the telemetry projection frozen in the published request so it does not depend on the publishing status write landing. Only native requests are fitted; fitting is deterministic. The verdict uses the current configuration only when it yields the same projection; a request projected under a different configuration keeps its recorded condition.
- **Published telemetry keeps its condition** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deriveExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady`): A request published under another controller telemetry configuration keeps the condition recorded with it, because re-fitting it to the current configuration would describe a projection the runtime does not have.
- **Readiness compares to configured signals** (`controller.fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry`): Telemetry readiness compares the frozen projection with every currently configured signal: a trace-only projection is incomplete while metrics are configured, and complete once metrics are turned off.
- **Empty model limits are omitted** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate omits empty model limit variables because the supervisor and controller treat them as unset, saving native environment entries; set limits stay.
- **Native ingress never selects the controller** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureExternalProcessIngress`): Native-process ingress grants are written only for the isolated provider proxy; a NetworkPolicy selecting the controller would isolate it and lock out its other clients.
- **Ingress cleanup uses saved targets** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCoreNetworkPolicies`): Ingress grant cleanup uses the targets saved when the grants were written; the current controller and provider-proxy namespaces are not authority for older grants.
- **Ingress targets are fixed before writes** (`controller.externalProcessIngressTarget`): externalProcessIngressTarget endpoints, including namespace, are fixed before any NetworkPolicy write, while policy UIDs remain Kubernetes identities fenced on every write and delete.
- **Frozen ingress targets come from admitted rules** (`controller.externalFrozenIngressTargets`): externalFrozenIngressTargets derives targets only from the admitted request's endpoint rules, each with one exact TCP port and one namespace/Pod peer, skipping Core's two-port DNS rule; no observed policy is input.
- **Labels never authorize ingress deletion** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalIngressPolicies`): deleteExternalIngressPolicies deletes only policies matching the exact target and binding identity; another object's labels never authorize its deletion or block Core's.
- **Policy deletion is UID-exact** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCorePolicy`): deleteExternalCorePolicy treats a same-name replacement with a different UID as not the policy whose deletion was authorized.
- **Frozen NetworkPolicy specs gate deletion** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCoreNetworkPolicies`): deleteExternalCoreNetworkPolicies requires observed policy specs to match only when the admitted request used a NetworkPolicy shape, since that shape is frozen intent.
- **Allowlist pool policies delete by name** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalCoreNetworkPolicies`): A gateway-enforced egress-allowlist request froze no Pod peers and Core grants its routed process no worker ingress, so its pool policies are not admission intent; Core ownership of the deterministic names alone authorizes deletion, keeping cleanup independent of live provider-proxy or controller settings and credentials.
- **Defaulted probe protocol is equivalent** (`controller.normalizeRuntimePoolWorkspaceHTTPGet`): Kubernetes writes HTTP1 into HTTPGet probes when H2CContainerProbe is enabled; Core's published template omits Protocol, which already means HTTP/1.1, so template comparison must treat them as equal.
- **Routed runtimes go through Core's router** (`controller.RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter`): RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter is the controller's configured Agent Substrate router; routed native runtimes are reachable only through it, it selects the Actor from the ate-target-actor header, ignores Host, admits only allowlisted pod-certificate mTLS clients, and provider evidence can never choose another router authority.
- **Router client carries mTLS, no proxy** (`controller.RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter`): RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter.HTTPClient carries the controller's pod-certificate mTLS identity from workspace.NewSubstrateRouterHTTPClient and must not use an environment proxy.
- **Routed endpoints are pinned to the router URL** (`controller.RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter.pinnedEndpoint`): pinnedEndpoint accepts a provider-reported routed endpoint only when it equals Core's configured router URL after normalizing only the default HTTPS port and trailing slash, and returns the configured origin.
- **Routed clients reach one process** (`controller.RuntimePoolSubstrateRouter.routedHTTPClient`): routedHTTPClient builds a client that reaches exactly one routed native process: every request must address the configured router origin and carry the attested target, redirects are never followed, and a zero timeout leaves deadlines to the caller for long-lived ACP streams.
- **Routed transport refuses other destinations** (`controller.externalRouterTargetTransport`): externalRouterTargetTransport adds the router target only to requests for the configured router origin and refuses every other destination, so a routed client can never carry controller credentials elsewhere.
- **Routed transport keeps httptrace** (`controller.externalRouterTargetTransport.DelegatesHTTPTrace`): externalRouterTargetTransport passes request contexts unchanged to the underlying transport so httptrace hooks still fire and harness v2 keeps exact written/unwritten request evidence for routed calls.
- **Startup evidence must match the saved binding** (`controller.externalStartupMatchesBoundEvidence`): externalStartupMatchesBoundEvidence fails closed unless provider startup evidence names exactly the instance, endpoint, router target, and worker or Pod that Core bound before delivering credentials; provider status can change after binding and must never redirect controller credentials to another Actor sharing the instance identity, and evidence for a pool with no binding is never trusted.
- **Egress shape comes from declared features** (`controller.externalProviderRequiresEgressAllowlist`): A provider's backend API version string alone never selects the egress shape; only the advertised runtime.egress-allowlist feature does.
- **Native templates drop scratch volumes** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate relies on the native process's fresh writable container filesystem for scratch and keeps only the special durable-workspace mount separate from Pod volume declarations.
- **Native security context is honest** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): The pinned native runtime runs the supervisor as root under gVisor and its API supports capabilities but not Kubernetes seccomp or privilege-escalation controls, so runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate publishes only the guarantees it can enforce.
- **Native placement is provider-owned** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate clears the node selector because native worker placement is fixed by the provider's pinned Linux WorkerPool.
- **Native health and shutdown are provider-owned** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate drops Kubernetes probes, lifecycle hooks, and grace periods because native Actor health and shutdown are provider-owned and those fields cannot describe them.
- **Native env drops Pod identity overrides** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate`): runtimePoolNativeProcessTemplate drops Pod name and namespace overrides because the native adapter uses supervisor defaults and SystemInfo identity, and the saved entries keep headroom under the backend's 32-entry environment limit.
- **Injected durable PVC matched by mount** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspacePodSpecsMatch`): The provider injects the durable workspace PVC from volumeClaimTemplates with a per-sandbox claim name the template cannot render, so runtimePoolWorkspacePodSpecsMatch compares the reserved-name PVC volume by presence of its mount; any other unexpected volume fails the match.
- **Scheduling-derived fields are ignored** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspacePodSpecsMatch`): runtimePoolWorkspacePodSpecsMatch ignores node name, priority, preemption policy, and similar fields derived from cluster scheduling and admission rather than the provider-visible template.
- **Deprecated service account alias is normalized** (`controller.normalizeRuntimePoolWorkspacePodSpec`): The core API mirrors the effective service account into the deprecated serviceAccount alias on Pods, but embedded PodSpecs in CRDs do not get that conversion, so normalizeRuntimePoolWorkspacePodSpec sets the alias from ServiceAccountName before comparing.
- **Read-only durable PVCs fail the match** (`controller.stripInjectedDurableWorkspaceVolume`): stripInjectedDurableWorkspaceVolume strips the injected durable workspace volume only when it is writable and uses the injected claim; a read-only PVC is kept so the spec comparison fails instead of serving a workspace whose clone, edit, and commit operations cannot work.
- **Recycling external instances goes through retirement** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recycleRuntimePoolInstance`): recycleRuntimePoolInstance for an external workspace only requests retirement, because runtime settlement must finish before provider termination is authorized; the retirement reconcile performs the authenticated drain and keeps durable lineage while the replacement is pending.
- **Legacy workspace shapes are rejected** (`controller.validateRuntimePoolExecutionWorkspace`): validateRuntimePoolExecutionWorkspace rejects legacy execution-workspace shapes even after the API server prunes their removed backend settings, while plain Deployment pools remain valid.
- **Routed native endpoints are Core's router** (`controller.externalRuntimeInstanceEvidence.RouterTarget`): When externalRuntimeInstanceEvidence.RouterTarget is set, the process endpoint is Core's configured Substrate router, never an authority chosen by the provider. This keeps provider evidence from redirecting Core's authenticated supervisor calls. See [ADR 0049](0049-agent-substrate-v1.md).
- **Core writes intent, never provider state** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRuntimePool`): reconcileExternalWorkspaceRuntimePool consumes provider evidence and writes only intent. It never writes the provider's state, attachment acknowledgement, native objects, or journal, so ownership of each field stays with exactly one party. See [ADR 0027](0027-substrate-data-only-suspension.md).
- **Failed and suspend-expired instances are terminal** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRuntimePool`): A provider-reported Failed instance, or one the provider stopped because its Suspend authorization expired, is retired and never replaced in place. Such an instance kept no data, and a data-only class admits a successor only with verified retained data.
- **Eviction outcome settles before cold boot** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRuntimePool`): An eviction whose outcome was never recorded settles against the stopped allocation (externalPoolStopped) before any cold boot replaces it, so the outcome is never lost to a replacement.
- **Routed processes get no Pod ingress grant** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRuntimePool`): A routed native process has no worker Pod ingress to grant: Core reaches it only through the configured Substrate router, and its egress leaves through the backend gateway under the admitted allowlist.
- **Provider error text stays out of Task status** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalPoolWorkspaceProgress`): externalPoolWorkspaceProgress copies the provider's operation error into RuntimePool progress only because the workspace shares the pool's namespace and already carries it. Task status never receives provider text.
- **Native Actors inherit template sizing** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload clears container resources for native requests because a native Actor inherits its infrastructure template's sizing, which the operator matches to the WorkerPool, and drops Pod scratch volumes. Pod-backed requests keep the in-cluster pool's resource and scratch quotas.
- **Telemetry env is fitted last** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload fits supervisor telemetry into the environment last, using only the room other native settings leave, because the provider refuses an environment past the backend limit.
- **Gateway-enforced backends use host allowlists** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): A gateway-enforced backend has no Pod network to describe: its runtime reaches only allowlisted host names and Core's supervisor calls enter through the backend router, so publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload compiles an egress allowlist instead of NetworkPolicy.
- **Credential injection requires the egress allowlist** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): Provider-token credential injection is a gateway feature. Without the egress allowlist there is no gateway policy to inject from and the token would reach the sandbox through the bootstrap, so publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload degrades instead.
- **Native requests freeze egress only** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): For native requests, infrastructure and router ingress is operator-managed, so publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload freezes only runtime egress policy types; Pod ingress rules do not describe that topology.
- **Only Core-generated network policies are intent** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload freezes only Core's generated network permissions as admission intent. Public labels on observed policies cannot authorize additional connectivity.
- **TelemetryReady is re-derived from the request** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload`): The TelemetryReady status write in publishExternalWorkspaceWorkload may lose the optimistic lock; this is safe because every later reconcile re-derives the same condition from the published request (deriveExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady).
- **Requests without telemetry carry no TelemetryReady** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.setExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady`): setExternalRuntimeTelemetryReady removes TelemetryReady for a request without supervisor telemetry, because a condition from an earlier request would describe a runtime that no longer exists.
- **Unfrozen required features fall back to predecessor** (`controller.externalWorkspaceRuntimeRequiredFeatures`): Older pools did not freeze required runtime features, so externalWorkspaceRuntimeRequiredFeatures takes them from the admitted predecessor workload, which proves the materialization kind across restarts and cold resume.
- **Native runtime UID is an opaque Core fence** (`controller.externalNativeRuntimeUID`): externalNativeRuntimeUID is an opaque Core fence for one immutable workload sequence. Provider identifiers stay in the separate allocation evidence and never become the fence.
- **Pre-fix downward identities only retire** (`controller.externalNativeRuntimeIdentity`): Workload requests published before downward instance identity was fixed are immutable. externalNativeRuntimeIdentity keeps their admitted identity usable for exact retirement while startup and Task dispatch deny it.
- **Terminating workers attest only for non-admitting paths** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.attestExternalRetiringWorkspaceStartup`): attestExternalRetiringWorkspaceStartup accepts the exact native worker while it terminates, so only paths that never admit new work may use it: drain, settlement of accepted prompts, and exact retirement. Identity, process, and endpoint fences are otherwise unchanged.
- **Routed and direct processes are distinct** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.attestExternalWorkspaceStartupFor`): attestExternalWorkspaceStartupFor requires RouterTarget to be set exactly when the request has gateway egress: a routed process is reachable only via the backend router and confined by the gateway allowlist, while a direct process keeps NetworkPolicy shape with frozen ingress targets.
- **Volume claim namespace must match the Pod** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.attestExternalWorkspaceStartupFor`): attestExternalWorkspaceStartupFor rejects claim evidence whose namespace differs from the Pod's, because a Pod resolves claimName in its own namespace regardless of what provider evidence says.
- **Provider labels must not widen network policy** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.verifyExternalRuntimeNetworkPolicies`): verifyExternalRuntimeNetworkPolicies checks every NetworkPolicy selecting the runtime because Kubernetes unions permissions across matching policies; provider bookkeeping labels must not select rules outside the admitted network.
- **Native ingress precedes credential delivery** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureExternalProcessIngress`): ensureExternalProcessIngress binds an exact infrastructure worker before any credential delivery, and takes endpoint namespaces only from immutable Core network intent.
- **Instance fence is saved before ingress policy** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureExternalProcessIngress`): ensureExternalProcessIngress saves the worker/instance fence before creating the ingress policy, so a lost Create response can be recovered or safely retired even if the provider later clears startup evidence.
- **Only the provider proxy gets an ingress grant** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.ensureExternalProcessIngress`): ensureExternalProcessIngress grants ingress only for the provider proxy target, the only one that ships isolation. A grant for the controller would itself isolate the controller and lock out its other clients; operators who isolate the controller admit native workers themselves.
- **Provider-proxy token delivery fails closed** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgressWithProviderToken`): externalRuntimeEgressWithProviderToken injects the provider-proxy bearer at the gateway from Core's private Secret when the provider supports it, so it never enters the sandbox. Otherwise admission fails closed unless the operator explicitly allowed delivery through the sealed bootstrap.
- **Unadmittable egress origins report directly** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRuntimeEgressWithProviderToken`): externalRuntimeEgressWithProviderToken reports an origin that cannot be admitted as a configuration error on its own, rather than treating it as a reason to consider delivering the token into the sandbox.
- **Injected tokens appear only as placeholders** (`controller.externalNativeCredentialBootstrap`): When a request injects the provider-proxy token at the egress gateway, externalNativeCredentialBootstrap gives the runtime only the public placeholder, never the token.
- **No Suspend after the eviction deadline** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement never authorizes a Suspend at or after the worker's eviction deadline, since it could not prove it captured settled work. The instance fails through the provider, its last checkpoint stays retained, and accepted prompts are never replayed.
- **Replacement serves queued demand** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): During retirement, queued demand needs the replacement boot rather than the retiring instance, so reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement proceeds while replacing; terminal deletion and scale-down still wait for queued Tasks to settle.
- **Lost native process may be stopped on Failed** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): A lost native process cannot answer an authenticated drain. Its Task already settled without replay and the provider can stop that exact Actor, so reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement lets its terminal Failed report authorize Stop; credentials stay until the provider confirms.
- **Eviction signal is collected until Suspend starts** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): Termination can begin after Suspend is authorized but before the provider starts it, so reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement keeps observing the exact worker's eviction signal and may move the authorization deadline earlier. Admission lets a written Suspend only narrow; the drain is never repeated.
- **Eviction deadline is rechecked before Suspend** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement rechecks the eviction deadline immediately before authorizing Suspend because the drain probe can outlast a short eviction window.
- **Eviction outcome persists before markers clear** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalPoolStopped`): externalPoolStopped persists the eviction outcome before removing the retirement and eviction markers: the outcome is acknowledged in the eviction record after a confirmed status write, and markers clear only on a later reconcile observing that acknowledgement, so conflicts or restarts retry the recording instead of cold-booting.
- **Credential deletion order on finalization** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.finalizeExternalWorkspacePool`): finalizeExternalWorkspacePool deletes core-owned runtime credentials only after provider termination is proved. Attachment credentials are revoked by workspace finalization, independently of the provider's disposition.
- **Core never deletes provider compute** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.deleteExternalRuntimePoolCoreResources`): deleteExternalRuntimePoolCoreResources only observes termination; Core never deletes provider compute to make a provider's deletion assertion true.
- **Only current termination claims release credentials** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalObservedInstanceTerminated`): For native workers and unbootstrapped replacements, externalObservedInstanceTerminated does not treat the infrastructure worker or the terminated predecessor as the current runtime Pod; only the exact core-authorized current termination claim releases credentials.
- **Predecessor binding is compatible for cancelled replacements** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRetirementEvidenceCompatible`): A replacement may be cancelled before startup attestation while its predecessor's binding remains until credential delivery. externalRetirementEvidenceCompatible treats that binding as non-conflicting when the immutable request names that exact retired predecessor.
- **Predecessor references imply observed termination** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalRetirementEvidenceCompatible`): Core publishes a predecessor reference only after observing its authorized termination, so externalRetirementEvidenceCompatible accepts it even though the infrastructure worker may outlive that process.
- **Legacy evidence keeps its fence for cleanup** (`controller.externalRuntimeEvidence`): externalRuntimeEvidence derives RuntimeUID from the instance ID for old evidence so it keeps its exact already-admitted fence for cleanup.
- **Providers serve bindings by generation** (`controller.externalProviderServesBinding`): externalProviderServesBinding lets an Active provider act only at the frozen generation, matching Task dispatch, while a Draining provider keeps serving the workspace it already bound.
- **Startup Pod is the observed materialization** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspaceStartupPod`): runtimePoolWorkspaceStartupPod returns only the exact materialization whose identity and complete endpoint Core observed before releasing credentials.
- **Cleanup keeps legacy native fences** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspaceCleanupPod`): runtimePoolWorkspaceCleanupPod retains an already-bound legacy native fence for cleanup without admitting it to a new Task or replacing its immutable workload identity.
- **Cleanup reaches terminating workers** (`controller.runtimePoolWorkspaceCleanupEndpoint`): Cleanup never admits work, so runtimePoolWorkspaceCleanupEndpoint uses retiring attestation to keep reaching a runtime whose worker is terminating, letting already-accepted prompts settle before suspension.
- **Runtime Pod loss proves termination when quiescent** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.externalActivePodTerminationProven`): externalActivePodTerminationProven lets the absence of an independently observed runtime Pod's exact UID release retirement intent only after controller work is quiescent. Infrastructure worker Pods cannot prove this for a native process; that case uses externalNativeInstanceFailed.
- **Never-bootstrapped deletion proof** (`controller.externalNeverBootstrappedDeletionProven`): A provider may reject a request before allocating any instance. externalNeverBootstrappedDeletionProven lets that terminal no-compute proof release only core-owned resources, and only if Core never attested an instance or began credential delivery.
- **Quarantine authorizes Stop, never Suspend** (`controller.TestExternalRuntimePoolQuarantineAuthorizesStopForSuspendCapableClass`): External providers act on RuntimePool quarantine only through a Stop or Delete authorization, even for suspend-capable classes, because a Suspend authorization would leave the quarantined instance running.
- **Permission timeout env only when configured** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.runtimePoolEnvironment`): runtimePoolEnvironment adds the permission timeout variable only when PermissionTimeout is configured, so leaving it unset never changes existing RuntimePool templates and does not trigger rollouts.
- **Capacity env only for non-default shared pools** (`controller.runtimePoolCapacityEnvironment`): runtimePoolCapacityEnvironment passes a non-default pool capacity to the supervisor, which otherwise caps each instance at built-in limits. Workspace-backed pools are single-session and rely on the supervisor's own single-session enforcement, so they get no capacity override.
- **Cleanup evidence is recorded before retirement** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordDrainedRuntimePoolTaskCleanup`): recordDrainedRuntimePoolTaskCleanup preserves exact cleanup evidence before the pool lifecycle owner forgets a retired instance. It trusts that callers already validated the authenticated probe against the selected Pod and its deployed profile.
- **Profile match checks both digest sources** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordDrainedRuntimePoolTaskCleanup`): recordDrainedRuntimePoolTaskCleanup matches either the session profile digest or the AgentExecutionBinding profile, because standalone Tasks freeze the profile only in AgentExecutionBinding while the session digest is populated only for Session reuse.
- **Upgrades after the quiescent barrier still record receipts** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.recordDrainedRuntimePoolTaskCleanup`): A controller upgraded after persisting the quiescent barrier, without a session cleanup digest, must still record cleanup receipts before destroying the old runtime.
- **Skills reuse toolbox image delivery** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate`): Runtime pool skills are delivered with the toolbox mechanisms (copy init containers or read-only image volumes) under their own names, mounted read-only at /opt/orka-skills/<name>; the supervisor validates each source and copies it into every session's native skills folder.
- **Skill volume size bound** (`controller.runtimePoolSkillVolumeBytes`): runtimePoolSkillVolumeBytes bounds one copied skill as the content limit plus one filesystem block per permitted entry plus headroom for directory metadata and the copier's completion marker.
- **Per-session skill copy bound** (`controller.runtimePoolSkillSessionCopyBytes`): runtimePoolSkillSessionCopyBytes bounds the private copy of one skill each resident session receives: the content limit plus one filesystem block per permitted entry.
- **Skill env projection preserves order** (`controller.runtimePoolSkillsJSON`): runtimePoolSkillsJSON is the exact environment projection of the frozen skill list; order is preserved and never sorted, so the deployed template matches the frozen profile.
- **Skill template follows toolbox template** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate runs after applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate and skips shared mounts, handoff, pull secrets, and node selector that call already added. It is a no-op without skills so pools predating the feature render byte-identical templates.
- **Skill failures surface through termination messages** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate sets FallbackToLogsOnError because the supervisor skill check and copy init containers write one stable FAIL line that should surface as the termination message.
- **Startup probe outlasts the skill read** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate`): The supervisor reads every skill, after any toolbox check, before serving. applyRuntimePoolSkillTemplate extends the startup probe past that bounded read for either mount method, or kubelet would restart the container before a stalled read reports its classified failure.
- **Reserve sessions storage for skill copies** (`controller.reserveRuntimePoolSkillSessionCopies`): reserveRuntimePoolSkillSessionCopies raises the sessions volume and runtime ephemeral storage by the maximum per-session skill copies at full resident capacity. Without it many residents with large skills could exhaust the fixed sessions volume, and exceeding an emptyDir limit evicts the Pod with every session.
- **Skill image code never runs** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolSkillCopyTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolSkillCopyTemplate replaces the skill image's ENTRYPOINT and CMD with the handoff binary, as for toolboxes, so nothing from the skill image ever runs.
- **Native runtime environment limit is 32** (`controller.nativeRuntimeEnvironmentLimit`): nativeRuntimeEnvironmentLimit is Agent Substrate's per-container environment limit of 32 entries. The provider refuses a larger request and the runtime never starts, so every native environment must be fitted under it.
- **Supervisor telemetry projection rules** (`controller.runtimePoolTelemetryEnv`): runtimePoolTelemetryEnv follows the native AI worker scalar allowlist: traces gate the projection, so a metrics-only configuration enables nothing, and settings use as few entries as preserve SDK meaning. Collector egress is operator-owned unless --runtime-telemetry-collector-egress admits the origins for gateway-enforced runtimes. Headers, certificate paths, resource attributes, and Task context are never copied.
- **Native telemetry fit order** (`controller.fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry`): fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry re-projects telemetry into the room the native environment limit leaves after all other runtime settings, starting from every configured signal rather than a fixed budget: traces and metrics if they fit, else traces alone, else none.
- **Unchanged telemetry projection keeps its position** (`controller.fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry`): fitNativeRuntimeTelemetry returns an environment whose projection is unchanged as-is, so the frozen workload request does not change entry order for nothing.
- **Explicit grpc protocol is stored unset** (`controller.normalizedOTLPScalar`): normalizedOTLPScalar stores an explicit grpc protocol as unset because the supervisor exports gRPC by default, so it costs no native environment entry.
- **Generic OTLP endpoint only when it serves both signals** (`controller.encodeRuntimePoolTelemetryEnv`): encodeRuntimePoolTelemetryEnv projects a generic endpoint only when it resolves to both signals' URLs, so a dropped signal never falls back to it. A scalar both signals agree on is projected once under its generic name.
- **OTLP/HTTP generic endpoints are base URLs** (`controller.runtimePoolOTLPSignal.httpProtocol`): Under OTel SDK semantics, an OTLP/HTTP generic endpoint is a base URL to which the signal path is appended, while gRPC treats generic and signal endpoints alike; runtimePoolOTLPSignal.httpProtocol and signalURL encode this.
- **Trace-only projections never use a generic base** (`controller.runtimePoolSharedOTLPBase`): runtimePoolSharedOTLPBase returns a generic endpoint only if it resolves to every exported signal's URL. A trace-only projection never shares one, because the supervisor would also resolve metrics from it.
- **OTLP URL comparison follows exporter behavior** (`controller.sameOTLPURL`): sameOTLPURL compares endpoints as the exporter would use them; an OTLP/HTTP signal URL without a path posts to "/", so it equals one with a root path.
- **Metrics drop rather than fall back** (`controller.runtimePoolMetricsTelemetrySignal`): runtimePoolMetricsTelemetrySignal drops metrics when a metrics-specific endpoint is unreachable instead of falling back to another collector as worker Jobs do. Because the supervisor disables all telemetry on any setting it rejects, a rejected metrics setting drops metrics rather than traces.
- **Metrics opt-out wins over endpoints** (`controller.runtimePoolMetricsTelemetrySignal`): runtimePoolMetricsTelemetrySignal honors OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=none ahead of any endpoint, including the generic one.
- **Collector origins must be exact DNS http(s) URLs** (`controller.validateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorOrigin`): validateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorOrigin fails closed: a projected OTLP endpoint must be an absolute http(s) URL with a fully qualified DNS host, never an IP, wildcard, or scheme-less gRPC target, so the allowlisted origin is exactly the collector the supervisor exports to.
- **Collector egress opt-in is validated at startup** (`controller.ValidateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorEgress`): ValidateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorEgress refuses --runtime-telemetry-collector-egress at startup unless telemetry is enabled, at least one collector endpoint is projected, and every projected endpoint is expressible as an allowlisted origin.
- **Short collector names are refused at startup** (`controller.ValidateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorEgress`): Collector egress admits collectors only for gateway-enforced runtimes, whose admission refuses short Service names, so ValidateRuntimeTelemetryCollectorEgress refuses them at startup rather than leaving every such RuntimePool Degraded.
- **INSECURE must agree with the endpoint scheme** (`controller.runtimePoolTelemetryTransportConflict`): Collector egress rules follow the endpoint scheme (http as HTTP, https as TLS passthrough), but OTLP exporters apply INSECURE afterwards, so runtimePoolTelemetryTransportConflict refuses an https endpoint with INSECURE=true or an http one with INSECURE=false.
- **Native telemetry room is three entries** (`controller.runtimePoolNativeTelemetryRoom`): In tests, runtimePoolNativeTelemetryRoom is 3 because a native supervisor that cannot suspend already uses 29 of the 32 native environment entries.
- **Toolbox init identities are distinct** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxHandoffUID`): The toolbox init containers run as fixed non-root UIDs that differ from each other and from the 20000-29999 ACP session identity range, so no toolbox helper shares an identity with a session.
- **Toolbox volume sizing** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxVolumeHeadroomBytes`): runtimePoolToolboxVolumeHeadroomBytes covers directory metadata and the copier's completion marker, and runtimePoolToolboxBlockBytes reserves one filesystem block per permitted entry, so a toolbox at the documented limits, including many tiny files, still fits a size-enforced emptyDir.
- **Toolbox env projection preserves order** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxesJSON`): runtimePoolToolboxesJSON is the exact environment projection of the frozen toolbox list; order is preserved and never sorted, so the deployed template matches the frozen profile.
- **Toolbox template is a no-op without toolboxes** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate changes nothing when a profile has no toolboxes, so pools that predate the feature render byte-identical templates and do not roll.
- **Toolbox failures surface through termination messages** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate sets FallbackToLogsOnError because the supervisor startup check and toolbox init containers write one stable FAIL line, letting the controller classify failures from the termination message without reading Pod logs.
- **Linux node selector is fixed** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolImagePullPolicy`): Runtime images and toolbox helpers are Linux-only, so applyRuntimePoolImagePullPolicy never lets the operator's node selector override kubernetes.io/os, and ValidateACPToolboxNodeSelector rejects one that tries.
- **Toolbox image code never runs** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxCopyTemplate`): applyRuntimePoolToolboxCopyTemplate sets Command to Orka's copier and non-empty Args carrying every option, replacing both the toolbox image's ENTRYPOINT and CMD, so an image CMD such as ["--help"] can never reach the invocation.
- **Budget ephemeral storage for copied toolboxes** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxCopyTemplate`): Kubelet charges emptyDir usage against the Pod's summed container ephemeral-storage limit, so applyRuntimePoolToolboxCopyTemplate adds the handoff and per-toolbox volume bytes on top of the resource class defaults; otherwise the Pod is evicted while filling them.
- **Startup probe outlasts the toolbox check** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxImageVolumeTemplate`): The supervisor runs the unprivileged toolbox check before serving, so applyRuntimePoolToolboxImageVolumeTemplate extends the startup probe past the check timeout; otherwise kubelet restarts the container before the check reports a classified failure.
- **Toolbox failure messages are sanitized** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailure`): runtimePoolToolboxFailure maps toolbox init container, runtime toolbox check, and image-volume mount or pull failures to the stable ToolboxUnavailable reason, and sanitizes messages before they reach status.
- **Unexplained toolbox init failures are toolbox failures** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailure`): A toolbox init container exists only to bind a toolbox, so runtimePoolToolboxFailure treats an unsuccessful end without the stable FAIL line (OOM kill, early crash) as a toolbox failure once it exhausts a bounded number of retries.
- **Handoff failures are ordinary rollout failures** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailure`): The handoff init container runs the trusted runtime image, so runtimePoolToolboxFailure treats its pull or setup failures as ordinary rollout failures with usual retries. Only toolbox-copy-* containers run the untrusted image and produce toolbox failures.
- **Image-volume subPath errors are permanent** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailure`): Kubelet reports a missing or invalid image-volume subPath as CreateContainerConfigError on some runtimes and CreateContainerError on others; runtimePoolToolboxFailure treats both as permanent toolbox mount failures.
- **Toolbox pull retry window** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxPullRetryWindow`): runtimePoolToolboxPullRetryWindow gives kubelet five minutes to recover from transient registry failures before waiting Tasks receive a terminal toolbox failure.
- **Pull failure episodes are observed durably** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.observeRuntimePoolToolboxPullFailures`): observeRuntimePoolToolboxPullFailures records failure episodes durably on the exact Pod rather than using Pod creation or start time, which includes scheduling and sandbox delays. Episodes survive runtime-specific waiting reasons and missing status; only an observed start or termination or a different restart count retires one.
- **Older failures never classify recovered containers** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailedTermination`): runtimePoolToolboxFailedTermination uses the current non-zero termination, or the last termination while waiting to restart; a container that has since run or exited 0 is never classified by an older failure, preserving the copier's retry and recovery.
- **Unexplained toolbox failures get three attempts** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxUnexplainedRestartLimit`): runtimePoolToolboxUnexplainedRestartLimit allows three unexplained failed attempts (no FAIL line, such as SIGKILL or OOM mid-copy) before a toolbox init failure is permanent. The copier's retry is idempotent, so a transient interruption gets a bounded chance to recover before waiting Tasks fail.
- **Toolbox mount method is not part of the profile digest** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxTemplate`): The toolbox mount method (copy or image volume) is an operator policy that changes the rendered Pod template but never the frozen profile digest.
- **Image volumes require an unprivileged runtime container** (`controller.applyRuntimePoolToolboxImageVolumeTemplate`): Mounting untrusted toolbox images as image volumes is safe only while the runtime container stays non-privileged with no privilege escalation; toolbox wiring must never add capabilities.
- **Toolbox gate uses the live probe for idleness** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.Reconcile`): A pool that loses toolbox admission keeps its workload until the live supervisor probe reports it idle. The toolbox gate consults the probe directly because it runs before the rollout path that refreshes the pool's capacity counters.
- **Restarted active Pods report ToolboxUnavailable** (`controller.runtimePoolToolboxFailure`): A previously active runtime Pod that restarts and fails its toolbox checks reports ToolboxUnavailable rather than RolloutFailed, so waiting Tasks fail instead of retrying against a fence that can never serve again.
- **Pod API deletion is not process-death evidence** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.Reconcile`): For enrolled native Pods, API deletion alone is not evidence that the process died; recycle and retirement wait for the completed boot or termination evidence.
- **Session cleanup recovery is leader-only** (`controller.SessionCleanupRecoveryManager.NeedLeaderElection`): SessionCleanupRecoveryManager requires leader election so replay of durable cross-store Session deletion intents stays single-writer under the controller epoch fence.
- **Token usage is added with lock release** (`controller.SessionManager.ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage`): ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage adds a finished Task's token usage to the Session in the same write that releases its lock, so a completion replayed after a crash cannot count the usage twice.
- **Gateway Tasks release Session locks only through projection** (`controller.SessionManager.ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage`): For Gateway Tasks, the gateway terminal projection releases the Session lock atomically with its canonical assistant message and delivery outbox row. Generic Task finalization in ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage stays a no-op, even when a malformed session policy made the admitted Gateway Task fail.
- **Gateway projection is the only assistant message writer** (`controller.SessionManager.AppendMessages`): For Gateway Tasks, AppendMessages returns without reading SessionRef or the Task result, because the gateway terminal projection is the only writer of the canonical assistant message.
- **Only the active Task incarnation appends** (`controller.SessionManager.AppendMessages`): Rejected Tasks and scheduled parents can finish without ever acquiring the Session, so AppendMessages appends only when the Session's ActiveTask name and UID match the Task.
- **Session appends are idempotent by message ID** (`controller.SessionManager.AppendMessages`): AppendMessages uses stable per-Task message IDs so retrying a completion after an unknown write outcome is a no-op. The store keeps the first append time; a repeat leaves it unset to match instead of conflict, and a message the first append lacked is still added.
- **Session deletion order** (`controller.SessionManager.DeleteSession`): In ACP-enabled deployments, SessionManager.DeleteSession coordinates the Kubernetes-authoritative Session controls before removing the SQLite transcript.
- **Skill content hash is length-prefixed** (`controller.SkillReconciler.computeContentHash`): computeContentHash length-prefixes every field before hashing, so moving bytes between inline content, a file path, and a file's content always changes the SHA-256 hash.
- **Provider capacity exhaustion is a wait** (`controller.SubstrateActorPoolReconciler.updateSubstrateActorPoolExecutorError`): updateSubstrateActorPoolExecutorError reports provider capacity exhaustion as a retryable wait rather than a pool failure.
- **Rejected template replacement still allows deletion** (`controller.SubstrateActorPoolReconciler.Reconcile`): When a SubstrateActorPool's native template is replaced under the same name and the replacement is rejected, the old pool must still be deletable and cleaned up.
- **Pool lease name survives qualified actor IDs** (`controller.substratePoolActorLeaseName`): substratePoolActorLeaseName derives the Lease key from the actor name; because the deterministic actor name already hashes its template Atespace, the pre-native lease key is preserved when the routable actor ID becomes qualified.
- **Unknown lease holders are busy** (`controller.substratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveHolder`): substratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveHolder treats a lease without a recognized holder as busy, failing closed so an actor is never reused on unproven ownership.
- **Legacy Task leases stay busy until proven safe** (`controller.legacySubstratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveTaskHolder`): legacySubstratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveTaskHolder keeps pre-upgrade Task-held leases busy until the original Task is terminal and confirms workspace cleanup; a missing or replaced Task cannot prove the actor is safe to reuse.
- **Tool-held leases are always busy** (`controller.substratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveToolHolder`): substratePoolActorLeaseHasActiveToolHolder treats every Tool-held lease as busy because only the Tool controller releases it by deleting the Lease; holder presence or absence never proves reuse is safe. The holder Get is kept so transient reader failures surface to the caller.
- **Default egress policy propagation is 14s** (`controller.defaultSubstrateEgressPolicyPropagation`): defaultSubstrateEgressPolicyPropagation is 14 seconds: the Substrate v1 egress gateway's default 10s per-Actor policy cache (--egress-policy-cache-ttl), which nothing invalidates, plus up to 4s for a fetch that read the previous policy before caching it.
- **Egress credentials fail closed without a provider** (`controller.SubstrateConfig.EgressCredentialProvider`): SubstrateConfig.EgressCredentialProvider names the egress gateway's credential provider class (only "k8s.io"). When empty, MCP Tool egress credentials are disabled and Tools that request one fail closed.
- **Wait for egress policy propagation** (`controller.SubstrateConfig.EgressPolicyPropagation`): SubstrateConfig.EgressPolicyPropagation is how long the gateway may still apply other rules after an Actor's policy is replaced or after an Actor reuses a deleted Actor's name. Until it passes the Tool is not ready, its Actor does not run, and a running Actor is stopped. It should equal --egress-policy-cache-ttl plus 4s; zero is correct only with the gateway cache disabled.
- **Removed Substrate settings fail startup** (`controller.ErrRemovedSubstrateSetting`): Startup rejects any configured Substrate v0.1.0 setting (removedSubstrateEnv) unconditionally, independent of MCP Tool admission and recovery state, so an obsolete operator setting is never silently ignored.
- **Control-only validation for pool finalization** (`controller.SubstrateConfig.ValidateControl`): SubstrateConfig.ValidateControl validates only the authenticated control API, which is enough for control-only work such as SubstrateActorPool finalization and accepts either a client certificate/key pair or a bearer token.
- **MCP Actor traffic needs pod-certificate identity** (`controller.SubstrateConfig.ValidateMCPTools`): The Substrate router serves only mTLS from SPIFFE IDs on its ingress allowlist, so ValidateMCPTools requires the pod-certificate client identity even when a bearer token authenticates control calls.
- **Router URL must be https** (`controller.validateSubstrateRouting`): validateSubstrateRouting requires an https router URL because upstream Substrate v1 serves Actor traffic only over mTLS.
- **Router URL has no userinfo, query, or fragment** (`controller.validateSubstrateRouting`): validateSubstrateRouting rejects router URLs with userinfo, query, or fragment because Tool.status.endpoint republishes the URL to Tool readers and the MCP path is appended to it as text.
- **Dedicated Actor names hash the complete binding** (`controller.ToolReconciler.substrateMCPFinalizerMigrationContext`): The dedicated Substrate MCP Actor name hashes the complete template binding, so an edited template cannot qualify an Actor that belongs to the previous Tool spec.
- **Pool cleanup uses the immutable Atespace** (`controller.ToolReconciler.substrateMCPFinalizerMigrationContext`): Because a SubstrateActorPool's Atespace is immutable, Tool cleanup can use the pool binding even while the pool is deleting or its native template is gone, without admission.
- **Legacy MCP identity migration is in-place** (`controller.ToolReconciler.migrateSubstrateMCPIdentities`): migrateSubstrateMCPIdentities qualifies legacy Actor IDs from recorded template provenance or the validated current binding. It never deletes or recreates an Actor or transfers a lease; status, leases, and annotations converge through separate optimistic writes so an interruption cannot make an Actor its own replacement cleanup target.
- **Pool Atespace qualifies legacy IDs** (`controller.substrateMCPLegacyIdentityQualifier`): substrateMCPLegacyIdentityQualifier qualifies pooled legacy IDs with the pool's Atespace because pool Atespaces are immutable and the caller already validated the pool against the requested template.
- **No guessed Atespace for historical cleanup** (`controller.substrateMCPLegacyIdentityQualifier`): substrateMCPLegacyIdentityQualifier leaves an ID unqualified when there is no provenance rather than guessing an Atespace; the native client keeps rejecting unqualified operations.
- **Replacement templates need a new pool** (`controller.SubstrateActorPoolReconciler.Reconcile`): A SubstrateActorPool pins its template identity; using a replacement template requires a new pool with its own binding rather than rebinding the existing pool.
- **Recovery reports whether router identity is needed** (`controller.FindSubstrateMCPRecoveryState`): FindSubstrateMCPRecoveryState reports the first Substrate cleanup obligation and whether it needs the router client identity: SubstrateActorPool finalization is control-only, while dedicated and pooled MCP Tool finalization builds the router-capable executor.
- **Tool finalizer marks pending Actor cleanup** (`controller.FindSubstrateMCPRecoveryState`): FindSubstrateMCPRecoveryState treats the Tool Actor finalizer as the cleanup obligation because it survives backend changes; the mutable MCP spec is not evidence that a prior dedicated or pooled Actor was cleaned up.
- **Native templates are trusted operator selections** (`controller.validateNativeSubstrateRoutableTemplate`): validateNativeSubstrateRoutableTemplate treats a native template reference as an explicit operator selection: upstream templates have no Kubernetes labels, annotations, or Ready condition to check, and the Actor's native wakeup probe gates router ingress after each ResumeActor.
- **Templates without durable mounts cannot suspend** (`controller.nativeSubstrateTemplateSuspendUnsupported`): A Substrate VOLUMES snapshot captures only durable-directory mounts; without any, the worker refuses the checkpoint before stopping, Substrate records the Actor as crashed, and its sandbox may keep running. nativeSubstrateTemplateSuspendUnsupported marks such templates so their Actors, which hold no data a suspend would keep, are stopped by deletion.
- **Approval notices are projected durably before delivery** (`controller.projectApprovalNotices`): Approval events of a Task that selects them in spec.webhookEvents are projected during reconcile into a bounded queue in status.approvalWebhooks. The cursor and new notices persist in one status write before any attempt, so a restarted controller never projects a transition twice; delivery reuses terminal-notice machinery (dial-time checks, Ed25519 JWS, attempt lease, bounded attempts). See ADR 0053. See [ADR 0053](0053-approval-webhook-events.md).
- **Approval notice retry budget** (`controller.defaultApprovalWebhookMaxAttempts`): Approval review windows are about ten minutes, so defaultApprovalWebhookMaxAttempts allows five attempts at 10s, 20s, 40s, and 60s spacing that settle within about three minutes and never hold later notices of the same Task back longer.
- **Approval notices wait at most five minutes for signing keys** (`controller.approvalWebhookSigningWindow`): approvalWebhookSigningWindow bounds how long a queued approval notice waits for signing keys to the retry window plus two minutes. Unlike terminal notices, an approval notice is worthless after its review window, and stream-order delivery would otherwise let one unsignable notice block every later one until the queue overflows.
- **One approval send per delivery pass** (`controller.approvalWebhookAttemptsPerPass`): approvalWebhookAttemptsPerPass is 1 so, as for terminal notices, a slow receiver holds a delivery worker for at most one request per pass while other Tasks' notices proceed on other workers.
- **Broker records live approval expiry first** (`controller.approvalExpiryGrace`): approvalExpiryGrace delays controller-recorded approval expiry by five seconds so the broker, which owns a live review, records its own expiry first; the controller records it only for lost reviews.
- **Approval notices carry identifiers only** (`controller.ApprovalWebhookPayload`): ApprovalWebhookPayload carries identifiers only: never argument previews, action or risk text, reasons, digests, bindings, or the identity of whoever decided.
- **Overflow notices are always selected** (`controller.approvalWebhookSelection.allows`): approvalWebhookSelection.allows treats the overflow notice as part of any approval event selection, so a receiver that selected any approval event learns about omitted transitions.
- **Approval delivery IDs are deterministic** (`controller.approvalWebhookDeliveryID`): approvalWebhookDeliveryID is UUIDv5 of the Task UID, approval ID, and transition, so the same transition has the same delivery ID across restarts and replicas and receivers can deduplicate.
- **Only a Task's own approvals reach its receiver** (`controller.taskApprovals`): taskApprovals decides approvals only from explicit approval events, as the controller and approval API do, and drops any approval whose request names another Task UID or none, so it never reaches this Task's receiver.
- **Approval expiry enforcement scope** (`controller.approvalExpiryEnforced`): approvalExpiryEnforced is true only for brokered reviews and parked sign-offs; AI-worker approvals resolve only by explicit decision and never expire by clock.
- **Approval notices keep only safe identifiers** (`controller.newApprovalNotice`): newApprovalNotice keeps only identifiers that match their safe shape and refuses to build a notice when the approval ID itself is not safe to send.
- **Notice times are truncated to seconds** (`controller.sealApprovalNotice`): sealApprovalNotice truncates OccurredAt to seconds because status times have second precision and the body digest must survive a status round trip.
- **Approval notice overflow policy** (`controller.projectApprovalNotices`): projectApprovalNotices never removes a pending notice and prunes settled notices oldest first. When every slot but the reserved one is pending, it records a single overflow notice in that slot, stops notifying later transitions, and counts them as omitted; the cursor still advances so nothing is regenerated. See ADR 0053. See [ADR 0053](0053-approval-webhook-events.md).
- **Clock expiry is recorded durably under the cleanup fence** (`controller.recordDueApprovalExpiries`): recordDueApprovalExpiries appends ApprovalExpired once for a brokered review past expiresAt that nothing recorded; the store's single terminal event per approval lets an earlier decision win. It copies the Session association from a fresh read under the namespace cleanup fence, so cleanup that commits after the read refuses the append and the controller never recreates cleaned history or Session association.
- **Approval projection never blocks the Task** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.prepareApprovalNotices`): prepareApprovalNotices records notices and due expiries before Task phase work but sends nothing; sendApprovalNotices delivers in the delivery runnable so a slow receiver never delays the Task. An event store failure is logged and retried later without blocking the Task, and recorded notices still deliver.
- **Opt-out retires notices immediately** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.prepareApprovalNotices`): prepareApprovalNotices and deliverApprovalNotices retire deselected notices as soon as an opt-out is observed, before handing anything to delivery and even during retry backoff, so re-selecting the event never revives a notice from before the opt-out.
- **Re-selection starts from the high-water mark** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.prepareApprovalNotices`): Opting out of approval events ends tracking, so selecting them again starts from the stream's high-water mark instead of replaying what happened while nothing was selected.
- **Unsignable notices retire before projection** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.prepareApprovalNotices`): prepareApprovalNotices retires notices whose signing wait has ended before projecting, because projection decides overflow irreversibly and those notices must free their slots first.
- **Selection boundary persists before expiry appends** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.projectTaskApprovalEvents`): projectTaskApprovalEvents persists a new selection boundary before anything can append to the event stream. Recording a due expiry first could append an event past an unpersisted boundary, and the next pass would raise the boundary over it and treat a selected resolution as earlier history.
- **Initial approval selection tracks from the start** (`controller.startTrackingInitialApprovalSelection`): startTrackingInitialApprovalSelection marks tracking while a new Task's status initializes; nothing has run yet, so every later approval transition is projected.
