- **Late approval selection skips history** (`controller.startApprovalWebhookTracking`): startApprovalWebhookTracking sets the selection boundary when a Task starts selecting approval events after status initialization or after selecting none, raising the cursor to the stream's high-water mark so earlier history is never notified and cannot fill the queue. A selection removed and restored between observations is not seen as removed.
- **Approval notices deliver in stream order** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.deliverApprovalNotices`): deliverApprovalNotices sends pending notices in stream order, holding each until every earlier one settles, so a receiver sees a resolution only after its request unless the request's notice failed.
- **Only the recorded notice body is sent** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.deliverApprovalNotices`): deliverApprovalNotices retires a notice whose rendered body no longer matches its recorded payload digest rather than sending a different body.
- **Signing deadline rounds up to seconds** (`controller.approvalSigningDeadline`): approvalSigningDeadline rounds the deadline up to whole seconds because status times have second precision, so the persisted value survives a round trip unchanged and never shortens the wait.
- **Retire unsignable notices before projection** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.retireUnsignableApprovalNotices`): retireUnsignableApprovalNotices retires pending notices past their signing deadline before projection; delivery also retires them but only afterwards, and a controller stopped or rarely reconciled during a key outage would otherwise count them pending and latch overflow. Keys load only when a notice is past its deadline.
- **Unsigned approval notices expire together** (`controller.expireUnsignedApprovalNotices`): expireUnsignedApprovalNotices runs while signing keys are unavailable and retires every pending notice at or past its persisted signing deadline, since all wait on the same keys. A notice queued by an earlier controller without a deadline gets one starting now. Only the head is ever attempted, so no other notice holds an attempt lease.
- **Scheduled run history holds runs with pending approval notices** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): Scheduled run history pruning keeps a run that keeps no history until its queued approval request and resolution notices settle; deleting it after the request alone would drop the resolution, which no later reconcile could recover.
- **Approval notice hold fails safe** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): approvalNoticesHoldRun keeps a run when reading the run or its continuation chain fails, rather than pruning it.
- **Unprojected approval events hold a run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): A decision that commits after a run's projection read but before history enforcement is not yet a notice; pruning then would delete the stream and lose the resolution, so approvalNoticesHoldRun holds the run until a later reconcile notifies it once.
- **Outstanding terminal notices hold a run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): Webhook delivery is asynchronous, so a finished run can still wait for a worker to send its terminal notice; history pruning keeps it until that notice settles because deleting the run drops the notice.
- **Running continuations do not hold their chain** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): A continuation that is still running inherits the webhook URL but owes no terminal notice yet, so it does not hold its chain's head from history pruning.
- **Event store errors hold runs only within the notice window** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): When the event store read fails, history pruning keeps a recently settled run because it cannot tell whether a decision is still to be notified, but only within the approval notice window, so an unavailable store holds no run indefinitely.
- **Only notifiable transitions hold a run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): Only events that projection would still turn into a notice hold a run; events that never become one never advance the cursor, so they must not hold the run forever.
- **Parked turns deliver sign-off notices but defer terminal** (`controller.TaskReconciler.reconcileTaskPhaseAndApprovalNotices`): A parked turn that Succeeded still has its sign-off approval notices delivered by the Task reconciler after its phase work, while its terminal notice stays deferred.
- **Terminal notices wait out signing outages** (`controller.approvalWebhookSigningWindow`): Terminal Task notices keep waiting for signing keys without consuming attempts however long an outage lasts, while the Task's approval notices are retired around them after their signing window.
- **Cancellation revokes authority before terminal write** (`controller.cancelTaskStatus`): cancelTaskStatus revokes the Task's job authority first and writes ACP Tasks under the controller epoch guard, as every ACP terminal projection must be. The ACP dispatcher then stops and settles the run and the MCP broker refuses further calls.
- **Cancellation re-reads the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedAgentTask`): cancelRequestedAgentTask re-reads the Task before ending it so a Task that finished or was replaced meanwhile is left alone.
- **Cancellation before execution status settles orphan attempts** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedAgentTask`): A Task cancelled before its execution status was projected (during class workspace attach, or after a lost status write following the PromptAttempt) may have a persisted attempt that recovery never visits. cancelRequestedAgentTask settles it and records the terminal execution a queued timeout would, so cleanup recognizes it and terminal settlement revokes a linked attachment.
- **Cancel settles a PromptAttempt with no execution status** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedAgentTask`): A PromptAttempt can be persisted before a status write is lost, leaving a Task with no execution status, and recovery never visits such a Task. controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedAgentTask must therefore settle that attempt itself and bind it to the terminal execution when the Task is cancelled.
- **Cancelled phase precedes workspace status projection** (`controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile`): For a cancelled class-backed Agent Task, controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile writes the Cancelled phase, which stops the dispatcher's run, before the workspace status projection, so a projection that keeps failing cannot keep the Task running; a later reconcile still cancels the Tasks it delegated.
- **Runtime watcher stops a run on the cancel request itself** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.requestedCancellationMessage`): The ACP runtime watcher stops a run when it sees the cancel-requested annotation, not only the Cancelled phase, so a cancel whose Cancelled status write keeps failing still stops the run.
- **Cancel cascade scope** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks`): controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks asks Pending and Running children to cancel, finding children in other namespaces by the parent UID they record and reaching work that outlived a finished child. It leaves finished children, children the parent does not control, and scheduled templates alone.
- **A refusing child does not block the parent's own completion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cascadeTaskCancellation`): When a child's cancel keeps failing, controller.TaskReconciler.cascadeTaskCancellation still runs and reports the refusal for a retry, but the cancelled parent's own completion, including deleting its Job, still proceeds.
- **Cancel cascade works with namespace-scoped Task access** (`controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex`): The standard install lets the controller list Tasks only in namespaces it is bound in, so controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex must still cancel the parent's local children when a cluster-wide list is forbidden, and must not try a cluster-wide list at all when namespace isolation is on.
- **Cascade descends through already cancel-requested children** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks`): controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks walks past a child already asked to cancel, because that child's own cascade may never run (for example when its status write keeps failing); the cancelled ancestor itself reaches the running grandchild.
- **Job creation re-reads the Task for a cancel request** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob re-reads the Task from the API server before creating the Job, because a cancel request written after the cached copy was read must start no workload.
- **Cascade descends through deleting children without patching them** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks`): A child being deleted stays readable while its finalizer holds it; controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks does not patch it but still cancels its running grandchildren.
- **Usage storage outage still cancels delegated work** (`controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile`): When usage registration fails, controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile still stops the Task and runs cascadeTaskCancellation before returning the error, because the cascade needs no usage storage and delegated work must stop with its cancelled parent even during a storage outage.
- **Cancel cascade lists a delegation tree in at most two requests** (`controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex`): controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex reads a whole delegation tree in at most two list requests regardless of its size or depth, so a wide, deep tree cannot multiply API load during cancellation, and the walk still reaches every running descendant.
- **Cancel cascade reaches parked children and their continuations** (`controller.TaskReconciler.childContinuationChain`): Delegated children that parked their turns finish Succeeded but a waiting one can still start another turn, and a resumed one continues in a Task it does not control. controller.TaskReconciler.childContinuationChain lets the cascade cancel the wait and the continuation and walk what the continuation delegated (ADR 0041). See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Requested cancellation messages are bounded** (`controller.requestedTaskCancellation`): Both cancel paths read the requested cancellation message bounded to acpPromptFailureMessageLimit, so the terminal status write can never be refused because the message is too long.
- **Late delegated children cancel themselves under a cancelled ancestor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.requestCancelForCancelledAncestor`): A child whose Create commits after a cancelled ancestor's cascade ran may wake no parent. While Pending, controller.TaskReconciler.requestCancelForCancelledAncestor walks its UID-pinned chain, through finished parents and into each parent's recorded namespace, and requests its own cancellation. Only a cancel is a signal: an unproven chain, a live chain, an ancestor deleted by retention, a namesake, or a scheduled template leaves the child alone.
- **Stopping a cancelled Job does not finish finalization** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedNativeTask`): Stopping a cancelled native Task's Job must not mark finalization done: after a storage outage, recovery must still collect the worker's result and finish cancellation.
- **A cancelled Task stops before its children are cancelled** (`controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile`): controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile marks a cancelled native Task Cancelled, which stops its run, before asking any delegated child to cancel, so a large delegation tree never delays stopping the parent.
- **A cancelled Task's Job is deleted before its children are cancelled** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompletedThenCascade`): For a native Task already Cancelled whose Job still runs, the Job is deleted before any child is asked to cancel (controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompletedThenCascade), so a large tree never keeps the parent's container running.
- **Applying a cancel request deletes the Job in the same reconcile** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedNativeTask`): controller.TaskReconciler.cancelRequestedNativeTask deletes a running native Task's Job in the same reconcile that applies the cancel request, before any child is asked to cancel, rather than on a later reconcile.
- **Task CRD rule enforces immutability without the webhook** (`corev1alpha1.Task`): The Task CRD validation rule alone, with no admission webhook, must keep a soul-bound Task pausable while freezing its execution inputs, and keep the whole spec of an ACP-bound Task frozen.
- **Job creation visibility grace period** (`controller.TaskReconciler.isWithinJobCreationVisibilityGracePeriod`): jobCreationVisibilityGracePeriod (30s) keeps the controller from failing a Task whose Job it just created but whose informer cache has not observed the Job yet.
- **Child gateway grants require Task provenance admission** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): TaskReconciler.TaskProvenanceProtected reports that built-in or external Task provenance admission is declared. Child gateway grants are honored only when it is set, because without admission a child's provenance metadata is forgeable; root grants do not require it.
- **Static execution mode classifies legacy built-in Agents** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): TaskReconciler.Mode is the controller's static execution mode and is what classifies built-in Agents that omit contractVersion, so changing the mode changes how those Agents execute.
- **Toolbox admission rules are re-checked at planning time** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): TaskReconciler.ACPToolboxPolicy repeats the Agent toolbox admission rules when a Task is planned, so a Task never binds a RuntimePool that cannot host its toolboxes, even if admission was bypassed or rules changed.
- **Provider use authorization refuses cross-namespace Agent Providers** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): When TaskReconciler.ProviderUseAuthorization (--provider-use-authorization-enabled) is set, the controller refuses an ai Task that takes its Provider from an Agent in another namespace, matching ProvidersFromAgentInAnotherNamespace.
- **Workspace settlement is non-destructive without provenance admission** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): TaskReconciler.WorkspaceSettlementProtected reports that Task provenance admission guards the reserved acp.workspace.orka.ai/ metadata that settlement reads. When false (a cleanup-only install without the webhook), class settlement never revokes or deletes a workspace based on that forgeable metadata; existing workspaces are cleaned only through explicit workspace deletion.
- **Workspace-backed Agent Tasks fail closed without dispatch enabled** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): When TaskReconciler.ACPWorkspaceDispatchEnabled is false, workspace-backed Agent Tasks fail closed before any workspace or RuntimePool demand is created.
- **Task controller RBAC grant rationale** (`controller.TaskReconciler`): The Task controller holds Agent and Provider use broadly because the controller, API, gateways and monitors create Tasks only for callers they already authorized. It needs sessions (a virtual resource the API authorizes with a SubjectAccessReview on core.orka.ai/sessions), list on memories and memoryproposals plus create on memoryproposals because memory consolidation admission checks the creator of each scheduled run, and create/patch on events.k8s.io events for the retention recorder.
- **Interrupted status-write authority checks are not reported as success** (`controller.TaskReconciler.updateStatusWithRetry`): retryTaskStatusOnConflict returns the callback's own error because RetryOnConflict can discard a callback deadline when no conflict was recorded, and an interrupted authority check must never be reported as a successful write.
- **Finalizers use a metadata-only patch** (`controller.TaskReconciler.patchTaskFinalizer`): controller.TaskReconciler.patchTaskFinalizer adds and removes the Task finalizer with a metadata-only optimistic patch, because a full-object update can re-serialize equivalent duration strings (12m as 12m0s) and trip the immutable-spec admission rules.
- **Cancel cascade runs right after the Task's own stop** (`controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile`): Work a cancelled Task delegated stops with it at every depth as soon as the cancel is requested. controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile runs the cascade right after the Task's own stop, which it never delays, and before anything that can keep failing, such as result storage or the workspace projection. A refused child requeues a finished Task only after its own completion work is done.
- **Deferred finalization and cancellation order** (`controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile`): controller.TaskReconciler.Reconcile settles a deferred finalization before a later cancel request because it records a stop already decided, and applies a requested cancellation before the workspace status projection so a failing projection cannot keep a cancelled Task running; the projection resumes on the next reconcile.
- **Approval notices never hold back Task progress** (`controller.TaskReconciler.reconcileTaskPhaseAndApprovalNotices`): controller.TaskReconciler.reconcileTaskPhaseAndApprovalNotices records new approval notices in status before the phase work, which history hold and expiry recording rely on, and hands them to the delivery runnable only after it, even when it failed. Sending runs outside the reconcile worker so a slow receiver delays no Task, and the Task requeues for the earlier of the phase requeue and the next notice attempt. See [ADR 0053](0053-approval-webhook-events.md).
- **Approval notice delivery runs outside the reconcile worker** (`controller.TaskReconciler.scheduleApprovalWebhooks`): controller.TaskReconciler.scheduleApprovalWebhooks hands due notices to the delivery runnable so a slow or hanging receiver delays neither this Task nor any other reconcile. Delivery reads the Task uncached and drops notices of a Task that is gone, replaced or deleting; its status writes re-trigger reconcile so phase work such as parent history enforcement runs once the last notice settles. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Workspace status projects before WaitingForCapacity** (`controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPTaskAdmissionStatus`): controller.TaskReconciler.projectACPTaskAdmissionStatus projects the execution workspace status before the WaitingForCapacity condition, because that condition reads the workspace admission conditions the projection just refreshed.
- **Chat Session attribution is for lifecycle events only** (`controller.chatSessionEventAttribution`): controller.chatSessionEventAttribution attributes a Task without spec.sessionRef to the chat Session named in the orka.ai/chat-session label, which API clients cannot set, and only when that chat Session exists. It affects lifecycle events only: Session continuity follows spec.sessionRef, and the ACP event journal keeps spec.sessionRef because a journal Session with earlier text events redacts later runtime text, which would blank every chat-created ACP Task after the first.
- **Wait cancellation precedes history erasure on deletion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion`): On deletion, the wait reconciler first cancels the Task's wait and closes its parked approval in the execution history, and controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion erases that history only afterwards, so the history is never recreated behind the erasure.
- **Terminal lifecycle event is published before cleanup erases history** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion`): controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion publishes the terminal lifecycle event while terminal status is still retained, and must not recreate erased history when finalizer removal later retries.
- **Execution events are erased before the finalizer is released** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion`): controller.TaskReconciler.handleDeletion deletes the Task's execution timeline events before releasing the finalizer, so a future Task with the same namespace and name never exposes the old Task's history.
- **Scheduled parent Tasks have no per-run timeout** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): Scheduled parent Tasks own a recurring fire-time lifecycle, so controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending routes them to handleScheduledTask before timeout enforcement; per-run timeouts start on each generated child Task, not at parent creation.
- **Autonomous pause is checked before the next iteration dispatches** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending stops an autonomous Task suspended between iterations before dispatching the next one, while handleAutonomousIteration handles a pause that arrives during a running Job. Only autonomous iteration advances Status.Iteration.
- **A persisted execution binding is the sole routing authority** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): Once an Agent Task has a persisted AgentExecutionBinding, controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending routes it only through handleBoundAgentTaskPending and must not resolve mutable Agent or configuration state or acquire the legacy SQLite Session lock.
- **Only non-agent Tasks take the Session lock** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): Non-agent workers use the SQLite Session lock lifecycle; Agent Tasks instead claim protocol lineage and a fenced SessionTurn in the ACP dispatcher, and only after the immutable execution binding is recorded.
- **Monitor validation children bypass the namespace Task cap** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): Controller-owned repository monitor validation children are exempt from MaxTasksPerNamespace, because their review parent may occupy the namespace's only ordinary Task slot and would otherwise deadlock.
- **Use the effective built-in contract before it is persisted** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending`): Admission allows a built-in Agent to omit its contract, so controller.TaskReconciler.handlePending applies withEffectiveBuiltInContract(agent, r.Mode) and uses that same effective Agent for routing, SOUL validation and binding, before the Agent reconciler has persisted the namespace-mode default.
- **Parent-created Agents satisfy allowedAgents** (`controller.TaskReconciler.validateCoordinationConstraints`): controller.TaskReconciler.validateCoordinationConstraints also admits a child whose Agent the parent Task created dynamically through the create_agent tool, even when that Agent is not listed in the coordinator's allowedAgents.
- **Memory consolidation runs never iterate or park** (`controller.isMemoryConsolidationTask`): A memory consolidation run (ADR 0051) is one model call that submits one result, so controller.isMemoryConsolidationTask makes it never iterate autonomously or park on an approval, whatever its Agent configures. See [ADR 0051](0051-offline-memory-consolidation.md).
- **Injected approvals mirror parking semantics** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolvedApprovalsJSONForTask`): controller.TaskReconciler.resolvedApprovalsJSONForTask injects only explicit terminal approval events, matching parking semantics. Orka does not persist consumed-action markers; workers pass the approval ID as the downstream idempotency key, so suppressing duplicates across Jobs is the downstream service's responsibility.
- **Worker env approvals keep newest decisions and old denials** (`controller.selectResolvedApprovalsForWorkerEnv`): controller.selectResolvedApprovalsForWorkerEnv reserves space for the newest decisions first so recent approvals can resume required tool calls despite a long history, then adds older blocking decisions before older approvals: dropping an old approval only re-requests approval, but dropping an old decline or expiry could let a denied target execute.
- **Missing OutboundAccessPolicy wait is bounded** (`controller.TaskReconciler.boundMissingToolPolicy`): Tools are classified strictly, so a missing OutboundAccessPolicy cannot be judged connector-backed or not. controller.TaskReconciler.boundMissingToolPolicy retries native dispatch for missingToolPolicyGrace (2 minutes) in case the policy is being applied, then fails the Task naming the policy rather than leaving it Pending without a reason.
- **Retry delay is rechecked on the uncached Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob rechecks the persisted retry deadline on the uncached Task, so an older Pending snapshot cannot admit a Job before its retry delay has elapsed.
- **Job inputs come from the fresh Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob builds validation Tasks from their verified immutable binding and AI Tasks from fresh status as well as spec, so soul preparation cannot miss an existing binding or execution attempt hidden by a stale cache snapshot.
- **Gateway Jobs wait for reply eligibility** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob requeues while gateway reply eligibility is not ready, so a Job is never frozen without its tools in the window between create and the durable gateway link.
- **Existing Job is checked by exact built name** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): A recorded Job UID can belong to an earlier attempt, so controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob checks for a Job with the exact name it would build and sends any existing Job down the identity recovery path.
- **Job inputs are one consistent Task revision** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): Compatibility and Provider resolution used the caller's snapshots, so when the fresh Task differs controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob defers the whole tuple instead of mixing fresh policy with old inputs; Agent and Provider inputs belong to the caller's Task revision even when the Agent had no soul.
- **First soul binding rechecks the Agent revision** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): The first soul binding pins the Agent revision, and a cached Agent can lag an update the API server accepted before this Task, so controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob rechecks the revision and lets compatibility, Provider selection and binding use one revision.
- **An unbound existing Job never acquires a soul** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): Soul bindings are persisted before Job creation, so an existing Job with no binding ran without a soul even if its start-status write was lost. controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob retires it without binding, because a new digest would misstate what already executed.
- **Requester Connections freeze before anything is created** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob freezes the requester's Connections before creating anything, so a transient read failure retries dispatch instead of starting a worker whose connector-backed tools could never bind. The freeze reads the same Task object the Job is built from, so its bindings never belong to another revision.
- **An observed gateway Job is recovered, never recreated** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): Once controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob has observed an existing gateway Job it recovers it instead of creating one, so a deletion between the reads cannot turn a skipped freshness check into a new execution from stale inputs.
- **A recovered Job keeps its own frozen Connections** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): A recovered Job was built against the Connections frozen for it, so controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob takes bindings from the Job (FrozenConnectionBindingsFromJob); a freeze taken now must not grant it authority over a Connection that changed since.
- **Frozen bindings land in the Running status write** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): The status retry in controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob re-fetches the Task, so it must re-apply the Connection bindings frozen before Job creation in the same write that marks the Task Running.
- **ACPDispatcher owns running ACP Tasks** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleRunning`): For ACP Tasks, controller.TaskReconciler.handleRunning only requeues: the leader-elected ACPDispatcher owns the non-reconnectable prompt stream, lease renewal, cancellation barrier and terminal status projection.
- **A Job that succeeded before the timeout completes normally** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskJobSucceededBy`): A Job that succeeded no later than the Task deadline records its outcome rather than a timeout, because its completion may have been deferred past the timeout (for example by an unaccepted storage write). controller.TaskReconciler.taskJobSucceededBy excludes Jobs finished after the deadline or with unknown completion time, and autonomous Tasks, whose timeout ends the loop; lookup errors leave the outcome unsettled.
- **Failed Job messages name a cause a coordinator can act on** (`controller.TaskReconciler.diagnoseFailedJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.diagnoseFailedJob inspects the failed Job's Pods so a delegating coordinator reading fetch_task_output can adapt, for example recreating the Agent with more memory. It reports OOMKilled first, then a non-zero exit code, else the generic "job failed"; a Pod listing failure falls back to the generic message rather than blocking completion.
- **Pod waiting messages carry a prefix** (`controller.podWaitingMessagePrefix`): controller.podWaitingMessagePrefix marks a Running Task message that reports why its Pod cannot start, so syncPodWaitingMessage can clear it once the Pod moves on and timedOutTaskMessage can keep it in the timeout failure.
- **A deleted Agent does not block timed-out completion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskJobSucceededBy`): When the Task's Agent has been deleted, controller.TaskReconciler.taskJobSucceededBy treats the Task as non-autonomous, matching normal Job completion, rather than leaving the outcome unsettled.
- **Failure diagnosis reads the last termination state** (`controller.TaskReconciler.diagnoseFailedJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.diagnoseFailedJob falls back to a container's LastTerminationState, because a Pod that crashed and restarted exposes the relevant terminated state only there.
- **Revocation epoch and detach barrier are stamped before revocation** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleFinalizing`): Attach and the Task epoch annotation are separate API writes, so controller.TaskReconciler.handleFinalizing persists the enforced epoch and the pending-detach barrier before generic revocation clears the attachment. The Finalizing gate defers class settlement, and without both stamps a continuation could attach in that window and the terminal settle could reapply or skip this Task's frozen Suspend/Delete action.
- **Workspace detach waits for Job cleanup and artifact retirement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompleted`): Detach can suspend or delete the RuntimePool that terminal recovery still needs, so controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompleted settles the class workspace only after Job cleanup and durable ACP artifact retirement have settled.
- **Terminal webhooks follow artifact retirement** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompleted`): controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompleted sends optional terminal webhooks only after durable ACP artifact identities retired; delivery state is durable and bounded in Task status, failed delivery never delays artifact reclamation, and the attempt runs in the delivery runnable so a slow receiver cannot hold the shared reconcile worker. Parent history enforcement waits until the notice settles.
- **A finished Task's Job is deleted before its cascade** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stopFinishedTaskJob`): controller.TaskReconciler.stopFinishedTaskJob deletes the Job of a Task its completion just made terminal before the cancel cascade runs, because an earlier Job delete may have failed and the cascade must not run while the Task's own container still runs. A failure is logged and the completed Task's own reconcile retries it.
- **Deleting a consolidation Task prunes its run history** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseMemoryConsolidationRun`): controller.TaskReconciler.releaseMemoryConsolidationRun abandons an unfinished run and clears its transcripts when the Task ends; on deletion it also clears the run report and lets the store remove runs no cursor needs, so a recurring run's history does not grow the store. Releasing again is a no-op.
- **A cancel request cascades even if the Task ended Failed** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cascadeTaskCancellation`): controller.TaskReconciler.cascadeTaskCancellation keys on the cancel-requested annotation as well as the Cancelled phase, because recovery records an interrupted cancel as Failed with an unknown outcome. Like every cascade call, it runs right after the Task's own stop, never before it.
- **Cancel cascade walks every real child** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks`): controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks walks every real child of a cancelled Task at every depth: a finished child keeps its outcome but is walked because work it delegated can outlive it, and a cancel-requested or deleting child is walked because its own cascade may never run (for example when its status write or finalizer keeps failing).
- **Scheduled templates are outside the cancel cascade** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks`): A scheduled child Task is a template that outlives whoever created it, so the cancel cascade (controller.TaskReconciler.cancelChildTasks) and controller.TaskReconciler.stoppedAncestor leave it to its own owner: cancelling it would end its future runs, and its runs answer only to it.
- **Continuation chains stop at an unverified link** (`controller.TaskReconciler.childContinuationChain`): controller.TaskReconciler.childContinuationChain verifies each continuation against the resumed wait that records it; a link that is gone or no longer verifies ends the chain, because nothing past it can run as this child's turn.
- **Delegated cancellation also cancels parked waits** (`controller.TaskReconciler.requestDelegatedTaskCancellation`): controller.TaskReconciler.requestDelegatedTaskCancellation gives an unfinished delegated Task a cancel request and also cancels a parked wait, which a finished turn can still hold, so it never starts another turn.
- **Delegation index reads the API server and lists only what it may** (`controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex`): controller.TaskReconciler.delegatedTaskIndex lists Tasks from the API server, not the cache, because a child in another namespace enqueues no reconcile of its parent when it reaches the cache. The root's namespace is always listed; other namespaces only when namespace isolation is off and the controller may list Tasks cluster-wide; delegatedBy keeps only real children.
- **Only a cancel is an ancestor stop signal** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stoppedAncestor`): controller.TaskReconciler.stoppedAncestor walks the delegation chain through normally finished ancestors and names the first one cancelled or cancel-requested. Deletion, as retention does, cancels nothing; a gone or replaced ancestor, or a link no immutable UID pins, ends the walk without a verdict. A link is pinned by the controller owner reference in one namespace, or by the parent UID a brokered delegation stamps, read in the parent's recorded namespace.
- **Cross-namespace delegation is proven by a trusted UID annotation** (`controller.delegatedBy`): controller.delegatedBy uses the controller owner reference in the parent's namespace; owner references cannot cross namespaces, so a child elsewhere must record the parent's UID in an annotation only the controller and trusted workers may write.
- **Agent and ACP Tasks never use the native cancel path** (`controller.requestedTaskCancellation`): controller.requestedTaskCancellation excludes Agent Tasks and Tasks with ACP execution from the native terminal path, because they are cancelled by cancelRequestedAgentTask under the controller epoch guard.
- **Completion stops work before storage finalization** (`controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome`): controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome collects available output before cleanup but stops failed or cancelled work even when storage finalization must wait for recovery, keeping the Task nonterminal in Kubernetes so those writes are retried. The staged intent and workload outcome keep the decided outcome; collection may change only phase and message.
- **Oversized results finish without the result** (`controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome`): A result above the store limit can never be stored, so controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome finishes the Task without it and records why (taskResultTooLargeOutcome) rather than retrying forever or reporting a success whose result was not stored.
- **Uncertain storage writes are retried during completion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome`): During completion, a result, transcript or Session lock release the store has not accepted yet, or may have saved, is retried on the next attempt instead of dropped. Transcript appends are safe to replay because messages have stable IDs, and the Session lock release adds the Task's token usage in the same write so a replay counts nothing twice.
- **A cancelled Task keeps its plan** (`controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome`): controller.TaskReconciler.completeTaskWithOutcome deletes a finished Task's plan state but keeps it for a Cancelled Task, because the plan shows how far it got; Task deletion removes it.
- **A recorded workload outcome is never replayed** (`controller.executionOutcomePreventsReplay`): controller.executionOutcomePreventsReplay treats every recorded outcome as final: retryable failed attempts go through retryTask before completeExecutedTask records an outcome, and once one is recorded no attempt is replayed.
- **MaxRetries counts additional attempts** (`controller.TaskReconciler.shouldRetry`): Status.Attempts counts the initial run plus completed retries while RetryPolicy.MaxRetries counts additional retries, so controller.TaskReconciler.shouldRetry retries while Attempts <= MaxRetries.
- **Retry resets to Pending before deleting the old Job** (`controller.TaskReconciler.retryTask`): controller.TaskReconciler.retryTask resets the Task to Pending and clears its Job before deleting the old Job, so a transient NotFound from asynchronous Job deletion cannot fail the Task.
- **Retry delay is enforced on every Job start** (`controller.TaskReconciler.remainingRetryDelay`): RequeueAfter does not stop Task or owned-Job events from reconciling sooner, so controller.TaskReconciler.remainingRetryDelay enforces the same retry deadline on every attempt to start a Job.
- **Retry deadline rounds up to the next second** (`controller.TaskReconciler.remainingRetryDelay`): metav1.Time JSON drops fractional seconds, so controller.TaskReconciler.remainingRetryDelay starts the delay at the next whole second after the transition time; persistence then cannot shorten a positive (even subsecond) delay, at the cost of at most one extra second.
- **Validation command output is never collected** (`controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult`): Repository monitor validation commands may process fixtures or source that contain credentials, so controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult deliberately collects no output for them; the durable review record keeps only the command digest and safe status.
- **Rejected Job names are never read for results** (`controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult`): A Job name whose identity was rejected is retained only for cleanup, so controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult never collects results from it.
- **Result collection prefers the durable finalization snapshot** (`controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult`): controller.TaskReconciler.collectResult prefers the durable deferred-finalization snapshot after outage cleanup removed the Pods; an intent with no chunks records that the workload had no output, so recovery never depends on logs from a Pod that never started.
- **Oversized stdout results are permanent failures** (`controller.extractStdoutTaskResult`): Stdout results are bounded at store.MaxResultBytes and logs at stdoutResultLogLimitBytes. controller.extractStdoutTaskResult refuses a base64 payload whose DecodedLen exceeds the limit plus two padding bytes before decoding, so no more than the limit is decoded, staged or stored. errTaskResultTooLarge and errStdoutResultLogTooLarge wrap store.ErrTooLarge because neither a result nor a log shrinks on retry, so both are permanent.
- **Oversized results fail an otherwise successful Task** (`controller.taskResultTooLargeOutcome`): controller.taskResultTooLargeOutcome turns a Task that would have Succeeded into Failed when its result exceeds store.MaxResultBytes and is not stored, keeps the phase of a Cancelled or Failed Task, and appends the reason to the completion message.
- **Stdout result logs cut at the limit are refused** (`controller.TaskReconciler.readStdoutResultPodLogs`): controller.TaskReconciler.readStdoutResultPodLogs reads one byte past stdoutResultLogLimitBytes so a log cut at the limit is refused (boundedStdoutResultLog) rather than searched: the cut can fall inside the result line and leave a base64 prefix that still decodes, or drop the result line entirely.
- **Agent Tasks never resolve a Provider** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveProviderRef`): controller.TaskReconciler.resolveProviderRef returns no Provider for Agent Tasks, because agent runtimes manage their own credentials; for other Tasks the Task-level providerRef wins over the Agent's.
- **Soul compatibility applies to every Agent Task path** (`controller.validatePlannedRuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions`): controller.validatePlannedRuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions checks soul runtime compatibility for every new Agent Task path, not only runtimeRef dispatch.
- **External path restrictions use the resolved plan** (`controller.validatePlannedRuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions`): planAgentExecution resolves runtimeRef before selecting the external path, so controller.validatePlannedRuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions applies harness v2 runtimeRef restrictions before any agent runtime receives work.
- **Legacy defaultMaxTurns 50 is tolerated for runtimeRef Agents** (`controller.validateHarnessV2RuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions`): Older CRDs defaulted Agent defaultMaxTurns to 50, so upgraded Agents may keep it. External v2 sessions receive no AgentConfiguration, making that historical default inert, so controller.validateHarnessV2RuntimeRefAgentTaskRestrictions accepts 50 and rejects any other value as an unsupported runtime-profile override.
- **Unhonorable Agent identities run no Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.validateTaskAgentCompatibility`): controller.TaskReconciler.validateTaskAgentCompatibility rejects an identity-bound Agent this controller cannot honor for every Task type, not only the runtime Tasks that freeze its identity (ADR 0039). See [ADR 0039](0039-agent-identities.md).
- **runtimeRef Agents are validated at planning** (`controller.TaskReconciler.validateAgentRuntimeTaskCompatibility`): controller.TaskReconciler.validateAgentRuntimeTaskCompatibility skips the runtime ceiling check for runtimeRef Agents, because they are judged during planning against the registered AgentRuntime policy that dispatch actually exposes.
- **Read-only Codex Tasks are supported** (`controller.validateReadOnlyBuiltInAgentRuntime`): controller.validateReadOnlyBuiltInAgentRuntime accepts read-only Codex Tasks because they run inside the RuntimeSession boundary with controller-rejected elevation requests, supervisor-mediated file writes, and read-intent workspace delta classification that fails any modifying turn, using the same per-session loopback provider credential as other runtimes.
- **An invalid edited schedule fails the Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled`): controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled fails a scheduled Task whose schedule was edited to an invalid cron expression, matching the initial parse failure in handleScheduledTask.
- **Missed schedule windows re-anchor the cursor** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled`): When a scheduled Task misses its starting deadline, controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled advances NextScheduleTime and re-anchors LastScheduleTime to now, the documented contract for a skipped window; otherwise a suspend or gap longer than the deadline freezes the anchor and every reconcile recomputes the same missed slot forever. A failed cursor write returns an error to retry promptly rather than sleeping to the next tick with the stale cursor.
- **Parked runs count toward schedule concurrency** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled`): Under ForbidConcurrent, controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled counts a parked run as active until its continuation chain's tail settles, because it still owns unfinished work; a resumed wait is terminal and its continuation may lack a phase or be missing from the cache, so the tail is resolved from the authoritative reader.
- **Scheduled runs are sealed against their parent** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled`): A scheduled run acts for the scheduled Task's requester, so controller.TaskReconciler.handleScheduled seals it against its verified parent with ACPChildTaskSealer; an unsealed run's connector tools fail closed.
- **History limits count logical runs** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceHistoryLimits`): controller.TaskReconciler.enforceHistoryLimits treats a parked run and its continuations as one logical run: the chain's head represents it, its tail supplies the outcome, and deleting the head cascades to every continuation through owner references. Linked continuations are counted and pruned with their head, a lookup failure counts as linked, and a chain still owning unfinished work (a parked wait or running continuation) is never pruned because deletion would cancel that work.
- **History pruning holds runs with unsent approval notices** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun keeps a scheduled run beyond the history limit while any Task in its continuation chain has a pending approval notice, an outstanding terminal notice, or approval events not yet projected (ADR 0053), because deleting a Task drops its queued notices and only its own reconcile can send them. It reads uncached state and follows at most the chain bound; a failed Task read keeps the run, and a failed event-store read keeps it only within approvalWebhookSigningWindow of the chain settling. The held run still counts so no newer run is pruned in its place. Without a notifier nothing is held. See [ADR 0053](0053-approval-webhook-events.md).
- **Worker RBAC is trust-tiered** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureWorkerRBAC`): Worker roles are bound per Task type: the AI worker role is deliberately broader because code_exec's Kubernetes backend creates per-job ServiceAccounts and Secrets, while vendor and container workers use separate, narrower roles so those cluster-wide capabilities are not shared with less-trusted Task types.
- **Static installs never prune trusted Service grants** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureWorkerRBAC`): Static installations reject cross-namespace trusted Service references at startup, so the trusted Service read binding sync never discovers or mutates legacy grants for them.
- **Stale worker RoleBindings are deleted until the name is free** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureWorkerRBAC`): RoleRef is immutable, so a worker RoleBinding with a stale RoleRef must be deleted and recreated; the API server may still serve the stale object while deletion propagates, or another actor may recreate it, so the controller keeps deleting and retrying until the name is available.
- **Native approval parking ignores expiresAt** (`controller.TaskReconciler.pendingApprovalsForTask`): controller.TaskReconciler.pendingApprovalsForTask evaluates pending approvals at zero time, so only explicit terminal approval events resolve a park. No producer records approval expiry, and passive expiresAt evaluation would silently resume consequential work.
- **Which approvals mark an ACP Task waiting** (`controller.TaskReconciler.syncACPWaitingForApproval`): controller.TaskReconciler.syncACPWaitingForApproval evaluates brokered approvals at the current time, so one past its expiry no longer blocks, and excludes parked sign-offs, because a parked sign-off ends the turn and its decision starts a continuation instead of blocking the running turn.
- **Unresolvable continuation chains count as unsettled** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settledTaskContinuationTail`): controller.TaskReconciler.settledTaskContinuationTail returns the Task whose outcome represents a continuation chain, and reports unsettled while the chain owns unfinished work or cannot be resolved, so cleanup never cancels work it cannot see; a chain whose continuation is already gone is settled.
- **A replaced Task name holds the run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): When another incarnation holds a chain member's name, controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun keeps the run, because it cannot judge a Task that is not this run.
- **Outstanding terminal notices hold a finished run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun`): The terminal notice is sent asynchronously after the run's own reconcile queued it, so a parent can see a finished run whose notice still awaits a delivery worker; controller.TaskReconciler.approvalNoticesHoldRun holds such a run. An unfinished continuation inherits the webhook URL but owes no notice yet.
- **Only approval transitions hold a run** (`controller.approvalWebhookTransitionEventTypes`): controller.approvalWebhookTransitionEventTypes lists only the approval events projection turns into transitions; execution updates never advance the projection cursor, so they must not hold a run.
- **Pending approval projection detection** (`controller.TaskReconciler.approvalEventsAwaitProjection`): controller.TaskReconciler.approvalEventsAwaitProjection reports an approval transition above the projection cursor only for a tracking Task that selects approval events and has not overflowed. One indexed read past the cursor answers the common case; only when it finds a transition event is the stream derived as projection derives it, because a non-transition event such as a duplicate request never advances the cursor and would otherwise hold the run forever.
- **Suspended autonomous Tasks stay Running** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleAutonomousIteration`): controller.TaskReconciler.handleAutonomousIteration keeps a suspended autonomous Task Running rather than terminal, so it resumes when Suspend is unset.
- **Provider use is rechecked on every reconcile** (`controller.TaskReconciler.resolveProvider`): With provider use authorization enabled, TaskReconciler.resolveProvider refuses Providers taken from an Agent in another namespace on every run. The Agent may have changed after API admission, and Tasks written directly to the cluster were never admitted, so admission-time checks cannot be trusted.
- **Usage observations of one call count once** (`store.UsageObservation.CounterID`): Token usage observations that share a CounterID, such as a running snapshot and the completed summary of one provider call, are counted once when Task usage is summed. Summing every observation would double-count calls.
- **Session usage is added in the lock-release write** (`controller.SessionManager.ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage`): ReleaseLockWithTokenUsage adds a Task's token usage to its Session in the same write that releases the Session lock. A completion replayed after a crash finds no lock held and adds nothing, so usage is never counted twice.
- **Agent Task usage is counted at turn finalization** (`controller.TaskReconciler.sessionTokenUsage`): Agent Tasks are ACP Session turns whose usage is counted when the turn finalizes, so sessionTokenUsage reports zero for them at Task completion. Adding usage there too would double-count agent turns.
- **Completion webhooks are sent by the delivery runnable** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleCompleted`): TaskReconciler.handleCompleted hands a due completion notice to the webhook delivery runnable and never sends it inline. Sending from the reconcile would block the reconcile loop on remote endpoints and bypass the dispatcher's backoff and fencing.
- **A recovered Job keeps its frozen Connection bindings** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): When createTaskJob finds that the Task's Job already exists, it adopts the Connection bindings frozen into that Job rather than freezing new ones, and only for a Job whose UID was recorded. The Job already runs with those bindings, so recording newer ones would misreport what the workload can access.
- **Job identity without bindings is redispatched** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): If a status write recorded a Task's Job identity but not its Connection bindings before a crash, the next reconcile in createTaskJob dispatches again to recover the bindings instead of treating the Task as fully dispatched.
- **Worker RBAC is namespaced only** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureWorkerRBAC`): ensureWorkerRBAC binds the worker ClusterRole only through RoleBindings in the Task namespace and never creates a ClusterRoleBinding. An installation may grant worker access only inside the namespaces it watches.
- **Chat-created Tasks attribute events by label** (`controller.chatSessionEventAttribution`): A chat-created Task has no sessionRef; its orka.ai/chat-session label attributes lifecycle events to that chat Session only when the label names an existing chat Session. This attribution never changes taskSessionName, so Session continuity and locking are unaffected.
- **Deferred result chunks stay below the Secret size limit** (`controller.deferredResultChunkBytes`): deferredResultChunkBytes is 512 KiB so each Task-owned output chunk Secret stays well below the Kubernetes 1 MiB object limit.
- **Deferred finalization survives storage outages** (`controller.deferredTaskFinalization`): A storage outage must not keep failed or cancelled work running, erase available output, or turn the missing Job into an execution retry. The deferredTaskFinalization intent is published immutably only after all output chunks exist, and only then is cascading Job deletion allowed. Output lives only in content-addressed Task-owned Secrets until the result provider accepts it, so interrupted staging can be retried as logs grow, and neither output nor Secret data appears in errors or logs.
- **Oversized results are refused, not staged** (`controller.deferredTaskFinalization.ResultTooLarge`): deferredTaskFinalization.ResultTooLarge records output above store.MaxResultBytes, which is refused rather than staged, so finalization can record why the Task has no result.
- **A refused stdout log is reported as a log refusal** (`controller.deferredTaskFinalization.ResultLogTooLarge`): deferredTaskFinalization.ResultLogTooLarge narrows ResultTooLarge to a stdout result log longer than its read limit, so finalization does not report a possibly small result as too large. It is valid only together with ResultTooLarge.
- **Stop decisions survive usage bookkeeping failures** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stopTaskAfterUsageRetentionFailure`): stopTaskAfterUsageRetentionFailure stops requested work even when durable usage bookkeeping cannot be written, and leaves the Task nonterminal so durable finalization completes once storage recovers.
- **Output is persisted before a blocked Job is deleted** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stopJobForDeferredFinalization`): stopJobForDeferredFinalization persists output and the original stop decision before removing a Job whose finalization is blocked on storage. Inactive failed Jobs keep their Pods so their output remains readable.
- **Deferred Job deletion is fenced by UID and version** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stopJobForDeferredFinalization`): The Job delete in stopJobForDeferredFinalization uses the observed UID and resourceVersion as preconditions, so a same-name Job recreated between staging and deletion is never deleted.
- **A staged finalization intent wins** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stageDeferredTaskFinalization`): stageDeferredTaskFinalization is used only before deleting an unsettled Job, and a pending intent wins over later observations of the missing Job so the original stop decision is not replaced by a retry or failure.
- **Unknown validation classification suppresses output** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stageDeferredTaskFinalization`): When the repository-monitor binding lookup is unavailable, stageDeferredTaskFinalization suppresses possible validation output without keeping cancelled or failed work running. Normal finalization retries the authoritative classification before recording status.
- **Unstorable output does not block a stop** (`controller.TaskReconciler.stageDeferredTaskFinalization`): Output that exceeds the result limit can never be stored, so stageDeferredTaskFinalization does not stage it and stopping the work never waits on it.
- **Finalization intent outlives result acceptance** (`controller.TaskReconciler.cleanupDeferredTaskFinalization`): cleanupDeferredTaskFinalization keeps the intent until Kubernetes records the Task's terminal phase. Result acceptance alone is insufficient because Session finalization or the status write can still fail.
- **Started Pod log read failures block cleanup** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskPodResult`): taskPodResult is shared by normal collection and outage staging. A Pod that never started has no output to preserve, but a read failure for a started Pod blocks destructive cleanup so recovery cannot silently lose output.
- **Unstarted Pod detection respects GetLogs container defaulting** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskPodResult`): GetLogs leaves the container name empty and the API defaults it only for a Pod with one regular container; init containers never supply Task output. taskPodResult therefore treats only single-container pending Pods as unstarted and keeps log errors for ambiguous multi-container Pods.
- **Only recorded Job UIDs are adopted** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recoverTaskJob`): recoverTaskJob adopts a Job only when the controller already recorded its UID. An unbound Job may have executed before a crash, so it is neither adopted nor automatically replaced; names and owner references alone are forgeable by anyone who can create Jobs in the namespace.
- **Unverified Jobs are fenced before deletion** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recoverTaskJob`): Before deleting an unverified Job, recoverTaskJob persists its name for cleanup and a replay barrier condition. The unverified UID never becomes worker authorization.
- **Lost-start Jobs are retired before terminal projection** (`controller.TaskReconciler.retireAISoulTaskJob`): retireAISoulTaskJob fences replay before retiring a Job that may have started even though its Running status write was lost. Terminal projection waits for foreground deletion to finish so the worker cannot outlive the Task.
- **Desired Task specs mirror CRD defaults** (`controller.taskSpecWithServerDefaults`): The controller mirrors the +kubebuilder:default values of TaskSpec in Go constants because a freshly built desired spec compared against a stored Task never matches unless the API server's defaults are applied first. Zero-value defaults need no mirror; TestTaskSpecWithServerDefaultsMatchesCRD keeps the mirror aligned with the generated CRD.
- **Nested CRD defaults apply only under present parents** (`controller.taskSpecWithServerDefaults`): taskSpecWithServerDefaults applies defaults exactly as the API server would on create: nested defaults apply only when the enclosing object is present, mirroring OpenAPI defaulting.
- **Always-serialized fields need no default mirror** (`controller.taskSpecWithServerDefaults`): Fields serialized without omitempty, such as sessionRef.append, are always sent by Go clients, so the API server never applies their CRD default and taskSpecWithServerDefaults does not mirror it.
- **Retry identity conflicts never echo spec values** (`controller.matchingReviewRetryTask`): The retry-identity conflict diagnostic is persisted into the scan run and RepositoryScan condition, so it names differing field paths only and never echoes spec values, which may hold secrets, from either Task.
- **A Job that succeeded before the timeout succeeds** (`controller.TaskReconciler.handleRunning`): When a Task's completion is deferred past its timeout, for example while the store refuses writes, handleRunning completes it as the Job's success if the Job finished before the deadline. A Job that succeeded after the deadline, or whose completion time is unknown, still times out, and a failed lookup never lets timeout finalization overwrite a real success.
- **Controller-side tool executors mirror worker transaction authority** (`controller.bindVerifiedTaskTransactionAuthority`): bindVerifiedTaskTransactionAuthority gives a per-request Tool executor the verified Task's transaction and credential authority exactly as the worker Job environment and owner-referenced token Secret would. Callers must authenticate the Task first and bind a fresh executor per request; the raw token goes only to that executor and is never logged, persisted, or reused across Tasks.
- **Transactionless Tasks get empty authority** (`controller.bindVerifiedTaskTransactionAuthority`): A Task without transaction context gets no credential authority and no task-scoped token, matching worker Jobs. The empty authority still disables controller-process token-file fallback, and an explicitly disabled exchange keeps controller-wide TTS settings from applying to the request.
- **Task transaction tokens stay request-scoped** (`controller.readTaskOwnedTransactionToken`): readTaskOwnedTransactionToken loads the delegated transaction token only from the Task's owner-referenced Secret and hands it only to the per-request executor; it is never logged, persisted, or reused across Tasks.
- **Parked turns are data-only** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler`): TaskWaitReconciler arms a wait only after the exact turn that requested it settled Succeeded, so no prompt, tool call, or publication is in flight. It never preserves or restores process memory: when the awaited event arrives it creates one continuation Task on the same Session, which re-freezes and revalidates policy like any other continuation. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Continuation lineage needs provenance admission** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.TaskProvenanceProtected`): TaskWaitReconciler.TaskProvenanceProtected reports that Task provenance admission reserves the continuation lineage annotations to the controller. Only then can an existing Task with the reserved continuation name be proven to be Orka's continuation.
- **Created continuations are remembered until linked** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.created`): TaskWaitReconciler records the UID of each continuation it created and has not linked yet. That in-memory record is the proof that lets a later reconcile link the continuation without provenance admission when the linking status write kept losing races.
- **Cached wait reads are safe under resourceVersion fencing** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reader`): TaskWaitReconciler serves frequent watch-driven reads from the cache because every wait transition is fenced on the Task resourceVersion, so a stale read can only lose a race and requeue.
- **Wait creation decisions read through the API server** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.authoritativeReader`): TaskWaitReconciler.authoritativeReader bypasses the cache wherever a stale cache miss would cause a redundant continuation create attempt.
- **Wait transitions act on the authoritative Task** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.Reconcile`): Before a wait transition, TaskWaitReconciler.Reconcile rereads the Task from the API server because a cached Task may predate its turn's settlement or a cancellation request.
- **Waits arm only on proven turn success** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileRequested`): reconcileRequested arms a parked wait only when the exact turn that requested it is proven to have settled Succeeded. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Deadline closes parked approvals before resuming** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileWaiting`): When a wait deadline passes first, reconcileWaiting closes an undecided parked approval before anything resumes; if a human decision races the close, the decision wins.
- **Parked approvals are pending until their request event is visible** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.evaluate`): The parked approval request event is appended when the wait arms, and evaluate treats the approval as simply pending until that event is visible.
- **Child waits confirm missing children authoritatively** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.evaluateTasks`): The cache may not yet have observed a just-created child or continuation, so evaluateTasks resumes a parked Task on a missing, replaced, or unlinked child only after authoritative reads confirm it.
- **Child waits are bound to child UIDs** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.evaluateTasksWith`): Only the exact child UIDs recorded when the wait was authorized count toward a child wait; a same-named replacement never controls when the parked Task resumes.
- **Continuations keep authority over delegated children** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.taskWaitChildOwners`): taskWaitChildOwners returns the parked Task and its verified predecessors, because a continuation keeps authority over the children its predecessors delegated.
- **Child waits recheck the parent link** (`controller.taskWaitChildBelongs`): taskWaitChildBelongs re-checks the parent link verified when the wait was requested, so a deleted-and-recreated child name is not mistaken for the awaited child.
- **Continuations are created exactly once** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileResuming`): reconcileResuming durably reserves the continuation name before creating it, so a restarted controller adopts the existing Task instead of creating another.
- **Unproven continuations are abandoned, not linked** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileResuming`): Without Task provenance admission, anyone who can create Tasks could forge the continuation lineage annotation, so reconcileResuming neither links nor deletes an existing unproven continuation; it ends the wait as abandoned. Execution holds every continuation until its predecessor links it, so the unproven Task never runs.
- **Continuation requesters are sealed before linking** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileResuming`): reconcileResuming seals the continuation's requester stamp before linking it, so a restart between creation and sealing retries the seal while the parked Task is still Resuming.
- **Continuations are marked API-sourced at creation** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileResuming`): A continuation that must carry a requester is created with the API requested-by-source annotation so admission waits for the seal instead of freezing a snapshot without the requester's connectors.
- **Unsealed continuations still link with connectors failing closed** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.reconcileResuming`): If sealing the requester fails, reconcileResuming still links the continuation because linking matters more than its connectors; admission waits out the stamp grace period and then fails connector tools closed, like an unsealed scheduled run.
- **Created continuations are linked by the creating reconcile** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.markCreatedResumed`): markCreatedResumed links the continuation this controller created. Creating it is the proof that it is Orka's, and a later reconcile can re-prove that only with provenance admission, so a lost status-write race is retried against the authoritative Task while its wait still reserves this continuation and is not cancelled.
- **Continuation link finalizer guards adoption** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.releaseContinuationLink`): releaseContinuationLink removes the link finalizer only once the predecessor recorded the continuation, ended its wait without it, or no longer exists. While the predecessor is still Resuming with this name, the finalizer stays so recovery adopts this Task instead of creating another.
- **Cancelling a wait deletes its continuation** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.cancel`): TaskWaitReconciler.cancel ends a non-terminal wait and deletes a continuation created in the same window, which cancels it like any other agent Task.
- **Cancellation reads the continuation authoritatively** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.cancel`): TaskWaitReconciler.cancel reads the continuation through the API server because a just-created continuation may not be cached yet, and missing it would leave it running after cancellation.
- **Only proven continuations are deleted on cancel** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.cancel`): TaskWaitReconciler.cancel deletes only a continuation proven to be Orka's, with a UID precondition. An unproven one is left alone because execution holds it until a link the cancelled wait will never record.
- **Human approval decisions win over wait closure** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.closeParkedApproval`): closeParkedApproval appends the terminal event for an undecided parked approval and reports false when a human decision landed first, so the caller re-evaluates and the decision wins.
- **Closing a parked approval ensures its request exists** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.closeParkedApproval`): A wait that never armed may lack its approval request event, so closeParkedApproval ensures the request exists before appending the closing event.
- **Wait transitions are doubly fenced** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.transition`): TaskWaitReconciler.transition applies one wait mutation fenced on both the Task resourceVersion and the exact prior wait state, and reports a lost race instead of overwriting.
- **Continuation chains share one deadline** (`controller.taskWaitChainDeadline`): Each continuation is created with only the remaining time of its parked Task, so taskWaitChainDeadline is the deadline of the whole continuation chain.
- **Continuations inherit policy labels only** (`controller.taskContinuationInheritedLabels`): A continuation inherits the parked Task's policy labels and annotations listed in taskContinuationInheritedLabels so its frozen runtime policy matches the parked turn; controller runtime state is never copied.
- **Continuations stay bound to the exact chat** (`controller.taskContinuationInheritedLabels`): The chat Session namespace and epoch labels are inherited along with the chat name so a continuation stays a turn of the exact chat incarnation that created it.
- **Continuations record the absolute chain deadline** (`controller.buildTaskContinuation`): buildTaskContinuation copies the parked Task's execution inputs and changes only the prompt, Session reference flags, and remaining timeout. It also records the chain's absolute deadline so creation latency or clock skew cannot extend it.
- **Continuations drop the pre-publication remote SHA** (`controller.buildTaskContinuation`): When the parked turn published a verified commit, its Session records that commit as the baseline for the next publication, so buildTaskContinuation clears workspace.expectedRemoteSHA; keeping it would conflict with the new baseline and fail the continuation.
- **Chain deadline truncation is safe** (`controller.buildTaskContinuation`): The continuation chain deadline is stored as RFC 3339 without sub-second precision, which can only shorten the deadline, never extend it.
- **Continuations are owned but not coordination children** (`controller.buildTaskContinuation`): A continuation has a non-controller owner reference to its parked Task, so deleting the parked Task cancels the continuation without making it a coordination child.
- **Continuation prompts are controller-authored** (`controller.taskContinuationPrompt`): taskContinuationPrompt is controller-authored: it describes the awaited event and never repeats the parked prompt, which the Session transcript already holds. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
- **Continuation requester seals prove lineage by owner and annotation** (`controller.sealContinuationRequesterStamp`): sealContinuationRequesterStamp binds the parked Task's verified requester to its continuation like SealChildRequesterStamp, but proves the link by the parked Task's owner reference and the continuation annotation.
- **Task changes wake waiting Tasks** (`controller.TaskWaitReconciler.SetupWithManager`): TaskWaitReconciler.SetupWithManager maps every Task change to the parked Task waiting on it, both a coordination parent for child waits and the predecessor a continuation resumes, so chained child waits settle promptly.
- **History limits count a continuation chain as one run** (`controller.TaskReconciler.enforceHistoryLimits`): enforceHistoryLimits prunes a parked scheduled run and its continuations as one run, and the run's outcome is the outcome of its last continuation, so a run whose continuation failed counts against the failed-run limit even though its first Task succeeded.
- **Webhook attempts are claimed durably before sending** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery`): WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery keeps the notice in Task.status.webhookDelivery so it survives restarts and leader changes. Each attempt is claimed by persisting the incremented attempt count and a lease (NextAttemptTime) with the Task resourceVersion before any request is sent, so only one of two racing controllers can claim it; a crash loses that attempt and the next waits out the lease. Receivers deduplicate on the stable delivery ID. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Parked Tasks retire notices recorded before the continuation rule** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery`): A continuation inherits spec.webhookURL and reports the chain's outcome, so a parked Task owes no notice of its own. reconcileDelivery retires a notice that a parked Task recorded before this rule existed instead of leaving it pending forever.
- **Stale webhook notices are superseded** (`controller.terminalNoticeStillCurrent`): terminalNoticeStillCurrent requires the Task's current state to owe exactly the pending notice: the same event, due now, with a byte-identical body. Otherwise the pending notice is superseded before its next attempt, as ADR 0042 requires. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Task completion work waits for webhook delivery** (`controller.terminalNoticeOutstanding`): terminalNoticeOutstanding is shared by reconcileDelivery and the Task reconciler so the reconciler holds back the Task's completion work exactly while its terminal notice still needs delivery, including while an attempt lease or failure backoff runs.
- **Newer Task state never accelerates webhook retries** (`controller.terminalNoticeOutstanding`): terminalNoticeOutstanding honors the attempt lease and failure backoff before anything else, so newer Task state never accelerates webhook retries.
- **Deferred parked notices are woken by wait transitions** (`controller.terminalNoticeOutstanding`): While a parked turn's wait can still resume the work, terminalNoticeOutstanding writes nothing and requeues nothing, because the wait's own status transitions reconcile the Task again.
- **Webhook delivery records are re-resolved after each write** (`controller.webhookRecordRef`): webhookRecordRef looks up a notice's live delivery record in Task status again after every status write, because each write replaces the decoded status and invalidates earlier pointers.
- **Notices wait for signing keys unless a policy retires them** (`controller.webhookRetryPolicy.signingUnavailable`): When signing keys are unavailable, webhookRetryPolicy.signingUnavailable may retire notices whose wait for keys has ended. Without that hook, as for terminal notices, a notice stays pending until signing is fixed.
- **Unrecorded webhook attempts still count** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.attemptNotice`): attemptNotice counts an attempt whose result was never recorded, after a restart or failed status write, and never claims past the attempt bound.
- **Webhooks are never sent unsigned** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.attemptNotice`): When no signing Secret is configured, attemptNotice fails the terminal notice permanently instead of sending it unsigned or requeueing a finished Task forever.
- **Webhook sends stay inside the persisted lease** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.attemptNotice`): attemptNotice starts the lease from a fresh clock reading after loading keys, and runs the claim write and the send under a timer started no later than that reading, so a replica cannot begin a POST after its persisted lease lets another replica claim the next attempt.
- **Persisted webhook leases round up** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.attemptNotice`): Status times serialize at whole seconds, so attemptNotice rounds the persisted lease up; truncation would let another replica claim the next attempt while this one can still send.
- **Webhook results require the same receiver** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.attemptNotice`): Notice bodies do not cover the destination URL, so a result is recorded on a re-read Task only when it still names the receiver the attempt was sent to; otherwise the notice is retried at the new receiver.
- **Webhook results are recorded only under the attempt's claim** (`controller.recordWebhookAttemptResult`): recordWebhookAttemptResult expects conflicting Task status writes and re-applies a result to the re-read Task only when it still names the same receiver, renders the sent notice, and still holds this attempt's claim. A replaced or settled claim is not this attempt's to record, and a changed Task state must supersede the notice rather than settle it with an older body; an unrecorded result leaves the lease to bound the next attempt, as a crash does. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Changed Task state gets a new delivery ID** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.currentNotice`): currentNotice rebuilds the pending notice for the Task's current state, and when the state no longer matches the recorded digest or the event changed, supersedes it with a fresh notice and a new delivery ID.
- **Notice timestamps match API second precision** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.currentNotice`): currentNotice truncates OccurredAt to whole seconds so the notice body stays byte-identical after a round trip through the API server's second-precision status times.
- **Parked Task webhook events follow the wait** (`controller.taskWebhookEvent`): A parked Task's phase is its own turn's outcome, not the work's, so taskWebhookEvent defers its notice while the wait can resume and leaves it to the continuation once resumed. A wait that ends without resuming reports task.cancelled when cancelled and task.failed when expired or abandoned; a failed or cancelled turn reports its phase, and a wait from a superseded earlier attempt does not decide the event. See [ADR 0041](0041-data-only-parked-turns.md).
