- **Missing signing keys do not consume attempts** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.recordSigningUnavailable`): recordSigningUnavailable keeps the notice pending without consuming an attempt, because nothing is ever sent unsigned and a configuration fix should resume delivery. A retry policy with a signing hook may instead retire the notice unsent.
- **Webhook delivery worker pool size** (`controller.DefaultTaskWebhookDeliveryWorkers`): DefaultTaskWebhookDeliveryWorkers bounds concurrent webhook attempts at 8. Each worker waits on one receiver for at most taskwebhook.DefaultRequestTimeout, so that many hanging receivers delay other notices but never Task reconciliation.
- **Webhook recheck interval bounds lost wakeups** (`controller.webhookDeliveryRecheckInterval`): webhookDeliveryRecheckInterval re-reconciles a Task with a queued or in-flight notice once a minute; the dispatcher's status writes normally reconcile the Task sooner, and this interval only bounds a lost wakeup.
- **Webhooks are delivered by a dispatcher outside the reconcile worker** (`controller.taskWebhookDispatcher`): The Task reconciler only decides a notice is due and enqueues the Task; taskWebhookDispatcher's bounded worker pool advances delivery against uncached reads with the durable claim-before-send lease. The work queue never hands one Task to two workers, so its notices go one attempt at a time in stream order, and a hanging receiver holds one worker for at most the request timeout, never the reconcile worker. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Terminal notices wait for completion work** (`controller.taskWebhookRequest`): taskWebhookRequest identifies one Task incarnation. Approval notices are owed in every phase, but the terminal notice only once the reconciler's completion work releases it.
- **Webhook delivery runs on the leader** (`controller.taskWebhookDispatcher.NeedLeaderElection`): taskWebhookDispatcher runs only on the elected leader alongside the Task controller, and the persisted attempt lease still fences any other replica.
- **Terminal notices are not sent after observed deletion** (`controller.taskWebhookDispatcher.deliver`): Recording an approval attempt may re-read the Task, so taskWebhookDispatcher.deliver checks deletion again before sending the terminal notice; a Task observed deleting sends no terminal notice.
- **Rereads treat replaced Tasks as gone** (`controller.rereadingTaskStatus.reread`): rereadingTaskStatus.reread reports a Task replaced under the same name as gone, so a new incarnation never receives an earlier incarnation's delivery result.
- **Scan workspaces canonicalize SSH roots to HTTPS** (`controller.repositoryScanTaskWorkspace`): The ACP workspace preflight admits only credential-free HTTPS repository URLs, so accepted GitHub-style SSH roots are canonicalized with security.CanonicalRepositoryCloneURL when building a scan Task workspace.
- **PR base branch is a write-only field** (`controller.repositoryScanTaskWorkspace`): prBaseBranch is a publication field that the ACP workspace preflight rejects on non-write intents, so it is set only on write workspaces.
- **Private Tool endpoints are a fixture-only allowance** (`controller.ToolReconciler.AllowPrivateConnectorEndpoints`): ToolReconciler.AllowPrivateConnectorEndpoints mirrors --connectors-allow-private-endpoints and lets only a Tool behind a connection-mode or agentIdentity outbound access policy target a private or cluster-local endpoint. It exists for local fixtures only.
- **Accepted is a remote-MCP-only condition** (`controller.ToolReconciler.Reconcile`): The Tool Accepted condition applies only to remote MCP Tools, including while a new actor backend is still being provisioned, so Reconcile removes it from every other Tool.
- **Task-bound MCP Tools have no controller-wide probe** (`controller.ToolReconciler.Reconcile`): There is no controller-wide probe authority for Task-bound MCP Tools; issuance and readiness are verified under the bound Task on every call, so Reconcile marks them available without a health probe.
- **Remote MCP readiness is not probed by the controller** (`controller.ToolReconciler.updateRemoteMCPStatus`): A controller-wide HEAD request cannot establish Task-authorized MCP readiness, so the remote MCP status path does not mark a Tool available from such a probe.
- **Forwarding Tools refuse parameter schemas** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateTool`): CRD admission cannot see the schemaless parameters field, so validateTool refuses a parameter schema on a forwarding HTTP Tool, matching prompt policy.
- **Private endpoint allowance is policy-scoped** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateTool`): Under the fixture allowance, validateTool lets only a Tool behind a connection-mode or agentIdentity outbound access policy point at a private endpoint.
- **Private fixture endpoints require HTTPS** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateTool`): The private-endpoint allowance relaxes where a credential-injecting call may go, never how: execution refuses plain http for credential-injecting policies, so validateTool accepts a private endpoint only over HTTPS and the Tool status matches what can actually run.
- **Infrastructure hosts stay blocked under the private allowance** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPURL`): With allowPrivate, validateToolHTTPURL skips only the private, loopback, and link-local address rules for local fixtures; metadata service and Kubernetes API server hosts stay blocked.
- **Tool URL host block list compares canonical names** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPURL`): DNS names are case-insensitive and a trailing dot names the same host, so validateToolHTTPURL lowercases and trims the trailing dot before comparing against the infrastructure block list.
- **Tools share the connector infrastructure block list** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPURL`): validateToolHTTPURL uses connectors.InfrastructureHostDenied, the same list the connector validator keeps under the fixture allowance: metadata services and the Kubernetes API service under any cluster domain, by name or address.
- **Substrate actor Tools reject outbound access policies** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateToolHTTPAuth`): Workers reach Substrate MCP actors only through the Orka MCP proxy, which never forwards caller-resolved outbound credentials or gateway routes, so mcp.substrateActor Tools reject outboundAccessPolicy.
- **Actor router targets stay inside the manager** (`controller.ToolReconciler.reconcileSubstrateMCPTool`): Workers and other callers reach a Substrate MCP actor only through the Orka API proxy path; the router URL and actor target are never published in Tool status.
- **Actor egress is verified before every resume** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForSubstrateMCPToolActor`): Substrate v1 routes all actor egress through the gateway under the actor's native policy, so the Tool controller installs and verifies that policy before every resume and leaves the actor suspended on any failure.
- **Unstarted actors need no suspend** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForSubstrateMCPToolActor`): An actor the current claim created has never been resumed, and one observed suspended has nothing running, so neither needs a suspend call after an egress failure.
- **Previous actors stop when their replacement fails** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForSubstrateMCPToolActor`): A Tool that moved to another actor keeps its previous actor running only until the replacement is ready; when the replacement's egress cannot be verified, the previous actor is stopped too.
- **Actors wait out egress policy propagation** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForSubstrateMCPToolActor`): After an actor's egress policy is stored, the gateway may still apply other rules under the actor's name until its cache expires; until then the actor must not run and the Tool is not offered.
- **Deleted actors are replaced immediately** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForSubstrateMCPToolActor`): When the actor was deleted, its replacement is created at once so the replacement's own propagation wait starts immediately instead of after the previous one.
- **Actors with unverified policy are stopped** (`controller.ToolReconciler.stopSubstrateMCPActor`): stopSubstrateMCPActor stops an actor the Tool still owns because it may run under a broader policy an earlier reconcile installed. An actor that keeps data is suspended and resumed by the next successful reconcile; one Substrate cannot suspend is deleted under its UID and recreated by the next claim.
- **Actor stops require the ownership lease** (`controller.ToolReconciler.stopSubstrateMCPActor`): stopSubstrateMCPActor acts only while the Tool holds the actor's ownership lease; without it the actor is not this Tool's to stop.
- **MCP actor release skips scrubbing** (`controller.ToolReconciler.stopSubstrateMCPActor`): MCP actors run no workspace daemon, so releasing one requests no scrub.
- **Deleted actors are forgotten before replacement** (`controller.ToolReconciler.forgetSubstrateMCPToolActor`): forgetSubstrateMCPToolActor drops the status record and booted marker of an actor the Tool deleted, because the actor created in its place under the same name has never run and a later reconcile must still boot it from scratch.
- **Booted marker is patched alone** (`controller.ToolReconciler.forgetSubstrateMCPToolActor`): forgetSubstrateMCPToolActor patches only the booted annotation on a copy, because the Tool in hand carries unwritten status changes that must not be persisted by a full update.
- **Failed reconciles stop actors with unverified policy** (`controller.ToolReconciler.failSubstrateMCPTool`): failSubstrateMCPTool handles a reconcile that failed before the actor's egress policy could be applied: if the policy is not verified for the current specification, the actor named in status is stopped and the reconcile retries until it is.
- **Unnamed actors are stopped by their reported template** (`controller.ToolReconciler.stopUnnamedSubstrateMCPActor`): stopUnnamedSubstrateMCPActor stops a held actor that Tool status does not name using the template Substrate reports for it, because the Tool specification may have moved on since the claim and the template decides between suspending and deleting.
- **Template identity must match to decide suspension** (`controller.ToolReconciler.stopHeldSubstrateMCPActor`): The current template under a name decides suspension only for an actor that booted from that exact template. A template recreated under the same name may differ in suspend support, so an actor whose template identity is unknown or different is stopped as one whose template cannot be read.
- **Template-less actors are denied egress and deleted** (`controller.ToolReconciler.denySubstrateMCPActorEgressWithoutTemplate`): Substrate cannot suspend an actor without its template, so denySubstrateMCPActorEgressWithoutTemplate replaces its egress policy with deny-all, which needs no template. Until the gateway's cached rules expire, an unsuspended actor is deleted, discarding its durable data, so nothing runs under rules the Tool no longer allows; once deny-all is enforced the actor is left alone and the error is still returned for retry.
- **Lease lookup failures do not hide held actors** (`controller.ToolReconciler.heldSubstrateMCPPoolActorLeaseRefs`): heldSubstrateMCPPoolActorLeaseRefs returns every pool actor ref proven held even when another lease cannot be read, together with the joined lookup errors, so one failure does not stop the others from being revoked.
- **Private fixture probes use the hardened dialer** (`controller.ToolReconciler.healthCheck`): Under the fixture allowance, a connection-mode or agentIdentity Tool skipped the DNS-based private-address rule, so healthCheck probes it through the hardened private-endpoint dialer that refuses infrastructure addresses, proxies, and redirects.
- **Tool health checks test reachability only** (`controller.ToolReconciler.healthCheck`): healthCheck treats any HTTP response, including 4xx and 5xx, as reachable and marks a Tool unavailable only when the connection itself fails.
- **MCP actor readiness status-code rules** (`controller.ToolReconciler.healthCheckMCPActorEndpoint`): An actor protected by spec.http.authSecretRef answers the unauthenticated readiness probe with 401 or 403 and is still reachable; because the router can also reject an unlisted client with 403, router identity problems surface on Tool calls instead. Redirects are never followed for actor traffic, so an actor answering its MCP path with one cannot serve through the proxy.
- **Actor readiness uses a fresh mTLS client** (`controller.ToolReconciler.waitForMCPActorEndpoint`): waitForMCPActorEndpoint builds a fresh router client so the rotated Pod certificate is reloaded, and releases its connections once readiness is decided.
- **Invalid MCP Tools lose actor egress** (`controller.ToolReconciler.failSubstrateMCPToolValidation`): failSubstrateMCPToolValidation first denies all egress to every actor the Tool holds, because an actor running before the Tool became invalid would otherwise keep egressing and using gateway-injected credentials under its last policy. It then stops the actors, since the gateway may apply the cached previous policy for a while; failures are retried, and Reconcile reinstalls the policy before an actor serves again.
- **Every held actor is stopped on validation failure** (`controller.ToolReconciler.failSubstrateMCPToolValidation`): Because a deny-all policy takes effect only after the gateway drops its cached rules and may not have been installed at all, failSubstrateMCPToolValidation stops every held actor, including a replacement the Tool holds while status still names the actor it replaces.
- **Actor egress revocation runs even with MCP Tools disabled** (`controller.ToolReconciler.revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress`): revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress installs deny-all egress on every actor whose Tool or pool lease this Tool holds, never touches actors leased by another owner, and never applies compiled Tool rules. It runs even with Substrate MCP Tools disabled so turning the feature off cannot leave an actor egressing with gateway-injected credentials; an actor Substrate no longer has counts as revoked.
- **Revocation proceeds despite lease lookup errors** (`controller.ToolReconciler.revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress`): revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress revokes and stops every actor proven held even when another lease cannot be read, and still returns the lookup error so the reconcile retries.
- **Every actor's egress revocation is attempted** (`controller.ToolReconciler.revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress`): revokeSubstrateMCPToolActorEgress attempts every held actor, so one unreachable actor does not leave the others with their previous egress rules.
- **Credential rules require a gateway provider** (`controller.substrateMCPEgressRules`): substrateMCPEgressRules compiles credential rules naming Secret keys in the Tool namespace with the gateway's configured credential provider class; an empty provider makes any credential rule fail validation instead of compiling without it.
- **Egress credentials are validated, never copied** (`controller.ToolReconciler.validateSubstrateMCPEgressCredentials`): validateSubstrateMCPEgressCredentials requires every referenced credential Secret key to exist and be non-empty so a misconfigured Tool reports a condition instead of running an actor whose requests the gateway denies. The value is never copied into the actor or its egress policy.
- **Failed egress apply suspends a running Actor** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): controller.ToolReconciler suspends a reattached, running Substrate MCP Actor when a later egress policy apply fails, because the policy it runs under is then unverified and may be broader than the Tool now allows.
- **Rejected Tool edits stop the running Actor** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): When controller.ToolReconciler rejects an edited Tool before reaching the egress policy step, it stops the running Actor rather than leaving it under the previous specification's allowlist, which can be broader than the edit asked for.
- **Pooled Actor leases live in the pool namespace** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): A pooled Substrate MCP Actor's ownership lease lives in the SubstrateActorPool's namespace, and controller.ToolReconciler must read it there on every path that stops the Actor.
- **Template-less invalid Tools deny egress and retry the stop** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): When an invalid Tool's Actor template is gone, controller.ToolReconciler cannot tell whether to suspend or delete, so it denies the Actor all egress and keeps retrying idempotently; once the deny-all is enforced it performs neither stop.
- **Template-less Actors get deny-all, and are deleted only while it propagates** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): Substrate lets an Actor outlive its template and cannot suspend it without one, so controller.ToolReconciler denies such an Actor all egress and keeps retrying the stop. A deny-all is enforced only after the Substrate egress gateway drops its cached copy of the previous rules, so until then it deletes a running Actor (delete needs no template, but discards data on its durable volumes) and retries until it is gone. A suspended or deleted Actor, or any Actor once the deny-all is enforced, is left alone with its data.
- **The lease defines Actor ownership** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): controller.ToolReconciler treats the ownership lease as what makes a Substrate Actor belong to a Tool; without a held lease it never stops the Actor or touches its egress.
- **Unsuspendable Actors are deleted by UID** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): Substrate cannot suspend an Actor whose template snapshots volumes only and mounts no durable directory (the attempt fails before the workload stops), so controller.ToolReconciler deletes such an Actor under its UID on every stop path.
- **A replaced template decides stop mode only for Actors booted from it** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): Because a Substrate template can be deleted and recreated under the same name with different suspend support, controller.ToolReconciler lets the template decide how an Actor is stopped only when the Actor booted from that template UID; otherwise it stops the Actor as one whose template is gone.
- **Invalid Tools revoke every held Actor** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): When a Tool becomes invalid, controller.ToolReconciler denies egress to and stops every Actor it holds, including a replacement not yet named in status, and continues past an Actor that cannot be reached.
- **Replaced egress policies wait out the gateway cache** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): The Substrate egress gateway caches an Actor's policy with no invalidation, so a replaced policy is stored before it is enforced; controller.ToolReconciler stops a running Actor and resumes it only after the propagation window has passed.
- **Recreated egress policies are treated as replaced** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): A policy deleted and recreated for the same running Actor restarts at version 1 while the gateway may still apply the deleted rules, so controller.ToolReconciler holds it back like a replaced policy and stops the Actor meanwhile.
- **Recreated unsuspendable Actors wait their own window** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): Deleting an unsuspendable Actor deletes its policy, and the gateway may still remember that policy under the same name, so controller.ToolReconciler makes the replacement Actor wait out its own propagation window before first boot.
- **New Actors wait one propagation window before first boot** (`controller.ToolReconciler`): Actor names derive from the Tool, so a recreated Tool gets a same-named Actor whose predecessor's policy the gateway may remember; nothing records that, so controller.ToolReconciler makes every newly created Actor wait one propagation window before its first boot.
- **Substrate MCP readiness never follows redirects** (`worker.NewSubstrateMCPRouter`): The Substrate MCP readiness probe built on worker.NewSubstrateMCPRouter never follows redirects, so a 3xx answer means the MCP path is not served and the Tool is not ready, even if the redirect target would answer 200.
- **Late Finalizing snapshots keep their transition time** (`controller.usageTaskSnapshot`): controller.usageTaskSnapshot keeps a delayed Finalizing snapshot's recorded outcome time so it never appears newer than an already-recorded terminal usage snapshot.
- **Deleting an unfinished Task cancels its usage lifecycle** (`controller.usageTaskSnapshot`): controller.usageTaskSnapshot records an unfinished Task that is being deleted as Cancelled at its deletion time, because otherwise its snapshot could pin its usage cohort forever after the Task disappears.
- **Only AHP usage retains the requester** (`controller.usageTaskSnapshot`): Usage is shown by requester only for AHP sessions and turns, so controller.usageTaskSnapshot keeps the requester only for AHP Tasks; an ordinary Task's identity is not retained past the Task.
- **Session token usage is never double counted** (`controller.TaskReconciler.sessionTokenUsage`): controller.TaskReconciler.sessionTokenUsage reports usage only for non-agent Tasks with spec.sessionRef: agent Tasks run as ACP Session turns whose finalization already adds prompt-attempt usage, and Tasks a chat creates carry no sessionRef, so chat Sessions count only their chat turns.
- **Usage requester is first-write and privacy is sticky** (`store.UsageStore.RegisterUsageTask`): store.UsageStore.RegisterUsageTask keeps the first requester recorded for a Task and never turns a Task once recorded as private back into an attributable one, so a later snapshot cannot rewrite who used tokens.
- **Policy-rejected webhook destinations are never retried** (`controller.webhookPolicyError`): A webhook destination rejected by policy (controller.webhookPolicyError) fails the notice without any network attempt and is never retried, since no retry can make it acceptable.
- **Same-namespace webhook Services are pinned to ClusterIPs** (`controller.webhookTarget`): controller.webhookTarget pins a same-namespace Service destination to its ClusterIPs with no DNS, and every other destination is resolved at dial time and must be public. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Webhook names are checked on every dial** (`controller.resolveWebhookTarget`): controller.resolveWebhookTarget defers public-name checks to dial time on every connection so a DNS failure or rebinding answer can never reach a non-public address. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Every webhook attempt sends byte-identical JSON** (`controller.WebhookPayload`): All attempts of one delivery send byte-identical controller.WebhookPayload JSON, so the body digest identifies the notice and a changed digest supersedes it. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Approval notices have a shorter retry bound** (`controller.WebhookNotifier`): controller.WebhookNotifier gives approval notices (ADR 0053) the same initial backoff and attempt lease as terminal notices but a shorter attempt and backoff bound. See [ADR 0053](0053-approval-webhook-events.md).
- **Webhook attempt lease exceeds the request timeout** (`controller.defaultWebhookAttemptLease`): controller.defaultWebhookAttemptLease must exceed the webhook request timeout so a restarted controller never duplicates an attempt that is still in flight. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Webhooks without a key source fail closed** (`controller.NewWebhookNotifier`): controller.NewWebhookNotifier connects only to public addresses or validated same-namespace Services, and without a configured key source deliveries fail closed and are never sent unsigned. See [ADR 0042](0042-signed-durable-task-webhooks.md).
- **Webhook attempt errors are safe for Task status** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.deliver`): controller.WebhookNotifier.deliver never returns the URL, signature, or transport internals in its error, so the error can be recorded in Task status.
- **Webhook transport errors map to fixed text** (`controller.sanitizeWebhookTransportError`): controller.sanitizeWebhookTransportError maps client errors to fixed descriptions because url.Error embeds the request URL, which may carry credentials.
- **Disabled webhook signing fails terminally** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery`): When webhook signing is disabled, controller.WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery fails the notice terminally instead of requeueing a finished Task forever; a configured key source whose keys are temporarily unavailable retries instead.
- **Persisted webhook lease never ends early** (`controller.WebhookNotifier.reconcileDelivery`): Task status times serialize at whole seconds, so the persisted webhook attempt lease must be rounded so it never ends before the in-process lease that bounds the send.
- **Core routes checkpoints, providers own export** (`controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler`): controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler establishes checkpoint routing from the exact source workspace only; the selected provider owns artifact export, observed status, and reference cleanup.
- **Published and legacy checkpoints keep their owner** (`controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler`): controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler leaves published exports and in-progress legacy checkpoint references with their prior owner, even when the source is gone and no new routing is established.
- **Operator-tainted sources are never routed for export** (`controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler`): controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler never routes a checkpoint whose source is operator-tainted, via its own marker or any Task linked to that incarnation, so no fork consumes its data. See [ADR 0044](0044-operator-workspace-access.md).
- **Only authoritative provider status proves no Data export** (`controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler`): controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler treats Data export as unsupported only on current, authoritative provider status; a provider that has not reported yet stays retryable.
- **Routing failures are Core metadata and permanent** (`controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler.failRouting`): controller.WorkspaceCheckpointRoutingReconciler.failRouting records a routing failure as Core-owned metadata, not a provider export or cleanup result, and it is final because a lost immutable source UID cannot come back.
- **Workspace cleanup is proven by pool absence** (`controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted`): controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted proves cleanup by the absence of reverse-linked RuntimePools, never by the mutable annotation, so a lost or stale annotation cannot finalize a workspace while provider resources live.
- **Pool absence is read uncached** (`controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted`): controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted proves pool absence through the uncached API reader, because a pool created just before workspace deletion can be missing from the informer cache.
- **Only immutable links prove pool ownership** (`controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted`): controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted treats the mutable name link and visible metadata as non-ownership; only the controller-stamped incarnation pin and immutable workspaceRef prove a pool serves the workspace, and a foreign pool is never deleted while the finalizer stays held fail-closed. controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted refuses to delete a pool linked only by visible metadata and holds the workspace finalizer fail-closed.
- **Linked pool deletes use UID and resourceVersion preconditions** (`controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted`): controller.workspaceCoreCleanup.ensureLinkedRuntimePoolDeleted deletes with UID and resourceVersion preconditions so a concurrent pool update after the read becomes a retried conflict instead of deleting a pool whose linkage just changed.
- **Direct-provider class profile hashes are frozen** (`controller.TestLegacyWorkspaceClassProfileHashIsStable`): Direct-provider class profile hashes are frozen into class bindings and execution snapshots, so their canonical inputs must not change or every existing binding looks drifted after an upgrade.
- **Published Ready conditions never bypass validation** (`controller.TestWorkspaceProviderNameCompatibilityAtGenericAdmission`): Generic workspace provider admission revalidates live on every reconcile; an already-published Ready condition never bypasses that validation.
- **Core RBAC excludes provider-native resources** (`controller (workspace_rbac.go)`): The generic workspace coordinator's RBAC covers generic API objects only; provider-native resources stay adapter-owned and intentionally absent from Orka core RBAC, and separately installed providers grant Core access to their own parameter kinds.
- **Generic providers cannot claim the legacy ACP route** (`controller.TestGenericWorkspaceProviderRejectsReservedLegacyControllerRoute`): A generic workspace provider without the lifecycle contract must not claim the reserved legacy ACP controller identity, because Core treats workspaces and checkpoints carrying it as legacy-owned and skips their generic routing.
- **Provider status is provider-written only** (`controller.WorkspaceRuntimePoolReconciler`): controller.WorkspaceRuntimePoolReconciler owns only the core-side link and demand; provider status and attachment acknowledgement are written exclusively by the provider.
- **Retention owns workspace lifetime expiry** (`controller.WorkspaceRuntimePoolReconciler`): The retention controller owns lifetime expiry and requests API deletion with the exact workspace UID; controller.WorkspaceRuntimePoolReconciler must not set desiredState first, which would stop retention before it establishes the deletion/finalizer path.
- **Schema pruning never authorizes recreating legacy allocations** (`controller.ValidateExternalWorkspaceUpgrade`): controller.ValidateExternalWorkspaceUpgrade runs before dispatch controllers start, and pool schema pruning is never treated as authority to recreate a legacy allocation.
- **Sealed bootstrap never follows redirects** (`controller.seedSealedWorkspaceCredentials`): controller.seedSealedWorkspaceCredentials never follows redirects: the provider route is the trusted transport boundary, and the sealed body also binds the destination process across replacement between challenge and credential write.
- **Closed bootstrap still needs the v2 probe** (`controller.seedSealedWorkspaceCredentials`): When the fully started supervisor has closed bootstrap, controller.seedSealedWorkspaceCredentials reports success, but admission still requires the authenticated v2 identity and capacity probe.
- **Class profile hash baseline provenance** (`controller.TestResolvedClassProfileHashMatchesSharedModuleBaseline`): The workspace-class-profile-hashes.json fixture was captured before extraction at Orka commit 1cd16c88b43410e0ea46463f8a39b473d8e5250e; its capture source and matching SDK regression live in orka-workspace/testdata/baseline/orka-1cd16c88b.
- **Archived recovery test waits for the second Task list** (`controller.runArchivedRecoveryRestart`): runArchivedRecoveryRestart stops the dispatcher when the second unfiltered Task list starts, because ACPDispatcher.Start recovers stale attempts before its first dispatch scan and both begin with an unfiltered Task list. A fixed run deadline is rejected because it would also bound recovery itself and flake on a slow API server.
- **Dispatcher start waits out an installation storage hold** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.Start`): When ACPDispatcher.Start runs while the installation is held for storage recovery, it waits for the release instead of returning an error. Failing the manager would only restart the controller into the same hold; while held it dispatches nothing.
- **Only final delivery-recovery verdicts are cached** (`controller.acpDeliveryRecoveryVerdictSettled`): acpDeliveryRecoveryVerdictSettled caches a nothing-to-recover verdict only when every durable input it read is final: a terminal execution state, a terminal delivery other than NotRequested (which may still move to Validating), or for Failed/Cancelled/OutcomeUnknown attempts complete runtime cleanup, plus a settled Task phase. Deleting Tasks are never cached because terminal recovery owns their pending runtime cleanup.
- **Settled delivery-recovery cache is bounded** (`controller.acpSettledDeliveryRecoveryCacheSize`): acpSettledDeliveryRecoveryCacheSize bounds the settled verdict cache. An evicted or never-cached Task is simply read from the store again, so the bound costs only extra reads, never correctness.
- **Settled delivery-recovery cache is guarded by the dispatcher mutex** (`controller.acpSettledDeliveryRecoveryCache`): acpSettledDeliveryRecoveryCache is an LRU with no lock of its own; every access must hold ACPDispatcher.mu.
- **Settled verdicts are keyed by Task resourceVersion and epoch** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.deliveryRecoverySettled`): ACPDispatcher.deliveryRecoverySettled skips a Task only for the exact resourceVersion and controller epoch at which recovery found nothing to do. Any Task write, including a projection or cleanup receipt, changes the resourceVersion and forces the next scan to read the store again.
- **Dispatch wakeups are rate-limited to one scan per interval** (`controller.acpDispatchWakeMinInterval`): acpDispatchWakeMinInterval bounds how often wakeups can start a dispatch scan, because each scan lists every Task and reads durable state. A burst of events coalesces into at most one extra scan per interval; the periodic ticker is unaffected.
- **Dispatch wakeups only move a scan earlier** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.Wake`): ACPDispatcher.Wake never blocks and coalesces pending wakeups. A wakeup only moves the next scan earlier; reservation still re-reads the Task, the epoch fence, and RuntimePool admission state, so a wakeup never authorizes dispatch by itself.
- **Admission openings skip the wake interval** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.wakeForAdmission`): ACPDispatcher.wakeForAdmission starts the next scan as soon as the current one ends, bypassing acpDispatchWakeMinInterval. This is safe because admission openings happen once per runtime instance, not per prompt, so they cannot become a burst; scan and reservation checks are unchanged.
- **Any dispatch scan consumes a pending admission wakeup** (`controller.waitForNextDispatchScan`): waitForNextDispatchScan consumes the pending admission flag with whatever starts the scan (ticker, ordinary wakeup, or admission), and consumes it before testing the interval. Otherwise a stale admission flag could let a later ordinary wakeup skip acpDispatchWakeMinInterval.
- **Dispatch wake handlers are an optimization over the periodic scan** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.registerDispatchWakeHandlers`): ACPDispatcher.registerDispatchWakeHandlers wakes the dispatcher when a Task becomes queued or a RuntimePool starts serving, accepting, or frees capacity. Without informers or if registration fails, the dispatcher still works from its periodic scan, so wake handlers must never be required for correctness.
- **RuntimePool dispatch wake mirrors reservation admission** (`controller.acpRuntimePoolOpenedForDispatch`): acpRuntimePoolOpenedForDispatch must mirror runtimePoolReservationAdmission: prompt slots need a Serving pool with an active instance, and only a new resident session also needs Accepting. It therefore wakes when the pool starts serving, starts accepting, changes active instance, or frees prompt or session capacity while serving; diverging from the admission rule would leave reservable Tasks waiting for the next tick.
- **Reopening to sessions is always an ordinary dispatch wake** (`controller.acpRuntimePoolOpenedForDispatch`): acpRuntimePoolOpenedForDispatch always wakes dispatch when a pool reopens to sessions, even when that is only capacity turnover on a full pool. Such turnover is not an admission opening, so it stays behind acpDispatchWakeMinInterval.
- **Capacity turnover on a full pool is not an admission opening** (`controller.acpRuntimePoolAdmissionOpened`): A full pool is Serving and Closed only because it is at capacity. acpRuntimePoolAdmissionOpened excludes a Closed-at-capacity to Accepting transition, so ordinary capacity turnover stays behind the minimum scan interval instead of taking the admission fast path.
- **Dispatcher start waits out an installation storage hold** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.Start`): When ACPDispatcher.Start runs while the installation is held for storage recovery, it retries recoverAtStart until the release instead of returning an error. Releasing the hold needs this process running, and failing the manager would only restart the controller into the same hold.
- **Storage holds log once per hold** (`controller.logACPScanError`): logACPScanError logs a scan stopped by a storage recovery hold once per hold ID, at Info, rather than as a failed scan on every tick, because dispatch resumes on its own once the hold is released.
- **Startup recovery waits for the fence and isolates blocked Tasks** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverAtStart`): ACPDispatcher.recoverAtStart waits for the controller fence and recovers stale attempts before the first dispatch. An ACP Task whose recovery is blocked is logged and retried later rather than failing startup, so independent work proceeds.
- **Pool retirement runs about once per dispatch interval** (`controller.acpDispatchPoolRetirementDue`): Wakeups can start a scan every acpDispatchWakeMinInterval, but idle/stopped RuntimePool retirement waits minutes-long idle TTLs and reads each linked workspace uncached. acpDispatchPoolRetirementDue runs it at most about once per dispatch interval so it never delays a wakeup's reservation; the quarter-interval slack keeps the periodic tick retiring even when a wakeup scan ran just before it. Skipping retirement on a scan only keeps capacity longer.
- **Session lineage reuses the verified namespace UID** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.loadVerifiedACPDispatchExecution`): loadVerifiedACPDispatchExecution reads the namespace uncached at the start of each turn and requires its UID to equal the immutable binding's, so Session lineage takes the namespace UID from the binding. That verification must stay in place for the binding UID to be a trustworthy lineage identity; a second read would only repeat it.
- **Pre-dispatch milestones are reported after the last requeue** (`controller.acpPreDispatch.report`): acpPreDispatch.report runs only after every early requeue of the reservation, just before the Session lease and runtime session, so each attempt's pre-dispatch milestones are reported exactly once. Moving it earlier would report attempts that later requeue.
- **A plan update commits with its event** (`controller.acpJournal.AppendPlanUpdateIfNew`): AppendPlanUpdateIfNew commits the execution event and its plan update in one transaction, so neither is visible without the other; a failure of either is an execution update persistence failure.
- **Reserved write checks the binding on its own patch base** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchReservedExecutionForPool`): patchReservedExecutionForPool re-reads the Task uncached and writes Reserved only when that same read, which the optimistic patch is based on, still shows the attempt bound to the pool. Checking a different read would let a rebinding between check and patch go unnoticed.
- **Unpersistable execution updates settle fail-closed** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.handlePromptUpdatePersistenceFailure`): handlePromptUpdatePersistenceFailure settles a prompt whose execution update could not be persisted. Because the journal itself failed, it attempts no further lifecycle record; the prompt is Failed only when its settlement is proven and otherwise OutcomeUnknown.
- **External runtime test proxies run full-duplex** (`controller.newExternalRuntimeTestProxyServer`): newExternalRuntimeTestProxyServer enables full-duplex mode because the fixture proxies forward requests with their original bodies. In HTTP/1's default mode, flushing a forwarded response such as a prompt stream's acceptance closes the request body, the proxy transport's trailing EOF check fails, and the upstream connection drops mid-stream.
- **Late-EOF proxy test pads the body to the write buffer** (`controller.TestExternalRuntimeTestProxyServerKeepsStreamWhenBodyEOFCheckIsLate`): TestExternalRuntimeTestProxyServerKeepsStreamWhenBodyEOFCheckIsLate pads the JSON body to an exact multiple of the 4 KiB transport write buffer so the complete body reaches the upstream before the trailing EOF check runs. Changing the payload size can stop the test from reproducing the late-EOF race.
- **Idle outbox projectors claim on a slow cadence** (`controller.defaultACPOutboxIdleClaimInterval`): Each outbox claim takes the controller-epoch mutation interlock that every fenced control-store write shares, so an idle ACPOutboxProjector claims only every defaultACPOutboxIdleClaimInterval, when a projection is enqueued, or when a retry it scheduled falls due, never on every tick.
- **Outbox wakeups are process-local** (`controller.acpOutboxWakeups`): acpOutboxWakeups fans an enqueue out only to projectors in this process. That is sufficient because every terminal projection is enqueued through enqueueDurableTaskTerminalProjectionForUID under the controller-epoch fence, so only this process's leader enqueues.
- **Outbox retry deadlines persist across passes** (`controller.acpOutboxSchedule`): acpOutboxSchedule remembers every scheduled retry deadline across projector passes, so a pass that claims nothing (for example one an enqueue woke early) or one where only the earliest retry succeeds never forgets a later deadline and falls back to the idle interval.
- **Outbox schedule settles deadlines only on clean passes** (`controller.acpOutboxSchedule.observe`): acpOutboxSchedule.observe settles a claimed projection's earlier deadline, and drops a due deadline that a pass claiming fewer than its limit did not return, since that projection is no longer pending. A failed pass may still have committed retries before failing: those are added, and nothing is settled or pruned.
- **Outbox projector claims immediately at start** (`controller.ACPOutboxProjector.Start`): ACPOutboxProjector.Start claims at once rather than waiting for an enqueue or the idle interval, because pending projections may have been enqueued before this process started.
- **Outbox passes record a retry only after it commits** (`controller.ACPOutboxProjector`): An ACPOutboxProjector pass records a retry deadline in pass.retries only once the retry is committed, so a pass that fails later still reports exactly the retries it made and acpOutboxSchedule keeps them.
- **Pre-dispatch creation step carries one-second rounding** (`controller.acpPreDispatchInformerAdded`): The step from Task creation to acpPreDispatchInformerAdded, and the total from creation, include up to one second of rounding because Kubernetes creation timestamps have whole-second precision; the later milestone steps use process clock times and do not.
- **Pre-dispatch timing records are bounded** (`controller.acpPreDispatchMaxRecords`): acpPreDispatchTimings tracks at most acpPreDispatchMaxRecords Tasks, and records for Tasks that never dispatch are pruned after acpPreDispatchRetention, so timing instrumentation cannot grow without bound.
- **Pre-dispatch timings store no Task content** (`controller.acpPreDispatchTimings`): acpPreDispatchTimings holds, per Task UID, only the first time each milestone was reached in this process, never Task content, so the in-memory timing store and its logs cannot leak prompts or specs.
- **Early informer Adds wait for the record** (`controller.acpPreDispatchTimings.added`): acpPreDispatchTimings.added holds informer Add times that arrive before the Task's first reconcile creates its record; the record takes the time when created.
- **One pre-dispatch timing store per process** (`controller.acpPreDispatch`): acpPreDispatch is a single process-wide instance because the Task controller and the ACP dispatcher, which run in the same process, both record milestones into the same per-Task record.
- **Started milestone is the latest attempt's** (`controller.acpPreDispatchTimings.markStarted`): acpPreDispatchTimings.markStarted replaces an earlier attempt's start, so an attempt that requeues before its turn begins leaves the record for the attempt that does run.
- **Queued milestone is marked before the status write** (`controller.acpPreDispatchTimings.markQueueWriteIssued`): markQueueWriteIssued records the queued milestone immediately before the Queued status write is issued, because the write is watch-visible before it returns and the dispatcher can scan and even start the Task first. Marking beforehand keeps queued ahead of every dispatcher milestone and never recreates a record a start already reported; each call replaces the earlier time, so a cleanly failed write is superseded by the retried pass, and an abandoned record is pruned by retention.
- **Only pre-execution passes create timing records** (`controller.acpPreDispatchCreatesRecord`): acpPreDispatchCreatesRecord lets only the Observed and StatusInitialized milestones start a Task's record. A mark from a later stage can run after the Task's start already reported and removed its record, and must never recreate one that nothing would report.
- **Informer Add timing tolerates either order with reconcile** (`controller.acpPreDispatchTimings.informerAdded`): The controller's own handler enqueues the Task independently, so the first reconcile can run before or after informerAdded. An Add that arrives first is held until the first reconcile creates the record; one arriving after the reconcile was observed or after the record finished would mismeasure and is dropped. informerAdded never creates a record itself.
- **Default commit identity is omitted on the wire** (`controller.taskPublicationWireCommitIdentity`): taskPublicationWireCommitIdentity sends empty author fields when the Task uses the publisher's built-in default identity, so older publishers with strict JSON decoding still accept ordinary unsigned publications during mixed-version rollouts.
- **Persisted prepare requests predating commit identity still replay** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.loadPersistedPrepareRequest`): loadPersistedPrepareRequest must keep replaying unsigned default-identity preparations that were recorded before CommitAuthorName and CommitAuthorEmail existed, treating their missing fields as the default identity.
- **Storage-held recovery does not log every pass** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.scheduleStaleAttemptRecoveries`): scheduleStaleAttemptRecoveries does not log a Task blocked by a taskStorageRecordHoldError: the Task stays blocked until its hold is released and the hold itself logs once when it changes, so logging each pass would only repeat it.
- **Storage-held Settling deleting Tasks are recoverable** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.deletingTaskRecoverable`): Besides the two cases in ADR 0064, deletingTaskRecoverable re-fences a deleting Task whose attempt recovery held for a storage record while Settling, after recovery settled it. Only recovery can release that hold, from the restored receipt or the operator's acknowledgement, and the finalizer waits on the terminal Task and runtime cleanup it leaves; such a Task recovers exactly like a non-deleting one, never replaying the prompt, writing with the current fence, and keeping the frozen runtime identity so cleanup keeps its authority.
- **Settled deleting Tasks are handed to the current epoch** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.handOffSettledDeletingTask`): handOffSettledDeletingTask moves a deleting Task whose attempt recovery just settled to the current epoch, because stale recovery passes over a deleting Task once its attempt is terminal. The current epoch's terminal recovery then owns its pending runtime cleanup, which the terminal projection's frozen runtime identity still authorizes.
- **Recovered workspace validation settles before the terminal event** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleRecoveredWorkspaceValidation`): recoverStaleTask settles a recovered workspace validation before the journal records a terminal event, so a retry that finds the event takes the journaled path. Because an earlier pass may have journaled the terminal event without finishing that settlement, recoverJournaledPromptTerminal also settles the validation first.
- **A journaled completion does not replace the settling receipt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): Recovery of a journaled prompt terminal still calls verifySettlingResultReceipt: a completion in the journal does not stand in for the result receipt the settling transition stored.
- **Settling attempts must still hold their result receipt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): The settling transition (P5) stores the result receipt before moving an attempt to Settling, so verifySettlingResultReceipt requires the store to still hold it. A matching receipt repairs the Task result; a missing receipt with no result matching the recorded digest, or a receipt that differs from it, holds the Task for storage recovery.
- **Validating attempts still require their settling receipt** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): The live path starts workspace validation while the attempt is still Settling, recording a later operation on it. Only an attempt the settling transition moved to Settling gets that far, so verifySettlingResultReceipt still requires its receipt; since the attempt no longer holds the settling digest, the receipt's own operation ID names the version it was stored at.
- **Unverifiable receipts only fill a missing result** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): Once workspace validation replaced the attempt's settling operation, the attempt no longer carries the digest to cross-check the receipt. verifySettlingResultReceipt then keeps an existing Task result intact and repairs only a missing one, rather than overwriting it with an unverified receipt.
- **A missing receipt after validation holds the Task** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): When the receipt is missing and the attempt already records a later operation, verifySettlingResultReceipt holds the Task with StorageRecoveryMissingRecordReason: the Task result the receipt protected cannot stand in for it, because nothing can check it any more.
- **No synthesized completion after workspace validation starts** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recoverSettlingResultTerminal`): recoverSettlingResultTerminal does not synthesize the prompt's completion from the receipt once the attempt moved on to workspace validation, because the journal no longer shows that validation's outcome.
- **Recovery keeps a Session turn's finalized settlement** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.validateExistingSessionTaskProjection`): For a session-bound Succeeded attempt, the terminal record is the projection its finalized SessionTurn carries, which the finalizing settlement also projects onto the Task. validateExistingSessionTaskProjection keeps that settlement instead of rewriting the Task's message and transition times; Failed or Cancelled attempts still take their reason from the attempt, and a projection whose states differ from the attempt's is rebuilt.
- **Session settlement is kept only once the Task shows it** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.validateExistingSessionTaskProjection`): validateExistingSessionTaskProjection keeps the Session settlement only when the live Task already shows it; a Task the projection has not reached yet is settled from the attempt record rather than left waiting on the outbox.
- **Releasing a record hold is admitted work** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.patchRecoveredTerminalEpoch`): patchRecoveredTerminalEpoch admits a hold-releasing condition write through Epochs.AdmitWork like every other record-hold condition write, so an installation hold raised during the pass keeps the record hold until that installation hold clears.
- **Unfinishable recovered validations fail closed** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleRecoveredWorkspaceValidation`): When recovery cannot finish a workspace validation because the runtime workspace it validated is gone, settleRecoveredWorkspaceValidation ends it as DeliveryConflict before recording the prompt's outcome as unknown. Session turn finalization and Task reclamation both need a terminal delivery; the Task records that delivery so its terminal projection carries it, and a later pass only completes the Task's record.
- **Recovered unknown outcomes are recorded before cleanup** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.recordRecoveredValidationOutcome`): Runtime cleanup abandons a validation the runtime still holds only for a Task that records the unknown outcome. recordRecoveredValidationOutcome therefore writes that record for a Session-less Task whose attempt recovery settled as OutcomeUnknown when the settling pass stopped before the Task's terminal record (for example, the projection could not be enqueued); otherwise later passes would stop at cleanup forever. It records either the recovered validation conflict or no delivery, and writes in the settling pass's order (projection, then Task execution state) so the Task's runtime identity is kept.
- **Missing or conflicting store records hold the Task** (`controller.taskStorageRecordHold`): A taskStorageRecordHold is a store record that a Task's Kubernetes records require but that is missing or differs from what they recorded. Recovery then holds the Task instead of acting on it: it never runs the prompt again, never reports success, and deletes nothing until the record is found again or an operator acknowledges the hold.
- **Record hold IDs are derived from the evidence** (`controller.taskStorageRecordHold.id`): taskStorageRecordHold.id hashes the reason, record, key, and expected digest, so the ID stays the same every time recovery finds the same record missing and an operator's acknowledgement keeps applying to it. A random or per-pass ID would invalidate acknowledgements.
- **Record holds are fenced by epoch and attempt** (`controller.taskStorageRecordObservation`): taskStorageRecordObservation captures the controller epoch fence and the PromptAttempt recovery read. The Task's storage condition is written only while both still stand, so neither a replaced controller nor a pass that read the attempt before it moved on can leave a hold on a Task that was settled without it.
- **Record hold errors read as work that cannot proceed yet** (`controller.taskStorageRecordHoldError`): taskStorageRecordHoldError matches store.ErrStorageRecoveryRequired and store.ErrNotReady, so recovery treats a held Task as work that cannot proceed yet rather than as a failure to classify.
- **Acknowledged record holds release into safe recovery** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.settleTaskStorageRecordHold`): settleTaskStorageRecordHold releases a hold when the Task's acknowledgement annotation names its ID, ignoring surrounding whitespace as the ControllerEpoch acknowledgement does; recovery then continues as it did before holds existed, which never reruns the prompt or reports success. Otherwise it records the StorageRecoveryRequired condition and returns a taskStorageRecordHoldError.
- **Late record-hold writes on settled attempts are released** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.releaseSettledTaskStorageHold`): Recovery holds a Task only while its attempt is Settling, and an acknowledged hold is already False, so a True hold on an attempt settled from its store record (success, or unknown outcome with a receipt matching the attempt's own settling operation) is a late condition write from a replaced controller. releaseSettledTaskStorageHold releases it; a hold whose receipt is gone is kept. The caller writes with the Task's resourceVersion through Epochs.AdmitWork, so a late write landing earlier is cleared and one landing later fails its resourceVersion precondition.
- **Record hold conditions target the exact Task incarnation** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.setTaskStorageRecordCondition`): setTaskStorageRecordCondition writes the storage condition only to the exact Task incarnation recovery observed, and each try runs under the controller epoch after rechecking that the observation still stands, so a recreated Task with the same name never inherits a hold.
- **Record hold writes need epoch authority and admission** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.withTaskStorageRecordAuthority`): withTaskStorageRecordAuthority runs a record-hold write only while the fence is the current controller epoch and storage is not held. With an epoch mutation interlock the store keeps a successor from taking over until the write returns; otherwise the authoritative epoch record is read first. The write also goes through Epochs.AdmitWork, like Job creation and success status writes, so an installation hold raised after the pass started, or an unverified dataset, blocks it.
- **A receipt bound to another Task conflicts** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.verifySettlingResultReceipt`): The settling transition digest does not cover the Task, so verifySettlingResultReceipt must also check the receipt's Task binding: a receipt bound to another Task is a conflicting record even when its data, operation, and digest match, and recovery holds the Task instead of taking that result as its own.
- **Image provenance proof survives replica-only generation changes** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recordACPRuntimePoolImageProvenance`): recordACPRuntimePoolImageProvenance keeps an existing provenance condition from an earlier generation, because suspend and resume change only replicas, not the immutable image and profile it proves. Re-stamping would write the pool status just as the provider's Ready report wakes the pool to admit the resumed runtime, making the pool's optimistic Serving write lose a whole admission pass.
- **Turn timing logs carry only step names and durations** (`controller.acpTurnTimings`): acpTurnTimings records only step names and durations for one ACP Task execution, from reserved dispatch through runtime retirement, and logs them once when the execution returns; it must never carry prompt, result, or credential content.
- **Prompt acceptance is timed before it is persisted** (`controller.acpTurnTimings.markPromptAccepted`): markPromptAccepted records the promptAccepted step the first time the runtime is seen to accept the prompt, before any fallible persistence of that acceptance, so a turn whose persistence later fails still logs at info verbosity. Later calls record nothing.
- **Unprompted executions log turn timings at debug** (`controller.acpTurnTimings.log`): acpTurnTimings.log logs executions that never reached prompt acceptance, such as capacity requeues and admission waits, at debug verbosity so a waiting queue does not flood the log. Its returnedError field reports whether the execution returned an error, not the turn outcome: a turn whose failure was persisted returns nil.
- **Abandoned validation recovery never touches published work** (`controller.ACPDispatcher.finishAbandonedWorkspaceValidation`): finishAbandonedWorkspaceValidation abandons only local, unexported work. A published or in-flight publication cannot reach it, because its durable delivery state is neither NotRequested nor the conflict recovery records for an unfinished validation, and its runtime is no longer Validating.
- **Recovered validation conflicts still need runtime abandonment** (`controller.abandonedValidationDelivery`): abandonedValidationDelivery treats an unknown or failed attempt as leaving a runtime validation to abandon both when no delivery was recorded and when settleRecoveredWorkspaceValidation recorded its validation conflict for an unknown outcome, because recovery could not finish that validation and the runtime may still hold it.
- **Default window reads only the exact frozen class** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acpWorkspaceDefaultSuspendAfterDetachAnnotations`): acpWorkspaceDefaultSuspendAfterDetachAnnotations reads the exact frozen ExecutionWorkspaceClass for its override with an uncached read: a class that is gone or whose UID changed gets no default window, while any other read error fails and retries the workspace creation.
- **Empty external SuspendMode forbids Suspend** (`controller.externalACPProfileRetention`): externalACPProfileRetention.SuspendMode is the permitted suspension scope read from an external profile; an empty value forbids Suspend rather than defaulting to any scope.
- **Speculative claims are released before any settlement shortcut** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace releases a staged attachment or pending speculative resume the Task never had installed before anything else, including the settlement-receipt shortcut, so an interrupted release is always retried (ADR 0057).
- **Revocation deadline is restamped except after suspension** (`controller.TaskReconciler.settleACPClassWorkspace`): settleACPClassWorkspace ensures the revocation deadline stamp exists for exactly the current epoch before enforcing it, so a restart cannot lose it, but skips this once retention has already moved the workspace to Suspended (for example warm-window expiry after epoch release): restamping then would leave a stale revocation stamp that blocks a continuation even after cold resume.
- **Losing a resume race releases the staged attachment** (`controller.TaskReconciler.requestACPWorkspaceSpeculativeResume`): When another writer moves the workspace out of the settled suspended state, requestACPWorkspaceSpeculativeResume stops and releases its staged bearer Secret and Lease so the winning Task can attach.
- **Cold-resume flip keeps only the requester's own speculative start** (`controller.TaskReconciler.flipACPWorkspaceForColdResume`): flipACPWorkspaceForColdResume drops a speculative start left by an earlier cycle but keeps one owned by the same requester, open or expired, so the queue path neither loses nor renews that Task's original speculative budget before attachment or settlement clears it.
- **Staged attachment objects are deletable only while uninstalled** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects`): releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects deletes the bearer Secret and Lease of an attachment no workspace spec names, because the Lease is held by the Task and fenced to the workspace's next epoch, so no installed attachment can depend on it; it is idempotent.
- **Renew the staged Lease before deleting its Secret** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects`): releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects renews its Lease with an optimistic patch before deleting the Secret, so a concurrent successor cannot take over an expired Lease and install that next-epoch Secret mid-delete; if the Lease changed hands the patch conflicts and the successor's Secret is left alone.
- **Reconfirm the workspace epoch before deleting a staged Secret** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects`): releaseACPWorkspaceStagedAttachmentObjects re-reads the workspace and confirms it has not moved past the staged epoch before deleting the bearer Secret.
- **Malformed frozen warm window disables the window** (`controller.acpWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetachFromAnnotation`): acpWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetachFromAnnotation treats a malformed controller-written window as no window, so the frozen Suspend applies at detach as it would without a warm-detach window.
- **Corrupt warm-window metadata ends the window without deleting** (`controller.ACPWorkspaceRetentionReconciler.reconcileWarmDetachWindow`): reconcileWarmDetachWindow treats invalid controller-authenticated workspace content as corruption, not evidence that Delete is safe: it only ends the window so the runtime stops with data-preserving retirement, and idle retention stays fail-closed.
- **Absent warm window stays admissible for backfill** (`controller.validateACPWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetach`): validateACPWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetach admits an absent window so workspaces created before the annotation can be backfilled, and admits a default-sourced window (ADR 0057) only for a binding whose profile sets none, whatever default the current controller uses.
- **Warm window backfill never rewrites a present value** (`controller.TaskReconciler.backfillACPWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetach`): backfillACPWorkspaceSuspendAfterDetach migrates a workspace only when the protected window annotation is absent; a present mismatch is rejected by verifyACPClassWorkspace and never rewritten.
- **Agent LastUsed only moves forward** (`controller.AgentReconciler.updateStatus`): AgentReconciler.updateStatus only advances Agent.status.LastUsed, because a Task completion may have recorded a newer use since this pass read the Agent and moving LastUsed backward would expire the Agent's TTL early.
- **Snapshot omits suspendAfterDetach when unset for downgrade safety** (`controller.agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceClass`): agentExecutionSnapshotWorkspaceClass.SuspendAfterDetach is omitted when unset because controller releases from before ADR 0055 decode snapshots with DisallowUnknownFields and reject a nonempty suspendAfterDetach field; downgrading below ADR 0055 requires no Task snapshot to carry one.
- **Binding write refreshes the Task from the uncached copy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.persistAgentExecutionBinding`): persistAgentExecutionBinding is an uncached compare-if-absent write-once CAS (see ADR 0064); when it returns the persisted binding it refreshes the Task in place from the uncached object, so later optimistic-lock patches start from the resourceVersion the binding write produced instead of a stale cached copy that would always conflict once.
- **Pending binding passes yield to a Task that moved on** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureAgentExecutionBinding`): ensureAgentExecutionBinding requeues instead of acting when a fresh uncached read shows the Task past the stage the pending binding pass decided from (another phase, deletion, or a recorded execution); lifecycle handling re-enters once the cache catches up.
- **Binding pass adopts the uncached bound Task** (`controller.TaskReconciler.ensureAgentExecutionBinding`): ensureAgentExecutionBinding copies the uncached bound Task into the caller's object because the caller queues that same Task next and must see the binding and current resourceVersion, not the cache's pre-binding copy.
- **Controller epoch manager opens storage fences per dataset and holds on lost writes** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager`): ControllerEpochManager acquires one durable epoch after leader election and every ACP control-store mutation uses CurrentFence. It advances each storage dataset to the Kubernetes-granted epoch, checks it against what Kubernetes recorded as acknowledged, and opens each contract's fence independently; while running it advances write watermarks and rechecks restarted or switched databases, and a dataset that lost acknowledged writes holds the whole installation for storage recovery.
- **Holds and fence closes publish first, then drain admitted work** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.admission`): ControllerEpochManager.admission is read-held while an admitted side effect runs; setting a hold or closing a fence publishes the change first and then takes the lock for writing, so work admitted before the change finishes and work admitted after it sees the change.
- **Watermark advances are fenced writes** (`controller.EpochDataset`): EpochDataset.AdvanceWriteWatermark is a fenced write, so the dataset the storage client hands the ControllerEpochManager must attach its write envelope.
- **Reopened contract fences take the new dataset** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.openContractFence`): openContractFence updates a fence that was opened earlier, because after an acknowledged storage switch the contract is served by another dataset.
- **Terminal storage errors at takeover** (`controller.definiteEpochStorageError`): definiteEpochStorageError stops takeover retries only for a dataset bound to another installation or holding a newer or conflicting epoch (ErrFenced, which also matches ErrConflict), a binding naming another dataset, an invalid request, an incompatible schema, a dataset holding a lower epoch than it acknowledged, or a missing installation record; everything else is retried.
- **Storage holds refuse write fences at once** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.WriteFence`): ControllerEpochManager.WriteFence waits for the contract's dataset to advance to this epoch, but while the installation is held for storage recovery it returns a store.StorageHoldError immediately, whether or not the fence had opened.
- **No unguarded side effect runs before storage checks pass** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.StorageHold`): ControllerEpochManager.StorageHold guards side effects no storage write protects, such as creating a Job: it reports a hold, and with storage datasets also fails closed (matching ErrNotReady) while any contract fence is closed, before takeover checks pass, after a release, or while a restarted or switched database is rechecked, so a restarted controller starts no work before it knows its storage holds what it acknowledged.
- **AdmitWork runs unguarded side effects only while no hold exists** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.AdmitWork`): ControllerEpochManager.AdmitWork runs create only while StorageHold reports nothing; a hold or fence close takes effect for everything else at once and then waits for an admitted create to return, so create either started before the change or never runs.
- **Storage holds refuse CurrentFence at once** (`controller.ControllerEpochManager.CurrentFence`): ControllerEpochManager.CurrentFence waits until the epoch is acquired and the state dataset's fence is open, waits again whenever that fence closes, and returns a store.StorageHoldError immediately while the installation is held for storage recovery.
- **Storage takeover checks acknowledgements and persists holds** (`controller.storageTakeover`): storageTakeover runs the storage side of one controller epoch: it waits for operator acknowledgement of any recorded storage recovery hold, then for each contract compares its dataset with what Kubernetes recorded it acknowledged, raising a hold for a switched dataset or one holding a lower epoch or watermark, otherwise advancing it and opening its fence; a watermark loop rechecks restarted or switched datasets. A raised hold closes every fence at once and is recorded in Kubernetes so a restart keeps it until an operator acknowledges it.
- **Workers of an ended takeover attempt change nothing** (`controller.storageTakeover`): storageTakeover numbers its attempts; a contract worker of an attempt no longer in progress, which may still be finishing a provider call, tracks no dataset and opens no fence.
- **An unacknowledged hold is recorded again** (`controller.storageTakeover.run`): When a storage recovery hold leaves the record without operator acknowledgement, for example through a status edit, storageTakeover.run records it again with every fence closed and held datasets still fenced, because the takeover step cannot redetect a switch of an async-replicated dataset that kept its epoch and watermark.
- **Held datasets are fenced from the recorded hold** (`controller.storageTakeover.run`): storageTakeover.run fences the datasets of a hold it recorded even when the hold left the record before this manager read it back.
- **A released hold is never recorded again** (`controller.storageTakeover.run`): storageTakeover.run forgets a hold once its release succeeds, because the release succeeded only because the annotation named that hold, so a later record without the annotation must not re-record it.
- **Fences reopen only after the takeover step passes** (`controller.storageTakeover.run`): After any acknowledged hold, including one that left the record without this manager releasing it, storageTakeover.run opens each fence only once its dataset passes the takeover step again.
- **Fences close before a hold is recorded** (`controller.storageTakeover.run`): storageTakeover.run closes every fence before recording a hold in Kubernetes, so no write reaches a dataset known to have lost writes while Kubernetes is slow; a hold that cannot be recorded stays in force until the process stops.
- **Held datasets are fenced with their acknowledged epoch as floor** (`controller.storageTakeover.fenceHeld`): storageTakeover.fenceHeld advances, after the hold is recorded and in the background, every dataset that serves a held contract the record says it acknowledged, so a replaced controller cannot write to it; each advance uses the acknowledged epoch as floor so a dataset that lost it refuses and keeps evidence. A contract now served by another dataset is left alone, since advancing it would bind it to this installation before an operator chose the switch; advances retry until success, final refusal, or the hold ends.
- **Only a lower stored epoch makes an advance refusal final** (`controller.storageTakeover.fenceHeldDataset`): fenceHeldDataset and storageTakeover.advance treat a refused advance as final only when the dataset holds a lower epoch than the floor; a dataset that is briefly not ready is tried again.
- **Opened datasets are watched while others are unreachable** (`controller.storageTakeover.openAndMaintain`): storageTakeover.openAndMaintain runs the watermark loop alongside every contract's takeover step, so a dataset that opened is watched even while another is still unreachable.
- **A raised hold never waits on a stuck provider call** (`controller.storageTakeover.openAndMaintain`): storageTakeover.openAndMaintain returns a raised hold immediately so the caller closes all fences and publishes it without waiting on a possibly stuck maintenance call for an already-opened dataset.
- **Ending a takeover attempt does not wait for its workers** (`controller.storageTakeover.endAttempt`): storageTakeover.endAttempt does not wait for contract workers, because a provider call that ignores its context would hold back the hold that ends the attempt; workers of an ended attempt change nothing.
- **Switch checks own fence opening while running** (`controller.storageTakeover.acknowledged`): storageTakeover.acknowledged marks a contract's acknowledgement recorded but leaves its fence for the watermark loop's check to open while a restart or switch of the dataset is being checked; workers of an ended attempt, or of an untracked dataset, open nothing.
- **Hold release records acknowledgement without advancing** (`controller.storageTakeover.release`): storageTakeover.release records what each dataset holds now as acknowledged and clears the hold in one update but advances nothing, so a dataset that lost its acknowledged epoch keeps that evidence until the new acknowledgement is recorded; the next takeover step advances against the new record.
- **Released holds reopen fences only after a new takeover check** (`controller.storageTakeover.release`): storageTakeover.release closes every fence; each reopens only after its dataset passes the takeover step against the new record, which may name another dataset.
- **Switch watching starts from the advance's incarnation** (`controller.storageTakeover.check`): storageTakeover.check tracks the dataset from the incarnation the advance described, shared by every contract on it, so a switch after the advance is checked even if this contract described the dataset later.
- **Datasets that lost writes are left as found** (`controller.storageTakeover.advance`): storageTakeover.advance compares a dataset's epoch and write watermark with the highest any contract acknowledged on it; a dataset holding less is not advanced so its evidence stays even if the hold cannot be recorded, and otherwise the advance supplies the acknowledged epoch as floor so the provider refuses a dataset that lost it meanwhile.
- **State dataset watermark writes wait for the state fence** (`controller.storageTakeover.track`): storageTakeover.track makes the state contract's dataset wait for the same fence CurrentFence waits for, because that dataset writes the watermark.
- **Unfinished switch checks rerun at the same incarnation** (`controller.storageTakeover.tickDataset`): storageTakeover.tickDataset reruns an unfinished check even when the dataset reports the incarnation checked last, as after a switchover that was seen and then rolled back.
- **Restarted or switched datasets get no writes until rechecked** (`controller.storageTakeover.tickDataset`): storageTakeover.tickDataset closes the fences of every contract on a dataset whose database restarted or switched until it passes its check again.
- **Returned watermarks count as acknowledged** (`controller.storageTakeover.tickDataset`): storageTakeover.tickDataset counts a watermark as acknowledged once the dataset's advance returned it, so a later switch check uses it even if recording it in Kubernetes fails.
- **Switch checks fail closed** (`controller.storageTakeover.checkSwitch`): storageTakeover.checkSwitch leaves a check unfinished when a read fails, so the caller neither trusts nor writes to the dataset until a later check finishes, and any switch of an async-replicated dataset raises a hold whatever it holds.
- **A dataset taken over by another controller stays fenced without a hold** (`controller.storageTakeover.checkSwitch`): When a dataset holds a later epoch or this epoch for another holder, storageTakeover.checkSwitch leaves its check unfinished so this controller's fences stay closed, and raises no hold on the new owner's behalf.
- **Task success is checked on every surface** (`controller.taskSuccess`): taskSuccess tracks phase, recorded workload outcome, and ACP execution separately because each is written on its own: the outcome before the phase, an ACP execution before its delivery settles, and a failed or cancelled delivery keeps a successful execution under a non-success phase.
- **No Task reports new success while storage is held** (`controller.admitTaskStatus`): admitTaskStatus routes a status write through AdmitWork whenever it reports success on a surface that did not before, so no Task reports success while storage is held for recovery, even when the hold is raised after the storage writes that preceded the status write; other status writes run directly.
- **Successor reuses the open SQLite store** (`controller.TestRestoredSQLiteDatasetThatLostAnEpochKeepsItUntilReleased`): TestRestoredSQLiteDatasetThatLostAnEpochKeepsItUntilReleased hands the second controller the same open SQLite store instead of reopening the file, so the database file is never reopened while the first store's connections are still winding down.
- **Watermark baseline is read after the switch** (`controller.TestSwitchCheckDoesNotOpenAFenceWhoseAcknowledgementIsUnrecorded`): TestSwitchCheckDoesNotOpenAFenceWhoseAcknowledgementIsUnrecorded reads the dataset watermark only after triggering the switch and waits for it to rise by two, because the watermark loop advances it again only after the switch check passed; reading earlier would let a pre-switch tick satisfy the wait.
