- **Failed-attempt cleanup preserves an attached or staged bearer** (`controller.WorkspaceAttachmentManager`): When cleaning up a failed attachment attempt, WorkspaceAttachmentManager keeps the bearer Secret if the workspace's current attachment or a staged attachment intent names it, because a handed-off orphan or staged intent can race the paused original attempt; the workspace's optimistic patch decides which attachment won.
- **Delegated gateway tools stay out of the parent's policy** (`controller.TaskReconciler.freezeGatewayTaskGrant`): freezeGatewayTaskGrant adds spec.coordination.delegatedGatewayTools to the frozen gateway grant so children can inherit them, but never to the Task's tool policy, so the broker refuses the parent's own calls; a Task with no root or inherited grant passes nothing on.
- **External thread lookup errors do not block CI repair** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorAutomaticRepairIntent`): repositoryMonitorAutomaticRepairIntent defers an error from looking up external review threads until it knows CI is not broken, so a transient GitHub failure never blocks a broken-CI repair; the error surfaces only when there is no CI fix to make.
- **Job log tail failures never abort CI evidence** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.repositoryMonitorAddActionsJobLogTail`): repositoryMonitorAddActionsJobLogTail adds a bounded, sanitized, redacted tail of a failing GitHub Actions job log as optional evidence; any failure fetching it leaves the tail empty and never aborts collecting the rest of the CI evidence.
- **Job log fetches never forward the token across origins** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.fetchRepositoryMonitorActionsJobLog`): fetchRepositoryMonitorActionsJobLog never forwards the GitHub bearer token to a cross-origin redirect target, because GitHub Actions answers log requests with a 302 to a pre-signed blob URL that needs no credential.
- **CI log timestamps are stripped before sanitizing** (`controller.repositoryMonitorEvidenceTailText`): repositoryMonitorEvidenceTailText strips GitHub Actions runner timestamp prefixes before sanitizing, because the sanitizer recognizes multiline credential headers such as `password: |` only at the start of a line, and it sanitizes the whole bounded log before keeping the trailing lines.
- **External threads are trust-gated per comment** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread`): buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread admits an external review thread only when its opening author is trusted, and includes a follow-up comment only when that comment's author is also trusted, so untrusted text never reaches a repair prompt.
- **Thread revisions ignore the prompt budget** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread`): buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread computes a thread's revision from every trusted comment, excluding Orka's own durably recorded replies, before applying the prompt's per-thread and rune limits, so trimming the prompt never changes whether a thread counts as already handled.
- **Acknowledged threads do not consume the rune budget** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread`): buildRepositoryMonitorExternalReviewThread checks whether a thread revision was already acknowledged before charging the global rune budget, so already handled unresolved threads never starve later unacknowledged ones.
- **No replies without structured confirmation** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair`): replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair posts no reply and resolves no review thread unless the repair agent returned explicit structured addressedThreads; a missing result or unavailable result store fails closed instead of fabricating fixed replies.
- **Threads resolve only on the pushed head** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair`): replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair resolves review threads only after a live pull request read confirms the head SHA still equals the repair's pushed SHA; a failed read or a moved head resolves nothing.
- **Unknown thread statuses fall back to human** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair`): replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair maps any status the repair agent returns other than fixed, wont or human to human, so an unrecognized status is never promoted to fixed and never resolves a thread.
- **Handled threads are acknowledged by revision** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair`): replyExternalReviewThreadsAfterRepair records the handled thread's revision so an unchanged unresolved thread is not requeued on the pushed head, while a new trusted comment on the same thread changes the revision and makes it eligible again.
- **Rebase commit timestamps come from the persisted mutation** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.submitRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranchRebase`): submitRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranchRebase derives the rebase commit timestamp from the persisted mutation (its creation time truncated to seconds, or its pending time) rather than the clock, so a recovery replay submits the exact same immutable ExternalEffect digest.
- **Rebase retries reuse the captured base SHA** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.submitRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranchRebase`): submitRepositoryMonitorUpdateBranchRebase rebases onto the base SHA captured on the mutation, falling back to the pull request's base only when none is stored, so a retry submits the same immutable ExternalEffect digest even after the base branch advances.
- **Only explicit publisher rejections reset a rebase** (`controller.RepositoryMonitorReconciler.recordRepositoryMonitorRebaseRetryableError`): recordRepositoryMonitorRebaseRetryableError resets an update-branch mutation to started only when the publisher explicitly rejected it as retryable (such as HTTP 429 busy) with no transport error, because the push then provably did not run; any other retryable error keeps the mutation submitting so it is reconciled rather than resent as new.
- **update-branch 422 is permanent** (`controller.repositoryMonitorUpdateBranchSubmissionRetryable`): repositoryMonitorUpdateBranchSubmissionRetryable treats GitHub's 422 from PUT /pulls/{number}/update-branch as permanent, because it means merge conflicts or an expected_head_sha mismatch that no retry can resolve, and retrying would burn repair budget.
- **Admission timings use controller-local times** (`controller.acpRuntimeAdmissionTimings`): acpRuntimeAdmissionTimings measures workspace RuntimePool admission from the first reconcile that sees current startup evidence, through credential bootstrap, to serving and accepting, using times taken in the controller process; the provider's DataPlaneReady condition time has whole-second precision and is not used. A pool that closes and reopens within one workload sequence logs a readmission line naming the lifecycle it reopened from.
- **Admission is logged from the stored pool** (`controller.acpRuntimeAdmissionTimings.served`): acpRuntimeAdmissionTimings.served logs an admission only once the stored RuntimePool status serves and accepts, reading it back, because the pass's own copy is set before a status write that can conflict without an error; a still-pending admission keeps its record for the pass that lands it.
- **Booting supervisors are re-probed after one second** (`controller.runtimePoolBootingRequeue`): runtimePoolBootingRequeue re-probes a supervisor that answered while booting after one second, because booting is a short in-process phase that writes no Kubernetes object the pool watches, so the general requeue would add up to that whole interval to admission.
- **RuntimePool reconciles may run concurrently** (`controller.DefaultRuntimePoolMaxConcurrentReconciles`): RuntimePools reconcile concurrently (DefaultRuntimePoolMaxConcurrentReconciles) so independent cold starts do not queue behind each other. This is safe only while every object a pool reconcile creates or writes is named for or linked one-to-one with that pool, writes to shared objects are create-if-absent or resourceVersion-checked, and the reconciler keeps no mutable state between reconciles.
- **A Suspend authorized before a taint stays** (`controller.externalRetirementAction`): externalRetirementAction keeps a Suspend already authorized for the exact current instance even after the workspace becomes tainted, because rewriting it to Stop mid-capture would abort a provider operation in an unspecified state without removing data already captured; the workspace's Delete disposition is what removes that data.
- **Runtime timing annotations only measure** (`controller.externalWorkloadPublishedAnnotation`): The execution-workspace timing annotations externalWorkloadPublishedAnnotation, externalRetirementAuthorizedAnnotation and externalRuntimeStopRequestedAnnotation each record "<sequence> <RFC3339Nano>" and are used only to measure how long a runtime took to start or stop, never to make a reconcile decision; the stop-requested one is written in the retirement authorization patch and is absent when this process did not see the retirement begin, such as after a restart.
- **DataPlaneReady times are second-truncated** (`controller.externalRuntimeReadyAt`): externalRuntimeReadyAt treats a DataPlaneReady report in the publication's own second as ready at publication, because condition times keep only whole seconds and can read as slightly before it; readiness from an earlier second or an earlier generation can predate the sequence and is ignored.
- **Stop phases ignore detach and settlement times** (`controller.externalRuntimeStopPhases`): externalRuntimeStopPhases measures Core's drain from the first observed retirement to its authorization, and the provider's retirement up to Core's proof the instance stopped; it does not use detach or settlement times because settlement can suspend the workspace after the pool already began or finished its drain.
- **Stop proof time is the first proof** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.noteExternalRuntimeStopProven`): noteExternalRuntimeStopProven keeps when Core first proved the retirement's termination, so a Stopped status write that fails and is retried does not count Core's retry delay as the provider's retirement time.
- **Timing report dedup lives in memory** (`controller.externalRuntimeTimingReports`): externalRuntimeTimingReports deduplicates start and stop timing reports per RuntimePool in memory only so it costs no write; after a controller restart one already-reported transition can be reported again, and a retirement already under way is not given a start.
- **Timing reports reuse the reconciled workspace** (`controller.withExternalRuntimeTimingWorkspace`): withExternalRuntimeTimingWorkspace carries the workspace the reconcile already read and patched, so a persisted transition is reported from it rather than lost when a second read fails.
- **Runtime transitions are reported after persistence** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.observeExternalRuntimeTransition`): observeExternalRuntimeTransition runs only after a status write persisted the transition; it reports a start the first time an instance admits work, even if an earlier probe recorded it while booting, and a stop when the pool first records Stopped for a retirement.
- **Nothing republishes before the provider reports the allocation** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspace`): While the provider has not yet reported the allocation for the published request, the RuntimePool neither republishes the request nor rotates credentials; it waits for the provider to catch up.
- **Attachment lag keeps eviction checks** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspace`): When only an attachment or its re-admission moved the workspace generation, the admitted instance keeps serving untouched until provider and core catch up (ADR 0057), but worker eviction checks stay active so a terminating worker still enters its deadline-bound drain.
- **A published successor waits for the provider** (`controller.externalPublishedSuccessorAwaitsProvider`): externalPublishedSuccessorAwaitsProvider recognizes the window between publishing a cold boot that succeeds a stopped allocation (same allocation key, next sequence, naming that instance as predecessor) and the provider's first report of it, so a still-reported stopped allocation in that window is not treated as failing to prove the current runtime terminated.
- **Attach racing scale-down restores demand** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement sets DesiredReplicas back to 1 when an attachment landed concurrently with scale-down to zero on a Ready workspace with no retirement, instead of draining and retiring a live attached runtime.
- **Early rotation rests on the termination proof** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement`): reconcileExternalWorkspaceRetirement writes the Stopped report first, unchanged, and the credential rotation after it relies on the independent termination proof just observed, not on the Stopped record (ADR 0057).
- **Rotation steps run on freshly read state** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.rotateWorkspaceRuntimePoolAuthAfterProvenStop`): rotateWorkspaceRuntimePoolAuthAfterProvenStop unpublishes the consumed Secret, replaces it, and then clears the bootstrap-instance binding, one step per loop iteration and each on freshly read state, so the binding is cleared last (ADR 0057).
- **Deleted pools report stop at finalizer release** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.observeExternalRuntimeStop`): A deleted RuntimePool never records Stopped, so its released finalizer is the persisted proof that the runtime stopped and is where the stop timing is reported.
- **Evidence and credential binding share one write** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.bindExternalRuntimeInstanceAdmission`): bindExternalRuntimeInstanceAdmission writes instance evidence and the bootstrap credential binding in one optimistic pool write but validates each as if written separately: conflicting evidence writes nothing, while a credential binding to another workload still records the evidence and skips only the binding.
- **Admission binding patches keep their locks** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler.patchExternalRuntimeAdmissionBindings`): patchExternalRuntimeAdmissionBindings writes instance evidence under an optimistic lock and a credential binding alone with a plain merge patch, matching how each was written on its own.
- **Published cold boot waits for the provider** (`controller.RuntimePoolReconciler external cold boot`): After a resume publishes a cold boot, Core re-admits the new generation before the provider reports the new allocation, so passes in between still see the predecessor's stopped allocation. The RuntimePool treats that as a wait, not Degraded, and never publishes again (TestExternalRuntimePoolPublishedResumeWaitsForProviderWithoutDegrading).
- **Tests advance Generation by hand** (`controller runtime pool resume tests`): The controller-runtime fake client does not bump metadata.generation on spec updates, so RuntimePool and ExecutionWorkspace resume tests advance Generation explicitly to model what the API server does for each spec change.
- **Task handshake must not conflict a resumed pool's Serving write** (`controller.TestExternalRuntimePoolResumeServesWhileTheTaskHandshakeLands`): A resumed runtime must reach Serving with one probe per pass and no conflicted status write even while the waiting Task finishes its workspace handshake. A replica-only generation change must not make the Task re-stamp image provenance, because that status write conflicted the pool's optimistic Serving write and reran attest, bootstrap, probe and fence.
- **ACP wake watches only wake reconciles earlier** (`controller.registerACPTaskWakeWatches`): The ExecutionWorkspace and RuntimePool watches on the Task controller only wake the Tasks whose ACP workspace gates read the changed object sooner. Every gate still re-reads and re-verifies its inputs, and the RequeueAfter polls stay as the fallback for a missed event.
- **Session index matches the selectable field** (`controller.acpTaskSessionNameIndex`): acpTaskSessionNameIndex indexes Tasks by spec.sessionRef.name under the same name the Task CRD exposes as a selectable field, so cached and uncached readers use one identical field selector.
- **Settled Tasks are not woken by their workspace** (`controller.addLinkedACPWorkspaceTasks`): addLinkedACPWorkspaceTasks wakes only linked Tasks whose detach action has not settled; a settled Task's workspace gates are all behind it, so a long-lived Session workspace does not wake its whole history.
- **Workspace wakes ignore churn gates do not read** (`controller.acpWorkspaceTaskWakePredicate`): acpWorkspaceTaskWakePredicate passes creates, deletes and updates that change something a Task workspace gate reads; resourceVersion or managedFields churn, condition message rewording, and endpoint or connection-secret refreshes do not wake Tasks.
- **Task-written demand annotations never wake Tasks** (`controller.acpWorkspaceTaskWrittenDemandAnnotations`): The resume/materialization demand and frozen detach action annotations are rewritten by waiting Tasks themselves, and two live Tasks of one Session can rewrite them in turn while a suspension settles. Waking on them would let those Tasks re-trigger each other with no delay, so their changes are left to the fallback poll and the spec/status changes of real handshakes.
- **Pool wakes ignore capacity and probe churn** (`controller.acpRuntimePoolTaskWakePredicate`): acpRuntimePoolTaskWakePredicate wakes Tasks only for pool identity, lifecycle, admission, or active-instance changes (ignoring LastObservedTime); reservation and prompt-count churn and probe timestamp refreshes do not, because the dispatcher owns capacity admission.
- **Task admission limits use a slot ledger** (`controller task admission slot ledger`): --max-tasks-per-namespace and maxConcurrentChildren admit Tasks through a slot ledger Lease in the controller namespace, updated with resourceVersion checks so two contenders never both take the last slot and waiting Tasks never block each other. A grant counts only when it names a live, schedule-free Task of the scope by UID, and every admission requires the running Tasks and holders ahead of it to number fewer than the limit, so a forged or stale grant can only delay admission, never raise capacity.
- **Admission limits are capped at 4096 slots** (`controller.MaxTaskAdmissionSlots`): MaxTaskAdmissionSlots caps every admission limit at 4096 because a ledger holds at most limit grants of about 40 bytes of JSON each, keeping a full ledger near 160KiB, under the 256KiB Kubernetes allows for all of an object's annotations. --max-tasks-per-namespace is validated against it, and a larger maxConcurrentChildren is clamped to it.
- **Scope Tasks are listed after each ledger read** (`controller.taskAdmissionScope.list`): taskAdmissionScope.list is called after each ledger read, so every grant the read returns belongs to a Task the list can see unless that Task is gone; a fresh grant is never mistaken for a finished Task's and pruned.
- **Children count only for their parent incarnation** (`controller.taskAdmissionChildOf`): taskAdmissionChildOf counts a child against a parent only when its controller owner reference or parent UID annotation pins that parent's UID; a child pinned to an earlier Task of the same name is not counted, while a child with no pin is counted because that can only delay admission.
- **Running work holds a slot without a grant** (`controller.taskAdmittedByWork`): taskAdmittedByWork counts a Task as a slot holder when its own state shows it admitted (Running, Finalizing, or Pending with bound execution, a created Job, or an ACP attempt), so Tasks admitted before ledgers existed are not overtaken. A recurring Task's scheduling parent never holds a slot: it stays Scheduled and each run is a child Task with its own slot.
- **Retry backoff keeps its slot** (`controller.taskAdmissionRetryPending`): taskAdmissionRetryPending treats a Pending Task whose retry-pending condition is set as admitted, because retryTask clears its Job and that condition is the only sign it was admitted; it keeps its slot while it backs off.
- **Claimants over a lowered limit proceed in order** (`controller.taskAdmissionView.admits`): taskAdmissionView.admits lets a claimant wait only for the running Tasks and the claimants ordered before it, so claimants over a lowered limit proceed one at a time instead of each waiting for all others; any other Task waits for every running Task and claimant, and the admitted total never exceeds the limit.
- **Claimants are ordered oldest first in every scope** (`controller.newTaskAdmissionView`): newTaskAdmissionView orders claimants by creation time then UID (taskAdmissionOrderedBefore), the same in every scope, so a Task ahead in one ledger is ahead in all and two Tasks never each hold a slot the other waits for. No grant is dropped: an unelected claimant keeps its place.
- **Never grant a Task the scope does not list** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskAdmissionSlot`): taskAdmissionSlot refuses to write a grant for a Task the scope does not list as grant-eligible, because the next request would discard that grant and let such Tasks run past the limit.
- **Admission ledger writes are compare-and-swap** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskAdmissionSlot`): Every admission ledger grant or release is a resourceVersion-checked Update of the ledger Lease, so a concurrent grant conflicts and the slots are counted again; never replace it with a blind Patch or Apply.
- **Owned live Jobs count as running** (`controller.TaskReconciler.taskAdmissionJobOwners`): taskAdmissionJobOwners counts a Task as running while it owns a live Job even when its status does not name the Job, because a controller that stopped between creating the Job and recording it left no other trace. Jobs come from the cache, which syncs before any Task reconciles.
- **Held Tasks release grants in other scopes** (`controller.TaskReconciler.releaseTaskAdmissionSlot`): A Task held in one admission scope releases its grant in the others via releaseTaskAdmissionSlot, so no waiting Task holds a grant in one ledger while waiting in another and no two Tasks wait for each other. A Task running work keeps its grants.
- **Ledger reaping is conditional** (`controller.TaskReconciler.reapTaskAdmissionLedgers`): reapTaskAdmissionLedgers deletes a ledger only when none of its grants belongs to a live Task of its scope and its namespace or coordinator is gone or finished, or no grant was made for taskAdmissionLedgerStaleAfter. The delete is preconditioned on UID and resourceVersion so a grant made meanwhile keeps the ledger.
- **Test emulates delete preconditions** (`controller.TestRecreateWorkerRoleBindingKeepsAConcurrentReplacement`): TestRecreateWorkerRoleBindingKeepsAConcurrentReplacement intercepts Delete to return a Conflict on a UID precondition mismatch, because the fake client ignores delete preconditions that the API server enforces.
- **Task reconciles run four in parallel safely** (`controller.DefaultTaskMaxConcurrentReconciles`): DefaultTaskMaxConcurrentReconciles is 4 because independent Tasks may reconcile in parallel: a Task never reconciles concurrently with itself, admission limits grant slots through a resourceVersion-checked ledger, writes to objects Tasks share are create-if-absent or resourceVersion-checked, and reconciler state is lock-protected. New shared writes must keep those properties.
- **ACP agent Tasks chain admission in one pass** (`controller.acpTaskChainsAdmission`): acpTaskChainsAdmission lets an ACP agent Task continue in one reconcile from adding its finalizer through Pending into binding, workspace attachment and queueing, using each write's returned object; each step still writes and checks exactly as in its own pass. Job-backed and scheduled Tasks keep one step per reconcile.
- **Namespace slot before child slot** (`controller.TaskReconciler.acquireNamespaceAdmission`): A coordination child takes its namespace slot before its child slot, takes no namespace slot while its child slot is not free, and gives up any namespace grant while waiting for a sibling (releaseTaskAdmissionSlot), so two siblings never each hold the slot the other waits for.
- **Retry backoff takes durable grants** (`controller.TaskReconciler.holdRetryAdmission`): holdRetryAdmission takes or keeps a retrying Task's grant in every admission ledger before the backoff delay returns, so its slot rests on a durable grant and not only on the retry-pending condition. It takes slots in admission order, namespace before children, so two Tasks never each hold the slot the other waits for.
- **Pinned children re-read their parent uncached** (`controller.TaskReconciler.checkCoordinationConstraints`): A coordination child pinned to an earlier parent UID is failed rather than admitted under a same-named replacement whose children scope does not count it. Because the cache may still hold the earlier parent, every pinned child refreshes the parent from the API server before checking its UID.
- **Job creation checks the storage hold first** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): While storage is held for recovery Orka starts no work; no storage write guards a Job's creation, so createTaskJob checks ControllerEpochManager.StorageHold before preparing anything for the Job.
- **Job create runs inside AdmitWork** (`controller.TaskReconciler.createTaskJob`): createTaskJob issues the Job Create inside ControllerEpochManager.AdmitWork so a storage hold raised while the Job was prepared either waits for this creation or refuses it.
- **Agent LastUsed only moves forward** (`controller.TaskReconciler.updateAgentLastUsed`): updateAgentLastUsed re-reads the Agent on every attempt and never moves LastUsed backward, because Tasks of one Agent can complete in any order, concurrently under parallel reconciles, and the Agent TTL counts from LastUsed; the optimistic Update lets a concurrent newer write win.
- **Delete only the exact stale worker RoleBinding** (`controller.TaskReconciler.recreateWorkerRoleBinding`): Worker RoleBinding recreation deletes only the exact stale object read, preconditioned on its UID, because a concurrent reconcile of another Task in the namespace may already have replaced it with the desired binding that running workers depend on.

## Consequences

- These decisions are still in force. A change that breaks one must supersede that
  entry in a new ADR.
- Entries were recovered from comments in bulk, and a few original comments were stale or
  overstated. If an entry disagrees with the code, treat that as a bug in the entry: confirm the
  intended behavior, then fix the code or supersede the entry in a new ADR.
- Symbols get renamed. When an entry's symbol no longer exists, search for the behavior it
  describes.
- Entries recovered from test comments record why a test is shaped the way it is, so the test
  stays honest. A later ADR may supersede them once the tests explain themselves.
